# Bugs

Known compiler defects: incorrect output, crashes, miscompiles, rejection of valid code, missing diagnostics for unsafe code.

For missing features and language design gaps see `TODO.md`.

Each entry's first line is a **[priority size]** tag plus a one-sentence summary; the full description follows on the next lines. Priority is one of:

- `IMM` -- users hit this in their first non-trivial program (or basic Python idioms);
- `HIGH` -- users hit this regularly in non-trivial programs / stdlib work;
- `MED` -- specific patterns or advanced features;
- `LOW` -- latent / quality / hardening, no current user impact.

Size is one of `small` (hours-1d), `medium` (1-3d), `large` (~week), `xlarge` (bigger).

Entries tagged `deferred: MIR` are gated on the THIR/MIR migration (see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions 9/11/13) and must NOT be patched in the current AST/sema model -- the clean fix lands in the IR. Do not select them for `/tpy-next` or `/tpy-fix-bug`.

## Compiler bugs
- **[MED small] (rejects valid, loud) `await` inside an `except ... as e:` handler body reads `e` out of scope in the generated coroutine.** The handler binding is a C++ `catch (const MyErr& e)` parameter, but the resumable frame splits the handler body at the suspension, so the post-resume code reads `e` from a later, disjoint `case` block after the catch scope has already exited -- g++ reports `'e' was not declared in this scope`. Idiomatic async retry/logging code (`try: await op() / except TimeoutError as e: await report(e)`) is valid Python and gets raw compiler spew. Root cause is that an except capture is never given frame storage: it lives only in the catch parameter, so it has nowhere to survive a suspension. The sibling constructs each have partial coverage -- `_prescan_with_stmts` in `codegen_cpp/gen_async.py` allocates a `with` slot, and `needs_frame_field` promotes a match capture -- but both gates are narrower than the construct (see the two entries below), and `except` has no gate at all. Fix shape: give a handler binding read after a suspension a frame slot and copy the caught value into it at catch time, so the resume block reads the slot rather than the dead parameter. Verified on master, independent of the capture-registration work. Three separate review passes converged on this same missing-frame-storage gap, of which this is the sharpest form. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH small] (silent wrong output) A `with ... as NAME` target that is REASSIGNED after the block and read across a suspension gets two storage locations, and the read returns the uninitialized one.** In a generator, `with c as g: pass` / `g = 9` / `yield 1` / `yield g` prints `1` then `22390` (uninitialized memory) where CPython prints `1` then `9`. The reassignment registers `g` as an ordinary frame-resident local, so the frame struct carries a field, while codegen's with-target emit also declares a shadowing body-local -- `g = 9` writes the local, and the post-resume `yield g` reads the frame field, which nothing ever assigned. Silent, and the value is whatever the frame memory held. Pre-existing: the two storage locations come from codegen's with-target emit plus the ordinary var-decl registration, neither of which the capture-registration work touched (`frame_exempt` is overridden back to False by the later `bind_variable`, so it has no effect on this shape). Repro `/tmp/agents/scopens/p_with_rebind.py`. Fix shape: the with target must resolve to ONE slot -- if it is frame-resident, the body must bind through the frame field rather than declaring a shadow. Related to the two frame-storage entries above/below but distinct: this one is silent rather than a build failure. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects valid, loud) A `with ... as NAME` target read across a suspension gets NO frame slot when the `with` body itself does not suspend.** `_prescan_with_stmts` (`codegen_cpp/gen_async.py:2531-2533`) allocates the slot only when the `with` is `async` or its own body contains a suspension, so `with c as guard: pass` / `yield 1` / `yield guard.n` in a generator emits `auto& guard = ...` inside one switch-`case` and reads it from a later one -- g++ reports `'guard' was not declared in this scope`. CPython prints `1` then `5`. The gate needs to ask whether the target is read after any suspension anywhere in the body, not whether the `with` body suspends. Pre-existing (a with target was never namespace-registered before the capture-registration work, so it got no `generator_locals` slot either) and verified independent of it. Repro `/tmp/agents/scopens/p_with_later_yield.py`. This is the `with` member of the same missing-frame-storage family as the `except`/`await` and match-capture entries. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects valid, loud) A return-tier `try`/`except` in which EVERY path returns emits an unreachable fallthrough with no return, so `-Werror=return-type` rejects it.** `def outer(k: str) -> Int32: try: return inner(k) / except NotFound as e: return e.code` (with `inner` `@error_return`) emits the `goto`-based return tier ending `__after_try_1:; }` -- both real paths return, but the label is still a syntactic fallthrough point, so g++ reports "control reaches end of non-void function". A plain `uv run tpy` builds it (the harness compiles with warnings-as-errors, which is what surfaces it), so it bites the test suite and any `-Werror` build. Pre-existing and independent of name shadowing -- reproduced with a non-colliding binding, `/tmp/agents/scopens/p_rt_plain.py`. Existing return-tier corpus cases miss it because they re-raise or continue after the try rather than returning on every path. Workaround: assign in the handler and return after the try (what `tests/cases/name_shadowing/except_return_tier_shadow` does). Fix shape: emit an unreachable-return (or `__builtin_unreachable`) after the join label when every tier path terminates. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects valid, loud) A `match` capture that persists past the match and is read by a LATER suspension gets no frame slot.** `needs_frame_field` (`sema/match.py:171-175`) only scans for suspensions inside the match statement's own arms, but a capture is an ordinary function local in Python and stays live afterwards -- so `case 5 as hit: ...` followed later in the body by `yield hit` leaves `hit` as a C++ local scoped to one switch-`case`, read out of scope from a later one. Undiagnosed C++ build failure. The predicate needs to ask whether the capture is read after any suspension anywhere in the body, not just within the match. Mechanism verified against `sema/match.py`; the build failure itself was reasoned rather than reproduced, so confirm with a probe before fixing. Pre-existing (byte-identical on master). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects valid, loud) A `with ... as NAME` / `case ... as NAME` capture reusing a name already bound at an INCOMPATIBLE type is accepted, then fails the C++ build.** `_analyze_with` (`sema/statements.py`) calls `current_scope.define` unconditionally, bypassing the reassignment-type-mismatch check that guards a plain `x = ...`, and `_gen_with`'s `already_declared` branch (`codegen_cpp/statements.py:3512-3516`) reuses the prior C++ slot and type -- so the emitted `x = &(__ctx.__enter__());` assigns a `Counter*` into an `int32_t`. The match-capture form has the same shape (a `BigInt` field assigned into a `std::string_view` local). Both are pre-existing and were rejected on master too, but by accident and with a misleading message (`Record 'Int32' has no field 'n'`, pointing at the stale prior type); once name resolution resolves the capture correctly the program reaches codegen unguarded. A located sema reject is the minimum this owes. Repro `/tmp/agents/metareview/withas.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (misleading diagnostic) A read of a name shadowed by a later-arm `match` capture reports callability, not the shadowing.** `case Cat(lives=helper): ... / case Dog(): return helper()` where `helper` is also a module function reports `'helper' is not callable` -- correct to reject (CPython raises `UnboundLocalError`, since the capture makes the name a function-local), but the message names neither the `match` capture nor the scoping rule, so a user whose code compiled before this became reachable has no path from the message to the cause. Pinned by `tests/cases/name_shadowing/error_match_capture_shadows_function`. Fix shape: when the shadowing binding is a pattern capture, say so and point at the capture. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (accepts invalid) A `match` capture does not make its name a function-local for the arms BEFORE it, so an earlier arm can still reach an outer binding of that name.** Python makes a capture pattern's name a function-local everywhere in the enclosing function, so any arm referencing it raises `UnboundLocalError` unless that arm bound it -- verified: `case Cat(lives=helper): ... / case Dog(): return helper()` raises in CPython. Arms AFTER the capture behave correctly (the binding is visible and the read is rejected); arms BEFORE it still resolve the name to the module-level function and silently succeed. Same family as the post-handler `except` read entry below: TPy models captures as block-scoped where Python scopes them to the function. Closing both means function-scoped capture locals (bind-then-mark-dead), a variable-model change tracked in TODO.md -- not a local patch, and NOT fixable by resetting the namespace per arm, which would re-break the after-arms case that now works. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (missing safety diagnostic) The unconsumed-`Own` warning is WHOLE-TUPLE granular, so a dropped owned ELEMENT is never reported.** A scalar `Own[T]` param that is never consumed warns (`Own[Box] param 'x' is never consumed`), and a fully-owned tuple param that is never touched warns (`owned tuple param 'p' is never consumed`) -- but the tuple form is an all-or-nothing check on the aggregate. Two shapes slip through, both verified: (1) a fully-owned tuple that IS unpacked but then drops an element (`a, b = p; sink(a)` -- `b` is never consumed, no warning), since unpacking alone counts as consuming the tuple; and (2) a MIXED `tuple[Own[A], B]` param whose owned element is never consumed, which warns nothing at all because it is not an "owned tuple param" to begin with. Under the elementwise rule (`TODO.md`) element `i` should behave as `Ti` would as a singleton at that position, and a singleton `Own[T]` param warns -- so both should. The remediation is the same one the scalar warning implies: drop the `Own` from that element and take a borrow. Note shape (2) additionally cannot be consumed even if you try (see the mixed-param consume entry above), but the warning is a separate defect: it is missing for shape (1) too, where consumption works fine. Repro `/tmp/agents/mixstep1/consumed_matrix.py`. Surfaced by review of the pinned diag in `tests/cases/tuple/mixed_own_tuple_param_from_call`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (AST/THIR byte divergence, no corpus witness) A module GLOBAL of pointer-repr tuple type initialised from a value-captured tuple LITERAL emits an identity `tuple_to_storage` on the AST path and a bare literal on the THIR path.** `g: tuple[Int32, Cell] = (1, Cell(2))` at module level: AST emits `::tpy::tuple_to_storage<std::tuple<int32_t, Cell>>(std::tuple<int32_t, Cell>{1, Cell(2)})`, THIR emits the literal alone. Same runtime result (the wrap is a storage-to-storage identity), so it is a byte divergence rather than a miscompile -- but the THIR contract is byte-identity, and no corpus case holds the shape, so nothing catches it. Pre-existing: reproduces unchanged on master. Shows the identity-lift cleanup is partial -- `_wrap_for_owned_slot` gates on the SOURCE form and so drops the identity wrap, while `_maybe_wrap_tuple_to_storage` still emits it for this shape. Note `tuple/element_vs_singleton_global` does NOT cover it (that case falls back at top level). Fix shape: gate the remaining site on identity as well, or give the shape a case and pick one spelling. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready second opinion of the tuple-element storage-form wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A COMPREHENSION whose element is a tuple literal mixing a fresh constructor with a borrowed reference fails the C++ build.** `xs = [(Node(i), b) for i in range(2)]` (`b` a borrowed `Box` param) emits `tuple_to_storage<std::tuple<Node, Box>>(tuple_value_to_borrow<std::tuple<Node*, Box*>>(std::tuple<Node, Box*>{Node(i), &(b)}))` -- g++ rejects it (`invalid conversion from 'const Box*' to 'Box*'`, plus no matching tuple ctor). The comprehension round-trips the element through borrow form on its way to storage; the equivalent list LITERAL `[(Node(1), b)]` builds and runs (verified both). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Pre-existing: the emitted C++ is byte-identical at merge base `88c3867ee1`, so neither the tuple-element storage-form wave nor the @classmethod work introduced it. Distinct from the generic-element and borrowed-scalar list-literal entries, which cover different shapes. Repro `/tmp/agents/mergecheck/probe3/src/main.py`. Surfaced by the semantic-conflict pass of `/tpy-merge-master`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] (rejects-valid, loud) An `Optional` of a mixed owned+borrow tuple (`tuple[Own[A], B] | None`) fails the C++ build, at its RETURN and at any sink.** Spellable only as a return type (a field / local / container annotation rejects the `Own` as redundant). Two blockers, measured on `/tmp/agents/mixdecl/h4_optreturn.py` + `h6_optreturn_mut.py`: (1) **param-const inference does not survive the Optional wrap** -- `def make_opt(b: Box, ok: bool) -> tuple[Own[Box], Box] | None` renders `b` as `const Box&` where the unwrapped `-> tuple[Own[Box], Box]` sibling correctly renders `Box&`, so the returned literal's `&(b)` is a `const Box*` the signature's `Box*` slot rejects; the borrow-escapes-into-the-return analysis does not look through `OptionalType`. (2) **the return ABI renders the storage inner** -- `std::optional<std::tuple<Box, Box>>` against a value of `std::tuple<Box, Box*>`, while the CALLER's local already spells `std::optional<std::tuple<Box, Box*>>` correctly. Blocker (2) is a DESIGN fork, not a fix: either the Optional return mirrors the bare mixed return (`optional<tuple<Box, Box*>>`, no copy, consistent with the elementwise rule and with what the caller spells) or the wrapper counts as an owning sink (materialize the borrowed half, warn, `copy()` to acknowledge) -- and the same question decides `Optional[tuple[Box, Box]]`, whose return ABI is storage form today. **The previously-recorded fix shape here was WRONG and is disproven:** recursing `owned_tuple_storage_type` through `OptionalType` (mirroring `collapse_tuple_own_elements`) BREAKS an Optional-wrapped purely-owned tuple at an owning sink -- `xs = [make_opt()]` for `-> tuple[Own[Box], Own[Box]] | None` compiles and runs today and stops compiling with the recursion (`incomplete type std::tuple_size<std::optional<std::tuple<Box, Box>>>`; probe `/tmp/agents/mixdecl/h7_opt_owned_sink.py`). Implemented and reverted on that measurement. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` for blocker (2), then `/tpy-fix-bug` for (1).
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A HOISTED mixed owned+borrow tuple local fails to build when its OWNED element is not C++-default-constructible.** `try: p = make_mixed(c)` / `except: p = make_mixed(c)` for `-> tuple[Own[Guard], Cell]` emits the hoisted decl `std::tuple<Guard, Cell*> p;` and then assigns -- but a class with `__del__` (a user `__del__`, or `Box[T]` / `Rc[T]` / `Arc[T]`) suppresses the C++ default ctor, so g++ reports `no matching function for call to tuple<Guard, Cell*>::tuple()`. The straight bind and the rebind are unaffected (both declare WITH an initializer), and the all-borrow sibling is immune (its elements are pointers, always default-constructible) -- this is specific to the mixed tuple's by-value owned half at the one decl path that must declare before it can assign. Fix shape: give the hoisted decl the deferred-init form its siblings already use -- a `std::optional<<mixed render>>` slot with `(*p)` reads, the same shape the walrus pre-decl and the resumable frame field take. Loud, toolchain-caught. Residual of the mixed-local decl-path fix (the shape failed differently before it). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/mixdecl/n1_nondefault_owned.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) An `Optional` of a tuple containing a record (`tuple[Rec, Int32] | None`) is unusable as a param: the slot takes value-form storage while the body and call site use borrow form.** The param renders `std::optional<std::tuple<Rec, int32_t>>`, but the body reads an element as a pointer (`std::get<0>((*t))->v` -- "base operand of `->` has non-pointer type") and the call site builds the borrow form (`std::tuple<Rec*, int32_t>` -- "could not convert ... to `std::optional<std::tuple<Rec, int32_t>>`"). Both errors reproduce with NO default on the param, so this half is pre-existing and independent of default handling -- part of the borrow/storage-form duality class (`docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md` "Borrow Form vs Storage Form", `docs/IR_DESIGN.md` Open Questions item 9). Distinct from the nearby tuple entries: no `Own` marker, no rvalue source, and the mismatch is the Optional-WRAPPED tuple param rather than a bare tuple. **Second, separable defect on the same shape:** adding a default (`= None`) emits `= std::nullopt` at the free-function declaration, instantiating `std::optional<std::tuple<Rec, ...>>` over the still-incomplete `Rec` -- the hazard `default_needs_call_site_fill` (`typesys.py`) exists to dodge, which it misses here because it tests the Optional's inner for `NominalType.is_user_record` and that inner is a `TupleType`. Fix shape: recurse the record search through tuple inners (and mirror it for `UnionType` members) -- but note this only removes the third error, so it is worth doing WITH the form fix, not before it; on its own it leaves a program that still does not compile. Toolchain-caught throughout, no silent wrongness. Repros `/tmp/agents/tuple_default_probe.py` (all three errors) and `/tmp/agents/tuple_nodefault_probe.py` (the two form errors alone, proving provenance). Surfaced by the semantic-conflict pass of `/tpy-merge-master` on `fix-positional-defaults`, probing whether master's new tuple-form facts interact with the default-suppression rule. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH small] (SILENT CPython divergence) A module GLOBAL of tuple-of-reference-type takes owning storage form where the scalar global takes a borrow slot, so it COPIES instead of aliasing.** `V = Box(2); G: Box = V` emits `Box* G` -- a borrow slot -- and aliases, so `G.n = 42` is visible on `V` (TPy 42, CPython 42), and the store even warns that `G` will not keep the object. The tuple spelling `G: tuple[Box, Box] = (V, V)` emits `std::tuple<Box, Box> G` -- owning storage -- so `G[0].n = 42` prints TPy **2** vs CPython **42**, with NO diagnostic. The `local` position already gets this right for tuples (`std::tuple<Box*, Box*>`, aliases, verified 42), so the global slot simply fails to follow the local's borrow form. Element form matters: value / `int` / `str` / `bytes` / `Own` globals are all correct at both arities -- only a reference-type element diverges. Root cause is the global classification asking whether the WHOLE type is a value type (`pointer_globals` in `codegen_cpp/generator.py`) -- a tuple answers yes even when every element is a reference, so it can never become a borrow slot. Fixing it is not that predicate: a borrow-form global needs the local's machinery mirrored at global scope (`_global_cpp_type` emitting `tuple_borrow_cpp`, the matching `extern`, `->` element reads, the assign lifting a storage source to pointer form, const-element handling, cross-module externs). Note a MIXED tuple global cannot take borrow form at all -- its owned element would point at a dead temporary -- so mixed stays storage and wants the copy warning instead. **Deliberately deferred out of the storage-form wave as too large; the cell is therefore still SILENT, not merely wrong.** Note the wave did not simply inherit the MIXED shape here -- `G: tuple[Box, Box] = make_mixed(B)` failed the C++ build before it and copies silently after, so the wave converted a loud error into a silently wrong program and made this sink reachable. (The all-borrow `(V, V)` shape was already silent.) That is the same argument that raised the nested-tuple entry to HIGH, and it applies with more force here. Pinned by `tests/cases/tuple/element_vs_singleton_global`, which records the wrong-but-shipping output (TPy `42 2 2` vs CPython `42 43 44`) so the fix has a regression target; that case's expectations flip when the global slot learns the local's borrow form. Repro `/tmp/agents/tuplecensus/cases3/{s,t}_global/main.py`. Surfaced by the tuple-element-vs-singleton census (`/tpy-add-feature` on the TODO elementwise-form entry). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH small] (rejects-valid + silent divergence in the same cell) A borrowed tuple passed to an `Own[T]` CALL-ARG slot gets three verdicts depending only on the source's expression shape, where the scalar arg has one.** `xs: list[Box]` + `xs.append(v)` (borrowed param) WARNS `copies Box into owned storage; use copy()` and copies (TPy 2, CPython 42 -- acknowledged). The tuple spelling `xs: list[tuple[Box, Box]]` gives: a LITERAL source `xs.append((v, v))` -> hard **error** ("Cannot pass borrowed value as argument 'value' tuple element 0 Own[Box] without explicit copy()"); an LVALUE source (`t = make(v); xs.append(t)`) -> the per-element copy **warning**; a CALL source (`xs.append(make(v))`) -> **silent**. Not a container-vs-user-function issue: a user `def sink(x: Own[Box])` called with a borrowed param emits the IDENTICAL warning to `xs.append(v)`, and a local at last use moves in both while a reused local warns in both (verified) -- `Own[T]` at a param means one thing everywhere and the call site needs only the signature. The divergence is the per-element tuple check being deliberately stricter than the scalar rule at the ARG position: `check_own_lvalue_into_own(action="pass")` (reached via `own_tuple_target` from the tuple-literal arg check) rejects a borrowed element, justified in `sema/compatibility.py:2089-2094` as "the user opted in to ownership semantics by writing `Own[tuple[...]]`" -- reasoning that does not hold for a container insert, where the `Own[T]` was written by the stub, not the user. The RETURN position is already consistent (a borrowed source errors at both arities). Fix is the tier: the per-element arg check follows the scalar warn tier, in `check_own_lvalue_into_own` (the final borrowed-source raise, for `action != "return"`) and `check_name_borrow_into_own` (the `bp_borrow_into_own_idxs` raise); the `action == "return" and isinstance(unwrapped, OwnType)` early return must NOT be widened to args -- an arg has no coercion-path warning to fall back on, so widening it silences the copy entirely. **Prototyped and reverted, because the flip is wider than it looks: EIGHT existing cases stop erroring**, all of them call-arg shapes with varying source provenance -- `error_tuple_per_element_own_{param_borrowed,arg_name}`, `error_tuple_own_param_{param,subscript,field}_source`, `error_tuple_record_own_param_borrowed`, `error_tuple_optional_own_param_borrowed{,_element_1}`. The four `error_tuple_per_element_own_return_*` cases keep erroring. That is a real loosening of a deliberately-strict safety diagnostic (`@nocopy` sources still error), so it wants its own branch and review rather than riding along in a codegen wave. Repro `/tmp/agents/tuplecensus/cases3/{s,t}_collection/main.py`, `/tmp/agents/mixstep1/ownparam.py`. Surfaced by the tuple-element-vs-singleton census.
- **[MED small] (perf, hidden allocation) A `str` / `bytes` tuple element at a LOCAL materializes owned storage where the scalar local keeps a view.** `a: str = v` emits `std::string_view a = v` (free); `t: tuple[str] = (v,)` emits `std::tuple<std::string>(std::string(v))` -- one allocation plus a char copy per element, and `tuple[str, str]` pays it twice. `bytes` is the same shape: `std::span<const uint8_t>` vs `::tpy::bytes_copy(v)` per element. No semantic divergence (both are immutable value types, so copy-vs-alias is unobservable) -- this is pure hidden cost at a position whose scalar spelling is free. Sibling of the `str`/`bytes` tuple-PARAM view loss tracked in the TODO elementwise-form entry (instance 1); filed separately because the param fix changes the tuple param ABI while the local does not. Arity-independent: `tuple[str]` diverges exactly like `tuple[str, str]`. Repro `/tmp/agents/tuplecensus/cases/{s,t}_str_local/main.py`. Surfaced by the tuple-element-vs-singleton census.
- **[HIGH small] (SILENT CPython divergence + undiagnosed build failure) CONSUMING the `Own` element of a MIXED owned+borrow tuple param does not work: it copies the whole tuple, or fails the C++ build for a `@nocopy` element.** `def f(x: Own[Box])` renders `Box&&` and the callee may move `x`; `def f(p: tuple[Own[Box], Box])` renders `const std::tuple<Box, const Box*>&`, so unpacking to consume element 0 (`owned, borrowed = p; sink(owned)`) emits `auto __tup_1 = p;` -- a whole-tuple COPY off the const reference -- and then `std::move`s out of that copy. The caller's owned element is therefore never consumed, and the write is LOST: `owned, borrowed = p; owned.n = 99; borrowed.n = 77; return p[0].n + p[1].n` prints **78** under TPy and **176** under CPython -- the two elements of one tuple disagree, because element 1 aliases correctly while element 0 went through the copy. Silent. For a `@nocopy` element the copy is deleted instead and the build fails with raw libstdc++ spew pointing at `auto __tup_1 = p;`, with no TPy diagnostic (both verified). Compounding it, no `never consumed` warning fires for the element: a scalar `Own[T]` param warns (`Own[Box] param 'x' is never consumed`) and a FULLY owned tuple param warns (`owned tuple param 'p' is never consumed`, pinned in `tuple/mixed_own_tuple_param_from_call`), but the `Own` element of a mixed param says nothing -- so the one shape whose consumption is broken is also the one that never tells you it went unconsumed. The const-borrow param render is itself correct and deliberate (a mixed tuple's borrow slot aliases the caller, so the whole-tuple `&&` ownership ABI does not fit) -- what is missing is per-element move-out from it, which needs per-place partial-move tracking (see the aggregate-member move-out gap in `docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md`). Minimum fix is a located reject on the consume attempt plus the missing unconsumed warning; full support needs the partial move. Repros `/tmp/agents/mixstep1/lostwrite.py` (silent lost write), `consume.py` (the copy), `nocopy_unpack.py` (build failure). Raised LOW -> HIGH once the lost write was measured -- the same criterion that raised the nested-tuple and module-global entries. Surfaced by the tuple-element-vs-singleton census; the consume mechanism found reading the pinned diag of `mixed_own_tuple_param_from_call`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (misleading diagnostic) A missing MEMBER on `cls` inside a @classmethod reports the `cls`-as-a-value message instead of naming the member.** `cls.NOPE` where `NOPE` is not a class constant / ClassVar falls out of the class-member resolution path and reaches the bare-name value read, so the user gets "'cls' can only be used to construct ('cls(...)') or to access class members ('cls.NAME') -- passing or returning a class as a value needs 'type[T]'" when their actual mistake is a typo'd member name. Correct and located, just pointed at the wrong thing; the equivalent through the class NAME (`P.NOPE`) names the member. Fix shape: resolve the field against the record before falling through to the value read, so the miss reports the member. Surfaced probing `cls` in non-construct contexts on the classmethod branch.
- **[MED small] (rejects valid) Binding an `except ... as` capture to a local alias fails the C++ build -- the catch parameter is `const` but the alias decl is a non-const reference.** `except MyError as exc: e = exc` emits `MyError& e = exc;` while the handler catches `const MyError&`, so g++ rejects with "binding reference of type 'MyError&' to 'const MyError' discards qualifiers". Toolchain-caught, not silent. Reading a FIELD off the capture (`exc.code`) is fine -- only binding the whole object to a local trips it. Verified byte-identical with and without the capture-registration fix, so it is independent of name shadowing: reproducer `/tmp/agents/scopens/p_alias.py` (a 3-line handler, no name collision). Fix shape: either the alias decl follows the catch parameter's const-ness, or the handler catches by mutable reference where the binding is later aliased -- the decl and the catch signature must agree. Surfaced while probing the capture-registration fix (the fix moved resolution far enough to reach codegen, exposing this). Note for whoever fixes it: `tests/cases/name_shadowing/except_capture_shadows_class` is read-only because mutating through the capture trips exactly this bug -- once it is fixed, that case wants a mutate-and-observe assertion so the boundary stops being parity-blind if catch ever becomes by-value.
- **[LOW small] (accepts invalid) Reading an `except ... as NAME` capture AFTER its handler resolves to whatever NAME meant outside instead of raising.** CPython makes the capture a function-scoped local and deletes it at handler end, so a later read is `UnboundLocalError`; TPy unbinds the capture from the local namespace, which re-exposes the outer binding (a module-level class of that name, say) and the read silently succeeds. Probe: a class `Reg` with `code = 999` plus `except MyError as Reg: inside = Reg.code` then `return inside + Reg.code` prints 1006 under TPy where CPython raises. Accepts-invalid on pathological input, and the value read inside the handler is correct (that was the shadowing bug, fixed) -- only the post-handler region diverges. Closing it means modelling Python's function-scoped locals for captures (bind-then-mark-dead rather than unbind), which is a variable-model change, not a local patch: TPy's whole local model is block-scoped. Surfaced by the capture-registration fix.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) Binding `it = iter(c)` / `it = c.__iter__()` for a class whose `__iter__` returns `self` COPIES the object instead of aliasing it.** The standard "class is its own iterator" idiom: CPython gives `it is c`, so advancing `it` mutates `c`; TPy decls `auto it = ::tpy::__iter__(c);` where the helper returns `decltype(auto)` (a `Counter&`) but plain `auto` strips the reference -- a value copy, so mutations through `it` are invisible on `c`. Verified by probe: `PYTHONPATH=lib/cpy python3` prints `2`, `uv run tpy` prints `0` (advance via `it`, then read `c`'s counter). The `for x in c:` loop idiom is unaffected (verified separately). Shared by BOTH emit paths -- THIR's `_protocol_auto_slot` decl arm mirrors the AST's `_cpp_decl_type` `auto` spelling byte-identically -- and concealed in the corpus because `iterators/iter_next_builtin` never re-reads `c` after binding `it` (a parity-blind read-only shape). Fix shape: the protocol-typed iterator decl needs the reference-preserving spelling (`auto&&`, or `decltype(auto)`) when the init is a borrow-returning `__iter__`; both paths must change together (byte-identity), and the concealing test wants a mutate-after-bind observation. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) on `thir-decl-slot-track`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong call / wrong arity) A callable PARAM or local invoked under a name that also names a module function emits the MODULE function's call with the args zip-truncated to its params.** `def f() -> None: ...` + `def apply(f: Fn[[Int32], Int32], v: Int32) -> Int32: return f(v)` emits `return f();` -- sema correctly resolves the callable BINDING (CPython scoping: the param shadows), but the AST emit's registry-first ordering (`codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~3168, `get_function(expr.func_name)` before any local-binding check) takes the module function's params for the arg zip, silently dropping args; in C++ the name still denotes the param, so the emitted call invokes the callable with the wrong arity (build error at best, wrong call at worst). Same emit-order family as the nested-def builtin-shadow divergence tracked below (a closure shadowing an imported/builtin name; THIR's `call.closure_import_shadow` reject) -- registry/builtin lookup precedes the local-binding check in the AST emit. THIR renders the binding call correctly, so the composition is gate-rejected there (`call.callable_shadow` in `thir/lower/expressions.py`) per the broken-oracle rule -- un-reject when this is fixed. Surfaced by the THIR byte-diff while pinning the callable-field arg row. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent, unverified] `get_resolved_type`'s `var_types` short-circuit now resolves pending CONTAINER types too, and no test exercises that half.** The short-circuit was fixed to route through `resolve_type()` so a tuple-unpack target's `PendingStrType` no longer leaks out unresolved; `resolve_type()` also resolves `PendingListType`/`PendingDictType`/`PendingSetType`. That is the architecturally right chokepoint (the non-`var_types` path has always resolved both), but the container half is a behavior widening nobody has a witness for. The specific worry is one level down, in `_resolve_pending_container`'s BEST-EFFORT fallback (`make_list(elem)` / `make_dict` / `make_set`, taken when sema's resolution never ran). Before this change that path could not be reached from the short-circuit, so an unresolved pending list surfaced as a LOUD `PendingListType should be resolved before codegen` crash; now it can silently become `list[T]` where the truth is `Array[T, N]` -- a reference-vs-value guess, and a copy-vs-alias decision keyed on it would be a SILENT divergence, the project's most serious class. (The resolved answer itself is the truthful one -- returning it is strictly better than returning the raw pending node, which is why the fix is not to narrow the resolution back to views.) A whole-corpus comp run over both variants (view-only vs container-inclusive) produced byte-IDENTICAL output, and a review pass could not construct a reachable case across ~89 `get_resolved_type` call sites, so there is no evidence of a live defect -- this is filed because "unverified across 89 call sites" is exactly what resurfaces later as an aliasing bug, not because a symptom exists. Fix shape if a witness appears: audit the call sites that ask `is_value_type()` / a value-vs-reference question about a `var_types` entry, rather than narrowing the resolution back to views. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity, self-tagged low confidence) on `fix-pending-view-unpack`.
- **[MED small] (wrong answer from a type predicate, latent) `Own[T]` reports `is_trivially_destructible() == True` for every `T`, so `Own[bytes]` / `Own[list[int]]` claim to be cheap heap-free value types.** `OwnType.is_value_type()` returns `True` unconditionally (deliberate -- documented as "Own[T] uses T&& at param boundaries; treat as value type for other purposes", `tpyc/typesys.py:1673`), and `is_expensive_copy()` is NOT overridden, so it falls through to the base implementation whose `_nominal_td()` is None for a wrapper type and returns `False`. `is_trivially_destructible()` is `is_value_type() and not is_expensive_copy()`, so it is `True and not False` regardless of the wrapped type. Any consumer asking an `Own[T]` whether a move is cheap gets the wrong answer; live consumers are `codegen_cpp/statements.py:2724` and `thir/lower/statements.py:467` (neither confirmed reachable with an OwnType, hence latent rather than a live miscompile). Found the hard way: a candidate fix for the `_maybe_move` frame-local regression used `is_trivially_destructible()` directly and silently kept dropping the move on `Own[bytes]`; the shipped fix unwraps `Own` explicitly to sidestep this, which is a workaround at the consumer, not a fix. Fix shape: forward `is_expensive_copy()` (and probably `is_trivially_destructible()`) to `self.wrapped` on `OwnType` -- but check each existing consumer first, since `is_value_type()`'s unconditional `True` is load-bearing at param boundaries and the three predicates are entangled. Surfaced fixing the codegen regression on `thir-stdlib-oracle`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH medium] (silent CPython divergence) A local's type is inferred from its PRE-LOOP binding only; a back-edge widening (`z = z + 1.5`) leaves dependent locals typed at the stale width and silently truncates.** `z = 0` then in a loop `sq = z * z; z = z + 1.5`: `z` itself widens to `double`, but `sq` keeps the pre-loop `Int32` inference, so codegen emits `mul_check<int32_t>(z, z)` narrowing BOTH operands -- CPython prints `0, 2.25, 9.0`, TPy prints `0, 1, 9` (repro `/tmp/agents/loopwiden.py`, verified). Compiles clean, NO warning -- exactly the CPython-divergence case goal #5 promises to flag; in a mandelbrot port this made all 6084 points test in-set (solid `#` block). Fix shape, two independent parts: (1) body type inference needs a fixed point over the loop back-edge before dependent locals are typed (the real fix); (2) regardless, a float->int operand narrowing at a checked-arith emit site should WARN -- the silent `mul_check<int32_t>(double, double)` coercion is never intended. Sibling entry: the LOUD flavor of stale-width re-assignment ("A PLAIN re-assignment that widens an inferred-`Int32` variable to `BigInt` emits invalid C++") -- same missing-widening root, there caught by g++, here silently miscompiled, so a shared fix should close both. Workaround: initialize `z = 0.0`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (inference architecture). Surfaced porting mandelbrot.
- **[HIGH small] (runtime perf, ~50x) `BigInt::compare()` misses the small-int fast path -- two heap allocations to compare two tagged small ints.** `compare()` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/bigint.hpp:1331`) calls `abs_limbs()` on both sides, each returning a `std::vector` by value, unlike `operator+`/`-`/`*`/`==` which all guard with `if (is_small() && rhs.is_small())` (four sites, verified). Measured: 50M comparisons 0.02s with `Int32` vs 1.05s with `int` (~21ns each); mandelbrot 0.59s -> 5.58s. `floor_div()` (`bigint.hpp:1347`) has the same gap. Fix: the usual small-small guard in both -- and AUDIT the remaining limb-based operations, since only `+`, `-`, `*`, `==` were confirmed to have the fast path (`/`, `%`, `divmod`, shifts, bitwise ops all reach `abs_limbs()`/`to_twos_complement()` paths unverified). Every `<`/`<=` on default-int code hits this, hence HIGH. Surfaced profiling the mandelbrot port.
- **[HIGH small] (rejects-valid) `input(prompt)` is rejected -- "No matching overload for input(str)"; only the bare `input()` overload exists.** Textbook first-program Python (`name = input("your name: ")`) fails with an opaque overload error that never says the prompt form is unsupported. Bare `input()` works (verified; probe `/tmp/agents/inp.py`). Fix shape: add the one-arg overload to the `input` stub (write the prompt to stdout without a trailing newline, flush, then read the line -- CPython semantics), which also retires the diagnostic problem; if the overload is deferred for any reason, the minimum is a located "input(prompt) is not supported yet" message. Was previously tracked only as a RELEASE_PLAN bullet; filed here per the bugs-block-releases rule. Surfaced planning 0.6.0.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) An unbound-self read of a `@native`-RENAMED base field spells the SOURCE name, so the C++ build fails.** `_gen_field_access` computes `cpp_field = escape_cpp_name(expr.field)` at the top, applies the `native_field_name` rename much later, and the unbound-self early return (`this->{parent}::{cpp_field}`) sits BETWEEN the two -- so `BaseA.x` inside a descendant method emits `this->::BaseA::x` where the struct member is `m_x`. The ordinary receiver form (`self.x` / `v.x`) renders correctly because it reaches the override. Loud (C++ compile error), pre-existing, no corpus witness. Repro: an `@native` base with `x: Int32 = native_field("m_x")` and a subclass method returning `BaseA.x`. Fix is to move the rename override above the early return (or read `native_field_name` in it). THIR deliberately REJECTS this shape (`_unbound_self_field_ok`) rather than mirror the broken render -- un-reject it once this is fixed; boundary-pinned by `test_native_renamed_field_stays_ast`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) on the field-write/containers wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) Printing a NARROWED value-repr `Optional[scalar]` module GLOBAL emits the bare optional.** `g: Int32 | None = 5` then `if g is not None: print(g)` emits `std::cout << g` where `g` is `std::optional<int32_t>`, which has no `operator<<` -- the C++ build fails. The same construct on a LOCAL or a param renders `print_optional_val(g)` correctly, so it is the module-global binding that misses the narrowed-read wrap. Loud, pre-existing (reproduces on master), no corpus witness. Note the THIR overlay already emits the CORRECT `print_optional_val(g)` here, so this is one of the few places the two paths disagree with THIR being right; the field-write/containers wave widened admission enough that more bodies containing this construct now route, which is how it was found (no in-corpus case newly routes -- the whole-corpus byte-diff stayed green). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) A `nonlocal` rebind inside a NESTED DEF escapes a loop-body local past the loop without tripping the scope-escape reject.** `saved = Point(-1); for i in range(3): p = Point(i); def capture(): nonlocal saved; saved = p` + `if i == 0: capture(); p.x = 99` prints TPy `0`, CPython `99`, with no diagnostic. `check_escape` (`tpyc/sema/scope_tracker.py`) runs only at the two `TpyName`-target assignment sites in `tpyc/sema/statements.py`, and `nested_def_scope` resets `var_scope_depth` for the nested body, so the depth comparison never sees the outer name; codegen emits a plain value copy of a stale `p` where CPython aliases. Same family as the rejected direct spelling (`pointers/error_escape_loop_body`), just routed through one level of nested-def indirection. Pre-existing (the call sites are unchanged by the reject). Fix shape: treat a nested def's `nonlocal` write of an outer name as an assignment at the OUTER depth, so the escape check sees it -- the same detection gap as the nonlocal match-capture rebind entry below. Probe `/tmp/agents/escapekey/rev/closure.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the scope-escape reject. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) A WALRUS-bound loop-body local escapes past the loop without tripping the scope-escape reject.** `saved = (p := Point(i))` inside a loop body, with `saved` declared outside it, compiles clean and prints TPy `2`, CPython `99` after a `p.x = 99` in the same iteration. `is_scope_escape` resolves its source through `TpyName` / `TpyFieldAccess` / `TpySubscript` only, so a `TpyNamedExpr` source is never walked and `check_escape` is not reached; the walrus codegen then materializes an independent copy where CPython aliases. Pre-existing and untouched by the reject. Fix shape: resolve a `TpyNamedExpr` source to its bound name in `_get_source_name` / `get_expr_scope_depth` so the walrus form takes the same path as the bare name. Probe `/tmp/agents/escapekey/rev/walrus.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the scope-escape reject. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (warned wrong output) A loop-body local captured CONDITIONALLY mid-loop observes the LAST iteration's value, not the captured one.** `saved = Point(0); for i in range(3): p = Point(i); if i == 0: saved = p` then `return saved.x` gives TPy `2`, CPython `0`. The loop-body local's storage is hoisted to ONE function-scope slot, so every iteration emplaces over the same object and an alias taken earlier follows it. WARNED since the escape diagnostic was reworded to state the consequence ("'saved' will not keep the object it was given") and name `copy()`, so this is an acknowledged divergence rather than a silent one -- but the emitted program is still wrong for anyone who does not act on the warning. Needs no post-loop code, which makes it the most reachable shape of the family. Reproduces identically in a generator/frame context. Fixing it needs per-iteration storage: a slot per BINDING still leaves iterations of one binding sharing a slot. Two mechanisms were prototyped and discarded (see TODO.md) -- a two-slot rotation and a move-at-escape. Repro:
  ```python
  saved = Point(0)
  for i in range(3):
      p = Point(i)
      if i == 0:
          saved = p
  return saved.x   # TPy 2, CPython 0
  ```
  Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the block-local scope-revoke branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (warned wrong output) Rebinding a loop-body-hoisted record local AFTER the loop overwrites the storage an earlier alias captured.** `saved = Point(0); for i in ...: p = Point(i); saved = p` then a READ of `p` after the loop (which pre-declares it at function scope) followed by `p = Point(9)`: the post-loop rebind reuses the single hoisted slot, clobbering the object `saved` points at -- TPy `saved.x == 9`, CPython `2`. Carries the same escape warning as the entry above. `_gen_pointer_local_init` already allocates a SEPARATE rebind slot for exactly this reason when a var has future rvalue rebinds, but the loop-predecl path never registers one. **The obvious fix does not work -- do not re-attempt it without reading this.** A slot per rebind SITE fixes the aliasing (probe goes 9 -> 2) but slot REUSE is what destroys the old object eagerly the way CPython's refcount drop does; with a slot per site the old object survives to function exit and `cases/records/destructor_reassign` flips from `drop b | alive: c` to `alive: c | drop b`. The two properties are in tension under a static slot model -- a real fix needs liveness/escape information at the rebind site, or heap storage. Verified by implementing and reverting it (2026-07-27). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) A local declared ABOVE a loop, aliased by another local and then rebound, still overwrites the alias -- the single-slot clobber reached without a scope escape.** `saved = Point(-1); p = Point(-1); for i in range(3): p = Point(i); saved = p` then `p = Point(9)` gives TPy `9`, CPython `2`. Moving the `p = ...` declaration INSIDE the loop body makes the same program REJECTED (the scope-escape error), so the two spellings disagree on whether the program is even legal -- and the legal one is the wrong one. Hoisting a declaration out of a loop is a routine edit, which is what makes this a trap. `check_escape` (`tpyc/sema/scope_tracker.py`) keys on scope DEPTH, and here source and target are both function-scope, so no escape is recorded and the local keeps one reused rebind slot. The straight-line form without a loop (`p = Point(5); saved = p; p = Point(9)`) is correct -- `p`'s separate rebind slot covers it -- so the loop is what collapses the two bindings onto one slot. Fix shape: the escape check must key on an ALIAS fact (is a live name pointing at this local's storage at the rebind?) rather than scope depth -- the borrow tracker is the natural owner. Being conservative is cheap here: the outcome is a reject, so over-approximating costs false positives, not wrong code. Pre-existing: reproduces identically on master (verified at f74bbacec). Probe `/tmp/agents/escapekey/loopsame.py`. Surfaced writing the escaped-local rotation tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (CPython-parity gap in the stubs, not the compiler) A `Deref` type's transparent dereference is TPy-only -- the `lib/cpy` stub forwards no attribute access, so the same source raises `AttributeError` under CPython.** `r = Rc.new(Point(1)); r.x` reads the payload's field in TPy (`__deref__`), but `lib/cpy/tpy/__init__.py`'s `Deref` protocol only declares `__deref__` and nothing forwards `__getattr__`/`__setattr__`, so CPython looks the attribute up on the handle and fails. Affects every `Deref` type the stubs expose (`Rc`, `Arc`, `Box`, the `Mutex`/`RwLock` guards), so any test using the idiomatic spelling needs `no_cpython.txt` -- which costs exactly the parity check such a test exists to provide (`pointers/escape_explicit_rc`). Fix shape: give the stub `Deref` base a `__getattr__`/`__setattr__` that forwards to the payload, mirroring TPy's implicit deref. Surfaced writing the escape-reject's escape-hatch coverage. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent UAF, garbage output) A pointer-local's rvalue INIT slot in a generator/async body is emitted as a stack local inside the state-machine `case` block, so the frame field pointing at it dangles after the first suspension.** `def g() -> Iterator[int]: saved: Optional[Point] = Point(42); for i in range(3): yield i; if saved is not None: yield saved.x` yields `0 1 2 70366646647656` where CPython yields `0 1 2 42`: the frame carries `Point* saved` but the slot is `Point __slot_1 = Point(42);` inside `case S_INITIAL`, which dies when `__next__` returns. Silent, memory-unsafe, no diagnostic. Reachable via any shape that makes a resumable local a pointer-local with an rvalue init (the `Optional[T]` spelling above; the escape-hoist route is now rejected at sema, see LANGUAGE_FEATURES Scope Escape Detection). Fix shape: a resumable pointer-local's init slot must be a frame field, not a `case`-block local -- the same placement the `frame_slot<T>` locals already get. Pre-existing (identical emission on master). Probe `/tmp/agents/escapekey/gen_opt.py`. Surfaced by the escaped-local rotation work. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A reference-typed for-loop variable READ after the loop and then reassigned fails the C++ build -- `cannot convert 'const Point' to ...`.** `for p in points: print(p.x)` + `print(p.x)` + `p = Point(9)`: the trailing read forces sema's pending-loop-var promotion, and the promoted (pre-declared) binding is emitted const, so the later rebind cannot assign it. Without the read the same rebind compiles and runs correctly (the block-local scope-revoke path gives it a fresh declaration), so the PROMOTION path is the broken one -- which is why block-local reassignment was NOT routed through promotion. CPython accepts the program (prints 1 2 2 9). Loud, pre-existing: reproduces identically on master. Probe `/tmp/agents/twoobj/promote.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) An `int | None`-annotated local initialized from an `Int32`-typed expression is rejected -- `Type mismatch in variable 'v': expected int | None, got Int32 | None`.** `v: int | None = i if flag else None` (with `i: Int32` from `range`) fails, though the un-annotated `int` sibling widens fine and `v: int | None = None` followed by `v = 9` compiles. The BigInt-vs-IntN widening that applies to a bare `int` target is not applied through the `| None` union arm, so the ternary's `Int32 | None` never widens to `int | None`. Loud (sema-rejected). Pre-existing: reproduces identically on master. Probe `/tmp/agents/loophoist/optloop.py`. Surfaced adding Optional-typed coverage to the block-local scope-revoke tests (which use `str | None` instead to route around it). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A local first declared in a try's `else` body and ASSIGNED after the try emits a `goto` across its initialization -- or, before that, a write to an out-of-scope name.** `try: ... except E: pass else: n = 7` then `n = 9` after the try. Every other block-emitting statement revokes its declarations at the block close so the post-block assignment declares the function-scope local (`cases/control_flow/block_local_reassign_after`), but the `else` body emits UNBRACED in the enclosing C++ scope with the handlers' `goto __after_else_N` jumping past it, so a fresh declaration there is crossed by the jump (g++ `jump to label ... crosses initialization`). Fix shape: brace the else body (`{ ... }` with the label outside) and scope-revoke it like the other blocks -- `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`, `emit_try_except`. NB that brace changes emitted C++ for every try/else case (5 snapshots: `control_flow/try_except_shapes`, `control_flow/with_exit_isinstance_class_dispatch`, `exceptions/throw_else`, `exceptions/throw_else_var`, plus the THIR unit `test_thir_try.py::TestThrowTierElseFinally`) and THIR emits the else body unbraced, so it needs a matching THIR mirror -- deliberately excluded from the block-local scope-revoke fix, which is AST-only and snapshot-neutral. Repro:
  ```python
  try:
      print(100 // d)
  except ZeroDivisionError:
      pass
  else:
      n = 7
  n = 9        # g++: jump to label '__after_else_1' crosses initialization
  ```
 Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent UAF, garbage output) A generator's captured pointer aiming at a local of the FACTORY function reads destroyed storage: the factory returns before the closure is ever pulled.** `def gen(n): p = Point(1); p = Point(7); i = 0; while i < n: yield p.x; i += 1` captures `p` by value into the `make_generator` closure while `p` points at a `__slot_N` local to `gen`, which dies when `gen` returns -- TPy prints garbage (`398837392 ...`) where CPython prints `7 7 7`. No scope MISMATCH occurs (the slot is reserved and consumed in the same scope), so the cross-scope reject in `use_rebind_slot` does not see it; the unsoundness is the broader "a captured pointer outlives its slot", of which the reject covers only the mismatched slice. Pre-existing and byte-identical to master. Silent: no diagnostic, garbage output. The method-generator spelling is affected identically. Fix shape: the same init-capture that would give the slot closure lifetime (filed in TODO.md), or extend the reject to any rebind slot whose pointer is captured by an escaping closure. `generators/rebind_slot_in_generator` deliberately does NOT cover this shape. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid on clang, warns on gcc) A generator's capture list is built with no dedup, so a rebound init-statement local is captured twice.** `_build_capture_list` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py`) appends one capture per init-stmt `TpyVarDecl`, so `p = Point(11); p = Point(12)` before a generator's loop emits `[n, p, p, i]() mutable`. GCC only warns (`already captured 'p'`); **clang treats a duplicate capture as an error**, so the shape is a hard build failure on a supported toolchain -- and CI's gcc-only default hides it. Pre-existing. Fix: dedup by name when assembling the list. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A local declared and rvalue-rebound entirely INSIDE a nested `def` gets no rebind slot, so an alias to its first value is clobbered.** `scan_reassigned_vars` (`tpyc/prescan.py`) builds `rvalue_reassigned` as a flat per-function name set and does NOT recurse into nested defs, so the inner body inherits the OUTER function's set: the inner local gets a separate rebind slot only when its name happens to COLLIDE with an enclosing rvalue-reassigned name. Non-colliding is the common case and is wrong: `def inner(): q = Point(1); alias = q; q = Point(100); alias.bump(); return alias.x + q.x` returns 202 where CPython gives 102 (the rebind reuses `q`'s storage, so `alias` observes the new value). Silent: no diagnostic, wrong output. Pre-existing (reproduces on master). Same root cause as the `nonlocal` sibling already filed. Fix shape: make the prescan recurse per nested-def scope instead of sharing the enclosing set -- which would also retire two of this branch's `no_thir` markers, since the slots they guard are collision artifacts. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) A nested `def`'s local whose name shadows a resumable frame field is emitted as a write to the FRAME field, so calling the closure clobbers the generator's own variable.** Same prescan-scope root cause as the entry above: the nested body is rendered against the enclosing resumable's local classification, so `p = ...` inside the nested def lowers to `p.emplace(...)` on the frame member rather than a fresh local. Reported by the /tpy-ready second opinion with a probe; the shape's argument-evaluation order makes which wrong value surfaces nondeterministic, so treat the exact output as unstable. NOT independently reproduced here -- confirm before fixing.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) The rvalue rebind slot is allocated unconditionally, so a superseded value stays alive in the init slot until function exit instead of dropping at the rebind.** The split exists so an alias to the init value is not overwritten (`_gen_pointer_local_init`'s own docstring: "so aliases to the init value aren't overwritten"), but it is gated on `rvalue_reassigned_vars` alone -- whether anything actually aliases the value is never asked. So `r = Noisy("a"); r = Noisy("b")` with no alias emits TWO objects: `b` lands in the rebind slot while `a` sits in the init slot until scope end. CPython drops `a` at the rebind. `records/destructor_reassign` shows it -- TPy prints `drop b / alive: c / drop a / drop c`, CPython `drop a / drop b / alive: c / drop c`. Gating the split on the init value actually being aliased would emit ONE slot for the unaliased case, whose move-assign destroys the superseded value at exactly the CPython point -- so this is a parity fix, not just one fewer live object per rebind. It should retire `destructor_reassign`'s `no_cpython.txt` and the `no_cpython` marker any equivalent block-scoped case would need. Secondary, decide after the slot count is right: a held-back declaration always lands in the FUNCTION prologue, so a slot reserved inside an `if`/loop body outlives that body; for the unaliased single-slot form function scope is what parity wants anyway, but a genuinely block-local slot could stay in its block. Pinned by `records/rebind_slot_block_scope_drop` and `records/destructor_reassign`, whose `no_cpython.txt` markers both name this entry and are expected to be retired by the fix.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) The separate-rebind-slot protection against alias clobbering is missing at MODULE scope and inside a NESTED `def`.** Same single-slot-clobber family as the loop-predecl and declared-above-a-loop entries above, same mechanism (a rebind reuses the single init slot, overwriting the object an earlier alias points at) in two more scopes that never register a separate slot: (a) module scope -- `p = Point(1); alias = p; if flag: p = Point(10)` emits `p = &(__global_slot_1 = Point(10));` so `alias.bump()` then reports `alias: 11 p: 11` where CPython gives `alias: 2 p: 10`; (b) a nested `def` -- the inner body reuses the OUTER function's prescan sets, so the rebind is never classified as rvalue-reassigned at all, and `p = Point(1); a = p; p = Point(9); a.bump()` reports `a: 10 p: 10` where CPython gives `a: 2 p: 9`. Ordinary function locals are correct -- `ctx.declare_rebind_slot` reserves the separate slot for exactly this reason -- so the fix is to make these two paths reach it. Silent: no diagnostic, wrong output. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the held-back rebind-slot declaration change.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A record local declared inside a TOP-LEVEL loop body is emitted as a C++ function-local `static`, so it is constructed ONCE and every later iteration reuses the stale object.** At module scope the slot carries `static` so it outlives `__tpy_init` (`slots.global_scope`), but inside a top-level `while`/`for` body that makes the initializer run only on the first pass: `i = 0` / `while i < 3: q = Point(i * 10); print(q.x); i += 1` emits `static Point __global_slot_1 = Point(i * 10);` and prints `0, 1, 2` (the value carried over and mutated) where CPython prints `0, 10, 20`. Silent: no diagnostic, wrong output. The same code inside a `def` is correct -- only module-level loops are affected, so it is easy to miss. A loop-local at module scope needs a non-static slot scoped to the loop body (or a static slot plus a per-iteration assignment), not a static initializer. Found probing `declare_rebind_slot`'s global-scope branch for test coverage.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A `match`-arm local READ after the match is pre-declared at the wrong width -- `cannot convert 'tpy::BigInt' to 'int32_t' in assignment`.** `match c: case 1: n = c + 1 ... case _: n = 0` then `return n` (with `c: int`) pre-declares the merged local as `int32_t` while the arm assigns the `BigInt`-typed expression, so g++ rejects. The loop-body flavor of the same read-after promotion picks the right type; only the match-arm branch-decl merge is wrong. Loud (toolchain-caught), pre-existing and unchanged by the block-local scope-revoke fix. Probe `/tmp/agents/loophoist/m_read.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A `for`, `with ... as` or `match`-capture binding whose target is `global`-declared writes a function-LOCAL shadow, so the module variable never changes.** `global g` then `for g in range(7, 8): pass` emits `for (int32_t g = 7; ...)`, `with CM() as g:` emits `auto g = __ctx_1.__enter__();`, and `case g:` binds an arm local -- in each the write is invisible to every other function (probes under `/tmp/agents/walrusglobal/matrix/`: TPy leaves the global at 1 where CPython has 7). Same root cause as the walrus form fixed on `fix-walrus-global-write`: only the plain-assign path consults `func.global_declarations` (`sema/statements.py`) with the matching `global_declared_vars` render arm in codegen; every other binding form defines into the function scope instead. The three lower to structurally different C++ (loop induction variable / `__enter__` result decl / arm capture decl), so each needs its own sema route + AST arm + THIR arm -- no shared emit chokepoint. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small, THIR-only] (silent divergence from the AST oracle) A walrus inside a NESTED def whose target NAME collides with a module global drops the local pre-declaration, so the lambda writes the module variable.** `counter = 5` at module level + `def a(): def inner() -> int: return (counter := 3)` -- with no `global` statement anywhere, AST emits `int32_t counter; return (counter = 3);` (a local shadow, CPython-correct) while THIR emits only `return (counter = 3);`. A non-colliding target (`(fresh := 3)`) keeps its decl on both paths, so the trigger is the module-global name collision in a nested-def body, not the walrus itself. No corpus case covered the shape; surfaced by the inverse test of `fix-walrus-global-write` (`globals/global_walrus_local_shadow`, which now uses a non-colliding name). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH small] (silent UB, garbage output) A `global` statement inside a NESTED def is ignored: the write emits an UNINITIALIZED local shadow that self-initializes from itself.** `g = 1` at module level + `def outer(): def inner(): global g; g = g + 10; return g` emits `int32_t g = (::tpy::add_check<int32_t>(g, 10));` inside the lambda -- reading `g` before it is initialized -- so TPy prints a nondeterministic value (24918 observed) where CPython prints 11, and the module variable never changes. Reproduces with a plain assignment, an augmented assignment and a walrus alike, so the gap is the nested-def scope of `function_global_decls` / the codegen predecl bookkeeping, not any one binding form (the enclosing function's own `global` writes are handled correctly). Probe `/tmp/agents/walrusglobal/nested_global.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of `fix-walrus-global-write`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A binary operand is re-read AFTER a sibling operand's walrus writes it, so the left operand sees the new value.** `n = 1; return n + (n := n + 100)` yields 202 where CPython yields 102 -- C++ leaves the two operands of `+` unsequenced, and codegen renders the left operand as a bare name read rather than materializing it before the walrus assigns. Generalizes the membership/chained-comparison entry above to plain binops; affects locals and (since `fix-walrus-global-write`) `global`-declared targets alike. Probe `/tmp/agents/walrusglobal/order.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A membership needle or chained-comparison operand is re-read after a sibling operand assigns to it.** `c.n in (bump(c), 1)` and `0 < a.x < bump(a)` (where `bump` writes the operand) answer TPy `False` / CPython `True`: CPython reads each operand once, before the siblings run, while TPy renders it where the write is already visible. Probes `/tmp/agents/chain-dup/{widen,nameneedle,chainwrite,subscript2,walrus4}.py`. Affects every operand shape that names storage -- a bare name, a field, a subscript, a walrus target and a ternary of lvalues were each confirmed on this branch; an operand that renders its own value (a call, a property) is unaffected, since nothing can write it. Same root as the two-operand ordering entry below and unfixed for the same reason -- a fix must capture the operand's VALUE before the sibling runs (a reference re-reads), which costs a copy on every chained compare with a call in it, to fix code that assigns to an operand mid-comparison. Implemented and REVERTED on `fix-operand-snapshot` after review: correct, but it bought a rare guarantee for four new operand predicates plus THIR node fields, and left the two-operand form still wrong, so the guarantee was incoherent. Do it with the two-operand entry, or not at all. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) Tuple-literal membership short-circuits the ELEMENTS, but CPython builds the tuple first and so evaluates every element.** `"A" in (a(), b())` runs only `a()` in TPy (the `==` OR-chain stops at the first match) where CPython runs both before `CONTAINS_OP` -- so an element's side effects are silently skipped. Probe `/tmp/agents/chain-dup/b2_probe.py` (TPy `evaluated: a`, CPython `evaluated: ab`). Distinct from the needle-vs-haystack ORDER entry above: that one is about which side runs first, this is about an element not running at all. The sibling half of the needle entry above: that one is about the needle being re-read, this one about an element never running. Both are open, and a fix for either should consider the other. A fix has to give up the OR-chain's short circuit (materialize every element, then compare) and so trades code size on the common all-literal form, which is why it is not a follow-on tweak. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A PLAIN re-assignment that widens an inferred-`Int32` variable to `BigInt` emits invalid C++ instead of a diagnostic or a coercion -- `cannot convert 'tpy::BigInt' to 'int32_t' in assignment`.** `acc = 0` then `acc = acc * 10 + n` with `n: int` (BigInt): the inferred `Int32` slot keeps its width while the RHS analyzes as `BigInt`, so g++ rejects. Reproduces for a LOCAL (`/tmp/agents/chain-dup/localwiden.py`) and a module GLOBAL (`/tmp/agents/chain-dup/globalwiden.py`) alike, in a 10-line program with no match, loop, or narrowing -- so it is the ordinary assignment path, distinct from (and broader than) the match-arm branch-decl entry above, which shares only the error text. The AUGMENTED form `acc += n` compiles and runs correctly, so the coercion the compound path applies is missing from plain assignment. CPython has one arbitrary-precision int and accepts both. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: annotate the accumulator `acc: int = 0`. Fix shape: apply the compound path's widen-or-diagnose rule at plain-assignment re-binding, so the slot either widens with the RHS or the narrowing is reported by sema. Surfaced writing an evaluation-order test during /tpy-fix-bug of the chained-compare hidden-call double-eval. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid) The nested-capture retype reject is READ-INSENSITIVE: it fires even when the outer name is never read after the nested bind, where the program was correct before.** `_reject_nested_capture_retype` (`tpyc/sema/match.py`) keys on `rebound_captures`, which carries no post-bind-read condition, so `case Cat(lives=v): print(v); match b: case Dog(lives=v): pass` (Int32 outer, Int64 nested, `v` unread afterwards) is rejected although master compiled it and printed the CPython answer. Narrow: it needs BOTH an implicitly C++-convertible type pair AND no later read -- a non-convertible pair (`int`/`str`) already failed the build on master via the branch-decl hoist, and a later read is the truncation the gate exists to catch. The rejected programs are latent rather than correct (the truncating write into the shared slot does happen; only its result is unobserved), so the reject is a rename away and a future read would have made it a silent wrong answer -- that is why the gate ships read-insensitive rather than guessing. Fix shape: gate the error on the name being read after the nested bind, or restrict it to names the definite-assignment predecl would hoist anyway (a proxy for readable-after) and skip the hoist entirely when types disagree otherwise; a wrong read-analysis reinstates the silent truncation, so it needs its own verification pass. Probe `/tmp/agents/nestedmatch/r_noread.py`. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion of the nested-match capture reuse fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) A HOISTED `match` capture of a reference type drops the subject's const, so a never-mutated param subject yields `T* q;` against a `const T&` -- ill-formed C++.** `def show(h: Holder): match h: case Holder(inner=q): print(q.n)` emits `Inner* q;` then `q = &(__match_subject_1.inner);` where `h` renders `const Holder&`, so g++ reports `invalid conversion from 'const Inner*' to 'Inner*'`. Root cause is phase ordering: `record_stmt_borrow_binding` (`tpyc/sema/context.py`) derives the stmt-borrow const flag from the DECLARED type in Phase 1, but a borrowed param's const-ness is decided by mutation inference in Phase 2, so a capture of a never-mutated param reaches `_emit_branch_decls`' pointer arm with const=False. Only the hoisted path is affected (a block-scoped `auto&` capture takes the subject's const-ness for free); the hoist fires via the branch-decl predecl (post-match read) or the rebound-capture predecl. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. The nested-match-reuse route re-asks the verdict at the decl site (`_match_capture_borrows_const`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) and so is already correct -- that re-ask is deliberately NOT widened to the single-match shape, because it would change the emit for shapes THIR still routes and THIR's hoist admission cannot carry a const pointer rung (it rejects the existing const-indirect rung outright). Fix shape: settle the borrow const verdict in Phase 2 (or route the decl through `codegen_cpp/param_const.py`'s chokepoint), then widen the re-ask and mirror it in THIR. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/nestedmatch/p_constpre.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the nested-match capture reuse. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) Mutating through a `match` class-pattern KEYWORD capture does not mark the subject param as mutating, so the emitted C++ writes through a `const` alias and fails to compile.** `def f(h: Holder): match h: case Holder(items=xs): xs.append(9)` emits `int32_t f(const Holder& h)` and then `push_back` on the resulting `const std::vector<int32_t>&` -- ill-formed. Same for a dict field (`t["b"] = 7` -> `__setitem__` on a `const ordered_map&`) and a record field (`q.n = 42`). Mutation propagation enumerates writes through names and field paths but not through a match capture's alias into the subject, so the param keeps its const-borrow verdict. CPython accepts all three (the capture aliases the matched object). Blocks a corpus case that would give the reference-typed keyword-capture family an exec+cpy mutation witness -- today that family is covered only by THIR unit pins and read-only cases, which are parity-blind. Sibling of the HOISTED-capture const entry below and probably one fix: that one loses const on the pointer where the subject IS const, this one never makes the subject non-const in the first place -- both hang on where the borrow const verdict is settled. Distinct from the sibling-arm stale-alias and nested-rebind entries: this one is the subject param's const verdict, not the capture's own binding form. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] A hoisted match capture's SIBLING arm keeps a stale sema alias/borrow fact while codegen emits a copy.** When one arm rebinds a capture name (hoisting it, `fix-match-capture-rebind-hoist`), a sibling arm binding the SAME name is emitted by codegen as an assignment into the hoisted slot -- a copy -- but sema still annotates that arm's capture `bind_by_value=False` and records `record_stmt_borrow_binding` / `match_borrow_captures` for it (`tpyc/sema/match.py`), so the borrow fact describes an alias that codegen no longer emits. This breaks the documented "one sema fact drives both codegen and the dangle warning, so they cannot drift" invariant for sibling arms specifically. Latent: no observable divergence found -- the arm-subject-mutation dangle warning fires identically to master on the probe shape (verified: the warning is pre-existing, not introduced; it only becomes VISIBLE because the program now compiles). The risk is a future borrow-based rejection reading the stale fact. Fix shape: when a capture name is hoisted, clear the alias/borrow facts for every arm binding it (codegen's copy is the CPython-correct side). Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion of the capture-rebind hoist. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) `with <ctx> as <name>` where `name` is a PREDECLARED/hoisted local emits the pointer-alias form (`name = &(...)`) instead of a value assignment -- ill-formed C++.** `if flag: v = 5 else: v = 6` then `with Ctx(77) as v:` renders `v = &(__ctx_1.__enter__());` against the hoisted `int32_t v`, so g++ reports "taking address of rvalue" + "invalid conversion from 'tpy::BigInt*' to 'int32_t'". Independent of `match` (reproduces with a plain if/else hoist) and pre-existing on master. Consequence: it also blocks the `with`-as REBIND of a hoisted match capture, the one form the rebound-capture hoist (`fix-match-capture-rebind-hoist`) does not make work -- the bare-name, walrus, aug-assign, for-loop-var, tuple-unpack and nested-block forms all do. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: bind a different name in the `with`. Fix shape: the `with`-as target emit must assign (not take the address of) `__enter__()`'s result when the target is already declared and is not a pointer-local. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/capreassign/withas_nomatch.py`. Surfaced by the rebound-capture hoist fix's form sweep. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid) The same `match` capture NAME bound to DIFFERENT types across arms is rejected -- the first arm's type wins.** `case Cat(lives=v): v = v + 1` / `case Dog(name=v): v = "d:" + v` errors `Invalid operand types for '+': str and int` on the second arm: the post-match scope merge (`tpyc/sema/match.py`, the arm-bindings re-define loop) only defines a name absent from `bindings_before`, so the first arm's binding type is what later arms analyze against. CPython is dynamically typed here and accepts it. Loud (sema-rejected), not silent. Workaround: use distinct capture names per arm. The same one-slot-two-types family covers a capture name that reuses a local declared BEFORE the match at a different type (`v = 0` then `case Cat(lives=v)` -- `tpy::BigInt` assigned into `int32_t`, ill-formed; the pre-existing `declared_vars` bind arm, not the rebound-capture hoist, which only compares types ACROSS arms). Pre-existing (both shapes reproduce on master, unrelated to the capture-rebind hoist). A THIRD shape joined the family with the nested-match capture reuse fix and is rejected the same way: a NESTED match binding the outer arm's capture name at a different type shares one hoisted slot (a `match` is not its own scope), so `_reject_nested_capture_retype` (`tpyc/sema/match.py`) errors at the nested capture. That gate had to be added rather than inherited -- the cross-arm `capture_bind_types` agreement check sees only the current match's own arms -- and it closes a silent hole that predated the fix: where the shared slot already existed via the branch-decl hoist, an implicitly-convertible mismatch (`Int32` slot assigned an `Int64` bind) silently TRUNCATED on master (probe: `705032704` vs CPython `5000000000`); only the no-implicit-conversion pairs failed the build. Test `error_nested_capture_retype`. Workaround is the same: distinct names. NOTE the reject is broader than the numeric case that motivated it: under POLYMORPHIC dispatch a nested reuse naming a different subclass (`case Square() as p:` / nested `case Circle() as p:` off a `@dynamic` protocol subject) is the idiomatic shape and is now rejected, where master silently returned the OUTER arm's object (probe: `4` vs CPython `27`). That sub-shape has a real resolution the numeric one lacks -- bind the shared slot at the common supertype (the protocol / base type) rather than the per-arm concrete type -- so it is the first thing to fix when this entry is picked up. Fix shape: analyze each arm against its OWN pattern-binding type rather than the merged scope entry. Surfaced by the multi-arm probe during the rebound-capture hoist fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] A value-typed match capture rebound via `nonlocal name; name = ...` inside a NESTED DEF in the arm evades the capture-rebind detector, leaving it aliased -- the nonlocal write goes through the `auto&` alias and corrupts the matched object.** `case Cat(name=v): def bump(): nonlocal v; v = "rebound"` -> `written_names`/`body_writes_name` (`parse/nodes.py`) never descends into a nested def (`TpyNestedDef.sub_bodies()` is `[]`, and its writes are a separate scope reached only via `nonlocal`), so the `str`/`BigInt` capture stays bound `auto& v` and `bump()`'s write mutates `subject.name`; CPython rebinds the enclosing local, leaving the matched object untouched (probe: TPy `orig orig` vs CPython `rebound orig`, no diagnostic). Same family as the two match-capture entries above; the fix (value-vs-reference split) never fires because detection misses the nonlocal path. Fix shape: when a nested def declares `nonlocal <capture>` and writes it, count that as a name-level rebind of the outer capture. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the capture-rebind fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `list[Ptr[T]]` (or other container) LITERAL drops the element address-of and fails the C++ build.** `items = [Node(1)]` then `ps: list[Ptr[Node]] = [items[0]]` emits `std::vector<Node*> ps = {::tpy::__getitem__(items, 0)};` -- the element render is the `Node&` lvalue, not its address, so g++ rejects (`could not convert ... to std::vector<Node*>`).
