The container-literal element path has no `Ptr[T]` arm. AST-side and pre-existing, NOT a THIR divergence (dualgen IDENTICAL; THIR falls back at `expr.container_literal`). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Workaround: `ps: list[Ptr[Node]] = []` + `ps.append(items[0])`, which lifts correctly. Also why `tests/cases/tuple/borrow_tuple_ptr_element` carries `no_thir.txt` -- its `main` must build the `list[Ptr[T]]` via `.append`, which falls back at `expr.method_call`. Surfaced writing the corpus case for the borrow-tuple `Ptr[T]` element fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small, latent] A borrow-form tuple param's element alias in a RESUMABLE body mis-renders as address-of the element SLOT.** `def show(p: tuple[A, A]) -> Iterator[Int32]: a = p[0]; yield a.x` emits `a = &(std::get<0>(p));` -- the element already IS the pointer (`A*`/`const A*`), so the render produces `A**` into an `A*` frame field, g++ rejects-valid. Pre-existing (reproduces with the old mutable spelling too); corpus-invisible (no resumable case aliases a borrow-tuple param element). The const half of the shape now agrees (frame field, window seeding, and alias classification all read the deep-const verdict since `fix-yield-escape-verdict`); the remaining fix is the alias-ASSIGNMENT emission arm: a pointer-repr tuple element alias must copy the element pointer (`a = std::get<0>(p);`), not take its address -- the `&`-wrap is correct only for value-storage element sources. Probe `/tmp/agents/yieldfix/w3_probe.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small, latent] A bare non-value borrow yield never climbs a field-path alias to its owning param/receiver -- the yield arm omits the `through_field=True` the return arm uses.** `b = o.f` then `yield b` (non-value elem type): the return branch marks with `through_field=True` (statements.py TpyReturn, comment names exactly this climb `b = o.f; return b -> o`), but the pre-existing non-value yield arm calls plain `mark_param_mutated(root)`, so the `"o.f" -> "o"` climb (context.py, through_field-gated) never runs and the owner stays unmarked -- the enclosing method can be wrongly inferred readonly (const receiver vs mutable `val_or_ref<T>` yield slot), the identical failure class the borrow-tuple yield fix closed. The new tuple arm three lines below DOES pass `through_field=True` -- the asymmetry is now intra-yield. Related when fixing: `mark_param_returned` has no 8a.5/field climb at all (loop-iterable + bp sources only), so `b = items[i]; return b` under-records `return_borrows_from` on BOTH sides -- a combined "yield marking mirrors return marking" fix (this + the transitive-rule entry below) should sweep it. Surfaced by the fix-yield-escape-verdict readiness retrospective. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW-MED small, latent] `yield inner(items)` never propagates the callee's borrow contract -- the return branch's transitive rule (8b rule 3) has no yield analog.** `_analyze_yield`'s escape marking walks `addr_taken_roots(stmt.value)`, which returns `[]` for calls, so a yield of a borrowing call result marks nothing: the callee's `return_borrows_from` is consulted at RETURN sites (`tpyc/sema/statements.py` TpyReturn branch, "8b rule 3") but not at yields. Mechanism verified; the free-fn shape compiles and runs correctly (`/tmp/agents/yieldfix/transitive_probe.py`), so the observable break needs a shape where the missing mark flips a verdict -- e.g. a METHOD generator yielding `pick(self.items)` staying wrongly readonly-inferred (const receiver vs mutable yield slot), or a param verdict staying const. Fix shape: mirror rule 3 at `_analyze_yield`'s marking block (same `resolved_function_info.return_borrows_from` walk, same readonly-callee exemption). Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the yield-escape tuple verdict (the non-transitive half is fixed). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] `xs += xs` (self-extend) relies on `tpy::list_extend`'s `v.insert(v.end(), other.begin(), other.end())` with `other` aliasing `v` -- standard-UB if the vector reallocates mid-insert.** Verified working on the current toolchain (g++-14/libstdc++) for both no-realloc and forced-realloc sizes, so latent, not observed; CPython doubles the list. Fix shape: a one-line self-alias guard in `list_extend` (`if (&other == &v)` reserve first or copy the source). Pre-existing (any list `+=` reaches it); surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the pending-list aug-assign fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (diagnostic quality) An unsupported aug-op on a PENDING container target leaks the internal type repr in the error message.** `xs = [1, 2]; xs *= 2` errors correctly but prints `Operator '*=' is not supported for PendingList[IntLiteral(1), 2]#0` -- the pending type's debug repr, not a user-facing name (the annotated form prints `list[Int32]`). Fix shape: render pending container types through their user-facing resolution (or a `list[...]` placeholder) in diagnostics. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of the pending-list aug-assign fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence, pre-existing) A local rebound BEFORE and AFTER another local borrows it silently redirects the borrow to the new value -- the TpyName-reassignment path never runs the while-borrowed check its field/subscript-target siblings have.** `a = Acc(1); a = Acc(50); c = a.peek(b); a = Acc(99); print(c.n)` prints 99 where CPython prints 50: the pre-borrow rebind makes `a` slot-resident (`std::optional<Acc> __slot_N`), `c`'s `const Acc&` binds into that cell, and the post-borrow rebind writes the same cell through the shared rebind slot. NO diagnostic (contrast `a.n = ...`, which warns "Mutation of 'a' while borrowed"). The single-rebind shape (`a = Acc(50); c = ...; a = Acc(99)`) is NOT affected -- the pre-borrow rebind is required. Reproduces via the borrow-returning METHOD spelling on master (pre-existing); the operator spelling (`c = a + b`) newly reaches it since the operator-dunder borrow-return fix. Fix shape: run the borrow-tracker while-borrowed check (`has_borrow_of_kinds`) on TpyName reassignment targets like the TpyFieldAccess arm does (expect warning churn on rebind-heavy code -- audit before enabling), or retarget/invalidate the borrow at the rebind. Probes `/tmp/agents/opreview/reb3.py` (method spelling), the double-rebind operator form per the meta-review. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the operator-dunder borrow-return fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid by design, tracked for the real fix) `a += b` falling back to a borrow-returning `__add__` (no `__iadd__`) is REJECTED with a located error -- CPython rebinds `a` to the returned object (aliasing).** The loud reject (sema/statements.py aug-assign fallback) replaced a silent divergence: the emitted in-place update copy-assigned the returned object's fields into `a`'s existing storage, so post-`+=` mutations of the aliased operand were invisible through `a` (TPy 1/1 vs CPython 1/77). The real fix is rebind-as-alias for aug-assign targets: promote the target to a pointer slot (the `aug_assigned` prescan set does not feed the `reassigned` set that drives `T* a` codegen) and register the borrow, mirroring the plain-assignment path. Escape hatches until then (named in the error): define `__iadd__`, or return `Own[...]` from `__add__` (the fresh-value fallback works and matches CPython). Test `error_augassign_borrow_fallback`; inverse in `op_own_return_fresh`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the operator-dunder borrow-return fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid) A `__call__` with a concrete `Own[T]` param emits an `operator()` shim that forwards the param by bare name -- no `std::move` -- so the T&& delegation fails the C++ build unconditionally.** The method takes `T&&`; the shim's forward (`return __call__(x);`) passes an lvalue. The property-setter emit already handles this shape (`extension.py` moves Own-typed setter values); `_gen_call_operator` needs the same per-param `std::move` wrap. Loud, pre-existing (identical emit on master; probe `/tmp/agents/opreview/owncall.py`); adjacent to (but not introduced by) the shim-mirroring rework. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (diagnostic noise) "Mutation of 'x' while borrowed (field assignment may invalidate references)" fires on plain RECORD borrows, where a field write cannot invalidate the alias.** The invalidation rationale is container/view reallocation; for a `Cls&` borrow of a record, `a.n = 42` after `c = a.get()` / `c = a + b` is CPython-normal aliasing (the write is correctly visible through `c`) yet warns -- and the operator-dunder borrow-return feature now pins this noise into its own happy-path snapshots (`operators/op_borrow_return_alias` diag). Fix shape: scope the while-borrowed FIELD-assignment warning to borrows whose storage can actually move (containers, views, str/bytes buffers), keeping it for those. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (misleading diagnostic) An UNIMPORTED tpy type name used as a generic type ARGUMENT silently resolves to `?`; the error surfaces later at a use site as e.g. "Cannot index type ArrayList[?]" instead of the missing-import hint.** `al = ArrayList[Int32, 4]()` without `from tpy import Int32` compiles the construction with an unresolved type arg and errors only at `al[0]` ("Cannot index type ArrayList[?]") -- no location pointing at the subscript, no `'Int32' requires: from tpy import Int32` suggestion. Value/annotation positions get the hint (bare `Char("x")` -> "'Char' requires: from tpy import Char"; `Own` in an annotation -> parse-time "requires"), so only the expression-level subscript type-arg slot misses it. Correct rejection, wrong message + location. Fix shape: route unknown names in generic-subscript type-arg position through the same missing-import suggestion machinery instead of yielding an unresolved `?` arg. Repro `/tmp/agents/relcheck` smoke (2 lines). Surfaced by the 0.5.0 wheel-packaging smoke test. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent CPython divergence) A STEPPED slice of a `bytearray` returns `bytes` where CPython returns `bytearray`.** `bytearray(b"abcd")[::2]` is typed (and emitted) as `bytes` per the stub `bytearray.__getitem__(index: slice) -> bytes` (`lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_bytes.py`), so downstream `isinstance`/mutation behavior diverges from CPython's fresh `bytearray`. The basic-slice overload returns `BytesView`, a separate documented view design; only the stepped form's concrete owned type is wrong. Fix shape: a `bytearray_stepped_slice` returning `Own[bytearray]` (mirror of `bytes_stepped_slice`; bytearray is a reference type, so unlike the value-typed `bytes` returns the `Own` spelling is load-bearing there). Surfaced by the operator-dunder borrow-return fix's stub-contract sweep (cpython-parity agent). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) `a is b` between two reference-type records is rejected -- "'is' / 'is not' can only compare Optional/Ptr/union types with None" -- where CPython answers identity.** Reference types have stable addresses in TPy, so `is` is expressible as an address compare; today only None/enum/bool-literal compares are allowed (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py:983`). With borrow-returning dunders/methods now genuinely aliasing, identity is only observable via mutation -- a first-class `is` would let tests and users assert aliasing directly. Value-type operands should stay rejected (no identity). Loud, pre-existing. Surfaced writing the operator-dunder borrow-return aliasing tests. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (semantics decision for mixed value/reference operands).
- **[LOW small] (accepts-invalid) A bound `self.__init__(...)` call from a NON-`__init__` method passes sema and emits `this->__init__(...)` -- a call to a nonexistent C++ member (only the real ctor exists), failing the C++ build.** Only the unbound `Parent.__init__(self, ...)` and `super().__init__()` spellings are sema-gated to inside `__init__` (`methods.py`); the bound form slips through. Loud today (g++ rejects), but note for any future fix: a naive destroy+reconstruct re-init emission would BYPASS the CPython-interop `has_views_into` guard (tp_init's registry scan protects only the boundary path), so a TPy-side re-init must either be rejected like the unbound forms or route through the same live-view check. Probe `/tmp/agents/reinit_probe.py`. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion on the borrow-view rung.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) A by-value FIELD typed as a DERIVED class emits an incomplete-type C++ error regardless of source order -- derived-class definitions are sorted after all plain records.** `class A: ...; class B(A): pass; class C: b: B` fails `field 'b' has incomplete type 'B'`: `sort_records_by_inheritance` emits every non-derived record's full definition before any derived one's, so a plain record holding a derived-typed field can never see the complete type first (moving `B` earlier in the source does not help). CPython accepts the program. Loud (toolchain-caught). Fix shape: make the record sort field-dependency-aware (a by-value field edge is as hard a dependency as a base-class edge). Probe `/tmp/.../ordprobe/main.py` (10 lines). Surfaced building the borrow-view upcast test (which it blocks -- the classifier's exact-match gate is unit-pinned in `tpyc/interop/test_export_shape_views.py` instead). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent CPython divergence) A REFLECTED record-dunder binop (`x // y` resolving `__rfloordiv__`) evaluates the right operand before the left -- CPython evaluates left-to-right.** The `is_reverse` swap (`wl, wr = wr, wl` in `_gen_binop_from_result`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) happens before `expand_cpp_template` embeds the operand renders positionally (`({self}).__rfloordiv__({0})`), and C++ sequences the receiver expression before the argument -- so `make_n() // make_b()` with side-effecting operands prints `make_b, make_n` where CPython prints `make_n, make_b` (reflected dispatch happens AFTER both operands evaluate, left-to-right). Silent -- output-only, observable only when both operands carry side effects (no corpus case; `truediv_floordiv_distinct` uses names/literals). Both paths share the machinery (THIR's `_emit_binop` mirrors it exactly), so NOT a THIR drift -- same class as the membership needle/haystack entry below; the fix is an evaluation-order temp for the left operand before the swapped render, in BOTH paths. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) probing the record-dunder rvalue admission (2026-07-22 wave); pre-existing, newly REACHABLE through THIR's happy path but byte-identical either way. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) A sema-NARROWED storage-form Optional field passed to a `Ptr[T]` param still emits `&(field)` -- the address of the `std::optional<T>` itself -- a hard C++ compile error.** The `Optional[T]`-destination widen-back was fixed on `fix-narrowed-field-optional-widen` (arg-pass, `is None` compare, return lift all take the `optional_to_ptr` recovery), but the `Ptr[T]`-destination arm of `gen_call_arg` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:1169`, the `@value_ptr_coercion` lift) still gates on the analyzed (narrow-able) type, so a narrowed field misses the lift and g++ rejects with `cannot convert 'std::optional<Pod>*' to 'Pod*'`. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Pre-existing: reproduced identically on master (`49824d2`) via `/tmp/agents/probe_ptr_narrow.py`, unreached by the corpus. Same bug class as the branch's fix; the clean resolution is the shared-classifier centralization filed in TODO.md ("narrowed-field storage-Optional recovery"), which would cover this arm for free -- otherwise extend the same `_is_narrowed_value_optional` recovery to the `Ptr[T]` arm. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + safety-model) of `fix-narrowed-field-optional-widen`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent CPython divergence) Membership with a CALL haystack evaluates the haystack before the needle -- CPython evaluates the needle first.** `needle_expr in f()` emits the haystack call's render first (the receiver of `.contains`/`.find`/`__contains__`), then the needle; CPython's bytecode evaluates the needle expression before the container expression, so when BOTH sides have side effects the observable order differs. Silent -- output-only divergence, only observable when both operand expressions carry side effects (rare; no corpus case). Both codegen paths share the shape (the AST membership arms and THIR's mirrors emit identically), so this is NOT a THIR drift -- fixing it means an evaluation-order temp (`auto&& __in_lhs = <needle>;` before the receiver render, like the tuple-literal membership arm already does) in BOTH paths, or accepting and documenting the divergence. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) probing the wave-next membership widenings; pre-existing, unchanged by that wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid) An auto_readonly accessor whose body returns a reference field via a TERNARY into `T | None` is rejected on the const twin, though the equivalent statement form compiles.** `def __getitem__(self, w: Int32) -> Rec | None: return self._v if w > 0 else None` (`_v: Rec`, a reference type) errors `Cannot pass readonly[Rec] as mutable readonly[Rec] | None in ternary branch`; the statement form (`if w > 0: return self._v` / `return None`) compiles and aliases correctly. Root cause: the implicit-auto_readonly const twin (readonly `self` => `self._v` is `readonly[Rec]`) checks the ternary return arm against the UN-projected mutable `Rec | None` return type, where the statement-return path projects const onto the return. Distinct from the mixed-arm ternary silent-copy entry below (that is a local-binding value-category copy with no diagnostic; this is a hard sema reject on a return). Loud (sema-rejected), not silent. Workaround: use the statement form. Pre-existing (unrelated to the `getitem_function_info` tagging); surfaced writing the accessor-generic-returns temporary-receiver test. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (project const onto the const-twin ternary return arm, mirroring the statement-return path).
- **[MED small] An accessor returning a ptr-variant union (`-> Dog | Cat`) through a `readonly` receiver misses the `&()` pointer lift and emits uncompilable C++ -- the Union sibling of the `Optional` const-twin return fix.** The `readonly[Optional[T]]` return unwrap on `fix-accessor-generic-returns` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) is deliberately scoped to `Optional`, but `_apply_implicit_auto_readonly` (`tpyc/sema/method_expansion.py`) wraps ANY non-value accessor return in `ReadonlyType`, including a ptr-variant union; `is_ptr_variant_union` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/forms.py`) never unwraps `ReadonlyType`, so the const twin of a `__getitem__`/`__deref__`/`@property` getter returning `Dog | Cat` binds a stored value into a `std::variant<const Cat*, const Dog*>` return (g++ rejects). Identical on master (pre-existing, NOT introduced by the branch); toolchain-caught, not silent; no corpus case exercises it. Fix mirrors the Optional arm: unwrap `ReadonlyType(UnionType)` (or teach `is_ptr_variant_union` to see through the readonly wrapper) so the ptr-variant lift applies. Same accessor-return family as the `@property` pointer-repr `Optional` entry below. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + meta-review reproduction) of the accessor-generic-returns branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `@property` getter returning a pointer-repr `Optional[T]` (`-> Rec | None`) emits an `std::optional<T>&`-returning accessor whose body returns rvalues/nullptr -- uncompilable C++ for any non-field-backed getter.** The return-codegen property arm (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`, `in_property_getter` + pointer-repr check) assumes a property getter returns a storage `std::optional<T>&` and skips the pointer lift, which only holds when the body returns a stored optional FIELD; a computed return (`return self._v` of a plain `Rec` field, `return None`) binds an rvalue / `nullptr` to the reference (g++ rejects). Same accessor-optional family as the record-`__getitem__` fix on `fix-accessor-generic-returns` (there the CONSUMER was wrong; here the EMISSION convention is) -- the fix must pick one convention for optional-returning property getters (likely the method convention: return `T*`, adjusting the property-read consumption sites symmetrically). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/map-generic-sub/concrete_prop_opt.py`. Surfaced by the accessor-generic-returns analysis (Phase 4 sibling survey). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (miscompile) A plain record pointer-local reseated from a `readonly[T]` param emits `Point* p = &(q);` off a `const Point&` -- invalid const conversion, uncompilable.** `def f(q: readonly[Point], c: bool): p = Point(1); ...; p = q` renders the address-of without const-propagating the pointer decl (`const_indirect_locals` never sees the reseat-source constness). Identical on BOTH the AST and THIR paths (byte-identical, both broken); no corpus case exercises it. Either propagate const from the reseat source into the decl (making every read `const Point*`) or reject at sema (a mutable-pointer local aliasing a readonly param is arguably a readonly-model violation -- consider the sema-error tier first). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the escape-hoist cell.
- **[LOW small] (crash) A container literal first declared inside an if/try/with/match branch of a NESTED def crashes codegen with `PendingListType should be resolved before codegen`.** The nested def's `FunctionTrackingState` is discarded by `nested_def_scope` (`tpyc/sema/scope_tracker.py`) via `restore_function_state` without ever running `LocalTypeDeduction.resolve_all()` on it, so the nested body's pending stores -- `pending_branch_decl_maps`, and the sibling `pending_composite_exprs` / `pending_loop_vars` -- are lost unfinalized. The enclosing-function flavors are fixed (branch-decl snapshots finalized in resolve_all); this is the same class through a different lifecycle root (nested-def analysis never runs per-function finalization). Repro: `def outer(c): def inner(): if c: xs = [1, 2] else: xs = [3]; return xs[0]; return inner()`. Fix shape: run the deferred-resolution finalization for the nested state before `nested_def_scope` discards it (covers all three stores at once). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit + safety-model) of `fix-pendinglist-branch-decls`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) `list(d.values())` / `list(d.keys())` over a dict with REFERENCE-type elements copies where CPython aliases, with NO warning -- the bound-container form of the same copy DOES warn.** `d: dict[str, Node]; vals = list(d.values()); d["a"].v = 999; print(vals[0].v)` prints `1` under TPy and `999` under CPython, and `tpyc` emits nothing; the sibling `c = list(b)` over a still-live `b: list[Node]` emits `warning: copies Node elements; use copy_iter() to make this explicit (or copy() to copy the entire container)`. Both verified at `c95406e26` and at HEAD. The divergence itself is the accepted materialization semantics (`docs` calls this out for container ctors) -- the DEFECT is that the diagnostic that makes it non-silent does not fire for a dict-view source. Suspected site: the warning gate in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py` (~855-900) keys on an lvalue source or on an rvalue whose element type carries a semantic `Ref`, and a dict-view rvalue satisfies neither; NOT runtime-confirmed, so treat the mechanism as a lead rather than a root cause. Codegen-path-independent (the shape falls back to the AST path entirely). Fix shape: extend the warning gate's rvalue arm to dict/container VIEW sources, whose elements are borrows of the parent container. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the THIR instantiation-gate wave; pre-existing, unrelated to that wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (crash) A container instantiation over a literal with NESTED literal elements crashes codegen: `PendingListType should be resolved before codegen`.** `a = list([[1, 2], [3]])` (also the single-element `list([[1, 2]])`) at plain function scope -- no nested def, no branch topology, so this is NOT the nested-def lifecycle entry above. The OUTER literal resolves (it is the call's arg), but the inner literals stay `PendingListType` and reach `to_cpp()` through the ctor's `@cpp_template` substitution (`_resolve_cpp_template_type_params`, `tpyc/sema/calls.py:361` -> `typesys.py:4181`) -- the instantiation path never runs the nested-literal resolution that the equivalent annotated decl (`a: list[list[Int32]] = [[1, 2], [3]]`) does. Flat element literals are fine (`list([1, 2])`); the named-local form (`src: list[list[Int32]] = [[1, 2]]; a = list(src)`) compiles. Same expected-type-into-nested-literal family as the annotated dict-literal-of-list-literals entry below. Fix shape: resolve the arg literal's nested element types before the ctor template substitution reads them. Surfaced by adversarial dualgen probes around the THIR container-literal instantiation arm; pre-existing on master. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid, loud) A NESTED list literal first declared in sibling branches (`try: xs = [[1, 2]] except ...: xs = [[3]]`) fails the C++ build at the branch slot-assign render.** The branch-decl force now resolves both nesting levels to plain `vector` (the type side is sound -- `_force_branch_decl_lists` recurses into the pending element type), but the slot-assign arm renders the nested literal as a flat brace-init against `std::optional<std::vector<std::vector<int>>>`, which trips the explicit `vector(size_type, alloc)` constructor (g++ "would use explicit constructor"). Flat literals work; a nested literal in a PLAIN decl also works (different emit arm) -- only the branch predecl slot-assign spelling is wrong. Probe `/tmp/agents/plfix/p_nested1.py`. Fix shape: render the literal through the same nested-container emit the plain decl uses (typed construction, not bare brace-init) at the slot-assign arm. Surfaced by the readiness retrospective's nested-literal probe on `fix-pendinglist-branch-decls`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid, loud) Calling an `auto_own[Self]` method directly on a freshly-constructed temporary fails the C++ build.** `Pair[Node](a, b).first()` passes sema, selects the consuming (`&&`) overload, and moves the field -- but codegen still types the bound local as `Node&`, so g++ rejects it ("cannot bind non-const lvalue reference to an rvalue"). The named-local form (`p = Pair[Node](a, b); p.first()`) compiles: a general method call hard-codes `is_consuming_receiver=False` (`tpyc/sema/methods.py`), so only the rvalue-receiver syntax reaches the consuming overload. Fix shape: bind the consuming call's result by value (or reject the rvalue-receiver form at sema) -- decide which, the render is currently inconsistent with the overload it selected. Pre-existing on master (verified in a worktree); surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) while checking the auto_own clone-pair carve-out. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid) Reassigning an alias to a NESTED list element fails when the outer list is unannotated.** `m = [[Point(1)], [Point(2)]]; row = m[0]; row = m[1]` errors with `Type mismatch in reassignment to 'row': expected list[Point], got Ref[PendingList[Point, 1]#0]` -- the element's pending type never resolves before the reassignment compatibility check, so the SECOND bind compares a resolved `list[Point]` against a raw pending node. The annotated form (`m: list[list[Point]] = ...`) compiles and runs. Two defects in one: the code is valid and rejected, and the message leaks the internal repr (the same leak as the pending aug-op entry above, different trigger). Repro `/tmp/agents/thir-wave/bugcheck/main.py`. Fix shape: resolve the subscript result's pending element type before `coerce_reassignment` compares it. Surfaced by adversarial dualgen probes around the THIR element-borrow decl arm; pre-existing on master, unrelated to that cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A container literal first declared in a try's ELSE body (with the except-handler declaring the same name) emits assignments to an undeclared predecl slot -- g++ `'xs' was not declared in this scope` / `'__slot_N' was not declared`.** `try: ... except ValueError: xs = [3] else: xs = [1, 2]` then a post-try read: the branch-decl predecl machinery covers the handler's decl but the ELSE body's assignment renders the slot/pointer form (`xs = &*(__slot_2 = {1, 2})`) without the corresponding declarations reaching its scope. The try/except both-branch merge (decl in try body + handler) works; only the else-body topology is broken. Pending-type resolution is NOT the issue (the resolved brace-init shows in the bad emit) -- this is a predecl scoping hole in the try emit. Probe `/tmp/agents/plfix/p_try_else.py`. Surfaced pinning a cpython-parity probe during the `fix-pendinglist-branch-decls` review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A tuple ternary whose arms carry DIFFERENT C++ tuple forms emits ill-formed C++ -- no sema diagnostic.** `t = p if c else h.pair` (a borrow-form `std::tuple<Int32, Box*>` param against a storage-form `std::tuple<Int32, Box>` field, or two params differing only in const-qualification) renders `((c) ? (p) : (h.pair))`, whose operands have no common type -- g++ rejects the `?:` (or the deleted `operator=` at the sink). `_gen_if_expr` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) bridges mismatched arms for the str-family, Optional and Union results but has no `TupleType` arm, so nothing lifts one side to the other's form. Loud (toolchain-caught). Both codegen paths share the shape (THIR mirrors the render byte-identically), so a fix must land AST-side first and be mirrored. Fix shape: give `_gen_if_expr` a tuple arm that lifts a storage arm via `tuple_to_pointer` (or rejects the mixed pair in sema). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the wave-next13 tuple-ternary admission. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (misleading diagnostic) The per-element `Own`-coercion warning and error name the WHOLE tuple where a single element is at stake.** Rebinding a mixed tuple local to an all-borrow storage source (`p = h.pair` for `p: tuple[Own[Box], Box]`) warns `copies tuple[Box, Box] into owned storage; use copy() to make this explicit` -- but only element 0 is copied; element 1 still aliases, which the pinned case's own output shows (`elem0: 7`, `elem1: 66`). The `@nocopy` variant is worse: `cannot copy tuple[Guard, Box] into owned storage of type 'Guard'; target is non-copyable` conflates the whole-tuple SOURCE type with one element's TARGET type in a single sentence. Root cause: the tuple-to-tuple element-wise compat recursion (`_check_compat`, `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py` ~1141) passes the whole-tuple `source_expr` unchanged into each per-element check, so the generic Own-coercion message reads `get_expr_type(source_expr)` -- the aggregate -- instead of the element's own type. The sibling `warn_pointer_repr_tuple_copy` path (container append) already gets this right and appends `(tuple element i)`, so it is the fix shape: pass the element's source (or at least the index) into the per-element check. Not silent and not a miscompile, but it weakens the "warned, therefore acknowledged" premise the `tuple/mixed_own_tuple_local_realias` `no_cpython` pin rests on -- a user can reasonably read it as "the whole tuple is deep-copied". Pre-existing in `compatibility.py`; newly REACHABLE because the mixed-tuple rebind now compiles. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the mixed-tuple decl-path fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) Param mutation inference does not reach through a TERNARY call argument, so a param mutated only via a ternary is inferred const and the C++ build fails.** `def via(b: Box, c: Box, pick: bool) -> Int32: return bump(b if pick else c)` (where `bump(b: Box)` writes `b.val`) emits `int32_t via(const Box& b, const Box& c, bool pick)` and then `bump(((pick) ? (b) : (c)))` -- g++ `binding reference of type 'Box&' to 'const Box' discards qualifiers`. Passing the same param DIRECTLY (`bump(b)`) infers `Box&` correctly, so the gap is the ternary arg specifically: `mutation_propagation` walks call args but does not descend into a `TpyIfExpr` to attribute the callee's mutation to each arm's name. Loud (toolchain-caught), no TPy diagnostic, pre-existing (reproduces at merge base). Any arg shape that wraps names in an expression is worth checking with it (an `and`/`or` arm is the obvious sibling). Workaround: bind the selected object to a local first, or use an if/else statement. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/mixdecl/y2_ternary_const.py`. Surfaced probing decl paths during the mixed-tuple borrow-position work. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A container literal first declared inside a TRY body of a generator/async function types the frame slot from one arm's fixed-size Array while the writes emplace a vector -- g++ "no matching function for call to `emplace`".** `async def f(n): try: xs = [n, n] except ValueError: xs = [0]` + a post-try read emits `::tpy::frame_slot<std::array<int32_t, 1>> xs;` (the size from the HANDLER's literal) against `xs.emplace(std::vector<int32_t>{n, n});` -- the write site reads the sema branch-decl snapshot (forced off the Array optimization by `_force_branch_decl_lists`, so `list[Int32]`), but the frame-slot field is spelled from `func.generator_locals`, which keeps the unforced per-decl type. The if/else and match-arm topologies are correct (their frame local carries the merged list), so the gap is the generator-locals collection not consulting the branch-decl snapshot. Loud (toolchain-caught). Repro `/tmp/agents/thir-wave/smoke13/trybody4/main.py` (`tpyc --dump-code` shows the mismatch directly). Fix shape: apply the branch-decl snapshot type when collecting generator locals (the same override `_emit_branch_decls` performs on `var_types`), so the slot and its writes agree. NB THIR mirrors the WRITE side by applying that same override (`lc.frame_local_types` in `tpyc/thir/lower/resumable.py`); when the slot spelling is fixed, both paths stay in lockstep. Surfaced by the THIR branch-nested frame-decl cell (wave-next13). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small, latent] A MIXED async tuple return (pointer-repr record element + value element) emits uncompilable C++ at BOTH ends -- the surviving sibling of the fixed value-tuple untargeted render.** `async def mixed(r: Rec, n) -> tuple[Rec, Int32 | None]: return (r, None)` is sema-accepted and breaks twice: (1) the callee's return render stays untargeted -- `std::tuple<Rec, std::optional<int32_t>> __tpy_async_ret = std::tuple<Rec*, std::monostate>{&(r), nullptr};` -- because the tuple-literal-targeted arm in `_async_return_value_cpp` deliberately gates on `not has_pointer_repr_element()` (the async return slot is the STORAGE tuple even for mixed shapes, so the borrow-form spelling never converts); (2) the CALLER's await-bind declares the frame field in borrow form (`std::tuple<Rec*, std::optional<int32_t>> a;`) and assigns the callee's storage tuple into it (`a = std::move(__r0).value();` -- no operator=). Fixing (1) is not just widening the gate: a param-rooted record element at a storage slot needs the sync arm's deref-COPY discipline (`tuple_to_storage` family), and (2) is a borrow/storage-form decision at the frame boundary -- the recurring duality class (docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md "Borrow Form vs Storage Form"). Both shapes unreached in the corpus; probes `/tmp/agents/fix-async-ret/probe_retro.py` (return site) + `probe_mixed_call.py` (both sites). Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion on the value-tuple render fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (consider sema-rejecting the shape first if the form design is deferred).
- **[LOW small, latent] A future `@native` Throwable subclass with 2+ `__init__` overloads where one param is `Optional`/protocol/union-typed would diverge between the AST and THIR native-exception ctor arg-lowering.** THIR's native-exception ctor-into-var (`_ctor_shape_ok` `is_native_exc` -> `THIRCtorCall`) lowers each arg against the RESOLVED winning `__init__` overload's `fi.params`, while the AST oracle (`_gen_record_ctor_args`) is arg-BLIND for multi-overload native ctors (`record_info.get_method("__init__")` returns the FIRST declared overload, so `init_params` is effectively `[]` for OSError-family construction regardless of actual arity). No observable effect today: every builtin exception ctor param in `lib/tpy/tpy/_builtins/_exceptions.py` is a plain `Int32`/`str` (no Optional/protocol/union param -- the only shapes where AST's `ptype=None` vs THIR's real `ptype` triggers a different arg-lowering special arm). Would only bite a hypothetical future native Throwable subclass carrying such a param across overloads; if one is added, either make THIR mirror the AST's arg-blind first-overload lookup for native exception ctors, or fix the AST to resolve the winning overload. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of `thir-optstr-exc`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong output) An `async with` whose `__aexit__` is typed `exc_val: None -> bool` (a suppressing manager) compiles without a diagnostic but does NOT suppress a body exception -- TPy panics with an uncaught exception where CPython suppresses and continues.** The `None`-typed `exc_val` slots past the block that gates the `Optional[BaseException]` suppression form (`BUGS.md` async-with entry / the diagnostic at that path), so this shape silently emits non-suppressing cleanup. Distinct from the documented `exc_val: Optional[BaseException]` async-with-suppression gap (blocked by a diagnostic): this variant compiles clean and miscompiles. Pre-existing -- reproduces on `ffb79ebbef`, unrelated to the cleanup-double-run fix; surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) probing adjacent shapes. Fix shape: either reject the `exc_val: None` + `bool`-return async-`__aexit__` shape with the same diagnostic the `Optional` form gets, or implement suppression for it. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (coordinate with the async-with suppression design).
- **[MED small] (missing diagnostic) The `'raise' in '__del__' cannot propagate` warning skips `try` subtrees, so a `__del__` that raises from inside a `try`/`except`/`finally` compiles silently.** A bare `raise` in `__del__` warns at the raise line; the same raise inside any `try` subtree (handler body, `finally` body, or the `try` body itself) produces NO warning -- the walker that finds raise sites does not descend into `TpyTry`. The user's only signal is the runtime `TurboPython panic: uncaught exception in __del__` abort (`tests/cases/exceptions/panic_del_try_finally_raise` pins it). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/finally-fix/probe_del.py` (silent) vs `probe_del_bare.py` (warns). Fix shape: descend into `try_body`/handler bodies/`finally_body` when collecting `__del__` raise sites. Was previously noted only as an aside inside the finally-fall-through-elision entry (whose other half is now fixed); re-filed standalone so it isn't lost. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A `return` of a TUPLE with reference-type elements under `try`/`finally` captures the elements (copy) before the finally runs, so a finally mutating an element local is invisible in the returned tuple.** `try: return (b, 5) / finally: b.n += 1` -- TPy prints the pre-mutation value, CPython (whose tuple holds an alias to `b`) the post-mutation one. Residual sibling of the fixed scalar case (finally-deferred return capture): the scalar fix captures a borrow before the inline finally chain and materializes after it, but a tuple literal must still EVALUATE non-name element expressions before the chain (evaluation order/side effects), so deferral needs per-element borrow capture plus an rvalue-hoist for computed elements (the frame-capture rvalue arg hoist is the precedent machinery). Probe `/tmp/agents/finally-return-mut/probe_sib45.py` (`f_tup`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent CPython divergence) A `return <reference-type local>` under a finally that CONTAINS a suspension (`await`/`yield` -- the CFG pending-return lowering) still captures the value eagerly, so the suspending finally's mutation of the local is invisible in the returned object.** The CFG arm parks the value in the frame's `__finally_ret_<n>` slot before transitioning to the finally states; a deferred borrow there would be a frame-self-pointer surviving suspensions (the async-borrow-escape family, BUGS 309/311), and multiple return sites share one slot, so deferral needs a parked discriminant (which return fired) materialized at AsyncFinallyExit -- or frame pinning. Excluded from the finally-deferred-capture fix in `liveness.collect_finally_return_candidates` (suspension check suppresses the WHOLE subtree of a suspending-finally try, so this also covers a return under an inner NON-suspending finally with a suspending-finally ancestor -- probe `/tmp/agents/finally-return-mut/probe_cfg_inner.py`, same eager-copy divergence, and the list shape keeps its loud copy() error there). Probe `/tmp/agents/finally-return-mut/probe_cfg.py`: TPy 10, CPython 11. For a `@nocopy` payload the eager pending-slot store (`this->__finally_ret_<n> = ...`) is a deleted-copy-assignment C++ BUILD FAILURE, not just a silent divergence. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `nonlocal` REBIND inside a nested def overwrites the outer local's storage IN PLACE (no slot model), observable through any alias of that local.** `scan_reassigned_vars` deliberately does not recurse into nested defs, so a local rebound ONLY via `nonlocal b; b = ...` in a closure never gets the `__slot_N` pointer indirection a direct rebind gets -- the closure's assignment clobbers the same C++ storage, where CPython rebinding leaves the old object alive for existing references. The finally-deferred return capture EXCLUDES such names from deferral (`liveness._collect_nested_def_nonlocal_rebinds`) so the pure-rebind-from-finally shape keeps the CPython-correct eager copy; the residual: a closure that both MUTATES and rebinds the name, called from a finally around `return <name>`, loses the mutation in the returned object (eager copy, TPy pre-mutation vs CPython post-mutation). Fix shape: teach `scan_reassigned_vars` to count nested-def nonlocal writes as reassignments (slot model), then drop the deferral exclusion -- corpus-wide codegen change for closures that rebind. NOW ALSO REACHABLE IN RESUMABLE BODIES: since nested defs in async defs/generators compile (frame member functions), the same in-place rebind clobbers the frame FIELD -- an alias taken before the rebind sees the new object (TPy `1,999,999` vs CPython `1,1,999`); previously that shape was a loud C++ build error. Probes `/tmp/agents/finally-review/probe_closure_rebind2.py`, `/tmp/agents/nested_probe/p2_alias_rebind.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + cpython-parity) of the structural-deferral widening; resumable reachability by the nested-def frame-member review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects valid) The closure-del guard for finally-deferred returns fires without reachability analysis: a nested def deling the returned name is rejected even when the closure is only invoked from a handler or the normal path, never from a finally.** `_check_closure_del_of_deferred_returns` pairs any deferred `return x` with any nested-def `nonlocal x; del x` in the function; under structural candidacy this rejects programs that previously compiled (probe: return in try, closure-del called only from an except handler, finally empty). Sound but over-broad by design (documented in the helper). Fix shape: narrow to closures reachable from a finally body's call expressions, or accept and document. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A pre-try alias bind of a local read later in a finally consumes it eagerly: `a = b; try: return a; finally: b.n += 1` moves `b` into `a` at the bind, so the finally's mutation of `b` is lost.** The backward last-use walker's `TpyReturn` handling clears the live set, discarding the finally's `b`-is-read fact for code positioned BEFORE the try -- `a = b` compiles as `std::move(b)` although `b` is read afterwards on the return path (TPy prints the pre-mutation value, CPython the post-mutation one). Independent of the finally-deferred return capture (reproduces at its merge-base; `return a` is not a deferral candidate since `a` is a reference bind, not an owned var). Fix shape: the return-statement arm of the backward walk must seed liveness from the enclosing finally frames before clearing. Probes `/tmp/agents/finally-parity/probe_alias_reverse.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity + safety-model). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A union-typed local returned as `Own[A | B]` from inside ANY try/finally emits invalid C++ (ptr-variant to value-variant conversion).** `v: A | B = A(); try: return v; finally: ...` -- `_make_return`'s finally capture binds `std::variant<A, B> __tpy_ret = v;` where `v` is the borrow-form `std::variant<A*, B*>` local, with none of the ptr-to-value-variant conversion the no-finally return path applies; the C++ build fails loudly. Independent of whether the finally reads `v` (probe `/tmp/agents/finally-return-mut/probe_union_noread.py`); pre-existing, surfaced while gating union locals out of the finally-deferred return capture (whose recipe also does not cover variants -- fixing this shape should add a deferred recipe too, so a finally mutating the returned union local matches CPython aliasing like the scalar fix). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Initializing a pointer-repr Optional local with a ternary whose arm is a fresh constructor call emits address-of-rvalue C++.** `b: Box | None = Box() if flag else None` -> `Box* b = flag ? &(Box()) : nullptr;` -- the slot-model materialization (`__slot_N`) is not applied to ternary-arm rvalues, so the C++ build fails loudly (`taking address of rvalue`). The plain form `b: Box | None = Box()` materializes correctly. Surfaced while writing `finally_mutates_returned_local`; workaround: assign in an `if`/`else`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `finally` body that terminates on every path but doesn't END in a literal `raise`/`return` emits an unreachable `throw;` after its catch-path copy.** `_make_try_finally_emit` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`) computes `finally_terminates` as a SHALLOW `isinstance(last, (TpyRaise, TpyReturn))` on the finally's last statement, while the normal-path elision asks the DEEP `stmts_terminate`/`try_terminates_ignoring_finally` question. So `finally: if c: raise A else: raise B` gets `finally_terminates=False`. That flag feeds two places: `_emit_try_with_finally` emits a trailing `throw;` after a catch-path copy that already always throws, AND `_push_finally` stores it as `FinallyContext.terminates`, which drives `_emit_finally_chain`'s `[[maybe_unused]]` tagging and trailing `return`/`throw` suppression at EVERY return/break/continue site inside the try body -- so the dead code appears at each exit site, not just once. Harmless: the shallow check can only err in the SAFE direction (a literal `raise`/`return` last statement always terminates, so the flag is a possible false negative only, never a false positive), and a false negative merely emits unreachable code the C++ compiler drops. Behavior is correct (verified against CPython). Fix shape: compute `finally_terminates` with `stmts_terminate(stmt.finally_body)` so both questions use one predicate. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/finally-fix/probe_deep_finally.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-ready of the finally-fall-through-elision fix.
- **[MED small] An except clause naming a class AND a subclass of it emits an unreachable C++ catch arm -- fails a strict-warning build (`-Werror=exceptions`).** `except Base:` followed by `except Sub:` (Sub derives from Base), or the single-clause tuple spelling `except (Base, Sub):`, emits `catch (const Base&)` before `catch (const Sub&)`; g++-14 rejects the second under the test harness's `strict_warn_flags`: "exception of type 'Sub' will be caught by earlier handler [-Werror=exceptions]". Valid Python that CPython accepts (the later arm is simply dead), and TPy issues no diagnostic of its own, so a user hits it as a raw C++ build error. **Pre-existing** -- the hand-written sibling form reproduces identically and predates the tuple form -- but the tuple form raises exposure sharply, because the natural Python spelling of an errno/OS group puts a base and its subclass in one clause (`except (OSError, PermissionError):`; PermissionError derives from OSError). NB `uv run tpy` does NOT reproduce it: the CLI leaves `warn_flags` empty, so only the test harness (and any user passing strict flags) sees it. The spelling-based dedup in `_except_clause_types` (`tpyc/parse/parser.py`) collapses only a literally repeated name, never a subclass relationship, which the parser cannot know. Fix shape is a **design decision**, not a patch: drop an arm whose type derives from an earlier arm's (semantics-preserving -- the earlier arm already catches it) in sema where the hierarchy is known, and/or warn on an unreachable except clause (a genuine Python-level lint CPython does not give). Both faces (tuple + hand-written siblings) should be decided together. Reproducer: a `tests/cases/` case with `except (Base, Sub) as e:` -- fails the exec phase; the same case with two sibling clauses fails identically. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity flagged the shape; the coverage test the review asked for immediately failed the build). Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (the diagnostic/collapse design), not `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A QUALIFIED `asyncio.run(...)` call resolves to a USER function of the same bare name, emitting an undeclared-namespace C++ error.** A module-level `async def run(x: int) -> int` alongside `asyncio.run(main())` emits `::tpyapp::__main__::run<std::monostate>(::tpy::make_adapter<...>(main()))` -- the qualified `asyncio.run` bound to the user's `run` -- and g++ rejects it ("'tpyapp::__main__' has not been declared"). CPython runs the same source fine (the qualified name can only mean the module's). Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid; the wrong-namespace spelling suggests the call is resolved by BARE name with the qualifier dropped, so a user function shadowing any qualified stdlib call is the general shape -- same family as the qualified-`except mod.X` builtin-shadow entry below, and worth root-causing together. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/mx/shadow.py` (10 lines). Workaround: rename the user function. Surfaced while probing `await` inside an `except (A, B)` body (the async probe used a helper named `run`); unrelated to that feature. Independently re-surfaced authoring the Phase-D3 `await_in_for_else` test (a local `async def run` shadowed `asyncio.run`; a duplicate LOW entry from that surfacing was folded into this one). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `isinstance()` on an EXCEPTION binding takes the union/variant path and emits `std::holds_alternative` against a class reference -- ill-formed C++.** `class Sub(Base)` (Base an Exception subclass), then `try: raise Sub()` / `except Base as e: if isinstance(e, Sub): ...` emits `std::holds_alternative<Sub>(e)` and `auto& __e = std::get<Sub>(e)` where `e` is `const Base&` -> g++ "'const Base' is not derived from 'const std::variant<_Types...>'". The same isinstance-against-a-descendant on a NON-exception class correctly warns ("folds to False (static dispatch; use @dynamic for runtime type checks)") and folds -- so the exception binding is the only receiver that reaches the variant path with no union in sight. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent, but it rejects the natural disambiguation idiom for any base-typed catch. Note the correct behavior here is NOT obvious: a caught exception IS genuinely polymorphic (C++ catch does real RTTI matching), so folding to False like a plain class would be wrong; a `dynamic_cast` narrow is the honest lowering. That makes this a design question as much as a fix. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/mx/iso.py`. Workaround: none for exceptions -- restructure into one `except` clause per concrete type. Surfaced by the cpython-parity review of the `except (A, B)` tuple-form design (it blocks the join-typed-binding ergonomics). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A non-fixed-int ctor wrapper (`int(...)`) around a >int32 literal DEFAULT on a BigInt field still renders bare, and the parser mis-renders `int(x)` as a truncating C++ cast.** `big: int = int(90000000000)` as a record-field default: `records.py`'s field member-init uses `fixed_int_literal_value_from_expr`, which peels only FIXED-int ctor wrappers (`Int64(...)`) -- `int` is not in `_FIXED_INT_NAMES`, so it falls through to the parser's pre-rendered string. Separately, `_get_default_value` (`tpyc/parse/parser.py`) renders a non-fixed-int 1-arg ctor call as `f"{func}({args})"` -> `int(90000000000)`, which in C++ is a truncating `int` cast, not a BigInt. So the field emits `::tpy::BigInt big = int(90000000000);` -- both the macOS `long -> BigInt` ambiguity (via the inner bare token) AND a value truncation. `default_to_cpp` (function/ctor params) avoids this because it recurses through ANY 1-arg `TpyCall`. Pathological spelling (`int(...)` wrapping a literal whose field is already `int`); no realistic user impact. Fix shape: either broaden the field member-init to recurse like `default_to_cpp`, or fix `_get_default_value`'s non-fixed-int-ctor arm to not emit a C++ truncating cast. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/ctorwrap.py` (swap `Int64` for `int`). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the int-literal-default fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An empty (`pass`-body) subclass used as a struct FIELD emits an incomplete-type C++ error.** `class Sub(Base): pass` then `class Holder: _s: Sub` (or ctor `def __init__(self, s: Own[Sub])`) fails to compile: `error: field '_s' has incomplete type 'Sub'` -- codegen forward-declares `Sub` but emits `Holder` (which needs `Sub` complete for the inline field) before `Sub`'s full definition. Reproduces with NO iteration / readonly / generics -- just an empty subclass as a field (`/tmp/agents/gap2/emptysub.py`). A non-empty subclass (adds its own field) is unaffected. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. Emit-ordering bug: an empty subclass isn't ordered before a record that holds it by value. Fix shape: include empty subclasses in the same complete-before-use topological emit ordering non-empty records get. Surfaced writing an inherited-`__iter__` test for the for-loop const-receiver fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The for-loop const-receiver `__iter__` fix doesn't cover a self-METHOD-CALL iterable (`for v in self.get_container():`).** `_record_iter_receiver_mutation` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) bails when `_root_name_of_expr(iterable_expr)` is None (a call has no name root), so a for-loop iterating the result of a self-method call whose `__iter__` mutates the returned receiver won't demote the enclosing method -> the same const-receiver compile error the fix eliminates for name/field iterables. `methods.py`'s explicit-call path has a None-root chain-to-self fallback (methods.py ~923) this helper omits. Exotic (a self-returning-mutating-iterable call) and loud (compile error), so filed rather than handled in the fix. Fix shape: add the chain-traces-to-self fallback (mark self mutated) for a None-root iterable, mirroring methods.py. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the const-receiver `__iter__` fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An unannotated list mixing a small int literal with a >int32 one infers an `int32` element type, truncating the wide literal (C++ `-Wnarrowing` build error).** `a = [0, 1234567890123456789]` emits `std::array<int32_t, 2> a = {0, 1234567890123456789}`, which g++ rejects (narrowing `long` -> `int` inside brace-init). A list with ONLY the wide literal (`[1234567890123456789]`) infers `BigInt` correctly (`std::array<::tpy::BigInt, 1>`); the bug is the element-type JOIN over mixed int literals -- the small literal pins the common type to `int32` without checking that a sibling literal overflows it. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid; an explicit `list[int]` annotation avoids it (gets `BigInt`, and the >int32 element now renders ambiguity-safe -- the target-less int-literal render in `int_literals.py`). Fix shape: the int-literal list-element join must widen to a type holding EVERY member (BigInt when any element exceeds the fixed width), not collapse to the narrowest present. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/inf.py`. Surfaced fixing the macOS BigInt list-literal ambiguity. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `@error_return` is not rejected on `__del__` -- a `try`/`except <ReturnException>` (or any @error_return propagation) in a destructor lowers to a `return ::tpy::make_unexpected(...)` / `return {};` inside the C++ destructor body, which is invalid C++ ("returning a value from a destructor").** A destructor has no return slot, but the @error_return lowering emits its goto-based `return <expected>` unconditionally. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid-looking code; exotic (no reason to use @error_return machinery in a `__del__`). Reproduces on master (pre-existing, unrelated to the fail-fast wrap). Fix shape: reject `@error_return` / return-exception propagation in a `__del__` body at registration (alongside the other `__del__` shape checks in `tpyc/sema/registration.py`). Surfaced by the codegen-correctness review of the `__del__` fail-fast change. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `async def __del__` is accepted and silently mishandled -- it emits a synchronous destructor running the body (dropping the `async`) PLUS a dead coroutine struct.** A class with `async def __del__(self)` compiles: codegen emits a real `~T()` whose body runs the method statements synchronously (ignoring that the method is a coroutine -- there is no way to `await` during C++ destruction) AND a separate, never-instantiated `__coro_T___del__` resumable-frame struct. So the async-ness is silently discarded and dead code is generated. A destructor cannot be async (destruction is synchronous), so this should be a clean sema rejection (like `self: Own[Self]` on `__del__`, already rejected). Exotic -- no user writes an async destructor -- so no current impact; loud only in that the dead struct is emitted. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/deldrop/asyncdel.py`. Fix shape: reject `async` on `__del__` at registration (alongside the existing `__del__` shape checks). Surfaced by the codegen-correctness re-review of the conditional `__del__`-wrap change. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] The `error_return_stmt_handled` flag is consumed by the FIRST call that renders, so a statement-handled @error_return call with another @error_return call in its ARG subtree emits ILL-FORMED C++.** The statement handlers (`_gen_error_return_var_decl` / `_assign` / the return pass-through / `_gen_error_return_call`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:4649-4658`) set the flag meaning "the OUTER call renders raw; the handler unwraps it" -- but `_maybe_error_return_unwrap` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:2791-2793`) clears it at the first fallible call it post-processes, which is the INNER arg call (args render before the enclosing call composes). Result for `x = f(g(3))` / `return f(g(3))` (both @error_return): the inner `g` renders as a raw `std::expected` where its success value is needed (no unwrap -- a C++ type error), and the outer `f` gets an unwanted expression-level unwrap inside the handler's own unwrap block. Loud (C++ build error), so no silent miscompile and no corpus case exists. THIR gate-rejects the shape (`error_return.nested_call`, `_reject_nested_error_return_arg` in `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py`) per the ledger's never-mirror-ill-formed policy; re-admit it there when this is fixed (key the flag to the specific handled node, e.g. carry the handled node's id instead of a boolean). Surfaced by /tpy-review of the THIR error_return opening. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] The expression-level @error_return propagate unwrap skips active finally bodies; the statement-level one runs them -- an inconsistent early return that silently drops cleanup.** Inside an @error_return function, a fallible call handled at STATEMENT level (`x = f()` / bare `f()`) propagates through `_gen_propagate_check` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`), which walks the live finally stack inline before `return make_unexpected(...)` -- but the same call in EXPRESSION position (`return f(a) + f(b)`, an argument, a condition) unwraps via `_maybe_error_return_unwrap` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~2807), whose propagate arm emits a bare `if (!t.has_value()) return ::tpy::make_unexpected(t.error());` with NO finally-chain walk. A `try:/finally:` around an expression-level fallible call therefore skips the finally body when the error propagates out -- CPython would run it (the exception unwinds through the finally). Silent (output diverges only when the error path fires under an active finally). Repro shape: `@error_return(E) def g(): try: return f(a) + f(b) finally: print("cleanup")` with a failing `f`. Fix shape: route the expression unwrap's propagate arm through `_make_return` (the statement expression can hold multi-statement checks), or hoist expression-level fallible calls to statement temps under an active finally. THIR mirrors the AST byte-identically here (mirrored, not endorsed -- `_er_check_inline` in `tpyc/thir/emit.py` carries the same note); fix both paths together under the byte-diff. Surfaced by the THIR error_return opening. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The system-lib version guard only fires when pkg-config can report a version; a too-old system lib installed without a `.pc` file still fails deep in the C compiler.** `system_version_too_old` (`tpyc/build/third_party.py`) probes `pkg-config --modversion` and is deliberately permissive when the version is undeterminable (no pkg-config, no `.pc`) so a valid from-source install is not blocked -- but that means a too-old from-source install (e.g. mbedTLS 2.27 headers in a non-standard prefix, no `.pc`) bypasses the guard and hits the cryptic API-mismatch compile error the guard exists to prevent. Acceptable today (from-source installs of a managed dep are rare and usually ship a `.pc`), but the guard is best-effort, not complete. Fix shape: fall back to a `MBEDTLS_VERSION_NUMBER`/equivalent compile-probe against the resolved headers when pkg-config yields nothing. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective of the version-guard work. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] The CMake-emit path (`tpyc -o`) never compiles TPy-owned third-party glue, so a `sources.cmake`-based build of any `ssl`/`datetime` program is missing the FFI shim in EVERY mode.** `resolve_build_plan` puts the glue (`mbedtls_shim.c`/`cacert_data.c`, `date_shim.cpp`) in `ThirdPartyBuildPlan.c_sources`, which only the direct-compile path (`tpyc -b` -> `BuildLayout.build_cpp_commands`) consumes. `Compiler.generate_cmake` (`tpyc/compiler.py`) and `emit_cmake_snippet` (`tpyc/build/third_party.py`) receive `third_party_plan.libs` but NOT `c_sources`, so the generated CMake project never builds the shim -- `tpystd::ssl` would link with undefined `tpy_tls_*` (and datetime with the missing date facade). Pre-existing (the shim was in `bundled_source_files`, also c_sources-only, before the system-mode work), and untested (the harness + nightly use the direct-compile path). Fix shape: thread `plan.c_sources` (source + per-file flags) through `generate_cmake`/`emit_cmake_snippet` into the emitted `sources.cmake`, mirroring how the direct path adds them. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the system-mode glue fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `@overload`-dispatched methods and simple-generator inline factories still emit inline-in-struct -- the residual member-body kinds that need complete sibling types at struct scope.** The ctor/special-member fix moved `__init__` ctors, `__del__` dtor/move ops, and `__copy__` copy ops to namespace scope (`records.py` `_gen_ctor_def` / mode-aware `_gen_move_and_destructor` / `_gen_copy_ops`), and plain methods already default out-of-line -- but `@overload`-specialized methods stay inline regardless of size (the emitters take a `mode` param that is always "inline" there), and a *simple* generator method's factory+lambda emits inline via `gen_simple_generator_inline`. A body of either kind that references a mutually-recursive sibling record (or any record defined later in the module) odr-uses an incomplete type at struct scope -- clang rejects, gcc happens to accept (same mechanism as the fixed ctor case, `tests/cases/records/recursive_record_mutual`). Exotic combos, loud (toolchain-caught). Fix shape: partition-aware overload dispatch (thread the decl/def mode through the specialized-method emitters); for simple generators either fall off the peephole for cycle members or emit the factory after the structs. Surfaced by the recursive-record ctor fix survey. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (acknowledged divergence, no longer a nightly blocker) TPy `asyncio.Future()` is constructible without a running loop; CPython >= 3.14 raises `RuntimeError` there.** CPython 3.14 turned the "no current event loop" deprecation into an error: `asyncio.Future()` outside a running loop now raises `RuntimeError: There is no current event loop` (from `events.get_event_loop`). TPy's `lib/tpy/asyncio` still allows loop-less construction (matching pre-3.14 permissiveness) -- a silent parity divergence, but with no diagnostic/escape-hatch today. `tests/cases/async/future_basic` was restructured to construct the Future inside `asyncio.run`'s loop (so it no longer trips the divergence and the 2604 row passes), but the divergence itself is unfixed. To close it: bind Future construction to a running loop like modern CPython (raise when none), or accept it explicitly with a documented note. Surfaced by the nightly CI 26.04 rows (Python 3.14.4).
- **[LOW small] (silent misbinding, not unsound) A genuinely-new method type param whose NAME collides with an intermediate generic subclass's class param is misclassified as class-bound -- explicit type args silently discarded, mismatched args wrongly rejected.** `class Base[T]: def pick[U](self, other: U) -> U`; `class Mid[U](Base[U])`; `class Leaf(Mid[Int32])`: through `Leaf`, the composed class subst carries `U -> Int32` (Mid's param), so `_analyze_generic_method_call`'s classification (receiver params UNION subst keys, `tpyc/sema/methods.py`) treats the genuinely-fresh `U` on `pick` as constrained: `l.pick[StrView](5)` compiles silently dropping the type arg (interim: the hoisted arity check now rejects it loudly, though the "expects 0 type argument(s)" message is misleading -- there IS a genuine new param), and `l.pick("hello")` is wrongly rejected "expected Int32". Type-consistent end-to-end (not unsound); exotic shape. Pre-existing family: before the subclass-receiver fix this shape errored "Cannot infer" (the by-name pre-substitution in `substitute_method_type_params` erased `U` regardless -- that name-capture is the true enabler). Fix shape: return the DECLARING RecordInfo from `lookup_record_method_overloads` (3-tuple), classify against the declaring class's params, and filter the subst to those params before pre-substitution (alpha-scoping the by-name capture). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model + architecture-fit) of the subclass-receiver bound fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The super() and unbound-self `Base.m(self, ...)` dispatch paths never compose the class subst down to the DECLARING base -- a shadowed-bound method inherited from a generic base above the named/direct parent still fails "Cannot infer type arguments".** Both paths (`tpyc/sema/methods.py` ~1208 `resolve_ancestor_instantiation` and ~2247 `get_parent_type_subst`) build the subst from the direct/named parent only, unlike `_analyze_instance_method`, which gets the level-by-level composition from `lookup_record_method_overloads` -- so the exact bug fixed for instance calls on this branch persists there whenever the declaring generic base sits one level above. Loud failure (wrong rejection), same family. Fix shape: route both paths through (or share the composition of) `lookup_record_method_overloads` instead of the two parallel one-level lookups. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the subclass-receiver bound fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (diagnostic quality, not unsound) The class-shadowed method-bound check on OPERATOR dispatch sites silently short-circuits for a subclass receiver -- a violation surfaces as a cryptic C++ `requires` error instead of the clean sema diagnostic.** The method-CALL path resolves shadowed bounds through subclass receivers (fixed on `fix-method-bound-subclass-receiver`: classification uses the composed class subst), but the operator sites still derive the class-param set and subst from the RECEIVER's record: `operators.py` (`_check_operator_method_bound`) does `if record is None or not record.type_params: return` -- empty for a subclass, so the check is skipped; `expressions.py` `__contains__`/binop-dunder sites pass `record.type_params` + `build_type_substitution(receiver)`, both empty for a subclass (and the binop site's `record.get_method` doesn't walk bases at all, so an inherited dunder may miss this path entirely); `protocols.py:790` (protocol-conformance / `hash()` path) likewise passes receiver-own `record_info.type_params`, so `find_method_class_param_bound_violation` bails at its `not class_type_params` gate for a subclass. Not unsound -- codegen's `requires` clause still rejects at C++ build -- but the user sees a template error instead of `Method '...' requires type parameter 'T' to satisfy '...'`. Repro shape: `class NEBag(Bag[NotEq, 4])` + `x in b` where `__contains__[T: Equatable]`. Fix shape: derive (declaring-class params + composed subst) for operator sites the way `_analyze_instance_method` does -- likely by threading `lookup_record_method_overloads`'s composed subst into the operator bound check instead of the receiver-record fields. Surfaced by the subclass-receiver bound-inference fix's sibling survey. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A borrow/reference local reused across sibling/outer scopes is declared in the inner C++ scope, not hoisted -- the later use is an undeclared identifier (C++ build error).** A local bound from a reference-returning accessor (`s = arc.get()`) or a `with ... as g` guard, first assigned inside a nested block (a loop body or a `with` body) and then read/reassigned in an enclosing or sibling scope, is emitted with its C++ declaration INSIDE the inner block; the later use emits `s = &(...)` / `g = &(...)` against a variable no longer in scope -> `'s' was not declared in this scope`. Two `with m.lock() as g:` blocks in one function reusing `g`, and `s = shared.get()` inside a `while` loop then reused after the loop, both reproduce. A third face: two SIBLING `except` handler bodies reusing one name (`except X as e: v = e.code.get(...)` / `except Y as e: v = e.code.get(...)`) -- the decl lands in the first handler's scope and the second emits `'v' was not declared in this scope`; reachable from hand-written sibling clauses, so it predates (and is independent of) the `except (A, B)` tuple form, whose arms are ordinary sibling handlers. Value-typed locals hoist to function scope correctly; only borrow/reference locals mis-scope. Loud (toolchain-caught), workaround = distinct names / restructure so a borrow local lives in one scope. Fix shape: hoist borrow/reference locals to their common enclosing scope like value locals (or declare-at-first-dominating-assignment). Surfaced building the Condvar ping-pong test (tpy.sync). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects reasonable code; CPython-parity trap) A generic METHOD cannot infer its type parameter from an argument whose type is a generic-class instantiation matching a `Generic[T]`-shaped parameter.** `def wait[T](self, mutex: Mutex[T])` called as `cv.wait(m)` with `m: Mutex[PingState]` errors `Cannot infer type arguments for method 'wait'. Specify explicitly: .wait[T](...)` -- the unification `Mutex[T]` <- `Mutex[PingState]` (and its `readonly[...]`-wrapped form) is not solved for a method-level type param. The suggested explicit spelling `cv.wait[PingState](m)` compiles in TPy but is INVALID Python (`method[X]` raises `TypeError: 'method' object is not subscriptable`), so any stdlib API that needs it breaks CPython parity. Workaround: give the method a non-generic param instead of the generic handle -- e.g. a non-`@dynamic` structural protocol the handle conforms to (monomorphized, so still a static call), which is what `Condvar.wait(lock: _CondvarLock)` does to take a `MutexGuard[T]` without a method type-param. Fix shape: extend method type-param inference to unify into a generic-class-typed (and readonly-wrapped) parameter, so `wait[T](guard: MutexGuard[T])` could be written directly. Surfaced designing `Condvar.wait` (tpy.sync). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] A comprehension over a user-defined `__iter__` emits C++ range-for (`obj.begin()/end()`) instead of the `__iter__`/`__next__` protocol -- fails to compile.** `[v for v in src]` (list/dict/set comprehension) where `src` is a user record with a generator (or any user) `__iter__` emits `auto __beg = __obj.begin(); auto __end = __obj.end();` -- but the record has no `begin`/`end`, so g++ rejects it. Reproduces with a plain non-readonly local (`/tmp/agents/gap2/comp_local.py`), so it is independent of const/readonly: the comprehension codegen path simply doesn't support user iterators (the for-loop path uses `::tpy::__iter__` correctly). Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. Workaround: use an explicit `for` loop building the container. Fix shape: route comprehension iterable codegen through the same `__iter__`/`__next__` lowering the for-loop uses (not C++ range-for) for user-iterator element sources. **Also carries a latent readonly-inference gap** (masked by this): a comprehension over a mutating `__iter__` field in a read-only-inferred method won't be demoted -- `_resolve_comp_iterable` (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) never records the iteration as a receiver mutation, unlike the for-loop (`_record_iter_receiver_mutation`); fix both together. Surfaced fixing the for-loop const-receiver `__iter__` bug (the iteration-family sibling). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Readonly-inference gaps for `async for` and `in`/`__contains__` (iteration-family siblings, both latent behind bigger gaps).** The for-loop const-receiver `__iter__` fix (`_record_iter_receiver_mutation`, `tpyc/sema/statements.py`) marks a mutating `__iter__`'s receiver mutated so the enclosing method isn't wrongly inferred readonly; the same implicit-dunder-doesn't-record-mutation gap remains in (a) `async for` over a field (`_analyze_async_for`, marks only `TpyName` iterables, not field access -- but currently unreachable because a user `async def __aiter__` + `yield` hits "async generators are not yet supported"), and (b) `x in self._src` via `__contains__` (`_analyze_comparison`, `resolved_contains` set but no mutation edge -- near-dead since `__contains__` is conventionally readonly, and this call site also carries the separate const-dict `in` bug below, so fix them together). Fix shape: route each site's implicit dunder through `_record_iter_receiver_mutation` (or the shared edge helper). Surfaced fixing the for-loop const-receiver `__iter__` bug. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A NARROWED value-repr `Char | None` operand compared to a str literal (`if o is not None: ... o == "a"`) emits `((*o) == "a")` (`char` vs `const char*`, invalid C++).** `_comparison_targets` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) keys the char-literal coercion on the RESOLVED operand type; for a narrowed operand that is `Optional[Char]`, so `is_char_type` misses and the literal renders as a string against the `(*o)` char deref. Same position-blind Char/str-literal render family as the `Char` frame-local entry below. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid; reproduces for narrowed NAME and narrowed FIELD subjects. No corpus case exercises the shape; THIR REJECTS it (`binop.narrowed_char_eq_literal`, `_narrowed_opt_char_vs_str_literal` in `tpyc/thir/lower/expressions.py`) so the body falls back and stays byte-mirrored -- lift that reject when fixing this render. Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/char_eq_probe.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the wave-2 branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `Char` frame local of a generator/async function initialized with a char literal (`c: Char = 'a'`) emits `c = "a";` (`const char*` -> `char`, invalid C++).** A local live across a `yield`/`await` is captured as a frame field and written by the resumable frame arm (`tpyc/thir/lower/resumable.py` `_lower_leaf`, and the mirror AST codegen) with a POSITION-BLIND init render (plain `gen_expr`), which renders the char-literal SOURCE (`'a'`, a str literal in the AST) as a string `"a"` rather than the target-typed char literal `'a'`. The SYNC path is fine -- a plain `c: Char = 'a'` local renders target-typed via `_lower_char_targeted` (`char c = 'a';`). Position-blind rendering is correct for the other value scalars (`int`/BigInt literal stays `0`, bool/float neutral); only `Char` needs the target type to turn the str-literal source into a char literal. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid; reproduces for both generators and `async def`. No corpus case exercises a `Char` frame local, so byte-diff/exec never surfaced it; THIR mirrors the AST render byte-identically (the shape can never appear in a green exec case, so it stays routed per the AST-miscompile mirror criterion). Reproducers: `/tmp/agents/thir_char/gen.py` (generator), `asy.py` (async). Fix shape: the frame-field init render for a `Char` slot must apply the same target-typed char render the sync decl arm uses. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `thir-read-only-admission`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Truthiness of a value-repr `Optional[bytes]` (`if b:` / `not b` / `while b:` on a `bytes | None`) emits `::tpy::is_truthy(std::optional<std::span<const uint8_t>>)`, which the generic `is_truthy(optional<T>)` compiles to an invalid `static_cast<bool>(span)`.** The runtime has `is_truthy(optional<std::string>)` and `is_truthy(optional<std::string_view>)` overloads (str-optional truthiness works) but NO `is_truthy(optional<std::span<const uint8_t>>)` overload, so a bytes-optional falls to the generic template (`opt.has_value() && static_cast<bool>(*opt)`, `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/core.hpp:561`) and g++ rejects `static_cast<bool>` of a span. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. `b is None` (`has_value()`) and passing the optional whole are fine; only truthiness of the bare optional trips it. THIR mirrors the render byte-identically (the shape can never appear in a green corpus/exec case, so it stays routed per the AST-miscompile mirror criterion).
