Fix shape: add an `is_truthy(const std::optional<std::span<const uint8_t>>&)` overload (empty span / nullopt -> false) alongside the string ones -- or, more generally, a span/BytesView `is_truthy` so bare `bytes`/`BytesView` truthiness is covered too. Same root cause for a value-repr `Optional[user record]` with `__bool__`/`__len__` (e.g. `Own[Flag] | None`): the generic template's `static_cast<bool>(record)` fails to compile instead of dispatching to the dunder -- a record-dunder-aware overload (or per-inner dispatch) should land with the same fix. Surfaced routing value-repr Optional[bytes] params through THIR. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Returning a view-form `str`/`StrView` value whole into an `Optional[str]` (`-> str | None`) return slot emits an un-convertible bare `return <string_view>;` (invalid C++).** A function `def f() -> str | None: x = "y"; return x` (or any `return <StrView local/param>` where the WHOLE view value, not a param passed through the `Optional[str]`->`Optional[str]` shim, feeds an `std::optional<std::string>` return) emits `return x;` -- but `std::string_view` does not convert to `std::optional<std::string>` (the `optional<string>` converting ctor is explicit), so g++ rejects it ("could not convert 'x' ... to 'std::optional<std::string>'"). The AST omits the `std::string(...)` wrap it emits at a plain `-> str` owned return. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. A str LITERAL (`return "y"`) compiles (bare `const char*` -> `optional<string>` is implicit), and an `Optional[str]` param returned whole takes the correct `s ? std::make_optional(std::string(*s)) : std::nullopt` shim; only a view-form value SOURCE is wrong. Workaround: wrap explicitly (`return str(x)` is not spellable; bind through the optional). Fix shape: at an `Optional[str]` return, a view-form source must take the same `std::string(view)` wrap the owned `-> str` return applies (then materialize into the optional). This is why THIR gate-rejects the shape (`return.opt_view_source`) rather than mirroring it. Surfaced routing value-repr Optional[str] returns through THIR. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A NARROWED `str | None` FIELD returned at a view (`StrView`) return slot emits a bare `return this->field;` -- invalid C++ (`std::optional<std::string>` does not convert to `std::string_view`).** `class Box: opt: str | None` with `def f(self) -> StrView: if self.opt is not None: return self.opt` emits `return this->opt;`; the narrowed read needs the unwrap (`return (*this->opt);`). Sibling shapes at the same slot are correct -- a plain owned `str` field emits the bare member read, which IS right there -- so it is the narrowing unwrap that is dropped, not the member render. Mirror image of the view-into-`Optional[str]` return entry above. THIR deliberately keeps this shape unrouted (it would emit the correct `(*this->opt)` and diverge from the broken oracle): the view-target coerce arm types its str-field admission on the DECLARED field type. Surfaced routing str-family field reads at the view-return sink through THIR (adversarial dual-path smoke; g++-confirmed). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A non-copyable `Own` local assigned inside a `try:` block cannot be MOVED afterwards -- it is stored optional-form and any later use silently copies (a hard error for a non-copyable type).** A `resp` bound by `resp = getresponse()` inside a `try` (getresponse returns `Own[HTTPResponse]`) is emitted as `std::optional<HTTPResponse>` (the possibly-unassigned-on-exception representation), so a later `Response(..., resp)` that should MOVE it instead attempts a copy -- rejected for the non-copyable `HTTPResponse` ("cannot be copied ... Only the original owner can be moved at its last use"), and for a COPYABLE type it silently copies (warned) + then miscompiles the move-into-`Own|None`-field via `ptr_to_optional_move(std::optional<T>&)` (expects `T*`). Reproduced whether the move is in the same try-scope, in a sibling branch, or after the try. The fix in `tplib.requests` was to bind the value from a HELPER-function return (`_send_recv`) instead of a try-scoped local -- a normal local from a call return moves cleanly. Toolchain-caught for non-copyable types (loud); SILENT extra copy for copyable ones. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/streamprobe/optmove{3,5}.py` (miscompile), `optmove4.py` (silent copy+warn), `optmove{6,7}.py` (helper-return workaround compiles). Surfaced building `tplib.requests` streaming (moving the `Own[HTTPResponse]` into `Response._raw`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A QUALIFIED `except mod.X:` clause whose bare name `X` shadows a builtin exception wrongly matches the BUILTIN of that name (and its subclasses), not the user's `mod.X`.** `import tplib.requests as requests` then `except requests.ConnectionError:` also catches a raw builtin `ConnectionResetError` (a subclass of builtin `ConnectionError`), even though `requests.ConnectionError` and builtin `ConnectionError` are unrelated sibling classes (both merely descend from `OSError`) -- real Python `isinstance` would never match. Root-caused to the qualified/dotted spelling specifically: the UNQUALIFIED `from tplib.requests import ConnectionError; except ConnectionError:` resolves correctly to the user class. Confirmed with a synthetic `class MyErr(OSError)` reached via `except mymod.ConnectionError` wrongly matching builtin `ConnectionResetError`, so it is a general builtin-name-shadow in qualified-except type resolution, not requests-specific. SILENT wrong control flow (reroutes to the wrong handler on a plausible real failure). Workaround: use the unqualified `from mod import X` spelling in the except clause. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/probe_exc_module_shadow.py`, `probe_exc_unqualified.py`. Surfaced by the cpython-parity review of `requests` streaming (newly reachable: `iter_content` is the first public API that lets a raw unwrapped OS exception escape). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Forwarding a borrow-form `bytes | None` (Optional[bytes]) parameter into another call materializes a fresh owned copy instead of a zero-cost pass-through.** A function `f(body: bytes | None)` that calls `g(body)` where `g` also takes `bytes | None` emits `body ? std::make_optional(::tpy::bytes_copy(*body)) : std::nullopt` -- copying the whole payload -- even though source and destination are the same `std::optional<std::span<const uint8_t>>` borrow form. Not a miscompile, a perf gap; bites `tplib.requests`'s hot send path (extracting `_send_recv(body: bytes | None)` now copies the entire request body once per POST/PUT/PATCH, including large multipart uploads, where the pre-extraction code passed the owned body straight to `conn.request` with no copy). Same body-copy family as the requests `_encode_body`/`bytesview_to_bytes` perf residuals already tracked in TODO.md. Fix shape: forward a borrow-form Optional param by pass-through (no `bytes_copy`) when the destination param is the same borrow form. Workaround (lib side): keep `conn.request(body)` in the caller rather than behind a `bytes|None`-param helper. Surfaced by the codegen-correctness review of `requests` streaming. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Storing a generator's yielded REFERENCE-type value into an outer variable is neither copied nor rejected -- it aliases the generator frame and dangles once the loop ends (silent UAF).** `for chunk in gen(): first = chunk` then reading `first` after the loop (or after a `break`) prints garbage: the yielded `bytes` (a reference type) binds as a borrow into the frame's current-yield slot, and the plain outer-store copies the borrow, not the payload; the frame dies at loop end so `first` dangles. Using the loop var INSIDE the loop body, accumulating (`acc += chunk`), or copying explicitly (`first = bytes(chunk)`) are all safe -- only persisting the raw yielded value past the iteration step is wrong. Same generator-yield-borrow ABI family as the Safety-section resumable-frame borrow entries: the consumer-escape check is documented to reject re-yield/nested-capture and copy *value* stores, but a *reference-type* outer-store is neither rejected nor copied. No diagnostic; silent wrong bytes. Workaround: `x = bytes(chunk)` / `x = copy(chunk)`. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/streamprobe/breakcap2.py`. Surfaced adding `requests` streaming (`iter_content` chunks captured outward). Fix shape: the consumer-escape check must either reject or deep-copy a yielded reference-type value stored to a variable that outlives the iteration step. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `with EXPR as v:` whose EXPR is a call materializing a UNION-typed default argument emits the default's temp in a scope invisible to the with-header -- undeclared-variable C++ error.** `with obj.m("x", flag=True) as c:` where `m(self, a=None, verify: bool | str = True, flag: bool = False)` leaves `verify` to its default: codegen materializes the `bool | str` default as a `__tmp_N` variant temp but declares it in a block/statement-expression scope the with-header's `auto __ctx = obj.m(..., __tmp_N, ...)` cannot see -> g++ "'__tmp_N' was not declared in this scope". A SCALAR default (`Int32 = 1`) needs no temp so does not trip it; a single union default in a FREE-function call in the with-header also compiled in isolation -- the trigger is the union-default temp landing in the with-context lowering (reproduced with a method call carrying a union default plus a later explicitly-passed arg). Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. Workaround: bind the call result to a local first (`r = obj.m(...)`) then `with r:` -- the pre-bound form compiles. Fix shape: hoist union-typed default-arg temps for a with-header call into the enclosing scope (the same scope the `__ctx` temp is declared in), not the with-body block. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/streamprobe/withmethod.py`. Surfaced adding `requests` streaming (`with s.get(url, stream=True) as r:`, where `verify: bool | str = True` defaults). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A generator method that narrows an Optional reference-type `self` field is mis-inferred as `const` -- its default parameter is dropped from the emitted C++ signature AND the narrowed local is const-poisoned.** A `def gen(self, n: Int32 = 1) -> Iterator[T]` whose body does `x = self._field` (field `X | None`, `X` a reference type) then uses `x` gets emitted `const` (`__gen_C_gen C::gen(int32_t) const`, no `= 1` default), so (1) a call relying on the default fails "candidate expects 1 argument, 0 provided", and (2) if the method called on the narrowed local is `@readonly` (non-mutating) the local is `const X*` and a later non-const use is rejected ("invalid conversion from const X*"). Isolated triggers: yield-type is irrelevant (`Int32` and `bytes` both hit it); the trigger is *narrowing an Optional reference-type self field inside the generator*. A generator that reads a non-Optional scalar field (`self._n`), or one where the narrowed field's method *mutates* self (so the generator is correctly inferred non-const), avoids the const-poison half -- but the default-drop half persists whenever the generator is inferred const. Same "resumable generator method over a self field" family the async/generator TODO warns about. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. Workaround: make the parameter required (no default) and ensure the narrowed field's method mutates self (or copy the field's needed data into a scalar local before the generator). Fix shape: a generator method's self-const inference must account for the resumable-frame's mutation of captured `self` state, and (independently) the default-parameter value must survive into the emitted generator-factory signature regardless of const-ness. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/streamprobe` (`gen_default5.py` default-drop, `gen_c.py` const-poison). Surfaced building `tplib.requests` streaming (`Response.iter_content` over `_raw: HTTPResponse | None`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] (CPython interop) A boundary-crossed user exception's `self.message = ...` attribute is unreadable on the `.so` (`e.message` -> AttributeError) though CPython-native carries it.** In TPy `self.message = msg` assigns the inherited `BaseException::message` field (the `what()`/`str()` source), NOT a data field -- so `info.fields` excludes it and the data-field setter does not set a `message` attribute; the reconstructed instance (`pytype(e.what())`) has no `.message` either. Under plain CPython (and `uv run tpy`), `self.message = msg` creates a readable instance attribute, so driver-side code reading `e.message` works there but raises `AttributeError` against the compiled `.so`. **Pre-existing** (already true for message-only excs before the data-field rung; just untested -- no driver read `e.message`), surfaced by the `interop-exc-data-fields` cpython-parity pass. **Genuinely unresolvable without modeling `super().__init__`:** TPy conflates `self.message = msg` (would expose `e.message` in CPython) with `super().__init__(msg)` (would NOT), so unconditionally setting a `message` attribute would diverge for the super-init idiom instead. Outside tpyc's diagnostic reach (the reader is plain-Python driver code). Low practical impact (data fields cross via their own attrs; `str(e)`/`e.what()` carry the message). Revisit only if it bites; a fix needs sema to record whether the user explicitly assigned `self.message`.
- **[MED small] `k in <dict>` on a const/readonly dict whose VALUE type is not `Equatable` is wrongly rejected -- the membership falls to a generic element-scan instead of `dict.contains`.** `k in d` where `d` is a const/readonly `dict[str, V]` (an inferred-const `self._store` in a readonly method, or a `readonly[dict[str, V]]` param) and `V` has no `__eq__` is a hard sema error: "'in' requires element type 'V' to conform to 'Equatable' (no '__eq__' method)". Two wrongs: (1) `in` on a dict tests KEYS, so it should never require the VALUE type Equatable; (2) even the fallback scans keys (when `V` IS Equatable, e.g. `dict[str, str]`, it compiles and returns correct key-membership), so `dict.contains` should have been used -- it's O(n), not O(1). A MUTABLE dict[str, V] with non-Equatable V uses `dict.contains` and compiles fine; only the const/readonly receiver drops to the buggy path. So the trigger is receiver const-ness, not the `__contains__` method name (an earlier read misattributed it). Same const-receiver-drops-the-right-lowering family as the borrow-local const-drop fixes already landed (and the storage-tuple/borrow-tuple residual entry below). Loud (sema-rejected), not silent. Workaround: give `V` an `__eq__` (makes `in` compile and scan keys), test membership on a mutable binding, or use `.get(k) is not None`. Fix shape: `k in d` must resolve `dict.__contains__(readonly[K])` on a const/readonly dict receiver too (the const path is dropping the builtin container-membership lowering and demanding the value Equatable). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/cookieprobe` (`name in self._store` in a readonly method over `dict[str, Cookie]`). Surfaced building `tplib.requests` CookieJar. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects valid code) A class that explicitly defines `__hash__` + `__eq__` is still rejected as a `set` element / dict key -- "missing __hash__".** `class Counter: def __eq__(self, other): ...; def __hash__(self) -> Int64: return self.value` then `s: set[Counter]` errors `Type 'Counter' cannot be used as a set element (missing __hash__; use @dataclass(frozen=True) or define it explicitly)` -- even though `__hash__` IS defined explicitly. Reproduces on a PLAIN (non-`@export`) class, so it is a general Hashable-conformance gap, not interop-specific. Likely either the hand-written `__hash__` isn't detected by the Hashable-conformance check, or the `-> Int64` return type isn't accepted where a plain `int`/`Py_hash_t` is expected (unverified which). Rejects-valid: a user cannot put a hand-hashable class in a `set`/`dict` key at all; the only path is `@dataclass(frozen=True)`. Consequence for interop: an exposed class can never be a `set` element / dict key even in principle, which is *why* the container-element rung (`interop-container-element-exposed-types`) defers those positions rather than admitting them. Fix shape: make the set/dict-key Hashable check recognize an explicit `__hash__`+`__eq__` pair (and confirm the accepted `__hash__` return type). **When fixing, ALSO add + test a principled interop reject (`_unsupported_exposed_element` in `tpyc/interop/module_validators.py::_exposed_form_error`): an `@export` class cannot be a `set`-element / dict KEY at the CPython boundary regardless of its `__hash__` -- a class copies per element at the boundary (copy-on-cross), so it has no stable hash identity for a hash table. Today the over-broad Hashable check incidentally rejects it, so that interop reject would be un-observable dead code now; fixing THIS bug removes the shadow and would silently open the copy-vs-hash-identity footgun without the interop guard (see TODO container-boundary item 1-iv).** Reproducer `/tmp/agents/plain_hash.py`. Surfaced probing set-element admission during the container-element rung. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A view-form str source inside a NESTED list literal emits ill-formed C++ -- `m: list[list[str]] = [[sv]]` (StrView `sv`) renders `{{sv}}` with no `std::string` wrap.** The inner literal gets no element target at un-threaded nested positions (list elements do not thread, unlike Array elements and dict values), so the view->owned copy chokepoint never fires and the brace-init tries to seat a `std::string_view` in a `std::string` slot -- g++ rejects it. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid. Workaround: bind the inner list first (`row = [sv]; m = [row]`) or wrap explicitly (`[[str(sv)]]`). Fix shape: thread the element target through nested list-literal positions (the same threading Array/dict already get) so `_view_source_to_owned` fires. THIR gate-rejects the shape (`container_lit.nested_view`). Surfaced by the THIR container-literal cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `q: Ptr[DynProto] = None` emits `auto q = nullptr;` -- a `std::nullptr_t` local, so a later `q = p;` is a hard C++ error.** Only dynamic-protocol pointees hit it (record pointees spell `Cell* q = nullptr;` correctly): `_cpp_decl_type` sends any protocol-containing decl type to `auto` without excluding the null-literal init. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid. Workaround: initialize from a real pointer or declare at first real assignment. Fix shape: spell the pointer type (not `auto`) when the init is a None literal, or special-case the null init in the protocol-containing branch. THIR gate-rejects the first-decl shape. Surfaced by the THIR Ptr-pointee cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The mixed-element list-literal diagnostic can name the same type twice: `[b, b"m"]` with a view-resolved bytes local reports "element 2 is bytes, but earlier elements are bytes".** The message renders the resolved family name for both sides while the actual mismatch is view-vs-owned bytes; correct rejection, misleading text. Fix shape: render the resolved concrete types (BytesView vs bytes) in the mixed-type message, or special-case the view/owned split. Surfaced by the THIR container-literal cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Returning a str-VIEW source into a `... | str` value union emits bare `return s;` -- ill-formed C++ (no view->owned materialization).** `def f(s: str) -> Int32 | str: return s` (also a str local / field source) renders `return s;` where `s` is `std::string_view` and the return slot is `std::variant<int32_t, std::string>`; the variant converting ctor to the `std::string` member is EXPLICIT, so copy-init `return s;` does not compile ("could not convert 's' from std::string_view to std::variant<...>"). The member-INSERT boundary (a view into the owned `std::string` slot) needs the same view->owned wrap the arg-temp path already emits (`std::variant<...> __tmp = std::string(s);`), but the return arm skips it. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid. A str LITERAL (`return "x"`) compiles (target-typed bare render); an owned `String` source compiles (owned->owned copy). Workaround: wrap explicitly (`return str(s)`) or bind an owned `String` first. Fix shape: at a value-union return whose active member is owned `str`, materialize a view source to owned (mirror the arg-temp `_value_union_temp_slot` insert path). THIR gate-rejects the shape (`return.union_view_insert`). Surfaced by the THIR value-union-return cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An UNPROVEN `Optional[list]` field subscript emits raw `operator[]` -- no bounds check, no negative-index normalization (UB on OOB where CPython raises IndexError).** `self.maybe[1]` over `maybe: list[Int32] | None` renders `::tpy::deref_optional_check(this->maybe)[1]`: the `__getitem__` FunctionInfo lookup runs on the un-unwrapped `OptionalType` receiver, finds nothing, and falls through to the raw index render, skipping the checked dunder entirely. A PROVEN-non-None access (post-narrow) takes the normal checked path. Silent miscompile class (UB, wrong panic behavior). Workaround: narrow before subscripting (`if self.maybe is not None: self.maybe[1]`). Fix shape: unwrap the Optional before the `__getitem__` fi lookup on the runtime-checked receiver path so the checked dunder fires (mirroring what the proven path already does). Surfaced by the THIR field-receiver subscript cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) An UNALIASED module-variable read (`import helper; helper.G`) is rejected "Undefined variable: 'helper'" while the aliased form (`import helper as h; h.G`) compiles and renders qualified.** Sibling of the plain+aliased import-clobber entry above but reproducible with a single import statement: the module NAME binding is not visible to the variable-read path unless aliased. CPython accepts both spellings identically. Rejects-valid. Workaround: alias the import. Fix shape: bind the plain module name for attribute-based variable reads the same way the alias path does. Surfaced by the THIR global-spelling cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Aliasing a NARROWED union frame local in a resumable body emits an unnarrowed frame-slot pointer against the narrowed alias decl -- invalid C++ (build error).** `async def f(): b: A | B = A(); c = b; await ...` -- the alias `c` declares as the narrowed `A*`, but the RHS renders the raw frame slot (`std::variant<A, B>*`), so the assignment fails `cannot convert variant<A, B>* to A*`. The sync sibling binds through the narrowed cast alias correctly; the resumable frame render loses the narrowing at the alias-bind site. Loud, rejects-valid. Same declared-vs-narrowed render family as the `already_union` entry below. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/async-ret/p_union_eager.py`. Surfaced probing the async return-move review round. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `_gen_union_arg`'s `already_union` check trusts the DECLARED type, so a NARROWED pointer-variant union param passed as a union call arg renders bare where the `&`-lift is required -- invalid C++ (build error).** `def use(u: A | B): if isinstance(u, A): take(u)` -- the narrowed `u` renders as the cast-and-cache `A&` alias, but the already-variant check (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~355, keyed on `_get_cpp_declared_type`) sees the declared union and passes it bare instead of lifting `std::variant<A*, B*>{&(...)}`. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid. Same class as the `is_readonly_param` branch a few lines below, which already consults narrowing state -- fix shape: the `already_union` computation must account for `ctx.narrowed_vars` the same way. Module GLOBALS were briefly exposed to the same defect by the narrowed-valueopt-deref fix's resolver widening and are now shielded again (`_declared_type_incl_globals` is scoped to the narrowed-Optional family). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of `fix-narrowed-valueopt-deref`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A BARE int literal passed to a mixed scalar+record pointer-repr union param emits the bare literal where the pointer variant needs a temp+address lift -- invalid C++ (build error).** `def f(v: Int32 | Cat | None)` called as `f(7)` emits `f(7)` against `std::variant<std::monostate, Cat*, int32_t*>`; the literal needs the `int32_t __tmp_N = 7; f(...{&__tmp_N})` member lift the explicit ctor form gets (`f(Int32(7))` lifts correctly -- `union/union_none_commuted`'s oracle). The corpus never catches it because its witnesses use the explicit spelling while CLAUDE.md style prefers the (broken) bare form. Loud (toolchain-caught), rejects-valid. Workaround: wrap the literal (`Int32(7)`) or bind a local first. Fix shape: the ptr-union member-lift arm (`_gen_union_arg`'s member temp + `&__tmp` path) must admit bare int/float literals like it admits ctor-wrapped rvalues. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/bugverify/union_none_narrow.py` (`read_mixed(7)`). Surfaced by the design-track round-6 union/None narrowing probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `import m` and `import m as alias` in the same file clobber the plain binding -- reads via `m.` error "Undefined variable: 'm'".** Each import form works alone; together the aliased import overwrites (rather than adds to) the module-name binding, so a later `m.f(x)` is rejected as an undefined variable. Rejects-valid, loud sema error. Workaround: use one import form per module. Fix shape: module-import registration should bind BOTH names (each import statement adds its own binding; an alias must not remove the plain one). Surfaced by the THIR method-marker cell's cross-module probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An f-string format spec is spliced into the generated C++ format string unescaped -- a spec containing `"` or `\` (e.g. `f'{x:"<5}'`, a quote fill char) emits ill-formed C++.** `_gen_fstring` (and THIR's byte-identical mirror) inserts the spec text verbatim between `{:` and `}` inside a C++ string literal, so quote/backslash/NUL fill characters break the literal. Loud (toolchain-caught), exotic specs only. Fix shape: C++-escape the spec text at the single splice point (both paths share the render, so fix the AST arm and THIR inherits via the byte-diff). Surfaced by the THIR f-string cell's probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) Global-shadowing edge cases diverge from CPython: `G += 1` without a `global` statement compiles and mutates the module global (CPython: UnboundLocalError); read-then-shadow (`y = G; G = 5`) compiles reading the global (CPython: UnboundLocalError); a for-loop target shadowing a global is loop-scoped, so post-loop reads see the global (CPython: the loop var leaks).** Siblings of the existing write-before-`global` SyntaxError-parity entry -- all stem from `function_global_decls`/scoping being position- and binder-insensitive. Self-consistent TPy behavior, no miscompile; valid programs unaffected. Fix shape: the same source-order pre-pass that entry proposes should also classify aug-assign/read-before-write shadows and align for-target scoping (or document the scoping model as a deliberate divergence). Surfaced by the THIR global-read cell's parity probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Module-qualified and static method-call args skip per-callee render facts the free-call arg loop applies -- same call, different spelling by import form.** `import m; m.f(u)` renders union args dcbp-blind (`_gen_union_arg` without the callee's `deep_const_borrow_params` verdict) while `from m import f; f(u)` threads it -- a deep-const pointer-variant union param can spell different const-ness by call spelling; similarly the `function=True`-native and module-static arms render args via slot-blind `gen_expr_deref` when `_skip_first_pass` fires, and the method-loop ref-temp hoist covers only protocol/TypeParamRef slots vs the free loop's full `is_ref_param()`. Consistency family, not a standalone miscompile (both spellings compile today); THIR mirrors the blind forms byte-identically and gate-rejects the divergent rows. Fix shape: funnel the module-qualified/static arg loops through the same gen_call_arg pre-arms as free calls. Surfaced by the THIR method-marker cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `_gen_aug_assign_subscript_code` raises a bare `RuntimeError` (not a diagnostic) for a non-value-type element -- internal-error leak on `c[k] += v` over reference-type elements.** Should be a sema rejection or a proper diagnostic with a source location. Surfaced by the THIR subscript-write cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A member named like a recursive-union-alias wrapper still shadows it -- the last uncovered kind of the member/type-name collision class.** The shadow-qualification fix qualifies a local type reference inside a record whose member shares its name, but its `shadow_names` set (`sema/analyzer.py:_compute_record_shadow_facts`) and the flag-aware render paths cover only records, enums, and local `@dynamic` protocols. A recursive-union alias (`type Tree = int | list["Tree"]`) compiles to a wrapper struct named `Tree` with a *distinct* render path (`recursive_alias_cpp_names` / `RecursiveAliasInstanceType.to_cpp`, not `_cpp_base_name`), so a record with a member named `Tree` that references the `Tree` alias re-emits the original `'Tree' does not name a type` build error (compile-verified). Non-recursive aliases don't collide (they resolve to their target's C++ name). Toolchain-caught (loud), not silent; exotic shape. Fixing it conditionally means making a THIRD render path flag-aware -- more of the per-render-path parallel machinery the always-qualify consolidation (TODO.md `[consolidation debt]`) deletes outright; prefer closing it via that consolidation, not a 4th flag-aware branch. Repro: `type Tree = int | list["Tree"]` + `class holder: def Tree(self) -> int: ...; def wrap(self, t: Tree) -> int: ...`. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion of the member/type-name collision fix.
- **[LOW small] A generic dynamic-attribute dunder (`def __getattr__[T]`/`__setattr__`/`__delattr__`) passes comp and emits an uninstantiable template method -- C++ build error.** `_validate_dyn_dunder_kind` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py:219`) rejects @native/@staticmethod/@property/@overload/generator/@error_return on a dyn-attr dunder but NOT `type_params` (nor `is_async`), so `def __getattr__[T](self, name: str) -> str` translates cleanly to `template<typename T> std::string __getattr__(std::string_view) const` -- and the attribute-access lowering calls `__getattr__(name)` with no template args and `T` in no deducible position, so g++ can't deduce `T`. The parallel D16 `@export`-boundary shape validator was just unified so async/generic slip nothing (`tpyc/interop/export_shape.py`); this is the same missing-check bug class in the still-independent dyn-attr home. `async def __getattr__` is *incidentally* caught today (the async return wraps to a reference-type `Cancellable[T]`, tripping the return-type allow-list) with a misleading message, so add `is_async` for a clean diagnostic too. Toolchain-caught, not silent; exotic form, no current user impact. Fix shape: extend `_validate_dyn_dunder_kind` to reject `type_params` and `is_async` (the D16 dyn-attr feature is deliberately NOT coupled to the interop `export_shape` core -- narrower reject set, own messages). Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the export-class-validation consolidation. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A generic function that forwards a protocol-typed param into another generic function's protocol bound emits an undeclared template param -- C++ build error.** Inside `def forward[A, B](p: Pair[A, B]): return take(p)` where `take[K, V, T: Pair[K, V]]`, the inner call renders `take<A, B, T>(p)` with `T` never declared in the enclosing scope -> g++ "'T' was not declared in this scope". Inference itself is correct (K/V solve from the protocol param, and this is the only end-to-end exercise of the `_infer_protocol_type_arg_from_protocol` protocol-to-protocol path); the failure is downstream codegen not monomorphizing the inner bound's `T` when the conformer is itself a protocol-typed local. Pre-existing and NOT multi-param-specific: the single-param analog (`forward[A](p: Src[A])` calling `take[R, T: Cont[R]]`) fails identically. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Related to the async-frame template-loss entries but a distinct sync generic-call site. Fix shape: monomorphize the inner call's bound type-param against the inferred conformer (the protocol-typed param's concrete instantiation) instead of emitting the bare bound name. Consequence: the `_from_protocol` multi-param inference path has no end-to-end test coverage (blocked here) -- filed as a TODO follow-up. Surfaced building multi-param associated-type inference. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW medium] A generic call that fails inference because an argument doesn't satisfy a generic-protocol bound reports the vague "Cannot infer type arguments" instead of the precise "does not satisfy bound".** `unwrap(NoGet(1))` where `def unwrap[R, T: Container[R]](x: T) -> R` and `NoGet` lacks `get()` errors `Cannot infer type arguments for 'unwrap'. Specify explicitly: unwrap[R, T](...)` -- the real problem is that `NoGet` doesn't conform to `Container[R]` (so the associated-type pass can't solve `R`, and it's rejected at the reject-unresolved loop `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:1468` before ever reaching the bound-conformance check at :1477). The EXPLICIT-args spelling already gives the good message (`Type argument 'NoGet' does not satisfy bound 'Container[int]' for type parameter 'T'`, via `validate_type_param_bounds`); only the inferred path is vague. Root: `infer_type_params_for_function` returns a bare `None` for BOTH "genuinely uninferrable" and "bound violated", collapsing them so the caller can't tell which message to emit -- the `None` is deliberately silent so overload resolution can fall through to the next candidate (`type_ops.py:1481`). Rejects-invalid-code (correct rejection, misleading message), not a miscompile. Discriminator for the right message: when a sibling param stays unresolved, check whether the carrier conforms to the bound with the sibling left free -- non-conforming -> "does not satisfy bound"; conforming-but-sibling-unrecoverable -> "cannot infer" is correct. Fix shape (do NOT band-aid with a one-off out-param): the proper fix is a structured inference-failure result (reason + offending param/bound) returned by `infer_type_params_for_function`, routed so overload resolution stays silent (it discards the reason and tries the next candidate). SCOPE -- consolidate ONLY the tier that funnels through that primitive's `None` return: the free-function generic calls (`calls.py:3837/4353/5101/5128`), generic methods (`methods.py:2061`), and its record sibling `infer_type_params_for_record` (`calls.py:5484/5682`); the shared silent caller is `overloads.py:655`. Do NOT fold in two adjacent-but-separate mechanisms: the empty-container factory messages (`_raise_empty_container_from_arg` calls.py:761, and :1207/:1264) are `list()`/`dict()`/`set()` element inference keyed on `get_element_type`/`PendingListType`, a distinct path that may adopt the same failure vocabulary later but should not be force-merged here; and `_unsafe_cast_diagnostics` (:4727) is a bespoke per-function diagnostic with its own TODO to become a `@compiler_check` decorator. Touches overload resolution -- needs the `/tpy-fix-bug` design pass, not a spot patch. Regression guard `tests/cases/generics/error_assoc_type_unsolvable` pins the current (weak) message. Surfaced by user review of the associated-type-inference branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A None-typed generic result captured in a local cannot be used -- `std::monostate` vs `nullptr_t` hard C++ error.** `r: None = f()` where `f`'s type param inferred to `None` (e.g. `identity[R](x: R) -> R` called with `None`) emits a `std::monostate` local, and a later `r is None` renders `operator==` against `nullptr` -- no such overload, hard C++ error, no TPy diagnostic. Pre-existing (reproduces on merge-base with plain generic functions, independent of associated-type inference), but the `-> None`-satisfies-`R` protocol path makes it newly reachable via `spawn(none_task).join()` results bound to locals; consuming `join()` as a bare statement is unaffected. Toolchain-caught, rejects-valid. Fix shape: render a None-typed name read in an `is None` / boolean context as a constant `true`/`false` (its value is statically known), or map the comparison to monostate equality. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the associated-type-inference branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) A temp-hoisted call argument evaluates BEFORE a side-effecting receiver, reversing CPython's receiver-first order.** `make("R").eat(Box(mk("A")))` where `make()` (the receiver) and `mk()` (nested in a value-union-param arg) both print: the arg is hoisted to a `std::variant __tmp = ...;` statement emitted BEFORE the `make("R").eat(__tmp)` call expression, so the arg's side effect ("arg A") runs before the receiver's ("recv R") -- TPy prints `arg A / recv R`, CPython `recv R / arg A`. Only observable when BOTH the receiver expression and a temp-hoisted arg have side effects (a plain inline arg keeps correct order; a plain-name receiver has no side effect). Silent (no diagnostic, wrong order). Independent of the value-union double-eval fix (that fixed the COUNT; this is ORDER) -- the lazy-args change moved arg emission after receiver GENERATION but the arg's hoisted temp STATEMENT still precedes the inline-receiver call expression. Fix shape: when the receiver expression is side-effecting/non-trivial and any arg hoists a temp, lift the receiver into its own temp first (emit it before the arg temps), or emit arg temps after the receiver temp. Self-contained repro: a `Box.__init__(self, tz: Fixed | Zone | None = None)` (two value-record union arms force the arg hoist) plus `make(tag: str) -> Own[Box]` and `mk(tag: str) -> Fixed` that each `print(tag)`, then `make("R").eat(Box(mk("A")))` -- prints `A` then `R` (CPython `R` then `A`). Surfaced adding a side-effecting-receiver arm to the double-eval regression test; pre-existing. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (parity) Rebinding `self` inside a method silently reads the ORIGINAL receiver afterwards -- CPython reads the rebound object.** `def peek(self, other: "Chain") -> int: self = other; return self.n` -- codegen emits a fresh shadowing decl for the rebind (`const Chain& self = other;`) but every subsequent `self` read still renders against the receiver (`this->n` / `return (*this);`), so TPy returns the original object's state where CPython returns `other`'s. Silent (compiles clean, wrong value). Workaround: rename the local (don't rebind `self`). Root: codegen never seeds the receiver as a scope NAME, so the rebind creates a shadow local, but the `self`-read special-casing (the `this` render) doesn't know about the shadow. Fix shape: either reject `self` rebinds with a clear diagnostic (Rust-flavored; simplest and arguably better than the confusing CPython pattern) or route post-rebind reads through the shadow local. NB the THIR path GATE-REJECTS bodies with `self` rebinds (they fall back to this AST render), so fixing this fixes both paths. Self-contained repro: /tmp/agents/self_reassign_probe shape. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the THIR return-slot-tail branch; pre-existing. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Reading an Optional-REFERENCE element out of a tuple param drops const -- ill-formed C++.** `def read(pair: tuple[Box | None, int]): first = pair[0]` where `Box` is a reference type: the tuple param lowers to `const std::tuple<const Box*, ...>&` (element 0 is `const Box*`), but the extracted local is typed `Box* first = std::get<0>(pair)` -> "invalid conversion from `const Box*` to `Box*`" (g++, -fpermissive). The const of the borrowed tuple isn't propagated to the pointer-repr Optional element local. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. A plain (non-Optional) reference tuple element and a value-type Optional element are unaffected; the bug is specific to the pointer-repr-Optional element read. Fix shape: the tuple-element-extraction local for a pointer-repr Optional slot must inherit the source tuple's const-ness (`const Box*`), mirroring how plain reference elements already const-propagate. Self-contained repro: `class Box: def __init__(self, v: int) -> None: self.v = v` + `def read(pair: tuple[Box | None, int]) -> int: first = pair[0]; return first.v if first is not None else -1`, called `read((Box(5), 9))`. Surfaced adding an Optional-borrow-slot arm to the tuple double-eval regression test (union/tuple_arg_union_eval_once); pre-existing, unrelated to that fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A value of a SUB-union type does not coerce to a param/slot of a wider union (`timezone | None` arg to a `timezone | ZoneInfo | None` param is a type error) -- rejects-valid.** Sema reports "Type mismatch in argument 'tzinfo': expected None | ZoneInfo | timezone, got timezone | None"; CPython/typing accept member-subset unions. Workaround: annotate the local as the full target union upfront (datetime.py strptime/fromisoformat do this, with a comment pointing here). Fix shape: union-to-union coercion when every source member coerces to some target member (the C++ side needs a variant-widening conversion helper, mirroring to_value_variant). Surfaced by datetime v4's tz-slot widening. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An Optional (either repr) or value-opt scalar param on a NESTED def emits ill-formed C++ -- the lambda's params never get the pointer/optional classification `seed_param_locals` gives top-level params.** `def get(r: Rec | None)` nested inside a function: the AST renders body reads `r.v` (field access on a never-classified name; the correct render needs the pointer form) and call sites `get(a)` / `get(std::nullopt)` where the lambda param slot expects the converted form -- g++ rejects the combination. Value-opt scalar params hit the sibling shape (`return x;` returning `std::optional<int32_t>` as `int32_t` after a narrow). The shape never worked; `_gen_nested_def` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:3602`) only adds names to `local_scope_names` and sema `make_ref`s nested params, so neither the body classification nor the call-arg conversion runs. Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid. THIR gate-rejects the param shapes (`nesteddef.param_type`) rather than mirroring. Fix shape: run the param classification (or an equivalent lambda-scoped seeding) for nested defs on the AST path, plus the optional-ptr call-arg conversion at closure call sites. Probe-verified during the THIR nested-def review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (parity) A nested def shadowing a BUILTIN name is silently ignored at call sites -- `def abs(x)` inside a function, then `abs(-3)`, calls `::std::abs` (the builtin), not the closure; CPython calls the closure.** The AST's imported/builtin callee arm (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:3052`) precedes its `nested_def_locals` arm, so the builtin wins even though sema resolved the call to the closure's FunctionInfo. Silent wrong behavior (prints -3 where CPython prints the closure's result). A closure shadowing a builtin is exotic but valid Python. THIR gate-rejects the shadow (`call.closure_import_shadow`) so both paths stay byte-identical on the (buggy) AST render. Fix shape: check `nested_def_locals` BEFORE the imported/builtin arm in `_gen_call` (sema's resolution already points at the closure). Probe-verified during the THIR nested-def review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A package __init__ that calls submodule functions from record-method bodies emits circular C++ includes -- the build fails whenever a submodule TU is compiled first.** Package-import semantics emit `child.hpp #include parent.hpp` (importing `pkg._sub` imports `pkg` first), and the parent includes the child it uses -- a cycle. Harmless when the parent's uses are in free functions (defined out-of-line in .cpp; `os/` works this way), but record METHODS emit inline in the parent header, so a TU that includes `child.hpp` first sees the parent's inline bodies before the child's declarations (`#pragma once` skips the in-flight re-include) -> `'pkg::_sub' has not been declared`. Hit by the attempted `lib/tpy/datetime/` package split (class-heavy __init__ calling `_fmt`/`_parse` helpers from datetime methods); worked around by flat `_datetime_*` sibling modules (no parent edge, acyclic). Fix shape: emit forward declarations for the parent's cross-submodule uses, or drop the child->parent include when the child does not reference parent symbols, or emit record method bodies out-of-line in the .cpp when they reference submodules. Validation case once fixed: restructure datetime as a package with a pure re-export `__init__.py` + `_types.py` submodule holding the classes (the preferred target layout; a re-export-only parent does NOT dodge the bug today -- the parent must include the types module, so a sibling TU still enters it with its own header in-flight). Surfaced by the datetime v3 module split. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Tuple-unpack targets assigned in SIBLING branches are not hoisted -- the second branch emits an assignment to an undeclared name (invalid C++).** `if flag: a, b = pair(1) ... else: a, b = pair(2)` -- the first branch emits a block-scoped declaration (`::tpy::BigInt a = std::get<0>(...)`), the second emits plain `a = std::get<0>(...)` where `a` no longer exists -> g++ "'a' was not declared in this scope". Plain (non-tuple) assignments in sibling branches hoist correctly; the pre-scan hoisting misses tuple-unpack targets. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent; any multi-branch parser/dispatcher shape hits it. Workaround: unique per-branch names (datetime v3's `_apply_directive` ships with this workaround baked in -- revert to shared names when fixed). Fix shape: teach the pre-scan hoisting pass to treat tuple-assign targets like plain assign targets. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/dt_v3/repro_unpack_hoist.py`. Surfaced by datetime v3's strptime engine. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A format spec on a user-record f-string placeholder is misapplied to the `__str__` result instead of dispatching (or being rejected) -- silent divergence for `std::format`-legal specs.** `f"{rec:>30}"` compiles to `std::format("{:>30}", ::tpy::__str__(rec))` -- CPython calls `rec.__format__(">30")`, which for a plain record (`object.__format__`) raises TypeError on any non-empty spec, and for spec-aware types (datetime delegates to strftime) produces entirely different output; TPy silently string-pads. Specs that are NOT string-legal (`f"{dt:%Y}"`) fail as a raw g++ `std::format` error (leaky diagnostic, no TPy sema error). Sema only rejects specs on BigInt/IntLiteral (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py:3930-3966`); codegen applies the spec unconditionally around the `__str__` call (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:6396-6445`). Fix shape: sema rejects non-empty format specs on user types without `__format__` (loud, CPython-faithful for plain records) until a `__format__` protocol lands (TODO.md); datetime's strftime delegation rides on that feature. Surfaced by the datetime v3 design survey (blocks `f"{dt:%Y-%m-%d}"`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A ternary mixing a str slice with a str param is rejected: "Incompatible types in ternary expression: 'StrView' and 'str'".** `timestr = tstr[:i] if i > 0 else tstr` (with `tstr: str` a param) fails sema although both arms are the same runtime type (str IS StrView); spelled as an if/else assignment it compiles fine. Rejects-valid annoyance in parser-style code. Fix shape: unify str/StrView in the ternary join like assignment contexts already do. Surfaced by datetime v3's fromisoformat port. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A frozen dataclass with an `Optional[record]` field cannot synthesize `__hash__`: "No matching overload for hash(R | None)".** `@dataclass(frozen=True)` with `inner: R | None` fails at the synthesized `__hash__` because the `hash()` builtin overload set has no Optional arm (eq synthesis handles the field fine). Workaround: hand-write `__hash__` (datetime's aware `datetime` needed one anyway). Fix shape: an `Optional[T]`-accepting `hash` overload (hash the inner or a None sentinel, matching CPython's `hash(None)` stability) or field-wise None handling in the dataclass hash builder. Surfaced by the datetime v3 tzinfo-field probe. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] BigInt-default module: a LITERAL str/bytes slice bound emits ill-formed C++ (`1.to_fixed_check<int32_t>()` lexes as one pp-number token) -- build failure.** Under `--default-int BigInt` (or `default_int: BigInt`), `s[1:3]` emits `::tpy::BasicSlice{1.to_fixed_check<int32_t>(), 3.to_fixed_check<int32_t>()}` -- g++ rejects (`1.to_fixed_check` is a single preprocessing number; "unable to find numeric literal operator"). Root: `_gen_slice_bound` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~6215) appends the runtime-BigInt narrow to ANY bound render with no literal exemption -- unlike `gen_index_expr`, whose `_is_int_constant` skip renders literal indices bare (so `xs[1]` is fine under the same default). Negated literal bounds (`s[-2:i]`) hit the same token problem. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: bind the bound to a local first (`lo = 1; s[lo:hi]` -- the narrow then applies to a name, valid C++), or a fixed-int default. Fix shape: mirror `_is_int_constant` in `_gen_slice_bound` (literal bounds in int32 range render bare -- the BasicSlice int32 field absorbs them), or parenthesize the render before appending. NB the THIR slice-bound cell GATE-REJECTS literal BigInt bounds rather than mirroring the broken render; when fixed, widen `_slice_bound_ok` and the byte-diff validates. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probes-bigint/probe3.py` (`lit_slice`). Surfaced by the THIR BigInt-narrow probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The record-subscript BigInt-narrow width derives from `__getitem__`'s key param for ALL subscript operations -- a record whose `__delitem__`/`__setitem__` declares a different key width (or that has no single-int-key `__getitem__` at all) gets the wrong narrow on del/store.** `bigint_index_narrow_type` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`) consults `record_getitem_key_ret` only; sema's `del c[k]` requires just `__delitem__` and never validates the index against its key param (`tpyc/sema/statements.py` ~5166), so a record with `__delitem__(key: int)` and no `__getitem__` falls to the int32 default and `del c[2**40]` panics where CPython works -- the original BigInt-dict-key panic, alive on this rare path. A `__setitem__` key width differing from `__getitem__`'s narrows stores to the getitem width (AST and THIR share the helper, so both are consistently wrong the same way). Workaround: declare a single-int-key `__getitem__` with the intended key width. Fix shape: thread the operation's dunder into the helper (per-op key-param lookup via the same substitution-aware sema accessor, `__setitem__`/`__delitem__` falling back to `__getitem__`); the THIR mirror follows automatically (shared helper). Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion on the BigInt-dict-key fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A structural protocol declaring `__getitem__` with a BigInt key param fails C++ concept satisfaction for conforming user records -- the shape never builds.** A `Protocol` with `def __getitem__(self, key: int) -> str` emits a concept requiring `::tpy::__getitem__(t, std::declval<::tpy::BigInt>())`, which a conforming record (methods/operator[], no free-dunder arm for the record type) does not satisfy -- g++ rejects at monomorphization. Loud, not silent. When this shape is made to work, note the subscript index render also needs the receiver's declared key width: `bigint_index_narrow_type` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`) consults dict key types and concrete user-record `__getitem__` key params but falls to the int32 default for protocol-typed receivers, so a BigInt index would round-trip BigInt -> int32 and panic beyond int32. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/bigint-dict-key/probe_proto.py`. Surfaced by the BigInt-dict-key subscript fix survey. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Enum members outside int32 range are accepted at sema but emit `enum class E : int32_t { A = 5000000000 }` -- ill-formed C++ (build failure).** `class BigE(Enum): A = 5000000000` compiles cleanly through sema, but the emitted enum's underlying type is unconditionally `int32_t` while the enumerator value exceeds it -- g++ rejects the narrowing. `EnumUtil<E>::from_value` similarly spells `.to_fixed_check<int32_t>()` for a BigInt arg (`einfo.underlying_type` is always Int32 today), so even if the decl compiled, out-of-range lookups would panic. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: keep enum member values within int32 range. Fix shape: infer the underlying type from the member value range (int32 -> int64 widening; reject beyond int64), threading it through `EnumInfo.underlying_type` so the decl, `from_value`, and the fstring/print `static_cast<int>` arms all agree -- or reject out-of-int32 members at sema with a clear diagnostic. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probes-bigint/probe2.py` (`BigE`). Surfaced by the THIR BigInt-narrow probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects-valid) `not rec` is rejected for a user record without `__bool__`/`__len__`, although `if rec:` accepts the same operand.** `not make()` errors "Invalid operand type for 'not': Rec (expected bool, numeric, or type with __bool__/__len__)" while the bare truthiness context compiles and applies Python's always-true default. CPython accepts both (`not` is defined for every object). The asymmetry is sema-only -- codegen renders the negated always-true form fine, as the plain-enum sibling shows. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/truthy-arm/repro.py` (the removed `not make_rec()` line). Fix shape: let the `not` operand check accept any type the truthiness context accepts (the always-true default), rather than requiring a truthiness dunder. Do this together with the always-true truthiness WARNING queued in TODO.md -- both halves read the same no-truthiness-hook verdict sema already computes here, and fixing this one alone leaves `if rec:` silent about being unconditional. Surfaced fixing the folded-truthiness operand drop. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (missing diagnostic) The optional-truthiness warning does not fire for a `match` case GUARD, only for an `if`/`while` condition.** `case 1 if v:` with `v: Int32 | None` compiles silently, where the equivalent `if v:` warns "Truthiness check on optional value may also exclude falsy non-None values; use 'is not None' for None-only checks". The guard is a boolean context with the same footgun (a `Some(0)` payload is falsy), so the user loses the nudge exactly where the pattern-match reads most like a None test. Codegen is correct -- only the sema diagnostic is missing; the warning is raised where `if`/`while` conditions are analysed and match guards do not route through it. Witness: `tests/cases/match/guard_truthiness` (its `# tpyc: ok` annotation pins the current no-warning behavior). Fix shape: raise the same warning from the match-guard analysis path. Surfaced fixing the resumable/guard truthiness render. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Un-narrowed truthiness of a pointer-repr `Optional[container]` (`list`/`dict`/`set`/`bytearray`) ignores inner emptiness -- `if xs:` on a `list[int] | None` emits the bare non-null pointer test, so a non-None but EMPTY container reads truthy (silent CPython divergence).** The un-narrowed form renders `if (xs)` instead of `xs != nullptr && !xs->empty()`; CPython's `bool(container)` is length-based, so an empty non-None container takes the branch in TPy but not in CPython. The *narrowed*-by-name form (`if xs is not None: if xs:`) IS correct -- it dispatches `::tpy::__len__((*xs)) != 0` via the narrowed-occurrence swap in `gen_truthy_expr`. The sibling `Optional[user-record]` case dispatches `::tpy::ptr_truthy` in both forms; containers are excluded from the un-narrowed `ptr_truthy` path by the `is_user_record` gate (`ptr_truthy` dispatches `__bool__`/`__len__` *member* calls that the container runtime types do not expose). Workaround: test `len(xs) > 0`, or narrow and bind. Fix shape: extend the un-narrowed pointer-repr Optional truthiness dispatch to route builtin-container inners through their `.empty()`/`__len__` truthiness. Sibling of the storage-form `Optional[record]` truthiness entry below (same "the source shape is excluded from the dunder dispatch" family). Surfaced during the `Optional[record]` truthiness fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Truthiness of a STORAGE-FORM `Optional[record]` source (a record FIELD or a container ELEMENT) skips the record's `__bool__`/`__len__` -- `if self.f:` / `if xs[0]:` on a `Record | None` field/element emits the bare `std::optional` non-null test, so a non-None but falsy record reads truthy (silent CPython divergence).** The pointer-repr `Optional[record]` truthiness fix dispatches the dunder only for sources that render as a raw `T*` (pointer name / call return); a field or container element is `std::optional<Record>` storage (`is_storage_form_optional_source`), which is deliberately excluded (a `ptr_truthy`/deref there would fail to compile) and falls back to the pre-existing `has_value()` render. The narrowed field form (`if self.f is None: ...; if self.f:`) is worse -- it emits `::tpy::__bool__(this->f)` against the `std::optional`, a toolchain-caught build error (pre-existing, unrelated to the un-narrowed silent case). Workaround: bind to a local first (`x = self.f; if x: ...`) so the source is a pointer name. Fix shape: unwrap the storage-form `std::optional` (`optional_to_ptr` / `(*opt)`) before dispatching the inner record's truthiness, mirroring what the pointer-name path does; the narrowed field build-error needs the same unwrap. THIR keeps these on the AST path (`truthy.optional_field_narrow`). Surfaced by the /tpy-review of the `Optional[record]` truthiness fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `Any` holding a user record whose only truthiness hook is `__len__` is always truthy.** `any_to_bool_capable` in `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/any.hpp` recognizes bool construction, `.empty()`, and `.__bool__()`, but not `.__len__()`. It therefore installs Any's default always-true slot even though direct implicit truthiness correctly evaluates `::tpy::__len__(record) != 0`; an empty record wrapped in `Any` diverges from CPython. Workaround: define `__bool__` as well as `__len__`, or avoid erasing the value to `Any` before the test. Fix shape: add the `__len__` capability and dispatch to the same length-based truthiness used for direct records. THIR's `::tpy::to_bool(any)` mode faithfully preserves the existing runtime behavior. Surfaced by the THIR structured-truthiness probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A U3 isinstance-narrowed union member's truthiness skips its `__len__`/`__bool__` and emits the bare alias -- ill-formed C++ (build failure).** `def f(x: A | B): if isinstance(x, A): if x:` where `A` defines `__len__` emits `if (__x)` against the `A&` extraction alias (no `operator bool` -- g++ rejects), instead of the `::tpy::__len__(__x) != 0` dispatch the un-narrowed record form takes. Root: `gen_truthy_expr` keys the truthiness mode on the DECLARED union (which has no dunder -> modeless bare render) while the narrowed-occurrence swap substitutes the alias read -- the mode and the read come from different types. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: `len(x) != 0` on the narrowed name. Fix shape: key the mode on the sema-retyped narrowed occurrence (the same occurrence-vs-declared classification gap as the Any-narrowed print entry below). NB THIR's occurrence-typed mode would render the CORRECT dispatch here, i.e. diverge from the broken oracle -- so its truthy arm gate-rejects the composition (`truthy.narrowed_record_mode`, pinned by `test_narrowed_record_mode_truthiness_stays_ast`); un-reject when this is fixed. Surfaced by the indirection-consolidation truthiness probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A walrus-bound `with` manager (`with (m := CM()):`) emits its pre-decl AFTER its use -- ill-formed C++ (build failure).** The walrus registers `m`'s hoisted declaration (`std::optional<CM> m;`) with `ctx.temps`, but `_gen_with` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` ~3453) flushes pending temps BEFORE rendering the manager expression and never flushes again, so the decl lands at the first statement INSIDE the `try {}` -- after the `auto __ctx_N = (m = CM(...), *m);` line that reads it, and scoped so post-with reads dangle too. g++ rejects (`'m' was not declared in this scope`). Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Same latent flush-ordering gap applies to any manager expression that registers a hoisted temp/pre-decl; the common shapes (ctor calls with literal / record-rvalue args) render inline and are unaffected. Workaround: bind first (`m = CM(); with m:`). Fix shape: flush `ctx.temps` again after rendering each manager expression, before writing the `__ctx_N` line (the decls belong in the enclosing scope, so the second flush must precede the first header line, not follow it). NB the THIR `with` cell GATE-REJECTS temp-registering manager expressions rather than mirroring the broken flush order. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/with_mgr_temp_probe2.py`. Surfaced by the THIR `with`-cell manager-temp probe. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A global-variable write BEFORE its `global` statement compiles and runs; CPython rejects it as SyntaxError.** `def f(): g = 1; global g; g = 2` compiles in TPy (both writes hit the module global; `f()` leaves `g == 2`) where CPython raises `SyntaxError: name 'g' is assigned to before global declaration`. Root: `ast.parse` accepts the shape (CPython's check lives in the symtable stage, which TPy's parser never runs) and sema's `_analyze_global_stmt` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) has no ordering check -- `function_global_decls` is a whole-function set, so every write is treated as a global write regardless of position. Accepts-invalid parity gap, not a miscompile: TPy's behavior is self-consistent (and the AST and THIR paths emit byte-identical code for the shape). Workaround: none needed -- valid programs are unaffected. Fix shape: reject an assignment to a name that a LATER `global` statement in the same function declares (a source-order pre-pass over the function body in `_analyze_global_stmt` or the function pre-scan). Surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective second-opinion probing the THIR global-seeding invariant (reproducer `/tmp/agents/stmt-shapes/probe_global2.py`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] `is_const_union_source` does not see const TUPLE-local roots -- a chain rooted at a const storage/borrow-tuple local still loses const at every const-spelling sink.** The recursive "rooted in a const source" predicate (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py:is_const_union_source`, shared by the union/optional lifts, REF_ALIAS, and `is_const_storage_source`) checks the base name only against `const_ref_params` / `const_indirect_locals`. A name bound const as a TUPLE local (`const_storage_form_tuple_locals` / `const_borrow_form_tuple_locals` / `const_optional_borrow_tuple_locals` -- e.g. a const `auto&&` storage-tuple alias) rooting a deeper chain (`q = pair[1]` where element 1 is itself a pointer-repr tuple, or a union field off such a local) is missed, so the sink declares mutable against a const lvalue (same ill-formed-C++ class as the fixed deep-source entry, one level deeper). Latent: needs nested pointer-repr tuples (or a union field off a const tuple local), not exercised in-corpus. Fix shape: widen the base-name check to all five const name sets -- but that changes `is_const_union_source` for its union/optional consumers too (value-variant lift, storage-optional lift, REF_ALIAS, `.get()` accessor, ptr-variant init), so each needs its own verification + sibling tests. Surfaced closing the deep-source `is_const_storage_source` entry. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent CPython divergence) A value printed through an Any-ISINSTANCE-narrowed binding uses C++ default formatting, not Python's.** `def show(v: Any): if isinstance(v, float): print("x", v)` with `show(2.0)` prints `x 2` (CPython `x 2.0`); the bool face prints `1`/`0` instead of `True`/`False` (probes `/tmp/agents/while-arm/probe_floatfmt.py`, `probe_boolfmt.py`). Root: the print-arg classification keys on the DECLARED type (`get_resolved_type` reads the pre-narrow `Any` binding), so the narrowed alias streams raw (`std::cout << __v`) instead of taking the `::tpy::print_float` / `print_bool` formatter -- while a direct `print(2.0)` formats correctly. Un-narrowed Any prints route `::tpy::to_string`-family dispatch and are fine; only the narrowed-alias reads mis-classify. NB THIR mirrors this byte-identically (the D15 Any-narrow arm's print RAW / truthy TO_BOOL declared-type mirrors, `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py` print loop + `_lower_truthy`) -- when the AST classification is fixed to use the narrowed occurrence type, update both mirrors in lockstep (the THIR unit pins in `test_thir_any_narrow.py` will flag them). Fix shape: classify print args (and the truthiness arm's declared-Any `to_bool`) by the sema-retyped occurrence type when the arg is a narrowed name. Surfaced by the THIR Any-isinstance arm's byte-diff. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A temp-hoisting arg in a CONDITIONALLY-evaluated expression position -- a ternary branch or an `and`/`or` RHS -- evaluates unconditionally.** `x = 0 if True else take_vu(loud())` and `b = True or take_vu(loud()) > 0` both run `loud()`: the temp (`std::variant<int32_t, double> __tmp_N = loud();`) flushes at statement level, before the ternary/short-circuit that was supposed to skip it. Conditional-evaluation sibling of the (fixed) while-condition stale-snapshot miscompile -- same root, `ctx.temps` flushing at the enclosing statement hoists the init out of its evaluation region; the 2026-06-10 audit's `_gen_logical_value` eager-RHS finding is the value-result face of the same class. Side-effect-count divergence only (calls, allocations, exceptions from the untaken branch run anyway); values are correct when the branch IS taken. The while fix's loop-head restructure does not transfer (expression position, no statement boundary to restructure into); the natural fix is expression-scoped temps (GCC statement-expression wrap around the branch), which must NOT naively shrink temp lifetime -- a view returned into the enclosing expression (e.g. a Span into an array-literal temp) is valid until block end today and would dangle under a bare `({...})` wrap. `elif` conditions already restructure correctly (`_gen_if`'s nested-else recursion) and assert messages evaluate only on failure -- only the two expression positions remain. Probe `/tmp/agents/while-temp/probe_siblings.py`. Surfaced by the while-condition temp fix's sibling survey. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) A `while` condition combining a WALRUS binding with a temp-materializing arg keeps single-evaluation semantics, and the temp's init may read the walrus target before its first assignment.** The per-iteration loop-head restructure (`while (true) { <temps>; if (!(cond)) break; }`) is deliberately GATED to walrus-free conditions (`contains_named_expr` in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`): an in-head temp could run before the walrus assignment it reads (`while (n := head(xs)) > 0 and show([n]) >= 0:` -- `__tmp = {n}` precedes `n = ...`), and a borrow-form walrus (pointer-form, function-scope) aliasing into a per-iteration temp would DANGLE after the loop (verified UAF shape: `while (x := get_ref(Box(n))).val < 100:` + post-loop `x.val`). The gated fallback is the legacy pre-loop flush (byte-identical to the pre-fix emission): the temp evaluates ONCE -- stale across iterations -- and its init reads the walrus target's pre-assignment (default-constructed) value on first evaluation. The simple-generator peephole face instead REJECTS with a clear CodeGenError (on master it did not compile at all -- undeclared `__tmp` -- so the loud reject regresses nothing and avoids minting new silent-divergence surface). The read-before-assignment face is also live in `if`/boolop conditions today (statement-level flush precedes the condition); the conditional-position sibling entry above covers skipped-vs-run, not this value-ordering face. Underlying pre-existing safety gap: a borrow-return rooted at an rvalue-arg temp registers no loan, so nothing in sema stops such an alias outliving the temp in general. Workaround: bind the walrus in a separate statement (`while True:` + `if not ...: break`). Fix shape: hoist an optional slot pre-loop and emplace per iteration (`std::optional<T> __tmp_slot;` + `auto& __tmp_N = __tmp_slot.emplace(<init>);` in the head) -- function-scope lifetime with per-iteration re-init, matching CPython aliasing for post-loop borrows; the within-condition ordering face additionally needs the expression-order temp interleaving from the sibling entry above. Probes `/tmp/agents/while_probe/probe5.py` (ordering), `/tmp/agents/safety_check/repro3.py` (dangle). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + safety-model) of the while-condition temp fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A record rvalue passed to a *method* whose param is mutated (lowers `A&`) inlines instead of materializing a temp -- ill-formed C++ (cannot bind an rvalue to a non-const lvalue ref).** `a.absorb(A(4))` where `absorb(self, other: A)` mutates `other` emits `a.absorb(A(4));` against `void absorb(A& other)` -> g++ "cannot bind non-const lvalue reference of type 'A&' to an rvalue". Root: the user-record method-call arg loop (`_gen_method_call`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py` ~3871) has the TypeParamRef temp arm but is missing the concrete-param `is_ref_param() + is_temporary_expr` named-temp arm that the free-function path (~3125) and the ctor path (~3282) both have -- the ctor arm's comment even claims "Free-function and method paths already do this". The three paths are inconsistent: free functions temp EVERY record rvalue arg (even into a `const A&` slot, where the temp is unnecessary but harmless), ctors temp only mutated params, methods temp none. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent; the const-param method case (`a.combine(A(9))` into `const A&`) binds fine and is unaffected. Workaround: bind the rvalue to a local first (`t = A(4); a.absorb(t)`). Fix shape: add the missing arm to the method arg loop, keyed on the mutated-param verdict like the ctor's (and consider narrowing the free-function arm to the same key so the three paths agree). NB for the THIR call-arg cascade: GATE-REJECT this row (rvalue arg + mutated-ref method param), never mirror it. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probes/callarg_probe2.py` (the `absorb` line). Surfaced by the THIR call-arg-cascade micro-probe. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] THIR (`--thir-codegen` only): isinstance narrowing inside an INFERRED-readonly method drops the const spelling on a plain ptr-union param.** A method whose plain (unannotated) `A | B` param is const only by inference (`deep_const_borrow_params`) and which isinstance-narrows that param in its own body lowers to non-const `std::holds_alternative<A*>` / `std::get<A*>` where the AST emits the const forms (`const A*`). Root: `_narrow_member_cpp` (`tpyc/thir/lower.py`) keys the const-pointee verdict only on `lc.const_locals` (the U2 field-lift chain) and is never seeded from the method fi's `deep_const_borrow_params` for params. The `@readonly`-decorated case is safe only by accident: the decorator wraps params in `ReadonlyType` at parse time, and that shape stays gate-rejected. Flag-gated path only, corpus-invisible today; the divergent THIR C++ would not compile (a non-const `get` on a const param), and the whole-corpus byte-diff self-catches if a corpus case ever hits the shape. Fix shape: seed the param const verdict from `fi.deep_const_borrow_params` in `_narrow_member_cpp` (or gate-reject dcbp-const union params at narrowing). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probes/followups/ro_union_method.py` (verified divergent on the pre-branch base too). Surfaced by the THIR call-arg follow-up probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] `match` over a union of two same-canonical-named records is miscompiled: one covering `case` is treated as exhaustive, so the other member hits `std::unreachable()` (UB / silent wrong output).** `from m import Point as Foreign`, local `class Point`, then `def f(p: Foreign | Point): match p: case Point(y=v): return v` -- the union is correctly `std::variant<main::Point*, shapes::Point*>`, but the match is wrongly marked exhaustive, so codegen emits `switch(index())` with only `case 0` (the local `Point` arm) followed by `::std::unreachable()` and a dead `return -1`. A `Foreign` (index 1) subject falls through to `std::unreachable()` -> UB; observed silent wrong output (`f(Foreign(7))` returned `9`, the previous local value's register, instead of falling through). Root cause: union coverage is keyed by `str(member)` (bare name) -- `_match_missing_cases`'s `UnionType` arm (`str(m) not in seen_types`) and `_build_pattern_type_key` (`str(resolved) + constraint`) -- so the two same-canonical-named members both render `"Point"` and collapse to one coverage key, making a single `case Point` look like it covers both. The third and most severe site of the bare-`.name`-as-match-identity family (the concrete + union-membership identity sites were fixed on `fix-match-class-pattern-identity`). Pre-existing on `master`. Fix shape: key union coverage/missing-case tracking by qname (mirror the enum `_match_missing_cases` fix on `fix-match-aliased-enum`), so distinct same-named members stay distinct and the match is correctly non-exhaustive (fall through to the after-match code instead of `unreachable`). Sibling survey needed: same-named enum members or literal patterns collapsing in the same `str()`-keyed coverage. Surfaced by a user probe (`Point | ForeignPoint` matching) after `/tpy-review` of `fix-match-class-pattern-identity`; reproducer `/tmp/agents/fp_union/`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An alias-imported record used as a `match` class-pattern against a UNION subject is wrongly rejected as "not a member".** `from m import Widget as W`, then `match u: case W(...)` where `u: W | Other` errors `'W' is not a member of union 'Widget | Other'`. `_resolve_pattern_type` (`tpyc/sema/match.py`) mints the pattern's NominalType with the source spelling (`name="W"`) while the union member carries the canonical name (`name="Widget"`), so the exact-member match (`m == resolved`, `NominalType.__eq__` includes `name`) and the same-bare-name membership check both fail on the alias. Concrete (non-union) subjects work -- they compare the resolved record's qname, not the union-membership path. Pre-existing on `master` (independent of the class-pattern-identity fix; that fix preserved this branch's condition verbatim). Rejects-valid. Workaround: import the record under its own name, or match on a concrete (non-union) subject. Fix shape: resolve the pattern name to its canonical record and mint the NominalType with the canonical name (or match union members by qname), so the alias spelling doesn't defeat membership. Surfaced during `/tpy-review` of `fix-match-class-pattern-identity` while adding a union happy-path test. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `match` class-pattern against a union can still bind a wrong same-named *generic* record via the base-name fuzzy fallback.** `_resolve_pattern_type` (`tpyc/sema/match.py`) now rejects a wrong same-named record when all same-bare-named union members are non-parameterized, but when a same-bare-named member IS parameterized (e.g. `Box[str]`) the gate falls through to the pre-existing base-name fuzzy path, which -- for a single cross-module same-named generic member (`len(matches) == 1`) -- binds the pattern's fields against that member without a qname identity check. So `match u: case Box(...)` where `u: mod_b.Box[Int32]` and the source-scope `Box` resolves to a distinct `mod_a.Box` can bind the wrong record. Narrow (needs two same-canonical-named generic records across modules, one aliased/in the union); NOT introduced by the class-pattern-identity fix -- that fix strictly added rejections and left this parameterized path unchanged from master. Fix shape: in the fuzzy fallback, require qname identity for a concrete (non-type-arg-ambiguous) pattern before binding, mirroring the non-parameterized gate. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (safety-model, architecture-fit, cpython-parity) of `fix-match-class-pattern-identity`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Mutating through a `match` capture of a record-valued subject never reaches the subject's const verdict -- the write renders against a `const` binding, ill-formed C++.** `def f(x: Leaf | None): match x: case None: ... case v: v.n += 1` keeps the param `const Leaf*` (`decide_param_const` only sees DIRECT mutations of `x`; the capture is a separate binding), so codegen emits `auto& v = (*__match_subject_1); v.n = ...;` -- assignment through a const ref, rejected by g++. Same for an Optional field source (`q = h.opt; match q: case v: v.n += 1` off a const receiver). Direct subject mutation in the arm (`x.n += 1`) works -- sema attributes it and the param becomes `Leaf*`. The family is match-capture aliasing generally (union `as`-bindings share the alias mechanic), witnessed on the Optional-partition face; the mutation can also leak via passing the capture into a non-const param slot (same missing attribution). Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: mutate through the subject name, or narrow with `if x is not None:` instead of a match capture. Fix shape: propagate capture-alias mutations to the subject in sema's mutation/const scan (the capture is a declared alias of the subject's payload -- the alias_sources model fits). NB the THIR optional-partition tier MIRRORS written-through captures byte-identically (including this broken face -- toolchain-caught either way, no corpus case can exercise it); the lockstep pin `test_capture_through_write_mirrors` flags the THIR side when this fix changes the AST emit. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/thir_match_optpart/p5.py` (`mutate` / `local_subject`). Surfaced probing the THIR optional-partition match cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `min()`/`max()` reject record operands that define the full comparison surface.** `max(date(2021, 1, 1), date(2020, 5, 5))` -> "No matching overload for max(date, date)" even though `date` (dataclass `order=True` and hand-written dunders alike) supports `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=`/`==`. The min/max builtin overload set has no comparable-record arm, so every user value type is rejected. Rejects-valid, loud. Workaround: spell the comparison (`a if a >= b else b`). Affects all datetime types (date/time/datetime/timedelta). Fix shape: a protocol-bound generic overload (`min/max[T: Comparable]`) or routing through the comparable check `sorted()` uses. Surfaced by datetime v2 tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
