- **[LOW small] An explicit `bool(x)` call on a user value type without `__bool__` is rejected even though the implicit truthiness context works.** `t = time(14, 30); bool(t)` errors ("bool() cannot convert time") while `if t:` / `print(not not t)`-style truthiness correctly treats it as always-truthy (matching CPython's default object truthiness, e.g. time/date/datetime are always truthy since 3.5). The truthiness lowering has a default arm the `bool()` builtin overload set lacks. Rejects-valid, loud, generic (not datetime-specific). Workaround: use the value in a boolean context instead of calling `bool()`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of datetime v2. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) `round(huge_float)` in a bare (default-int) context silently wraps instead of overflowing.** `r = round(1751551805.5 * 1000000)` with no typed target resolves `round[T]` to the default Int32, and `round_to<int32_t>` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/builtins.hpp`) does an unchecked `static_cast<T>(std::nearbyint(x))` -> `-2147483648`, where CPython returns the exact int. With an `int`-typed target (`us: int = round(...)`) the BigInt arm is exact and correct. Silent wrong value in the bare case (same unchecked float->fixed-int cast family as `float(huge_int)` -> inf above, opposite direction). Fix shape: `round_to<T>` range-checks before the cast and raises `OverflowError`, or bare-context `round` defaults to BigInt. Surfaced probing datetime v2's `fromtimestamp`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] A class-typed local alias in a resumable (suspending) `async def` follows its SOURCE variable's reassignment instead of preserving object identity -- silent CPython divergence.** `p = w; w = Counter(999)` inside an async def that suspends: TPy reads the NEW instance through `p` (prints 999) where CPython preserves identity. The alias is frame-promoted to a slot that re-reads the source's storage rather than pinning the aliased object. General to resumable frames -- NOT coroutine-handle-specific (reproduces with plain class locals; predates the bound-coroutines feature) and NOT async-specific: an ordinary two-yield generator takes the same frame path, and there the frame is worse than sync -- `p = Point(11); while i < n: alias = p; p = Point(i); yield alias.x` yields `0 1 2` where CPython yields `11 0 1`, losing even the first generation (the sync sibling gets generation 0 right). Sync bodies are NOT correct here either, contrary to what this entry previously claimed: their separate rebind slot covers exactly ONE generation, so a loop rebind clobbers from the second iteration on -- the same single-slot-reuse root cause as the sync entry above. Probes `/tmp/agents/narrow/gen_trace.py`, `plain_trace.py`. Also underlies the bound-method-coroutine receiver capture: `w = Counter(40); c = w.bump(5); w = Counter(999); await c` silently reads the reassigned receiver. Silent wrong-value divergence, the top-priority parity class. Repro: /tmp/agents/coro/general_alias_reassign_async.py (from /tpy-review of the bound-coroutines zero-alloc round, cpython-parity specialist). Fix shape: frame-promoted alias slots must pin the aliased OBJECT at bind time, not re-derive through the source slot -- align with the sync pointer-local model. DISPOSITION (bound-coroutines /tpy-ready): the feature's receiver-borrow guarantee knowingly ships with this hole (rebinding the receiver between bind and consume reads the new object through the handle); an owning MOVE of the receiver is caught (demoted to copy+warn, test async_bind_receiver_moved), reassignment is not -- a conservative rebind-while-borrowed guard symmetric with the move demotion is a design option deferred to this fix. No committed test pins the rebind divergence (it would pin wrong output); repro above. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `.cancel()` is callable on a bare coroutine value (not just Tasks) -- CPython raises AttributeError.** Every async-def call result conforms to `Cancellable[T]`, so `f().cancel()` (or `c.cancel()` on a bound handle) type-checks and compiles; CPython's coroutine objects have no `.cancel` (only Tasks do) and raise AttributeError at the same line. Pre-existing conflation (the TPy async model deliberately unifies coro/task cancellation via `Cancellable`), but bound-coroutine handles make it reachable through a named value -- and a `c.cancel()` read also clears the "never consumed" warning (see the bound-coroutines TODO exclusions entry), so the coroutine silently never runs with zero diagnostics where CPython fails loudly. Crash-direction divergence (TPy accepts a superset). Fix shape: either hide `cancel` from the user-facing surface of a *bare* coroutine value (accept it only where an owned handle is spawned/driven), or document the widened surface as a declared divergence and exclude `.cancel` reads from clearing the unconsumed warning. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the bound-coroutines feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An `Own[@dynamic P]` value passed to a *structural*-protocol param mis-templates -- the `unique_ptr<P>` is passed as the template arg instead of the pointee.** `poll_once(aw)` / any `Awaitable[T]`-typed structural consumer receiving an `Own[Cancellable[T]]` value emits `poll_once<T>(handle)` where the deduced param is `std::unique_ptr<Cancellable<T>>`, failing the C++20 concept check (no `__poll__` on unique_ptr). Pre-existing (repros on master via an explicit `Own[Cancellable[T]]` param forwarded to `poll_once`). For coroutine handles specifically this is now unreachable -- the bound-coroutines borrow rejection ("cannot borrow a coroutine handle into argument ...") fires first with a clean sema error -- but other `Own[@dynamic P]` sources (non-Cancellable protocols) still reach the broken emission. Fix shape: the structural-protocol arg path should deref an owned-erased source (`*handle`) like the @dynamic borrow path does, or reject with the same clean diagnostic. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the bound-coroutines feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Slicing a readonly(-deduced) `list` param types the result `Span[T]` (mutable) -> ill-formed C++ (`span<const T>` does not convert to `span<T>`), no TPy diagnostic.** `def mid(items: list[Int32], sl: basic_slice) -> Int32: s = items[sl]; return s[0]` -- a read-only body const-deduces `items` (`const std::vector<int32_t>&`), so `::tpy::list_slice(items, sl)` returns `std::span<const int32_t>`, but sema types `items[sl]` as `Span[Int32]` (an explicit `s: Span[Int32]` annotation is accepted too), so the local declares `std::span<int32_t>` and g++ rejects the conversion. Same root family as the readonly-deduced `Own[Span[T] | list[T]]` return entry below (deduced receiver const-ness not propagated into the sliced Span's element); this is the plain-local face. Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: force the param mutable (e.g. the append/pop pair in tests/cases/operators/slice_var_index), or index elements directly. Surfaced drafting the THIR slice-ctor-call-arg corpus case. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `String`-annotated binding from a view-form `str` source emits ill-formed C++ (`std::string m = s;` copy-init from a `std::string_view`).** `def f(s: str) -> None: m: String = s` -- sema inserts the `str_to_string` coercion (INIT context), whose codegen lambda is identity everywhere outside ARG, so codegen emits `std::string m = s;`; `std::string`'s `string_view` constructor is explicit, so the copy-initialization is ill-formed and g++ rejects it. The same identity arm covers ASSIGN and RETURN, so any non-ARG `str_to_string` over a VIEW source (a `str` param, a `StrView`-resolved local) miscompiles; an OWNED source (`String` value, owned str local) is fine (plain copy ctor). Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: keep the binding as `str`, or go through an explicitly-materializing source (a slice -- `strview_to_string` always wraps). Fix shape: the non-ARG identity must materialize for view-form sources; the coercion lambda cannot see the source's C++ form, so the fix belongs at the `_view_source_to_owned` chokepoint (the same one a plain `t: str = <view>` decl-init uses), not in the lambda. NB the THIR incr-42 coercion cell mirrors this emit byte-identically (`test_string_init_identity_mirrors_ast_miscompile` pins it); when the AST arm is fixed, the byte-diff + pin flag the THIR lockstep update. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probe_string_init.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the THIR incr 42-45 branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `with ... as name` target hoist-predeclared as `std::optional<Record>` COPIES the `__enter__()` result where CPython aliases -- mutations through the target never reach the manager object `__exit__` runs against (silent divergence).** When an enclosing branch-decl pass predeclares the with-target as `std::optional<T>` storage (e.g. the inner target of a nested `with` -- `_emit_branch_decls`' optional arm), `_gen_with`'s assign-to-slot arm emits `name = __ctx_N.__enter__();`, copy-constructing into the optional slot; CPython binds `name` to the object `__enter__` returned (for `return self` managers, the manager itself). A mutation through `name` then diverges silently: CPython's `__exit__` observes it, TPy's does not (it landed on the copy). Probe-verified (a rename through the target prints the mutated name under CPython, the original under TPy). Reachable whenever a with-target name is hoisted to optional storage for a non-`@nocopy` record. The witnessing corpus case (`control_flow/with_statement` `nested_with_all_return`) only READS through the target, so the parity-blind cpy phase cannot see it; a mutating case would pin today's wrong output, so none is committed until the fix. NB THIR mirrors the arm byte-identically (`WithTargetArm.ASSIGN_OPT`, keyed on `optional_locals`); fixing the AST arm must update the THIR emit in lockstep. Fix shape: hold an aliasing form for record with-targets (pointer slot, like the PTR_DECL family) instead of value storage, or surface the copy (sema warning / nocopy-style rejection). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the thir-grind-wave5 branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Reusing a `with` as-target name whose binding is a VALUE (or value-repr Optional) slot emits `x = &(__ctx_N.__enter__());` -- ill-formed C++ (value slot assigned a pointer, address-of an rvalue).** Two faces of `_gen_with`'s already-declared arm: (1) two `with` blocks reusing a value-typed target (`with V() as x: ...` twice, `__enter__ -> Int32`): the first binds `auto x = ...` (int), the second hits the already-declared arm's `x = &(...)` -- `int = int*`, and the `&` is over the by-value `__enter__()` rvalue; (2) a value-repr Optional slot (`x: Int32 | None = None`, then `with V() as x:` in a branch): the slot is `std::optional<int32_t>` but the arm is keyed on `ctx.optional_locals`, which does not hold this shape, so the same `x = &(...)` mis-render fires. The RECORD reuse family is correct (`T* g = &(enter())` then `g = &(enter())`). Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: use distinct as-names per `with`. Fix shape: the already-declared arm must branch on the slot's C++ form (value slot -> `x = enter();`, optional value slot -> same, pointer-local -> the existing `&` assign), or sema should reject the value-target reuse. NB the THIR with gate (`_with_target_arm` in `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py`) rejects both bug faces (the pointer-local reuse and the hoist-predeclared optional-slot assign, `ASSIGN_OPT`, are the only already-declared shapes that route), so no THIR mirror exists to update -- when this AST arm is fixed, widen that gate in lockstep or the shapes become permanent silent under-routing. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/thir_with_tail/with_reuse.py` (`value_reuse`), `/tmp/agents/thir_with_tail/with_opt.py` (`opt_value_slot`). Surfaced probing the THIR already-declared with-target cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Mutating a `String` param (e.g. `name += "!"` in an `__init__` body) emits the mutation against the untouched `const std::string&` param -- ill-formed C++.** Sema accepts the aug-assign and records the param in `mutated_params`, but the emitted signature stays `const std::string&` and the body writes through it (`name += "!";` / `name = ...;` on the const ref), so g++ rejects every such body. Same missing owned-copy/mutable-binding family as the str/bytes aug-assign prologue entry below, on the `String` (owned, const-ref-param) face. Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: copy to a local first (`s = name + "!"`). Fix shape: a mutated `String` param must either lower `std::string&` (and callers adapt per `ctor_mutated`) or take the reassigned-param copy prologue; pick whichever matches the existing `param_needs_copy_for_reassign` model. NB the THIR ctor-arg check rejects mutated-String ctor slots (`_record_ctor_arg_supported`), so no THIR mirror exists to update. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/thir_with_tail/ctor_str5.py`. Surfaced probing the THIR str-arg ctor-slot cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A SIMPLE (peephole) generator with a reassigned copy-needing param emits the owned-copy prologue inside the lambda against a factory signature that never applies the `__param_` rename -- ill-formed C++ (undeclared identifier + param shadowing).** `def gen(s: str, n: int) -> Iterator[int]:` with `s = s + "x"` in the body (any owned str/bytes/String/BigInt param reassigned in a single-yield peephole generator): `_setup_body_scope`'s gen_body call emits `std::string s = std::string(__param_s);`, but the peephole signature emit (`gen_simple_generator_inline` -> `_gen_params`) passes no `reassigned_params` -- the generator-factory face of the ctor entry below. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Workaround: bind to a differently-named local. Fix shape: thread `reassigned_params` into the peephole signature emit like gen_function's (one family fix with the ctor face below). NB THIR's simple-generator gate rejects the shape (`sig.param_reassign_copy`), so no THIR mirror exists to update. Confirmed identical on master 97346c178f (pre-existing; not introduced by the leaf-seam routing). Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness probe) of thir-generators. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A ctor param needing the reassigned-copy prologue emits `T name = __param_name;` against a ctor signature that never applies the `__param_` rename -- ill-formed C++ (undeclared identifier + param shadowing).** `def __init__(self, s: str, v: Int32): self.tag = s; if v > 0: s = "pos"` (any owned str/bytes/String/BigInt param reassigned in a ctor body): gen_body emits the owned-copy prologue local `std::string s = std::string(__param_s);`, but the CTOR signature path (records.py `gen_params`) passes no `reassigned_params` -- unlike gen_function -- so the param keeps its source name and `__param_s` is undeclared; g++ rejects. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Workaround: bind to a differently-named local instead of reassigning the param. Fix shape: thread `reassigned_params` into the ctor signature emit exactly like gen_function's (same missing owned-copy/mutable-binding family as the mutated-String entry above). NB the THIR ctor gate rejects the shape (`ctor.param_reassign_copy`), so no THIR mirror exists to update. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/ctor_wave/params/probe_reassign.py`, `/tmp/agents/ctor_wave/body/probe_fw6.py`. Found independently by three THIR ctor-frontier cells. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (perf, not correctness) An `Own[T]` param forwarded to a base initializer COPIES instead of moving.** `super().__init__(q)` with `q: Own[Payload]` renders the bare `Base(q, ...)` -- `_extract_base_inits` renders target-less `gen_expr` with no `_maybe_move` arm, so the owned value is copied into the base's Own slot rather than moved. Not an aliasing divergence (the Own param is the only handle); a missed move only. THIR mirrors it byte-identically (`test_base_init_own_param_arg_copies_mirrors_ast` pins it); fixing the AST arm must widen the THIR base-init lowering in lockstep. Surfaced by the THIR ctor-frontier body cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A ctor param mutated only through a FREE-function call renders the call-site rvalue arg inline against the `A&` slot -- ill-formed C++ (prvalue bound to a non-const lvalue ref).** `class Sink: def __init__(self, a: A): mut(a); self.c = a.x` with `def mut(a: A): a.x += 1`, called `Sink(A(1))`: Phase-2 mutation propagation updates the REAL `__init__` FunctionInfo (the emitted signature is `Sink(A& a)`), but the call site's `ctor_mutated` reads the SYNTHETIC constructor fi (`expr.resolved_function_info.mutated_params`), which the fixpoint never refreshes -- so `_gen_record_ctor_args`'s rvalue-temp arm sees the slot as unmutated and emits `Sink(A(1))` inline instead of hoisting `A __tmp = A(1);`. Direct method mutation in the ctor body (`a.bump()`) lands on the synthetic fi and works. Toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: bind the arg to a local first (`a = A(1); Sink(a)`), or mutate via a method call in `__init__`. Fix shape: refresh the synthetic ctor fi's `mutated_params` in the Phase-2 fixpoint (or read the real init fi at the call sites -- expressions.py `_gen_record_ctor_args` callers + statements.py `_gen_raise_ctor_args` already reads the real one). NB the THIR ctor-arg row reads the same synthetic fi and mirrors the inline render byte-identically (`test_ctor_freefn_propagated_mutation_mirrors_ast` pins it); fixing the fact must update the THIR gate/lowering in lockstep. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/ctor_probe2/main.py`. Surfaced probing the THIR ctor-frontier foundation. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Reassigning a pointer-repr tuple PARAM emits the assignment against the `const std::tuple<..., const T*>&` parameter -- ill-formed C++.** `def use(t: tuple[Rec, Int32]) -> ...: t = (a, 5)` -- the signature renders the const borrow-tuple reference while the body emits the bare reseat (`t = ::tpy::tuple_to_storage<...>(...);`) against it; no viable `operator=`, g++ rejects. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Workaround: bind to a fresh local instead of reassigning the param. Fix shape: a reassigned borrow-tuple param needs the mutable-binding / copy-prologue family treatment (same missing-owned-copy class as the mutated-String and aug-assign param entries). NB THIR's borrow-tuple reseat dispatch EXCLUDES params (whole-body fallback, `_borrow_tuple_local_type` gate in `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py`), so no THIR mirror exists to update -- when the AST arm is fixed, widen that gate in lockstep. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/meta_w1/main.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness + meta) of the wave-5 borrow-tuple cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Mixed owned/view `bytes` reassigns and bare-operator compares emit invalid C++ (`vector` vs `span` share no `operator=`/`==`/`!=`).** Three shapes, one root cause -- codegen emits the bare operator/assign without the family's view->owned bridge when the two operands' C++ forms differ: (1) reassigning an owned `bytes` LOCAL from a view source (`t = mk(); ...; t = b` with `b: bytes` param) emits `t = b;` -> `vector<uint8_t> = span<const uint8_t>`, no `operator=` (the FIELD write has an explicit `bytes_copy` arm in `statements.py`; the local reassign was never covered -- str is immune via `string::operator=(string_view)`; the `Optional[bytes]`-local face is the same -- assigning a bytes-view source into an `std::optional<std::vector<uint8_t>>` slot, `acc = c;`, emits `optional<vector> = span`, hit by the `opt_str_local_narrow` THIR case's original bytes body and sidestepped there with an owned literal); (2) a derived `!=` between an owned bytes local and a view (`t != v`) emits the bare `t != v` -> `vector != span`, no operator (the resolved `==` dunder `::tpy::bytes_eq` takes spans and vector converts, so `t == v` works -- only the rb-less derived operators break); (3) `BytesView.__eq__` against an owned operand (`v == b"aa"`; a bytes literal renders owned at compare position) emits bare `span == vector`. All toolchain-caught (hard C++ error), not silent. Workaround: keep compared/assigned bytes values in one form (compare with `==` from the owned side, or copy to owned first). Fix shape: route the local reassign through the same `_wrap_view_to_storage` arm the field write uses, and bridge (or `bytes_eq`+negate) the mixed-form bare-operator compares. NB the THIR S6 cell mirrors these emits byte-identically (routing them is byte-safe); when this is fixed the byte-diff will flag the THIR arms to update in lockstep. Reproducer shapes in the S6 probe set (`owned_reassign`/`compares` in `/tmp/agents/probe_s6_2.py`). Surfaced by the THIR S6 bytes-cell probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Aug-assign on a `str` param skips the owned-copy prologue and emits `+=` on the untouched `std::string_view` param -> hard C++ error; the `bytes` face of the same root is a SILENT dangling span.** `def f(a: str) -> str: a += "x"; return a` emits `a += "x";` against the `std::string_view a` parameter (no `operator+=`) -- g++ rejects it. Root: the reassigned-param owned-copy prologue (`param_needs_copy_for_reassign` -> `std::string __param_a` rename + mutable local) keys on `scan.reassigned`, but the prescan records aug-assign targets in `scan.aug_assigned` only, so the prologue never fires for a param whose ONLY mutation is `+=`. A param that is also plainly reassigned (`a = a + "x"`) gets the prologue and compiles. The str face is toolchain-caught (won't compile), but the BYTES face is silent UB: `def f(a: bytes) -> bytes: a += b; return a` emits `a = ::tpy::bytes_concat(a, b);` on the untouched `std::span<const uint8_t>` param -- the span's range constructor accepts the rvalue `std::vector<uint8_t>` (const element type), so it COMPILES and rebinds the span to the concat's dying temporary; every later read of `a` is use-after-free with no diagnostic. Pre-existing on `master`; reproduces without `--thir-codegen` (the THIR S3/incr-46 cells gate both faces out rather than reproducing them). Workaround: `a = a + "x"`, or copy to a local first. Fix shape: include `aug_assigned` names in the `param_needs_copy_for_reassign` prologue/signature checks (functions.py, statements.py, param_const.py read `scan.reassigned` for it) -- one fix covers both faces. Surfaced by the THIR S3 str-concat cell's param-exclusion probe; the bytes face by the incr-46 bytes-tail cell's aug-assign probe (`aug` in `/tmp/agents/probe_bytes_concat.py`). The `int` (BigInt) face is confirmed too: `def f(n: int) -> int: while n > 0: n -= 1` emits `n = (n) - ...;` against the `const ::tpy::BigInt&` param -- toolchain-caught like the str face (probe `/tmp/agents/while-temp/probe_param.py`, surfaced by the while-condition temp fix's probes). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `float(huge_int)` and BigInt-to-double conversions silently produce `inf` where CPython raises `OverflowError`.** `BigInt::to_double()` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/bigint.hpp`) returns `HUGE_VAL` via `strtod` for values beyond double range (e.g. `float(10**400)`), and every implicit int-to-float widening site (mixed `int`-with-`float` arithmetic, float ctor) inherits that, whereas CPython raises `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float`. Silent value divergence, but only for astronomically large ints. (int/int true division no longer goes through this path -- its dedicated `truediv` overloads raise `OverflowError` correctly.) Fix shape: `to_double()` raises `OverflowError` when the magnitude exceeds double range; audit callers that intentionally want saturation, if any. Surfaced while fixing the int/int correctly-rounded-division bug. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An oversized `-> int` (BigInt) `__len__` panics UNCATCHABLY where CPython raises a catchable `OverflowError`.** `class Huge: def __len__(self) -> int: return 10 ** 30` then `len(Huge())` (or truthiness `if Huge():`) -- the BigInt `__len__` template in `runtime/cpp/include/tpy/dunder.hpp` narrows via `x.__len__().to_fixed_check<int32_t>()`, and `to_fixed_check` routes out-of-int32 magnitudes through `raise_fixedint_overflow` -> `tpy_panic` (`core.hpp:356`), an UNCATCHABLE panic ("Int32 overflow: value out of range"), whereas CPython raises a catchable `OverflowError: cannot fit 'int' into an index-sized integer`. So `try: len(Huge()) except OverflowError:` never catches in TPy. Only for astronomically large `__len__` results (>= 2^31). The negative-`__len__` -> `ValueError` guard directly above the narrow is fine (fits int32, catchable); only the oversized branch diverges. Sibling of the `float(huge_int)` -> `inf` OverflowError entry above (same "should be catchable OverflowError, isn't" family). Fix shape: the `len()`/`__len__` oversized branch should raise a catchable `::tpy::raise<OverflowError>(...)` (CPython's index-size message) instead of `to_fixed_check`'s panic -- either a dedicated checked narrow in the `__len__` template or a `to_index_checked`-style extractor. Surfaced by `/tpy-next` while fixing the negative-`__len__` `ValueError` gap. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An operator inside a generic function does not dispatch through the type param's protocol bound.** `def combine[T: Addable](a: T, b: T) -> T: return a + b` errors `Invalid operand types for '+': Ref[T] and Ref[T]` even though the `Addable` bound requires `__add__`. The protocol-operand arm in `sema/expressions.py` (`is_protocol_type` -> `lookup_protocol_method_return`) handles protocol-TYPED operands but not `Ref[TypeParamRef]` operands whose bound is a protocol. Rejects-valid. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/proto_dunder/repro.py`. Surfaced while fixing the self-referential-protocol recursion crash. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Operator dunders on a `@dynamic` protocol value do not dispatch.** `c // 2` where `c: Counter` (`@dynamic` protocol requiring `__floordiv__(self, other: int) -> int`) errors `Invalid operand types for '//': Counter and IntLiteral(2)`; same for other operators. The @dynamic Adapter forwards named methods but the binop resolution path never consults the protocol's dunders for a @dynamic-typed operand (the structural monomorphized path does). Rejects-valid; the `@dynamic`-protocol-with-operator-dunder surface is effectively unimplemented -- routing it likely needs `/tpy-add-feature` rather than a spot fix. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/proto_dunder/repro5.py`. Needs triage.
- **[LOW small] A protocol method returning `Own[Self]` does not match a conformer returning plain `Self`-equivalent.** Protocol `__add__(self, other: Self) -> Own[Self]` vs `Meters.__add__(other: Meters) -> Meters` (a `ValueType`) -> "does not conform": `_protocol_type_matches` unwraps `Own` on the ACTUAL side only, so expected `Own[Meters]` never matches actual `Meters`, though for a value type the two describe the same by-value return. Rejects-valid. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/proto_dunder/repro4.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A union-typed operand on a binary operator is accepted by sema but cannot be built in C++.** `a + u` where `u: A | B` and `A` defines a monomorphic `__add__(self, other: A) -> ...` compiles in sema (the union operand is not checked against the operator's param type) but fails the C++ build: `operator+(A, variant<A, B>)` matches no overload. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/dt_probe/mono_union.py` (also reachable via an `@overload` operator's union impl-signature, though that path's spurious operator was removed in the datetime v0 fix). Consequence: a genuine union operand on any operator is a sema-accepts / codegen-fails hole. Pre-existing, independent of `@overload`. The COMPARISON form reproduces the same class on value unions: `a == k` where `a: Int32 | Float64` and `k: Int32` emits the bare `(a == k)` -> `variant<int32_t, double> == int32_t` matches no `operator==` (same-type union compares work -- `std::variant` compares to itself). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probe_union_value3.py` (the `c()` shape). Fix shape: either sema rejects a union operand on an operator (require the user to narrow first -- consistent with TPy's narrow-before-use rule), or codegen `std::visit`-dispatches the operand to the matching concrete operator. The narrow-first rejection is the cleaner static-language fit. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`. Surfaced during datetime v0 (`docs/DATETIME_DESIGN.md`); the comparison form by the THIR F4 U1 probes. datetime v4 raised this class's exposure: `dt.tzinfo == tz` is now a natural user spelling (`.tzinfo` returns `timezone | ZoneInfo | None`) and hits the `==` form; TPy code narrows via isinstance first (see timezone.fromutc).
- **[MED small] A storage-form union value (a `variant<A, B>` field) at a borrow position emits without the `to_ptr_variant` bridge -- five sibling shapes, all hard C++ errors.** One root cause: the union storage->borrow conversion fires at the local-decl site but is missing at the other borrow sinks. (1) **return**: `def r(h: H) -> A | B: return h.u` emits `return &(h.u);` -- the plain-record `&(...)` pointer lift applied to a union, `variant<A, B>*` vs the `std::variant<A*, B*>` return (should be `::tpy::to_ptr_variant(h.u)`). (2) **call arg**: `take(h.u)` passes the bare storage field into a `const std::variant<A*, B*>` slot -- `_gen_union_arg`'s `already_union` arm returns None and the default `gen_call_arg` never lifts (`needs_to_ptr_variant_lift` is Own-param-name-keyed). (3) **const mismatch**: a const-lifted local (`std::variant<const A*, const B*> w = ::tpy::to_const_ptr_variant(h.u)` off a readonly receiver) passed to a mutable-pointee slot renders the bare name -- `variant<const A*, ...>` and `variant<A*, ...>` are distinct types (and bridging would be a const-safety hole; sema should reject or infer the param const). (4) **const-blind member lift**: `_gen_union_arg`'s member-into-ptr-variant arm ignores the SOURCE's const verdict -- a `const A&` param, a const loop var, or a readonly method's `self` passed as a member arg into a mutable `A | B` slot emits `std::variant<A*, B*>{&(a)}` where `&(a)` is `const A*` (no conversion); the narrowed-subject face (a bare `const`-extracted alias into a mutable variant slot) hits the same wall. Same sema-side const story as (3). All toolchain-caught, not silent. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/probe_union_ptr2.py` / `probe_union_ptr3.py`. Fix shape: route the return + arg borrow sinks through the same `convert()` union arm the local decl uses, and decide the const story at sema (narrow-first or const inference through union args). NB the THIR F4 U2 gates reject shapes (1) and (2); (3) renders the bare name byte-identically on both paths; (4) is mirrored byte-identically by the incr-57 member lift (THIRUnionArgLift) per the F4 precedent -- when the const story is fixed, the byte-diff flags the THIR arm to update in lockstep. Surfaced by the THIR F4 U2 probes; (4) by the incr-57 call-arg probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`. (5) **`@error_return` unwrap into a ptr-variant local**: `x: A | B = A(1)` then `x = f(...)` where `f` is `@error_return` returning `Own[A | B]` emits `x = ::tpy::unwrap_ref_move(*__try_tmp_N);` -- `variant<A, B>&&` assigned into the `variant<A*, B*>` local, the same missing bridge at the `@error_return`-bind sink (`_error_return_assign_to_name`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`). Toolchain-caught. Also the ONLY shape that reaches that function's `name in ctx.pointer_locals` guard, so it blocks a regression test for the guard until fixed.
- **[LOW small] `self.u = v` in a ctor with `v: Own[A | B]` warns "copies A | B into field; use copy()" although the emitted member-init-list MOVES (`u(std::move(v))`) -- a false-positive copy warning.** The Own-param move is the intended zero-copy path, but the field-assign copy warning does not recognize the ctor MIL move for union-typed fields (the plain-record Own-param MIL assign does not warn). Several committed union cases carry the spurious line in their `diag.txt`. No behavioral impact; diagnostics quality only. Fix shape: suppress the copy warning when the source is an `Own[...]` param at its last use feeding the MIL move (mirror the record-field arm's verdict). Surfaced by the THIR incr-57 corpus case (`callarg_own_ctor`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Char-slot literal arguments are sema-accepted for free functions but sema-rejected for methods and constructors.** `take('a')` into `def take(c: Char)` compiles (the target-typed char-literal render), but the same literal into a method (`p.set('q')`) or constructor (`P('a')`) Char param is a sema type error -- the literal-into-Char coercion only fires on the free-function call path. Inconsistent sema surface; reject-valid (loud), no miscompile. Workaround: bind the literal to a `Char` local first. Surfaced by the THIR incr-47 Char-field probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An assign-narrowed value-union local read at a member-typed sink renders the bare variant name (no `std::get`) -> hard C++ error.** `def m(k: Int32): x: Int32 | Float64 = k; v = x` -- the member-source decl narrows `x` to `Int32`, so `v` infers `Int32`, but codegen renders the read as the bare name: `int32_t v = x;` where `x` is `std::variant<int32_t, double>` -- no conversion exists. The extraction the narrowed read needs (`std::get<int32_t>(x)`) is never emitted at the decl-init sink (the isinstance-narrowing extraction sites in `expressions.py`/`match.py` don't cover assignment-narrowed bare-name reads). The value-union sibling of the narrowed-union match-capture entry below (the pointer-variant half); toolchain-caught, not silent. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/probe_union_narrowlocal.py`. Workaround: re-annotate the local (`v: Int32 | Float64 = x`) or avoid the member-typed re-read. Fix shape: an assign-narrowed union name read at a member-typed sink must extract (`std::get<M>`) like the isinstance-narrowed sites do -- or sema should not narrow across a variant storage boundary it cannot render. NB the THIR F4 U1 gate rejects any narrowing-divergent union read (`_union_binding_divergent`), so THIR neither routes nor mirrors the shape. Surfaced by the THIR F4 U1 probes. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `Send[Own[T]] | None` is not recognized as an owned (movable) param.** The `Send`/`Sync` marker distributes *inside* the Optional at construction (`Optional[Send[Own[T]]]`, not `Send[Optional[Own[T]]]`), so the owned-param peel `unwrap_optional_own(unwrap_readonly(unwrap_send_sync(ptype)))` sees `Send[Own[T]]` where it expects a bare `OwnType` and returns None -> the param is bound as a borrow instead of moved (a silent copy for a copyable T; a hard C++ error for `@nocopy`). Pre-existing (identical before the marker-peel fix; that fix covers bare `Send[Own[T]]` but not the Optional-wrapped shape). `tpy.thread.spawn`'s param is never Optional, so V1 is unaffected. Fix shape: either peel the marker per-Optional-member, or float the marker outside the Optional so the existing peel reaches it. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `Send[Own[ConcreteClass]]` param annotation fails at parse time with "not Send".** `def f(x: Send[Own[Box]])` where `Box` is a user class (even a Send-able one) raises `Parse error: 'Send[Own[Box]]': 'Own[Box]' is not Send`. Only the type-param form `Send[Own[T]]` (deferred to Phase-2) and the `@dynamic`-protocol form `Send[Own[Pet]]` are accepted; a concrete class is Send-checked too early (at parse, before the class's Send-ness is known). Workaround: use a bare `Own[Box]` param, or a generic `Send[Own[T]]`. Rejection-of-valid-code. Surfaced building the `tpy.thread.spawn` regression tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A tuple-typed parameter reassignment is not rejected by sema (unlike a record-typed param), so it emits an assignment against a `const std::tuple<...>&` param -> hard C++ error.** `def f(t: tuple[Int32, Int32], t2: tuple[Int32, Int32]): t = t2` emits `t = t2;` where `t` is a `const std::tuple<...>&` parameter, which g++ rejects (assignment to a read-only reference). A record-typed param reassignment is correctly rejected at sema ("Cannot reassign parameter 'x' of type 'Inner'"), but the tuple-typed param (and, presumably, other by-const-ref value aggregates) misses that diagnostic. Toolchain-caught (won't compile), not silent. Pre-existing on `master` -- reproduces without `--thir-codegen`; a pure AST/sema gap, not a THIR issue (the THIR value-scalar tuple subscript-read cell emits byte-identical broken text to the AST path). Workaround: assign to a fresh local instead of the param. Fix shape: extend the "cannot reassign parameter" sema check to tuple-typed (and other by-const-ref) value params, not just record params. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the THIR tuple subscript-read cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A bare `str` (view) argument doesn't auto-coerce into a `bool | str` (or other view-member union) parameter -- needs an explicit `String(...)`.** Calling `f(s)` where `f(verify: bool | str)` and `s` is a `str`/`StrView` fails the C++ build: `invalid initialization of reference of type 'const std::variant<bool, std::string>&' from expression of type 'const std::string_view'`. The view isn't wrapped to the union's owned `std::string` member at the call site. Workaround: `f(String(s))` (owned) compiles. Works once the value is already a `bool | str`-typed local (e.g. a reassignable `verify` local in curl.py), so the gap is specifically a fresh view -> union-param coercion. Same view->owned family as other union-member coercion gaps. Reproducer: `_ssl_context_for(CA_PATH)` in `tplib/requests_verify_context` (worked around with `String(CA_PATH)`). Toolchain-caught, not silent. Surfaced building the Increment 5 verify-context test. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] The clang-repl JIT backend (`--cxx clang-repl`, opt-in) cannot use modules needing runtime impls or managed third-party libs.** The default CompileBackend now compiles and links the session's build deps (runtime impl sources like os_impl.cpp, bundled third-party sources like PCRE2/Hinnant date, raw link flags) per eval, but the JIT backend has no link step to feed them into -- `import os` / `import re` / `from datetime import datetime` fail with unresolved symbols at JIT time. It accepts and ignores the `ReplBuildDeps` param (documented at the `execute` override). Fix shape: compile the support set to a shared object and load it into the interpreter (clang-repl `%lib`), or emit a clean "unsupported in the JIT backend, use the default backend" diagnostic when deps are non-empty. Split from the fixed CompileBackend link gap by `/tpy-fix-bug` (fix-repl-managed-libs). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] `--mbedtls=bundled` via the CMake-emit path (`tpyc -o`) fails to link `mbedtls_shim.c` and `cacert_data.c`.** The vendored `runtime/cpp/third_party/mbedtls/CMakeLists.txt` shim (generated by `scripts/vendor_mbedtls.py`) globs only `library/*.c`, so the TPy-owned glue `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/mbedtls_shim.c` and the CA-bundle blob `runtime/cpp/third_party/cacert/cacert_data.c` are never compiled in CMake mode -> undefined references to `tpy_tls_*` / `tpy_cacert_pem` at link. The direct-compile path (default; what `tpy file.py`, `tpyc -b`, and the test suite use) compiles both via `tpyc/build/mbedtls.py::_source_files` and works. Pre-existing for `mbedtls_shim.c` (since ssl Increment 2); the CA-bundle change adds a second file into the same untested CMake blind spot. Untested (no case exercises `tpyc -o` CMake emit with a managed lib). `--date=bundled` (datetime v2) has the same gap for `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/date_shim.cpp` -> undefined reference to `tpy::stdlib::datetime::local_utc_offset_seconds` (the vendored `tz.cpp` itself DOES build in CMake mode via the upstream `CMakeLists.txt` + `BUILD_TZ_LIB=ON` -- only the TPy-owned shim is missed). Fix shape: the CMake-emit wiring must compile the shim + bundled extra sources too -- ideally reuse the same source list `_source_files` returns so the two build paths can't drift. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the bundled CA store; date face added by the datetime v2 review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] The else/elif arm of an `isinstance(x, str)` check on a `bool | str` union is not narrowed to `bool` -- `elif not x:` passes sema but emits `!(variant)` (hard C++ error).** `def f(verify: bool | str): if isinstance(verify, str): ... elif not verify: ...` -- sema accepts the elif arm's truthiness read, but codegen emits `(!(verify))` against `const std::variant<bool, std::string>&` -> g++ "no match for operator!". The RETURN-shaped sibling narrows correctly: `if isinstance(verify, str): return ...` followed by `if not verify:` compiles (the after-return continuation is narrowed; `_ssl_context_for` in tplib/requests relies on it), so the gap is specifically the elif/else-branch narrowing of an isinstance on a 2-member union. Two acceptable fix shapes: narrow the else/elif arm to the non-matched member (bool), or emit union truthiness (CPython accepts `not <union>`) -- either unblocks the shape. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: restructure to isinstance + early return (what `_pool_key` in tplib/requests does). Surfaced building the Session connection-pool key. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) Duplicate `Content-Length` response headers make `http.client` fall back to connection-close framing instead of using the length.** Two `Content-Length: 5` headers -> `HTTPResponse.getheader("content-length")` joins them `"5, 5"` (the documented duplicate-join), which `_digits_to_int` can't parse (-1) -> body framing degrades to read-until-EOF. CPython's `email.message` keeps a usable value and frames by Content-Length, so on a keep-alive connection TPy would read past the body / block where CPython stops at N bytes. Malformed-server-only (a conformant server sends one Content-Length). Fix shape: in `_init_framing`, take the first value of a comma-joined Content-Length (or reject per RFC 7230 6.3.2), rather than feeding the joined string to `_digits_to_int`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of http.client. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A view arg passed to an owned exception-constructor param isn't view->owned-wrapped, unlike an owned arg.** `raise UnknownProtocol(ver)` where `ver` is a `str` VIEW (a `list[str]` element / `split()` result) fails the C++ build ("no known conversion from `string_view` to `const std::string&`"); the same raise with an OWNED `str` (a `decode()` result) compiles, because owned binds the `const std::string&` ctor param directly. Workaround: `raise Ctor(String(ver))` (used in `http.client._read_status`). Same root family as the `String.encode()` gap (below) and the tuple-unpacked-view-local-seeding-owned-accumulator gap -- each a different codegen sink (concat-result method, owned-local init, exception-ctor arg) where the view->owned init wrap codegen applies to owned-param slots but not that position. Fix shape: apply the view->owned wrap at exception-constructor arg positions -- ideally fix the family at one chokepoint, not as separate narrow patches (per CLAUDE.md bug-fix discipline). Surfaced building `http.client`'s `UnknownProtocol` raise. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `.encode()` is missing on `String` (the owned string-concatenation result), though present on `str`/`StrView`.** `("a" + b).encode()` fails with "Cannot call method 'encode' on type String", but `s: str = "a" + b; s.encode()` (binding the concat to a `str`-typed local first) works, as does encoding a `list[str]` element -- so str->bytes from a freshly concatenated value needs an extra binding hop. Loud reject-valid, no miscompile; trivial workaround. Affects building byte payloads by concatenation (worked around in `http.client._build_request`, which accumulates `ln.encode()` over `list[str]` elements). Fix shape: expose `encode()` on `String`'s method surface, mirroring `str`/`StrView`. Surfaced building `http.client`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `print(v)` / `str(v)` of a recursive-union (JSON-like) value holding a top-level string prints the repr form (quoted) instead of the str form.** `type V = int | str | list[V]; s: V = "hi"; print(s)` outputs `'hi'`, CPython outputs `hi`. The recursive-union wrapper struct's `__str__`/`operator<<` delegates each member through the repr path, so a top-level `str` member is quoted as if nested in a container. Uniform across all rendering paths (`operator<<`, f-string, and `print`/`str` via `::tpy::__str__`) -- pre-existing, not introduced by the union-print fix. Plain (non-recursive) unions are unaffected: `x: int | str = "hi"; print(x)` correctly outputs `hi`. Silent wrong output (only for the top-level-string member; nested strings are correctly repr'd in both). Fix shape: the recursive-union wrapper's `__str__` should dispatch members through the str path (member `__str__`), not repr -- only the top-level rendering differs (container-nested members stay repr per Python). Surfaced fixing the bare-union print/str gap. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) ASCII-only case/`is*` string methods give wrong results for Latin-1 (U+0080..U+00FF).** `upper`/`lower`/`capitalize`/`title`/`swapcase` and `isalpha`/`isupper`/`islower`/... only fold/classify ASCII: e.g. U+00E9 (small e-acute) is left unchanged by `.upper()` where CPython yields U+00C9, and `.isalpha()` on it is False vs CPython True -- silent wrong output for non-ASCII. Documented as ASCII-only in `docs/STRING_HANDLING.md`. Folded into the string-width/Unicode work: the fix is part of making `str` code-point-correct (`docs/STRING_WIDTH_DESIGN.md`), not worth a standalone patch ahead of that pass. Needs `/tpy-add-feature` (string-width design).
- **[LOW small] A jagged list literal WRAPPED in a tuple/dict in a ternary / `and` / `or` branch is rejected ("Incompatible types in ternary expression"), unlike the same wrapped literal as a list element or dict value.** `(1, [2, 3]) if c else (4, [5, 6, 7])` rejects what CPython accepts. The BARE different-size case (`[1, 2] if c else [3, 4, 5]`, `xs or [4, 5]`) already works -- a top-level pending-list branch is forced to `vector` by the `needs_list_type` path (`collect_pending_source_types`), so both branches share one C++ type. The gap is only the WRAPPED case: `collect_pending_source_types` collects top-level pending lists, not ones nested in a tuple/dict, and the ternary/logical common-type uses a separate `_ternary_common_type` / `_logical_op_result_type` path (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) that normalizes pending containers to concrete types and compares with `==` (keeping size) rather than routing through `unify_literal_types`. Loud reject-valid, no miscompile. Fix shape: route the pending-container case of both functions through `unify_literal_types(left, right, on_pending_pair=self.compat._demote_pending_pair)` so the same demotion that converges list/dict siblings also converges ternary/logical branches; mind the existing `needs_list_type` forcing so they don't conflict. Surfaced finishing the jagged-nested-list generalization. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A list of tuples of differing arity (`[(1, 2), (3, 4, 5)]`) is rejected as mixed types, though CPython accepts it.** The sibling tuple types `tuple[Int32, Int32]` and `tuple[Int32, Int32, Int32]` don't unify (different arity), so the list literal has no common element type. Distinct from the jagged-list class (this is about tuple arity, not pending-list size) -- a heterogeneous tuple list would need a variant/union element type to represent. Loud reject-valid, no miscompile. Fix shape: a feature -- infer `list[tuple[...] | tuple[...]]` (a union element) for mixed-arity tuple peers, as the error message already hints. Surfaced surveying adjacent shapes for the jagged-nested-list generalization. Needs `/tpy-add-feature`.
- **[MED small] An `isinstance`-narrowed union local read across a `yield` INSIDE a loop emits the `std::get` extraction twice in the same resume-case scope (`conflicting declaration 'auto& __t'`).** `def gen(t: A | B): if isinstance(t, A): for n in t.items: yield n` -- the narrowed `t` (a frame-resident `variant<A,B>`) is carried into the for-loop's resume state, where the narrowing alias `auto& __t = std::get<A>((*t))` is emitted both at the resume-case top (restoring the alias for resumed code) and inline before the yield, in the same `case S_JOIN_N: {...}` block -> g++ redeclaration. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/bottomup/dup_extract.py`. Exposed by (not caused by) the frame_slot-unwrap fix: before it, this shape failed earlier at `std::get`-against-`frame_slot`, so the double-emit was unreachable. Distinct from the simple narrow+yield+continue shape (no loop carrying the narrowed var across the suspension), which compiles -- so `os.walk` bottomup's union stack is unaffected. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Fix shape: the resume-restoration of an isinstance-narrowed alias must not re-declare a name already extracted inline in the same scope (dedup the per-scope alias, or skip the inline extraction when the resume case already restored it). Surfaced building the `isinstance_union_frame` regression test. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] Direct tuple-unpack of an rvalue tuple whose elements are reference types miscompiles (`to_pointer_form: cannot take the address of an rvalue`).** `a, b, c, d = stack.pop()` where the popped tuple is `tuple[str, list[str], list[str], bool]` (reference-type `list` elements) -> g++ static_assert in `format.hpp` `to_pointer_form`. Binding the rvalue to a named local first and then unpacking or indexing (`t = stack.pop(); a, b, c, d = t` / `t[0]`) works -- so it is specifically the unpack of a *temporary* (rvalue) tuple with reference elements, where an unpacked element binds as a borrow into a tuple that is not materialized to a named local. The value-tuple case (`(str, bool)`) unpacks fine. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/bottomup/b7.py` (`a,b,c,d = stack.pop()`). Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Workaround: bind the rvalue to a named local before unpacking. Surfaced exploring the stack-element representation for `os.walk` bottomup. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) `print(e)` / `str(e)` on a statically-typed USER exception subclass renders `<ClassName object at 0x...>` instead of the message.** Both `except ssl.SSLError as e: print(e)` (a stdlib `OSError` subclass) and `class NotFound(KeyError): ...; except NotFound as e: print(e)` (a user subclass) print the object-default form: codegen emits a `print_object_default` `operator<<` for every user record, including exception subclasses, and the static type selects it over the inherited `tpy::BaseException` message-printing `operator<<` / `__str__`. Catching the SAME raise via a BUILTIN base (`except OSError as e` / `except KeyError as e`) prints the message correctly (the base's friend `operator<<` applies). CPython prints the message in both cases (and `KeyError.__str__` additionally repr-quotes the arg -- `'msg'` -- part of the same `__str__`-fidelity family). Silent wrong output, cosmetic; tests should match exception TYPE, not `str(e)` text. Fix shape: skip the `print_object_default` `operator<<` for records inheriting a Throwable/exception base and route `__str__`/f-string/print dispatch to the inherited rendering (virtual, or a forwarding `__str__` when the user class defines none). Pre-existing (visible in committed ssl.hpp snapshots); surfaced re-probing exception text during the CPython-exact OSError feature and probing `ZoneInfoNotFoundError(KeyError)` for datetime v4. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A non-unpack tuple loop variable whose element tuple holds a reference type fails the C++ build in a resumable frame, with no TPy diagnostic.** `def walk(xs: list[tuple[Int32, Box]]) -> Iterator[Int32]: for t in xs: yield t[0]; yield t[1].v` -- the frame field is the borrow form `std::tuple<int32_t, Box*> t;` (via `has_pointer_repr_element`), but the loop advance assigns `t = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(*(*__for_r_0));` whose type is `std::tuple<int, Box>`, so g++ reports `no match for 'operator='`. Reproduces on BOTH for-strategies with the simplest possible shape -- no `Own`, no protocol, no readonly: `list[tuple[Int32, Box]]` (`begin_end`) and `Iterable[tuple[Int32, Box]]` (`iter_next`). Loud, rejects-valid. Distinct from the borrow-tuple *param*-element alias entry, which is a different site. Pre-existing and independent of the loop-var storage-form work: that change routes every TupleType element AWAY from the source-derived field (`elem_is_known_value` is True for TupleType), so the tuple path is byte-identical to before it. Fix shape: the advance must lift the storage-form element into the borrow-form field (`tuple_to_pointer`) exactly as the tuple-unpack sibling does, rather than assigning it raw. Repro `/tmp/agents/prereq/tupelem.py`. Surfaced by the merge-master semantic check on the loop-var storage-form branch.
- **[LOW small] (latent, believed unreachable today) A resumable frame whose loop var aliases a TEMPORARY iterable holds a pointer into its own sibling field, which the frame's move does not retarget.** When the `iter_next` source is a temporary (`for p in make_bag(): ...`), codegen stores it by value in `__for_src_N` and the loop var's borrow points *into that same struct*: `frame_slot<for_elem_next_t<Bag>> p` (a `Point*`) aliasing `frame_slot<Bag> __for_src_0`. `frame_slot<T&>`'s move copies the raw pointer verbatim, so relocating the frame after the loop var has been bound leaves the new instance pointing at the old one's storage. Introduced by the loop-var storage-form fix: before it, `iter_next` never aliased, so this self-reference could not arise. Same family as the already-filed hoisted-rvalue-temp and generator-captures-self entries. Reachability NOT demonstrated: rebinding the generator to another local is a pointer copy (no relocation), passing it to a generic consumer binds by reference, and the one path that does emit `std::move(g)` -- an `Own[T]`-taking generic record ctor -- currently fails to compile for an unrelated reason (`T` infers to the bare protocol type, producing a field of concept type, which the equivalent explicit annotation is correctly rejected for). So this is filed to be found later rather than fixed now: fixing that generic-field gap could turn this live without anyone connecting the two. Fix shape: either retarget the alias on frame move (needs the frame to know which field it points into -- awkward), or keep a temporary source out of the aliasing path by treating a self-referential `__for_src_N` element as owned. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the loop-var storage-form branch.
- **[MED small] (harness, data loss) A TRANSIENT case failure during `--update-snapshots` DELETES that case's committed `expected/output.txt` and `expected/.fingerprints`.** Observed on a full `update_snapshots.py` run (which force-execs the whole corpus in parallel): 14 cases failed to build under the resulting load, and for two unrelated async cases -- `async/async_bind_cross_module`, `async/async_with_as_borrow` -- the run removed their `output.txt` and `.fingerprints`. Both cases were fine: re-run individually they build and print byte-identical output, so nothing about them had changed. The update path treats "produced no output this run" as "this case has no expected output" rather than as a failure to leave alone, so a build race silently discards the runtime-output oracle. Dangerous because the follow-up signal is weak: on a later plain run the case compares against a missing file, which reads as an ordinary snapshot mismatch, and for a `no_cpython` case nothing else would ever catch the loss. Only the `git diff` review before committing surfaced it here. Fix shape: never delete an existing `output.txt`/`.fingerprints` for a case whose build or run FAILED during the update -- only rewrite on a clean produce, and report the skipped cases. Surfaced regenerating snapshots for the loop-var storage-form unification. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (harness) A compile error in a STDLIB module surfaces as a pytest INTERNALERROR (SemanticError escaping the xdist plugin) instead of a clean per-case failure.** Seen when lib/tpy/zoneinfo.py briefly had a return-by-reference error during datetime v4.1: `update_snapshots` recorded diag.txt cleanly (per-case comp path), but the plain `pytest -k` run crashed with `INTERNALERROR> tpyc.diagnostics.SemanticError: Cannot return local or temporary...` -- the shared stdlib .o cache builder (conftest) compiles the stdlib outside the per-case diagnostics collection and lets the exception escape. Only reachable while the stdlib itself is broken (a dev-loop annoyance, not a user bug). Fix shape: wrap the conftest stdlib-cache build in a try that converts compiler diagnostics into a pytest-level error message naming the stdlib module. Surfaced by datetime v4.1 (zoneinfo available_timezones). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A discarded constructor call with a single identifier argument emits C++'s most vexing parse -- `Ctor(name);` is a DECLARATION of `name`, and -Werror=parentheses fails the build.** `ZoneInfo(key)` as a bare expression statement (constructing only for the raise-or-not side effect, discarding the value) emits `::tpystd::datetime::ZoneInfo(key);`, which C++ parses as a parenthesized declaration of a variable `key` -> "unnecessary parentheses in declaration" under -Werror. Loud, not silent. Workaround: bind the result (`z = ZoneInfo(key)`). Fix shape: emit discarded ctor-call statements as `(void)Ctor(arg);` or `{Ctor(arg);}`-brace form. Surfaced by datetime v4's zoneinfo error tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A container PARAM or an UNANNOTATED container local in an f-string is rejected ("cannot be used in f-string"), though an annotated container local renders fine.** `def f(xs: list[Int32]) -> str: return f"{xs}"` errors `Type Ref[list[Int32]] cannot be used in f-string (no __str__ or __repr__ method)`; same for `xs = [1, 2]; f"{xs}"` (errors on `PendingList[...]`). The ANNOTATED-local form `xs: list[Int32] = [1, 2]; f"{xs}"` compiles and renders via `::tpy::list_to_str(xs)` -- so the renderability machinery exists and only the type-shape unwrap is missing: `_analyze_fstring` (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`) checks `container_to_str_template(resolved)` after peeling readonly/Own but not the `Ref[...]` param wrapper nor pending container types (`PendingList`/`PendingDict`/`PendingSet`). Loud reject-valid, no miscompile. Workaround: bind to an annotated local first (copies a param's container). Fix shape: extend the unwrap chain at that one check (Ref + resolve-pending), mirroring how print's arg path resolves the same shapes. Surfaced writing the THIR F6 S2 (f-string) eligibility tests. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) `print(e)` on a caught exception binding prints the object repr, not `str(e)`.** `try: raise E("hi") except E as e: print(e)` prints `<E object at 0x...>` in TPy but `hi` in CPython (`print` calls `str`, which routes through the exception's `__str__`/`message`). `str(e)` directly DOES give `hi` -- so the message is stored and `__str__` works; it's `print()` of an exception-typed value that fails to dispatch through `__str__` (it falls to the default object repr). Pre-existing, orthogonal to interop (surfaced writing the user-exc `@export` boundary tests, which dodge it by routing parity through real CPython and not printing caught bindings on the TPy side). Affects any `print(caught_exc)`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (print/`__str__` dispatch for reference/exception-typed args).
- **[LOW small] A list literal mixing a str-family LOCAL (pending-str or `String`) with a str literal is rejected as "mixed types", with a degenerate message.** `v = "vv"; ys = [v, "c"]` errors `List literal has mixed types: element 2 is str, but earlier elements are str. Use a type annotation like list[str | str]` -- the two "different" types PRINT identically because a pending-str local and a str literal are the same family but fail element unification. Same for a `String` concat element: `[p + q, "z"]` -> `list[String | str]`. A str PARAM element (`[p, "c"]` with `p: str`) unifies fine, and the ANNOTATED form (`ys: list[str] = [v, "c"]`) compiles and correctly wraps the view element (`{std::string(v), "c"}`) -- so the machinery exists; only literal-element unification misses the str-family peers (PendingStr/StrView/String vs LiteralType[str]). **No str literal is needed to trigger it:** two PENDING-str peers fail the same way, because each carries its own `var_id` and so compares unequal to its sibling -- `x, y = src` then `xs = [x, y]` (both unpack targets, `tuple[str, str]` source) gives the same degenerate `str` vs `str` message. That sub-shape is the more common one, since CLAUDE.md style prefers the unannotated local; reproducer `/tmp/agents/pendview/r8_listinf.py`. Any fix must unify pending-str peers with EACH OTHER, not only against literals. Loud reject-valid, no miscompile; trivial workaround (annotate the local). Reproducers `/tmp/agents/probe_s5_bug2.py`, `/tmp/agents/probe_s5_bug3.py`. Fix shape: unify str-family peers to owned `str` in list/set/dict-literal element unification (`tpyc/sema/expressions.py`, the "mixed types" check), mirroring how the annotated path resolves the element slot; the error message should also render distinguishable type names when it does fire. Surfaced probing the THIR F6 S5 (container-of-str) cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) A user-defined module-level `def print(...)` is silently never called -- every `print(...)` call site still emits the `std::cout` builtin.** `def print(x: Int32) -> None: return` then `print(5)` still writes `5` via the builtin stream in TPy, but CPython calls the user's `print` (which returns None, printing nothing). Codegen's `print` special-case (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:3022-3023`) intercepts `func_name == "print"` UNCONDITIONALLY, before the `is_shadowed` guard the generic imported-builtin branch below it uses -- so a same-module `def print` / `print = ...` shadow is ignored at every call site with no diagnostic. Silent wrong output (shadowing the builtin is unusual, hence LOW; internal-alpha audience). Pre-existing on `master` (reproduces without `--thir-codegen`); the THIR print cell's own `_is_builtin_print` guard handles this correctly (falls back to AST when shadowed), so THIR is stricter than AST here. Fix shape: gate the codegen `print` intercept on the same is_shadowed check the generic imported-builtin branch uses (or route `print` through normal function resolution when a same-module `print` exists). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity + safety-model, two independent confirmations) of the THIR print cell. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] The polymorphic-slicing guard over-rejects a polymorphic borrow passed to a `Callable[[Base], ...]` parameter, because `CallableType` param types skip the `RefType`-wrap ordinary params get.** `def f(cb: Callable[[OSError], None]): ...; cb(e)` where `e` is a caught (possibly-subclass) `OSError` is rejected by `_check_polymorphic_slicing` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:250`) with "cannot store 'OSError' borrow as owned 'OSError' in argument ... (slicing)". But the guard is WRONG here: it returns early for `RefType`/`ReadonlyType` targets and only fires on a *bare* polymorphic `NominalType`; ordinary reference-type params are `make_ref`-wrapped before the guard sees them (and emit `const Base&`), but `CallableType.param_types` are stored raw, so the guard sees a bare `OSError` and treats the call as an owned store. Codegen would actually emit the callable param as `OSError&` (`_callable_param_cpp`: non-value -> `T&`), which binds a polymorphic borrow with NO slicing -- proven by the regular-param analogue `def handle(e: OSError)` which compiles, accepts a caught exception, and emits `const OSError&`. CONSEQUENCE: a plain `Callable[[OSError], None]` callback (the CPython-natural spelling) is rejected; users must annotate the callback param `readonly[OSError]` (which makes the target a `ReadonlyType` -> guard returns early -> emits `const OSError&`). This is what forces `os.walk`'s `onerror` param to be `Callable[[readonly[OSError]], None]` -- a workaround for THIS bug, not a real divergence (CPython needs no annotation). When the symptom is a named-function arg (not a lambda) the fall-through error is the unhelpful "'<name>' is not a variable" rather than anything about slicing/readonly. **Fix shape:** `RefType`-wrap (or otherwise treat as by-ref) `CallableType` param types before the slicing guard checks an argument against them -- a callable param slot is by-reference, never an owned store. Once fixed, drop the `readonly` from `os.walk`'s `onerror` (and any other `Callable[[exc], ...]` slot) so plain/lambda callbacks need no annotation. Surfaced + root-caused verifying the os.walk onerror `readonly` requirement. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW] (perf) `key in self.<dict-field>` inside a `__contains__` method body emits O(n) `std::ranges::contains` instead of O(1) `.contains()`.** Precise trigger (bisected): membership whose RHS is a `self`-attribute, evaluated *inside a method named `__contains__`*, misses the `resolved_contains` fast path (`sema/expressions.py:1187`) and falls back to the linear `std::ranges::contains` emit (`codegen_cpp/expressions.py:1950`). The **identical** body in any other method (`def other(self, k): return k in self._store`), or membership on a local/param dict inside `__contains__` (`k in some_local`), resolves to `.contains()`. So it is NOT a return-vs-`if`-context split (both contexts emit `.contains()` normally) and NOT value-type-dependent -- it is specifically self-attr membership within `__contains__`. Looks like an over-broad self-recursion guard: suppressing self-RHS contains-resolution inside `__contains__` (to stop `x in self` recursing) but wrongly catching `x in self.<dict-field>`, where the target's `__contains__` is the dict's, not the enclosing class's. The exact suppression site is not a single obvious guard line -- needs an Explore pass over the membership/`resolved_contains` resolution. Correctness-equivalent (right answer, just O(n)); immaterial for small dicts (HTTP headers) but a cliff for large ones. Fix shape: scope the guard to "RHS resolves to the SAME class whose `__contains__` is being analyzed" rather than "RHS involves `self`". Surfaced by `CaseInsensitiveDict.__contains__` in `tplib.requests`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` + Explore survey.
- **[MED small] `@overload __init__` on a user-defined class is accepted then silently dropped -- only the first overload's constructor exists.** `class Point: @overload def __init__(self, x: Int32): ... @overload def __init__(self, label: str): ...` compiles without diagnostic, but codegen emits only `explicit Point(int32_t x)`; a call matching the second overload (`Point("hi")`) fails against the FIRST overload's signature, never mentioning overloads -- accepted-then-ignored, which the project's parity rules class as never acceptable. Registration accumulates the stubs, but user-record ctor resolution + emission consult a single `__init__`. Methods and free functions DO support overloads; ctors are the gap. Builtin-stub ctor overloads (the exception family) work via per-overload `@native(function=True)`/`@cpp_template`, a different path. The THIR ctor frontier (wave-6) deliberately keeps such records on the AST path (`_gen_record_ctor_args` reads first-stub `init_info` params, which may disagree with the resolved stub -- gate test `thir/test_thir_callargs.py::test_multi_overload_init_stays_ast`), so the shape is gated-legal, not rejected. Fix shape: either wire user-record ctor resolution + codegen through the same overload-group machinery methods use (emit one C++ ctor per overload body), or reject `@overload __init__` on user records with a clear diagnostic. NB a full symmetric-ctor-overloads implementation was built and then reverted -- it added an AST-only secondary-ctor emit path that fights the active THIR ctor migration (THIR would have to port it before AST ctor codegen can be deleted), and had no stdlib user once `timedelta` float ctor args were dropped -- so any redo should coordinate with the THIR ctor frontier. Consequence today: user OSError subclasses (`SocketError`, `gaierror`, the requests tree) keep a single `(errno, strerror)` `__init__`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (or `/tpy-add-feature`), coordinated with the THIR ctor migration.
- **[LOW small] A pending container LOCAL passed to an `@overload`-ed callable never matches: "No matching overload for f(PendingList[...]#N)" -- the internal type name also leaks into the message.** `ms = [5, 6, 7]; total(ms)` where `total` has `@overload` stubs taking `list[Int32]` (either arity) is rejected at overload resolution: the strict-match classifier has no pending-container arm, so a `PendingListType` arg matches no candidate (free functions and user-record ctor overload groups alike; pending-STR views DO match). Symmetric and loud, but it (a) rejects the idiomatic unannotated local the non-overloaded paths accept, and (b) prints the internal `PendingList[IntLiteral(5), 3]#0` repr in a user-facing error. Workaround: annotate the local. Also blocks witnessing the ctor-overload-group arg marks (`calls.py` `_resolve_ctor_overloads` loop, `statements.py` overloaded-raise loop) with container args. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/setbug/ovl_free_pending.py`, `ovl_pending.py`. Surfaced covering the pending-arg-marks fix's overload paths in /tpy-review. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `bytesview_to_bytes` coercion is ARG-context-blind (always copies), unlike `strview_to_str` (ARG-identity) -- an asymmetry that forces per-family special-casing at owned sinks.** At an ordinary ARG position a `bytes` param lowers to `std::span<const uint8_t>`, so a `BytesView` arg should be identity there (as a `StrView`->`str` arg is via `strview_to_str`'s `c == CoercionContext.ARG and not OwnType -> e` branch); but `bytesview_to_bytes` (`tpyc/coercions.py`) emits `::tpy::bytes_copy(e)` regardless of context, so a bytes view arg arrives already-owned. Consequence: the varargs-element view->owned sink (`_gen_vararg_pack`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py`) must special-case `STR_FAMILY` only (a bytes arg is already owned -- wrapping it would double-copy `bytes_copy(bytes_copy(bv))`). Fix shape: add the ARG-identity branch to `bytesview_to_bytes` matching `strview_to_str`; then the varargs sink can route BOTH families through `_view_source_to_owned` uniformly with a single copy and drop the `STR_FAMILY` guard (so this fix and that guard are coupled -- update both together). A cleaner long-term home for the "does this coercion already own?" question is a `produces_owned_copy` field on `Coercion`. Verify no bytes-param call site relied on the eager copy (a `bytes` param is a borrow/span, so identity should be sound) before flipping. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness, architecture-fit) of the varargs view->owned fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] The simple-generator peephole runs post-yield code BEFORE delivering the yielded value (observable ordering divergence from CPython).** A simple generator (single yield in a tail for-over-iterable / while loop) with a statement AFTER the yield -- `for x in xs: yield x; print("resume", x)` -- consumed in a for-loop prints `resume` BEFORE the consumer sees the value: TPy emits `resume:1 / got:1`, CPython `got:1 / resume:1`. The `make_generator` lambda computes the yield value, runs the post-yield body, then returns -- so the post-yield code executes within the same `__next__` call that should have already suspended. General (reproduces with `Int32`, `/tmp/agents/walk/p_yieldcross_int.py`); the resumable path orders correctly (`/tmp/agents/walk/p_yc_resumable.py`), so forcing such generators off the peephole (a post-yield non-control statement disqualifier, like the existing post-yield break/continue one) is the likely fix shape. Pre-existing; surfaced building `os.walk`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`. **DEFERRED to post-IR** -- the reroute is the right fix but is IR-entangled; see the sibling entry "the simple-generator peephole runs the body prologue eagerly..." for the full analysis (the disqualifier can't be made sound + non-regressing pre-IR; it rides the THIR/MIR migration).
- **[MED] A str/bytes view tuple-unpack target first-declared in a FOR-loop body from an owned-temp source, read after the loop, dangles (UAF).** `for i in range(n): a, b = split2(s)` then `print(b)` hoists `b` to the outer scope as `std::string_view` aliasing the per-iteration `auto __tup = split2(...)` temp, which dies each iteration -- the post-loop read is use-after-free (no TPy diagnostic; reads freed memory). Pre-existing (compiles+dangles identically at the merge-base; the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix closed the if/else + match branch variants but not this loop-body one). The branch-predecl owned-promotion (`deduction.promote_predecl_view_targets`) is NOT applied at the for-loop hoist site (`sema/expressions.py:_promote_pending_loop_var`) because a blanket promotion there would also wrongly OWN the safe leaked-loop-var case (`for k, v in pairs: ...; use(k)`, where the iteration var aliases the live container, not a temp). Telling the two apart requires source-lifetime analysis: own the target only when its source is a per-iteration owned `__tup` temp (call/owned source, `not source_binds_by_ref`), keep the view when it aliases a container element that outlives the loop. This is the same precise-escape-analysis deferred to the IR migration (TODO.md). Reproducer: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/forbody_str_after.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model, architecture-fit) of the tuple-unpack-hoisting fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] A reference-type local hoisted across an if/else, match, or try/except branch that aliases a BRANCH-LOCAL source dangles -- scalar form is a SILENT use-after-free, tuple-unpack form is a loud C++ build error.** When a reference-type (record/list/...) local is first-assigned inside both arms of a branch and read after it, codegen hoists it to the outer scope as a `T*` and assigns `name = &(source_element)` per arm. If the source is itself branch-local (declared inside the arm), the pointee dies at arm exit and the hoisted pointer dangles. (try/except is also a site, since its branch-assigned vars hoist the same way: `try: o = make(); a = o except E: o2 = make(); a = o2; a.v = ...` hoists+dangles like the if/else face -- repro `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_try_branch_local.py`.) Two faces:
  - **Scalar (silent UAF):** `if c: o1 = Box(1); a = o1 else: o2 = Box(2); a = o2` then `a.v = 99; print(a.v)` -> `Box* a; ... a = &(o1); ... a = &(o2);` with `o1`/`o2` branch-local. After the branch the pointee is freed; the post-branch read is a use-after-free with no diagnostic (reads freed stack -- "works" by luck). Repro: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_scalar_branch_local.py`.
  - **Tuple-unpack (loud error):** `if c: a, b = t else: a, b = t` (`t: tuple[Box, Box]`) then `a.v = ...` -- the two arms emit inconsistent forms: the is_new arm aliases (`a = &(unwrap_ref(tuple_elem_ref(std::get<0>(__tup))))`), the is_new=False arm takes the rvalue rebind-slot path (`a = &*(__slot = ...)`, `__slot` a `std::optional<Box>`) -> g++ "no known conversion from Box* to optional<Box>". The codegen asymmetry makes tuple fail to compile rather than silently dangle. Repros: `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_branch_hoist.py` (durable source -- this one is actually safe to alias, see below), `/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_branch_local.py` (branch-local).
  
  **DURABLE-source version is correct and must stay:** when the source outlives the branch (a function-scope tuple/var, a param, the leaked for-loop var aliasing the live container), aliasing is right and matches CPython's shared mutation -- the scalar durable case already works (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_scalar_branch.py`), and the for-loop tuple case works (`/tmp/agents/tupuaf/ref_loopvar_after.py`). So the bug is specifically the BRANCH-LOCAL source; a blanket reject or blanket-own would break the durable case (owning a record also breaks the shared-mutation aliasing CPython has). **CPython note:** CPython survives the branch-local case because the binding keeps the object alive (refcounting); TPy's inline/stack records have no such keep-alive, so the branch-local target would need to *own* (move the element out of the dying source), while the durable target must *alias*. **Root cause:** reference-local hoist-across-branch aliases its source with no check that the source outlives the hoist scope. **Fix shape:** source-lifetime / escape analysis -- alias when the source outlives the target's hoist scope, own (move out) when it does not; this is the reference-type sibling of the str/bytes Option-B over-own item in TODO.md (same analysis, different remedy: str/bytes can conservatively own since a copy is observationally identical, reference types cannot). A conservative sema rejection of branch-local-sourced reference hoists is a possible interim but was deferred (it over-rejects the durable case and is inconsistent with the silently-compiling scalar form). Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the (former) tuple-only S1; the scalar silent-UAF face was found during that analysis. Needs the escape-analysis work (IR) for the precise fix.
- **[LOW small] Single-member union narrowing's post-fall-through extraction matches members by `==`, so a generic-alias or cross-module same-short-name member may not match.** `_narrows_to_union_member` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py`, in `_gen_if`'s post-narrowing filter) checks `any(m == narrowed for m in union.members)` using `TpyType.__eq__`. If the narrowed fact and the declared member differ in a way `==` doesn't bridge (e.g. a generic-alias member like `list[int]`, or two cross-module records sharing a short name), the extraction is skipped and the original "variant has no member" g++ build error reappears -- a loud failure, not a silent miscompile. Not reproduced; the common nominal-record union case matches fine. Fix shape: match members by the same nominal/qname identity rule codegen uses elsewhere (`same_nominal_symbol_loose`) rather than raw `==`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `fix-union-else-narrowing`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] `os.strerror` uses non-thread-safe `std::strerror`.** `tpy::stdlib::os::strerror` (`runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/os_impl.cpp`) wraps `std::strerror`, which on some POSIX platforms returns a pointer into a shared static buffer -- a data race if `os.strerror` is called concurrently from multiple threads. glibc 2.32+ (the primary Linux target) is thread-safe, and CPython's `os.strerror` carries the same property, so this is latent hardening rather than an active bug. Fix shape: use `strerror_r` with a caller-supplied buffer. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness) of the os process/system batch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A module-global narrowed value-Optional (`str | None` / `bytes | None`) iterated inside a coroutine/generator for-loop is silently miscompiled.** The resumable for-source render `_for_src_access` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py`) calls `_maybe_unwrap_narrowed_optional(expr, gen_expr(expr), is_indirect_name(expr))`; for a module global `is_indirect_name` is True, so the helper early-returns the raw access without the `(*v)` optional-deref, and the global-slot deref is not applied in the begin_end/iter_next strata either (only the `is_async` / `async for` branch handles `is_already_pointer_source`).
