Workaround: pass all args explicitly, or call via `create_task`. Surfaced filling default args on generator factories. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Default arg values are not emitted on a *resumable* generator/coro METHOD factory.** The generator-default-args fix covers free functions (simple + resumable factories) and *simple* methods (the inline `_gen_params` path), but a resumable generator/coro METHOD with a defaulted param goes through `_emit_method_params_decl` (`gen_async.py`, 4 emit sites with their own in-struct-decl-vs-out-of-line-def split), which was deliberately left out -- so `obj.gen()` omitting a defaulted arg drops the default. No TPy diagnostic; C++ build error only. **Fix shape:** thread `func.defaults` through `_emit_method_params_decl` like `_emit_params_decl`, emitting on the canonical method-factory declaration only (respecting the decl/def placement across its 4 sites). Surfaced (scoped out) by the generator-default-args fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small]** Watch-list (mostly unverified): wrapper-aware sites that decide "does this type carry a wrapper struct?" by gating on a concrete carrier class -- `isinstance(x, UnionType) and x.needs_wrapper()`, `isinstance(inner, AliasRef)`, or `.members` access -- instead of the polymorphic `needs_wrapper()` / `recursive_union_alternatives()`, so they *could* mishandle `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` (the carrier for generic recursive aliases). **Triaged by probe on `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates` (2026-05-29): the genuinely reachable bug in this family was a *different* root cause** -- the generic-recursive finalize pass (`_finalize_generic_recursive_aliases`) skipped `RecordInfo.init_params`, so a ctor param typed as a generic recursive alias was checked against a stale `AliasRef` and valid `Summary(tree)` calls were rejected with `expected T | list[Tree[T]], got Tree[Int32]`. **Fixed** by finalizing `init_params` in that pass (regression: `tests/cases/union/generic_recursive_ctor_arg`). The concrete-class-gate sites below did NOT reproduce a bug in realistic probes; keep them as a watch-list, not asserted bugs. Proper unification is the `cpp_shape` redesign in `TODO.md`. A survey on this branch found ~12 same-shape sites (more than the originals below); none reproduced.
  - **Confirmed NOT a bug:** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py:373` / `:489` -- the `isinstance(own, UnionType) and own.needs_wrapper()` specialcase exists *because* `UnionType.to_cpp_param()` defaults to pointer-variant form; `RecursiveAliasInstanceType.to_cpp_param()` already defaults to the value-wrapper form, so `Own[Tree[T]]` params correctly emit `Tree<T>&&` via the default path (verified). The two carriers deliberately differ here -- routing through `needs_wrapper()` would be wrong.
  - **Not reproduced:** `variant_access.py:50` / `statements.py:3955` (`isinstance(peeled.inner, AliasRef)`) -- `Optional[Tree[T]]` narrow + value-use compiles and runs; no indirection/extraction bug observed.
  - **Latent, not separately probed:** `compatibility.py:1561` (`_check_union_member_type_args`, container-arg coercion), `expressions.py` `_gen_array_literal_with_target` / `_gen_union_arg`, `typesys.py:1640` make_ref skip-list, and the survey's additional `.members`-access sites (`calls.py:~3935/4669`, `statements.py:4119`, `expressions.py:2476`). Reachable only via shapes the generic recursive alias rejects or doesn't construct today; revisit if a probe finds one or `cpp_shape` lands first.
  - `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:1960` `is_dangling_return` TpyMethodCall branch -- **FIXED** on `fix-recursive-alias-wrapper-gates`. Once the emit-ordering + method-`FunctionInfo`-finalize fixes made `def m() -> Tree[Int32]` compile, this became reachable (`def g() -> Optional[Tree]: return obj.m()` emitted `&(obj.m())` -- address of an rvalue); the branch now mirrors the free-function wrapper-shape check. Regression: `tests/cases/union/error_generic_recursive_method_optional_return_dangle`.
- **[MED small]** A record method (or field) that returns/embeds a **non-generic** recursive union (`type Expr = Lit | Node`, `type Expr = Int32 | list[Expr]`) **by value** fails C++ codegen with `return type 'Expr' is incomplete` -- the wrapper struct's full definition is emitted after the inline method body that returns it by value. (Secondary symptom: a list-literal return like `[v, [v]]` then mis-emits as `std::vector<Expr>{v, ...}` because codegen can't resolve the incomplete type's variant alternatives.) This is the non-generic analogue of the (fixed) generic method-return ordering bug, but **harder**: the generic wrapper is a template whose members need not be complete at definition, so it could move before all records; a non-generic wrapper's `std::variant<...>` embeds its members by value, so when a member is a user record (`Lit`/`Node`) the wrapper must come *after* those records yet *before* any record that returns/embeds it -- a dependency-aware topological ordering, not a flat "before records" move. (For a primitive-only union like `Int32 | list[Expr]` the wrapper could move early, but the general case needs the topo sort.) Free-function returns work (emitted after all type defs); only record-method-return / field-embedding by value breaks. Reproducer: a `Calc` with `def wrap(self, v: Int32) -> Expr: return [v, [v]]`. Surfaced while writing the F2 protocol-param regression test (`union_recursive_protocol_method`).
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_array_literal` (tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:3911) unconditionally strips `ReadonlyType` from the target before the wrapper-alternative match. For non-wrapper `readonly[Array[T,N]] / readonly[Span[T]] / readonly[list[T]]` targets, the unwrap exposes a different result for `needs_explicit_element_target()` (False on ReadonlyType, True on Array/Span) -- elements then flow through `_wrap_for_owned_slot` with an `elem_target` they did not have before. PLAUSIBLE: no current test surface reaches `_gen_array_literal` with a bare `ReadonlyType` target (call-site machinery and sema strip readonly upstream), so the divergence is latent. Right shape: scope the unwrap to the wrapper-detection branches (UnionType / RecursiveAliasInstanceType), not at the top of the function. Surfaced by /code-review medium of `fix-recursive-alias-consumers`.
- **[MED small]** A `tuple[readonly[T], ...]` return whose element borrows an lvalue (param / field) and is then unpacked at the call site emits ill-formed C++: the unpack declares a non-const `T&` bound to the `const T` tuple element (`error: binding reference of type 'T&' to 'const T' discards qualifiers`). General to all reference types -- records and recursive-union wrappers reproduce identically. The borrow-form tuple element renders `const T&` (correct), but the unpack local (`Tn& x = ::tpy::unwrap_ref(std::get<i>(...))`) ignores the element's const-ness. Reproducer: `def f(a: Foo) -> tuple[readonly[Foo], Int32]: return (a, 0)` then `r, n = f(g)`. Pre-existing; surfaced by /tpy-review of the recursive-union reference-return (Family C) migration. Fix shape: the tuple-unpack local for a `readonly[...]` (const) borrow element must be declared `const Tn&`.
- **[MED small]** Inside a generic function body, a member-template call on a type-dependent expression omits the required `template` disambiguator, producing ill-formed C++. A widening/narrowing conversion on the result of a generic call -- e.g. `total += count(x)` where `count[T](...) -> int` (BigInt) and `total` is `Int32` -- lowers to `count<T>(x).to_fixed_check<int32_t>()`; because `count<T>(x)` is type-dependent, C++ requires `count<T>(x).template to_fixed_check<int32_t>()` (g++ reports `-Wmissing-template-keyword` then `expected primary-expression before '>'`). Affects any generic function that calls `.to_fixed_check<...>()` / `.to_fixed<...>()` (or any member template) on a dependent receiver. Pre-existing on master (independent of generic recursive aliases -- a plain `def total_count[T](xs: list[T]) -> Int32: total = 0; for x in xs: total += count(x)` reproduces it). Workaround: keep the accumulator and the generic call's return type the same width (e.g. both `Int32`, or both `int`) so no member-template conversion is emitted. **Fix shape:** at member-template-call codegen, emit the `template` keyword when the receiver expression's type is dependent (contains a `TypeParamRef`) inside a template context. Surfaced while writing the generic-recursive-alias match test (`leaf_count[T]` accumulating an `int`-returning recursive call into an `Int32`).
- **[LOW small]** `_check_union_member_type_args` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:~1559`) resolves only `(NominalType, AliasRef)` before its `UnionType.needs_wrapper()` branch, so a `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` container-element type-arg (e.g. a `list[Tree[int]]` slot whose element is the generic recursive alias) is not recognized as a wrapper and falls through to the `return False` rejection. **Likely unreachable today** -- the generic-recursive happy-path tests that coerce `list[Tree[int]]` into the wrapper pass (the path is reached via the equality short-circuit or a `UnionType` arg, not an instance arg), so this is a latent gap, not an observed failure. Same root cause as the other "instance unrecognized at wrapper-gated sites" entries: the new `RecursiveAliasInstanceType` was not threaded through every `needs_wrapper()`/`UnionType` consumer. Fix shape: recognize the instance (via `is_recursive_union_like`) alongside the `(NominalType, AliasRef)` resolution there. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the generic-recursive-alias branch.
- **[LOW small]** Returning a freshly-constructed exception as `Own[<Exception>]` emits a confusing same-type diagnostic. `def make_err(...) -> Own[ValueError]: return ValueError("boom")` is rejected with `Type mismatch in return value: expected ValueError, got ValueError`. The `Own[...]` wrapper is mishandled in the return-value type compatibility check for native exception types (same family as the Own-wrapper-stripping return bugs already tracked below). Workaround: return the exception by a different path or raise it directly. Surfaced while authoring the `error_raise_read_after_consume` regression test (switched the test to `raise ValueError(f"... {h.fd}")` to sidestep it).
- **[LOW small]** `_check_polymorphic_slicing` false-positive on `Box(exc)` where `exc: Own[BaseException]` (an Own-typed param of the enclosing function). `def store(exc: Own[BaseException]) -> Box[BaseException]: return Box(exc)` is rejected with the slicing diagnostic even though it's a legitimate ownership transfer -- moving an owned value into a Box preserves whatever the value holds, no slicing. The check at `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:_check_polymorphic_slicing` has an `if isinstance(actual, OwnType): return` escape at the top, but it doesn't fire here because sema strips the Own when typing the bare name `exc` at the Box-ctor argument site, so the check sees a borrow-shaped `BaseException` instead of `Own[BaseException]`. Rejects valid code. Fix: consult the name's *declared* type (not the use-context type) when applying the Own-skip rule. Workaround: use `Box[Throwable]` (the recommended polymorphic-storage convention) or `tpy.copy(exc)`. Surfaced during the (dropped) covariant-return exploration while attempting to narrow `asyncio.Future.set_exception`.
- **[LOW small]** `_check_polymorphic_slicing` false-positive on taking a pointer to a polymorphic lvalue: `take_ptr(d)` (or the implicit `d -> Ptr[Dog]` address-of coercion) where `d` is a value of a polymorphic class (one that transitively implements a `@dynamic` protocol) is rejected with "cannot store 'Dog' borrow as owned 'Dog'". Taking an address produces a `Dog*` to the exact object -- no copy, no slicing -- so the rejection is spurious and the suggested `Box(...)` remediation is wrong for the pointer case. Reproducer: `@dynamic class Pet(Protocol): ...; class Parrot(Pet): ...; bird = Parrot("x"); pp: Ptr[Parrot] = take_ptr(bird)`. The guard's `target_inner not NominalType -> return` early-out doesn't fire because the address-of coercion is checked pointee-against-value (`Parrot -> Parrot`) with a NominalType expected, so the polymorphic-source branch trips. Fix: skip the slicing check when the destination is a `Ptr[...]` (pointer borrow, never an owned value slot). Pre-existing (reproduces with no involvement from bound-coercion work); blocks a `@dynamic`-conformer `Ptr` round-trip, which is why `tests/cases/generics/bound_coercion_*` exercise class bounds for the exec path. Surfaced while writing tests for `feat-bound-subtype-coercion`.
- **[LOW small]** Sema/codegen asymmetry on Own-peeling for polymorphic-subclass narrowing facts. Sema's `_polymorphic_subclass_fact` in `tpyc/sema/statements.py:1384` applies a local `_peel` that strips `OwnType` and `ReadonlyType` before calling `is_polymorphic_subclass_fact` -- so an `Own[Pet]` parameter narrowed via `isinstance(p, Dog)` produces a fact. The three codegen call sites in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` (`_build_isinstance_init_clause`, `_emit_isinstance_extractions`, the `_gen_if` post-guard filter) pass `lookup_var_type(...)` directly, and `polymorphic_source_inner` (`tpyc/typesys.py:3389`) only strips `Readonly` -- so codegen sees the OwnType and the predicate returns False. Result: sema thinks the fact is alive, codegen drops it; the std::get/std::any branches don't match either and the emit falls through to the variant-extraction path or produces a C++ compile error. **Fix shape:** bake Own-stripping into `polymorphic_source_inner` itself so it benefits all 5+ call sites uniformly (sema fact filter, codegen if-init, codegen extraction, codegen post-guard filter, expressions.py inline isinstance). Pre-existing on master; widened to a 3rd codegen call site by `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`. Filed during /tpy-review of `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`.
- **[LOW medium]** `isinstance(x, C)` on a generic type-parameter subject is currently *rejected* for any subject the compile-time `tpy::isinstance_static` trait cannot resolve soundly: an **unbounded** `T` (`def f[T](x: T)`), a `T` bounded by a **@dynamic protocol / polymorphic class**, and a `T` bounded by a **structural protocol** all raise a clean sema error (`tpyc/sema/calls.py::_analyze_isinstance_type_param`). The non-polymorphic class-bounded case works per-instantiation (see `tests/cases/generics/isinstance_typeparam_bound`). Lifting the rejections needs a **per-instantiation runtime dispatcher** -- a C++ helper that `if constexpr`-dispatches on the instantiated `decltype(x)` shape: union -> `holds_alternative` (value-variant *and* pointer-variant reps), `Any` -> `typeid`, polymorphic/@dynamic -> `dynamic_cast` / `dyn_adapter_cast`, else -> the current same-or-derived trait. This is the only sound way to support those instantiations, because a generic body is emitted as one C++ template (sema can't pick a per-instantiation lowering). The polymorphic-bounded sub-case alone could instead be lifted more cheaply by extending `polymorphic_source_inner` to consult a `TypeParamRef`'s bound and threading the type-param case through the existing `dynamic_cast` codegen path. Deferred as the documented follow-up to the bounded-`T` fix; demand is currently hypothetical (no test exercises generic-subject isinstance beyond the supported case).
- **[LOW small]** `isinstance(v, Sub)` where `v` is a VALUE LOCAL of a polymorphic base (`@native`/exception, or any class with a vtable) emits a live `dynamic_cast<Sub*>(&v)` instead of folding to `false` -- and `-Werror` (the test toolchain) rejects it (`dynamic_cast ... can never succeed` / `address of 'v' will never be NULL`). A plain non-polymorphic user-class base correctly folds the same check to `print_bool(false)` + a sema warning ("isinstance() check against descendant type folds to False"); the polymorphic path skips that fold and emits the runtime cast, which for a value local (most-derived type == the base) is provably dead. Under a non-`-Werror` build it compiles and returns the correct `false`, so this is a build-robustness/quality gap, not a wrong answer: the value-local case should fold + warn like the user-class path (a caught `except Base as e` borrow is a genuine polymorphic reference where the `dynamic_cast` is correct and must stay). Reproducer: `e = OSError(2, "x"); isinstance(e, FileNotFoundError)`. Pre-existing (isinstance codegen untouched by the oserror-exact branch); surfaced trying to pin the CPython-exact-OSError un-raised-introspection divergence as a test -- that divergence is consequently NOT harness-pinnable (its only observable is `isinstance` on a value local, which trips `-Werror`; `except` requires raising, which maps). Fix shape: extend the fold-to-false + warn path to a value-typed local of a polymorphic base (the dynamic type is statically known == the base), keeping the runtime `dynamic_cast` only for genuine borrow/reference subjects. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small]** Soundness-gate hole in the trait lowering above, now **loud-guarded** (was silent): `_analyze_isinstance_type_param` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) rejects a *bound* that is polymorphic (`is_dynamic_dispatch_inner`), but does not stop a caller from instantiating `T` with a **polymorphic subclass of a non-polymorphic bound**. `validate_record_inheritance` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`) imposes no rule that a polymorphic class's @dynamic origin must come from its parents, so `class D(B, SomeDynProto)` is legal with `B` a plain (non-poly) class; `def f[T: B](x: T)` then accepts `T=D`, and the body's `isinstance(x, E)` lowers to `isinstance_static<E, D>()` -- a compile-time fold that cannot see `D`'s runtime dynamic type, so it would return False where `dynamic_cast` / CPython return True for an `E` instance behind a `D&`. **Stopgap shipped:** `tpy::isinstance_static` (`runtime/cpp/include/tpy/dynamic.hpp`) carries a `static_assert(!std::is_polymorphic_v<XT>)` that turns this into a (cryptic, C++-level) loud compile error at the offending instantiation instead of a silent wrong answer. Not a regression (this instantiation folded silently-False before the trait existed too); the hole is in the "non-poly bound => static type is dynamic type" invariant, which holds for the bound but not for its polymorphic subclasses. **Proper fix (removes the static_assert):** re-validate substituted type args at the generic call site (reject with a clean tpyc-level diagnostic, or route to `dynamic_cast`, when the concrete `T` is polymorphic) -- the same "re-validate substituted types at call sites" pass other entries need; per-module Phase-1 visibility means a cross-module caller's instantiation isn't seen during the function's own analysis, so the call-site pass is the principled home. No harness test today (the guard fires as a C++ static_assert, not a tpyc diagnostic); add one with the clean diagnostic. Use `/tpy-fix-bug`. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (safety-model + cpython-parity) of the bounded-`T` trait fix.
- **[LOW small]** Mutual hashed-container-element cycle between two records (`class A: s: Own[set["B"]]; class B: s: Own[set["A"]]`) silently emits ill-formed C++. The Kahn-algorithm cycle-fallback in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/records.py::sort_records_by_inheritance` appends both records in source order; whichever emits second sees a complete `std::hash<>` for its element, but whichever emits first does not -- the user sees a wall of C++ template errors instead of a TPy-level diagnostic. The new hash-element edges introduced on `validate-tuple-own-opt-poly` make this failure mode newly reachable (inheritance-only edges could not produce element-side cycles). **Fix shape:** detect cycles where the dependency type is `hash-element` (as distinct from `inheritance`) and emit a TPy diagnostic ("`set`-of / `dict`-keyed cycle between records X and Y") with guidance to break the cycle via an indirection (`Own[set[Box[Y]]]` etc.). Filed during /tpy-review of `validate-tuple-own-opt-poly`.
- **[LOW small]** Property setter for an `Optional[Polymorphic]` field emits a confusing diagnostic. The user writes `@pet.setter def pet(self, value: Optional[Pet]) -> None: ...` (where `Pet` inherits a `@dynamic` protocol). Sema's `_apply_property_setter_wrapping` (`tpyc/sema/method_expansion.py:270-276`) auto-wraps the first setter param in `Own[]`, producing `Own[Optional[Pet]]`. The centralized `validate_type` rejection then fires with "Use `Optional[Pet]` or `Box[Pet]`" -- but the user already wrote `Optional[Pet]`; the auto-wrap is invisible. **Fix shape:** at setter wrap time, skip the Own wrap for `Optional[<polymorphic class>]` params (the unwrapped Optional[Pet] shape is the right setter surface for polymorphic targets). Requires threading the type registry into `_apply_property_setter_wrapping` via `expand_methods_for_record`. Alternative: emit a setter-context-aware diagnostic from validate_type's OwnType branch when the call-site indicates an auto-wrap, though that requires passing context through validate_type. Filed during /code-review of the `own-opt-poly-reject` branch.
- **[LOW small]** Free-function and free-method signatures containing `Own[Optional[Polymorphic]]` skip the centralized sema rejection when the polymorphic target class is defined LATER in the same module. `register_function` (`tpyc/sema/registration.py:2619`) calls `validate_type` once at registration time, and `is_polymorphic_class_type` returns False for unregistered records (the "forward-ref defer" path documented in the OwnType rejection). The deferral works for record fields/methods because `validate_record_field_protocols` re-runs validate_type after inheritance is finalized; no analogous second pass exists for free functions. **Fix shape:** add a per-module post-Phase-1 pass that re-validates free-function param + return types after all records' inheritance is processed (analogous to `validate_record_field_protocols`). Filed during /code-review of the `own-opt-poly-reject` branch.
- **[LOW small]** Asymmetric variable-lookup between the two arms of the post-guard fact filter in `_gen_if` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` around line 4032). The `is_recursive_union` arm uses `self.ctx.var_types.get(k)` (locals + params only) while the new polymorphic arm uses `self.ctx.lookup_var_type(k)` (locals + params + globals + self). A recursive-union `self.field` or module-global narrowed via early-return is silently excluded from `post_facts` while a polymorphic one in the same shape is included. The polymorphic arm's behavior is the correct one; the recursive-union arm should align. Pre-existing; surfaced during /tpy-review of `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`.
- **[LOW small]** Termination-check on the early-return post-guard narrowing path uses `isinstance(then_body[-1], (TpyReturn, TpyRaise))` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py` around line 4024), which is strictly shallower than `stmts_terminate()` -- it misses then-bodies whose last statement is a `TpyIf` that terminates on every path (e.g. `if cond: return X` `else: return Y` as the trailing statement). Affects both the recursive-union arm and the new polymorphic arm; the polymorphic fix on `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing` widened the surface where the gap is reachable. **Fix shape:** swap the shallow check for `stmts_terminate(then_body)` so nested-if-on-all-paths termination triggers the post-guard extraction. Filed during /tpy-review of `fix-poly-early-return-narrowing`.
- **[MED small]** `await fn(*args, ...)` for a non-value vararg element type miscompiles silently. **Now guarded:** `*args` on `async def` functions/methods is rejected at sema registration (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`) with "Variadic positional parameters (*args) are not yet supported on async functions/methods", so the silent miscompile below is no longer reachable from source. The underlying codegen gap (and the work needed to actually *support* async-def varargs) is unchanged and described next; lift the guard once it lands. `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py::_gen_coro_emplace_arg` doesn't route `TpyVarargPack` through `_gen_vararg_pack`; it falls through to `_gen_expr`'s generic fallthrough, which emits literal `/* unknown expr */` C++. No TPy diagnostic -- the C++ compiler errors out with an opaque "no matching call to optional::emplace()". Two further-blocking issues even after the dispatch is wired: (a) the async-def factory param type is emitted as `std::span<const T>` rather than `tpy::varargs<T>` (sync/async vararg-lowering divergence in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/functions.py` vs `gen_async.py`), and (b) the call-site temp `std::array<T*, N>` is a `__poll__` stack local, so the borrow inside the constructed sub-coro frame dangles across the first suspension. Safe lowering would need to hoist the temp into the *awaiter's* coro frame (parallel to M4's rvalue-receiver lift). Reproducer: `async def f(*xs: SomeRefType): await asyncio.sleep(0); for x in xs: ...; async def g(): r1 = ...; await f(r1)`. Surfaced while probing whether a CPython-shape variadic `asyncio.gather(*tasks)` was feasible (it is not for async-def varargs today; v1.5 M9 shipped `asyncio.gather(*tasks)` as a *sync* factory returning an awaitable, plus `gather_list(tasks)` for the list shape -- both homogeneous, sidestepping this bug). All three pieces still need to land before any user-facing *async-def* vararg API (e.g. the CPython-shape heterogeneous variadic-tuple `gather[*Ts]`).
- **[LOW small]** Subclass `__init__` body that writes to an inherited `@nocopy + __del__` field (`self.base_nocopy_field = expr`) emits `this->base_nocopy_field = ...` which fails C++ compile -- the type has neither copy- nor move-assignment, so the body-form write is unbuildable. No sema or codegen guard rejects this; the path was always reachable, but `fix-mil-hoist-reorder` made it slightly more reachable because any inherited-field assign now also breaks the MIL chain for subsequent own fields. Real-world impact low: users typically initialize base fields via `super().__init__(...)` rather than direct assignment in the subclass body. Fix: reject in sema (writing to a non-default-constructible inherited field outside `super().__init__()` arguments) with a guidance message pointing at the super() pattern. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the MIL hoist fix.
- **[LOW small]** Phase 20 slicing-site sema rejection (`_check_polymorphic_slicing` in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`) misses borrow shapes beyond `(TpyName, TpyFieldAccess, TpyMethodCall, TpyCall)`. Specifically `TpySubscript` (e.g., `saved = exceptions[0]; self.exc = saved` after intermediate binding, or direct `self.exc = exceptions[0]`) and `TpyIfExpr` (`self.exc = a if cond else b`) both produce a polymorphic borrow that bypasses the gate. The original BUGS.md slicing footgun resurfaces through these source shapes. Fix: extend `is_borrow_shape` at `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:267-270` to include both. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch.
- **[LOW small]** Polymorphic `match` on a non-movable element of an *rvalue* container leaks a raw C++ error instead of a clean diagnostic. `match make_animals()[i]:` where `make_animals() -> Own[list[Box[Animal]]]` (or any subscript/field of an rvalue container yielding a `@nocopy` element) is accepted by sema but fails the C++ build with "use of deleted function `Box<Animal>::Box(const Box&)`". Cause: `_match_subject_is_lvalue` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py`) follows the *container* -- an rvalue container makes the subscript a non-lvalue, so the subject binds `auto __match_subject = make_animals()[i]`, which copies the element out of the dying temporary; `@nocopy` Box/Rc have a deleted copy ctor. There is no safe binding for this shape (an `auto&` would dangle once the temporary container dies; the element can't be moved out of a borrow), so the right outcome is a clean TPy rejection ("cannot match on a non-movable element of a temporary container; bind the container to a local first"), not a leaked C++ error. Not unique to polymorphic match -- any `match rvalue_container[i]:` with a nocopy element has the same shape; the expression-subject feature (`feat-poly-match-expr-subject`) just made polymorphic subjects able to reach it (before, the non-name subject was rejected cleanly at sema). The *moveable* rvalue case works (`match make_box():` moves the whole subject). Fix: at polymorphic-match subject analysis (or the shared subject-binding path), reject a non-lvalue subject whose effective element type is non-movable (nocopy without a move out), with a bind-to-local hint. Surfaced by user question on `feat-poly-match-expr-subject`.
- **[LOW small]** Phase 20 slicing-site rejection skips when source is `Own[T]` (correct in general -- move semantics preserve dynamic type), but the existing `Own[Child] -> Own[Base]` coercion guard in `_check_compat` (around line 867) only fires when both sides are `is_user_record`. For native exception types (`Own[ValueError]` move into `Own[BaseException]`) neither guard fires and the silent slicing is reintroduced for the move path. The `Box[Throwable]` user-facing pattern means real user code is unlikely to hit this in practice, but the gap is an ownership-invariant hole. Fix: extend the type-mismatch guard to native subclass-into-base owned moves, or add a Throwable-specific carve-out at the slicing-rule layer. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Phase 20 branch.
- **[LOW small]** `else:` on `async for` is rejected at parse time (`tpyc/parse/parser.py`; reproducer `tests/cases/async/error_async_for_else`). Sync `for`/`while`-`else` with a suspension is now supported (generator-resumable migration Phase D3: the CFG models break-vs-normal-exit with distinct edges -- normal-exit runs the else, `break` targets a separate after-BB that skips it -- so no "exited-via-break" flag is needed). `async for` stays rejected because its `__anext__`-driven advance (a Yield, not an `AsyncForAdvance` terminator) doesn't yet route the normal-exit edge through an else region. Fix when needed: give `_build_async_for` the same exit-BB/after-BB split as `_build_for` so the `async for` advance's exhausted edge runs the else.
- **[LOW small]** `__finally_ret_<n>` frame slot for M3.3.2 pending-return is declared with bare `T name;` (default-init), no NSDMI. The control flow guarantees the slot is assigned before any `std::move(this->__finally_ret_<n>)` reads it (return saves to slot, then state transitions to finally entry, then AsyncFinallyExit moves), so this is safe today. But unlike the sibling `__finally_pending_<n>` bool (which gets `= false` NSDMI), there's no in-class default initializer to make the invariant explicit. A future refactor that introduces a path reading the slot before the return-site assignment would silently `std::move` from an uninitialized value. Fix: add NSDMI for the value slot too -- value-types can use `{}` (zero-init / default ctor); reference-type slots already wrap in `std::optional`.
- **[LOW medium]** A borrow that outlives its source escapes with no sema guard -- general borrow-escape analysis is unbuilt. The headline shape is `__exit__`'s `exc_val: Optional[BaseException]` (a `const BaseException*` into the catch block's exception object, destroyed when the handler returns / on suppression): storing it somewhere longer-lived dangles. **The polymorphic owned-store paths are now already guarded** by the slicing-site rejection (`_check_polymorphic_slicing` in `tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`): `self.last = exc_val` into an `Optional[BaseException]` field, and passing `exc_val` to an owned `Optional[BaseException]` param, both error with "cannot store 'BaseException' borrow as owned ... (slicing)". The *residual* unguarded surface is the non-polymorphic / borrow-into-borrow case -- storing a borrow into a longer-lived `Ptr`/borrow slot, and a fresh `Own[...]` rvalue lent to a borrow param the callee escapes (`check(Rc.new(...))` -> the temp dies at end of full-expression). That residual is **general borrow-escape / region analysis**, the same lifetime feature that gates `create_task`'s ref-param escape -- deferred to the THIR/MIR region checker (see the async borrow-escape FEATURE_ROADMAP item; do not hand-roll a partial string-keyed version). No localized fix; tracked here so the `exc_val` shape is folded into that effort rather than rediscovered. A concrete instance now in shipped stdlib: `csv.writer`'s `_Writer[W]` stores `self._fp = &fp` (a borrow of its by-ref param into a `Ptr[W]` field); if the writer outlives fp (e.g. fp is a temporary), the field dangles -- same unguarded borrow-into-field shape, the first stdlib type whose core invariant rests on it.
- **[LOW small]** Reusing the same `as`-variable name across sibling `with` blocks miscompiles when the type is a value type (e.g. `int`).
  Reproducer: `with CM() as x: ...; with CM() as x: ...` where `CM.__enter__` returns an `int`. Generated C++ rebinds via `x = &(__ctx_2.__enter__())` -- pointer assignment to a value-type local -- and the C++ build fails. The pointer-local rebinding path in `_gen_with` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:2370` area) was designed for reference types and should fall through to value-copy assignment (`x = __ctx_2.__enter__();`) when `item.enter_type.is_value_type()`. Surfaced while writing follow-up tests for v1.5 M1; not v1.5-introduced (the pointer-local path predates this work). Workaround: use distinct names.
- **[LOW small]** `__exit__` returning `bool` may suppress `CancelledError` (matches CPython behavior but is a known footgun).
  v1.5 M1's `with` codegen catches `::tpy::BaseException&` -- which includes `CancelledError`. A user-defined `__exit__` that returns `True` on any caught exception silently swallows task cancellation. This matches CPython's `__exit__` contract (cancellation IS a BaseException subclass exactly so user code can choose to suppress it), but is worth flagging because production task-runner code typically wants to NOT suppress `CancelledError`. No sema guard today. (M5's `async with` is cleanup-only -- `__aexit__` returns `None` -- so the footgun doesn't apply to async with yet; it activates when E9 / Phase 20 enables `exc_val: Optional[BaseException]` inspection + suppression for `__aexit__`.) Possible mitigation: sema warn on bare `return True` in `__exit__` / `__aexit__` bodies that don't explicitly handle `CancelledError`. Until then, document in the language tour.
- **[MED small]** Match-class-pattern `as` bind is silently read-only when the bind ends up pre-declared as `std::optional<T>`. Writes to bound-record fields hit the optional's copy, not the live variant slot.
  Reproducer (plain `.py`): a record `class C: payload: A | B` whose method `set_x(v)` writes `self.payload.x` via narrowing -- `match self.payload: case A() as a: a.x = v; case _: raise ...`. The terminating wildcard arm causes sema's match analyzer (`tpyc/sema/match.py:260-271`) to mark the bind as "definitely-assigned" and add it to `if_branch_decls`. Codegen's branch-decl pre-pass (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:_emit_branch_decls`) then emits `std::optional<A> a;` ahead of the switch; the case body does `a = std::get<0>(__match_subject); a->x = v;` -- the second `=` writes into the optional's local storage, not back into `__match_subject` (and from there `self.payload`). Without the terminating wildcard, the bind stays inline (`auto& a = std::get<0>(...)`) and writes propagate; that's why simple two-arm patterns (no wildcard) work. **Fix:** either narrow the pre-decl criterion to only fire when the bind is used after the match (not just "definitely-assigned" inside it), or change branch-decl emission so a class-pattern-derived bind uses `T*` storage and assigns `&std::get<N>(__match_subject)` inside the arm. Surfaced while building Pascal variant-record `@property` setters; worked around at the Pascal level by routing setters through "construct a fresh variant, assign payload wholesale" -- but that workaround needs the second item below to also be fixed.
  **Resumable-path variant (generator / `async`), broader:** inside a suspending `match`, H1 materializes *every* match-arm reference binding (capture / field / `as`) as an owned frame field (`tpy::frame_slot<T>` + `.emplace(std::get<N>(__match_subject))`, e.g. `c.emplace(...)` in `tests/cases/iterators/gen_match_union/expected/src/main.cpp`), unconditionally -- not gated on a terminating wildcard, because the binding must survive a suspension. So a reference-type binding is *always* a copy: mutating it does not propagate to the subject and `is`-identity differs from CPython (observable only under mutation / identity check; pure reads -- like the `c.lives` read in that test -- are fine, and immutable bindings like `str`/`int` are observationally equivalent). This is the accepted consequence of the frame-level binding design (chosen over re-materialization, which would have preserved aliasing). The principled fix is the "re-materialize bindings from a frame-stored subject" direction noted in `docs/GENERATOR_RESUMABLE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` (Phase H discussion): keep one frame field for the subject and re-bind `auto& c = std::get<N>(__match_subject)` at each in-arm case entry, restoring the live reference. Low priority -- mutating/identity-checking a match-bound record across a suspension is exotic.
- **[MED small]** Reassignable record local is stored as `std::optional<T>`, which fails C++ overload resolution when assigning the local to a `std::variant<T, U, ...>` field of which `T` is a member.
  Reproducer (plain `.py`): a record `class C: payload: A | B` whose method builds a fresh `A` in a local and assigns it to `self.payload` -- `fresh = A(); fresh.x = 5; self.payload = fresh`. The local `fresh` is emitted as `std::optional<A> fresh` (TPy's reassignable-record-local storage convention), then `this->payload = std::move(fresh)` fails: `variant<A, B>` has no `operator=` taking `std::optional<A>`. Adding an explicit type annotation (`fresh: A = A()`) doesn't change the storage shape. **Fix:** at the assignment site, if the LHS is a `UnionType` whose members include the local's bare type and the RHS is an `optional`-stored local, codegen should unwrap (`*fresh` or `std::move(*fresh)`) on the way in. Alternatively, give locals annotated with a single record type the bare `T` storage shape rather than `std::optional<T>` -- but that's a wider invariant change. Surfaced while building Pascal variant-record `@property` setters that needed to construct a fresh variant alternative and assign it to a union-typed `payload` field.
- **[LOW small]** `is_type_non_copyable` walks `type_args` for phantom type params (type args not stored in any field), producing over-conservative copy errors.
  Reproducer (pre-Phase-4): `class Tagged[T]: tag: Int32` -- T is phantom (no T-typed field). `Tagged[Rc[X]]` (Rc is `@nocopy`) reports as non-copyable even though no Rc is actually stored. Surfaced visibly after Phase 4 extended `is_type_non_copyable` to recognize abstract `@dynamic` protocols: the `dynamic_generic_field` test had to switch from copy to `Own[...]` move because `Tagged[Greeter]` is now flagged. The fix walks `type_args` only when they're reachable through fields. Pre-existing imprecision; surfaced during the Phase 4 cleanup of the Box[P].set/clone/take work.
- **[LOW small]** An *infinite-size* self-embedding record is not cleanly rejected -- it crashes the compiler or emits invalid C++ instead of a "recursive type needs Box/Rc/list" diagnostic. `class Inner: nxt: Inner` (direct inline member) crashes in codegen's unguarded `_fld_type_cpp_default_constructible` / `_all_fields_default_constructible` walk (`records.py`); `class Inner: opt: Inner | None` (inline `std::optional<Inner>` member) compiles through tpyc but g++ rejects it as an incomplete type -- `cycle_detection._is_indirecting_type` wrongly treats `OptionalType` as size-indirecting, though `std::optional<T>` stores `T` inline (only `Ptr`/heap containers actually break the size cycle). The *valid* finite recursive shapes (`list[Self]`, `Ptr[Self]`, mutual recursion through containers, `Rc[Self] | None`) now compile -- the `is_type_non_copyable` no-visited-set crash that previously blocked them too was fixed by its record-walk re-entry guard (`tests/cases/records/recursive_record_list_field`, `recursive_record_mutual`). Residual fix shape: (a) drop `OptionalType` from `_is_indirecting_type` so inline-optional self-embed is caught by the existing cycle check, and (b) add a visited-set guard to the codegen default-constructible walk so a direct self-embed reaches the clean diagnostic rather than a stack overflow. Surfaced fixing the `is_type_non_copyable` recursion (the valid-case half is now done; this is the invalid-size residual). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small]** `unsafe_init` is not abstract-T-safe -- its `@cpp_template("::new(...) {T}({1})")` placement-new fails for abstract T.
  Latent today (no current caller after Phase 2's Box.set rewrite uses `unsafe_replace` instead). Any future stdlib type using `unsafe_init` for in-place reconstruction with abstract T will hit ill-formed C++ at template instantiation. Proper fix: either (a) add an abstract-T overload of `unsafe_init` that rejects/redirects via `if constexpr (is_dyn_protocol_base_v<T>)`, or (b) sema-reject `unsafe_init[T]` calls where T is abstract @dynamic. The primitive's semantics ("placement-new at this slot") don't have a meaningful interpretation for abstract T (the slot's actual size depends on the dynamic type, not T), so option (b) is arguably more honest.
- **[LOW small]** Pre-Phase-3 `Box.take()` body had latent UB on non-trivially-destructible T: `T val = std::move(*_ptr); ::operator delete(_ptr, alignof(T));` skipped `~T()` on the moved-from object before freeing storage.
  Fixed in Phase 3 by routing through the new `tpy::transfer_ownership` helper, whose concrete branch does `T val = std::move(*p); destroy_at(p); ::operator delete(...);`. Logging the historical defect here for the audit trail; the symptom was UB on any T with a non-trivial destructor that ran through Box.take. Surfaced by the Codex review of the Box[P] plan; fix landed alongside the abstract-T case.
- **[LOW small]** `heap_replace` / `transfer_ownership` exception safety: the concrete branches assume the move ctor / move-construct doesn't throw, but don't enforce it; a throwing move ctor would leave the slot in a UB state (double-destroy or leak).
  Reproducer (latent): hypothetical `class T: def __copy__(self) -> Own[Self]: raise SomeError(...)` -- or any T whose move construction has an observable side effect that throws. `heap_replace<T>` (concrete branch) runs `destroy_at(p)` before placement-new; if `::new(p) T(std::move(value))` throws, the Box's `_ptr` still points at a now-destroyed slot, and `Box.__del__` re-runs `heap_release(p)` -> double-destroy UB. `transfer_ownership<T>` (concrete branch) has the symmetric gap: `T val = std::move(*p)` throwing skips `destroy_at(p) + operator delete` -> leak. Safe today because TPy emits noexcept moves for all generated structs (no user-defined move constructors), and standard types T = `T` (record-by-value) get default noexcept moves. Fix: add `static_assert(std::is_nothrow_move_constructible_v<T>)` (matches `heap_take`'s implicit assumption) or annotate the helpers `noexcept(std::is_nothrow_move_constructible_v<T>)` so the precondition shows up in error messages. Pre-existing in `heap_take` too. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Box[P] Phase 1-5 work.
- **[LOW small]** Async executor multi-thread race: `_current_executor` (TPy module global in `lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py`) is a single process-wide `Ptr[Executor]`, not thread-local. The nested-executor backstop in `Executor.__init__` only checks `_current_executor`, so two concurrent `asyncio.run` calls on different threads will both pass the check and then race on `_set_current_executor` / `_clear_current_executor`. v1 asyncio is single-executor-per-process by design (documented invariant) so this is latent today; switch to a TPy thread-local module global when TPy gains that facility OR when multi-threaded async (v3+) is on the roadmap. (Originally filed against `executor_ops` in `async.hpp` -- step 7 deleted that table and made `_current_executor` the only shared-state remaining; the race shape carries over.)
- **[LOW small]** Cross-module `@dynamic` protocol inheritance lookup uses short names; two `@dynamic` protocols sharing a short name across modules silently misfire (correctness-critical for the forwarding shortcut, not just adapter-wrapping).
  After the subtype-lookup unification, the short-name dependency lives in three places: (a) `ProtocolInfo.transitive_supertypes` / `RecordInfo.transitive_supertypes` are populated with short names by `tpyc/sema/registration.py::finalize_protocol_closure` and the record-side closure block; (b) `tpyc/type_def_registry.py::is_subtype` does `supertype_name in sub.transitive_supertypes` (pure short-name lookup); (c) `tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py::dyn_protocol_forward_ok` calls `scan_by_short_name(source.name)`. Original surfacing was via the deleted `_protocol_inherits_from`; previously only used for adapter wrapping decisions where a misfire just took a different valid C++ path. After the Box[P] Phase 1b forwarding shortcut, the chain gates "forward Own[P] directly vs Adapter-wrap" -- a false positive there could produce a `unique_ptr` of the wrong dynamic-protocol type, silently. Fix: route through `qualified_name()` (already the canonical identity for protocols via the TypeDef registry) -- closures should be keyed on qname rather than short name. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the Box[P] Phase 1b forwarding shortcut.
- **[LOW small]** `heapq` ops on `@nocopy` element type T fail with a deep C++ template error (`use of deleted function 'T::T(const T&)'` inside `_siftup` / `_siftdown`) instead of a clean sema-level diagnostic. Root cause: `heapq[T: Comparable]` requires T to be copyable -- the sift algorithms stash and shift via `copy(heap[i])` -- but heapq's generic signatures don't yet carry a `T: Copyable` bound. The `Copyable` marker protocol now exists (shipped on `feat-copyable-bound`, gates `Box[T].clone`); the remaining gap is migrating heapq's signatures to use it. The same shape applies to any stdlib function that internally `copy()`s a generic T: today `@nocopy` slips through sema and explodes at C++ compile time. Reproducer: `@nocopy class B: ...; heappush(h, B())`. Fix: add `T: Copyable` to `heappush`, `heappop`, `heapify`, etc. signatures in `lib/tpy/heapq.py`. Alternatives (swap-based sift variant for `@nocopy` T) change the algorithm and are not needed now that the bound infrastructure exists. Workaround today: keep `@nocopy` types out of heaps; wrap in `Rc[T]` or `Box[T]` if shared ownership is intended. Pre-existing; surfaced during /tpy-review followup of the heapq Own[T] signature fix.
- **[LOW small]** Two `match` statements in the same scope generate a name-collision on the synthetic subject local.
  Reproducer: two sequential `match d: case ...:` blocks at the same scope level both emit `auto& __match_subject = d;` in the generated C++, producing `conflicting declaration ‘auto& __match_subject’`. Each match's synthetic subject name needs to be unique (counter / block-scoped). Workaround: wrap each match in a separate function or different scope, or use distinct subject expressions. Surfaced while writing the Pascal `case_range` regression test with two enum-typed `case d of ... end;` blocks. Lives in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py` -- the `__match_subject` name should be uniquified per-emit (mirrors how anonymous-local helpers do it elsewhere).
- **[LOW small]** Generic TPy function consuming a `list[T].pop()` into a local fails C++ build when `T` is a reference type (`val_or_ref_t<T> = T&` can't bind to `pop_back`'s rvalue return).
  Reproducer: `def heappop[T: Comparable](heap: list[T]) -> Own[T]: lastelt = heap.pop(); ...` from `lib/tpy/heapq.py`, instantiated with any user reference-type T (e.g. `class TimerEntry: ...; heap: list[TimerEntry]; heappop(heap)`). Generated code: `::tpy::val_or_ref_t<T> lastelt = ::tpy::pop_back(heap);` -- `val_or_ref_t<T>` for ref T is `T&`, but `pop_back` returns by value. Error: `cannot bind non-const lvalue reference of type 'T&' to an rvalue of type 'T'`. **Already documented** as a "language-level blocker" in the `lib/tpy/heapq.py` header (Phase-1-era comment), but never tracked here. Effect: ref-type heaps, ref-type generic stacks, and any other generic pattern that locally binds a `list.pop()` result. Surfaced again while attempting to swap `Executor.timer_heap` to `list[TimerEntry]` (ref-type variant) during async v1.x post-Phase-4 cleanup. **Workaround in heapq:** declare `lastelt: Own[T] = heap.pop()` (annotate the local as owning, side-stepping `val_or_ref_t`); requires a TPy-side check that `Own[T]` for value T resolves to plain T storage. **Fix:** codegen for generic-T locals bound from "consuming" calls (pop_back, dict.popitem, etc.) should use `Own[T]` storage shape rather than `val_or_ref_t<T>`. Sibling to the ValueType-specialization-ordering entry below: either fix independently unblocks `Executor.timer_heap` becoming a `list[TimerEntry]` (the natural shape).
- **[LOW small]** Non-`@native` ValueType record's `is_value_type` specialization is emitted *after* template instantiation sites in the same TU, producing C++ `specialization of X after instantiation` errors.
  Reproducer: `class TimerEntry(ValueType): deadline: float; waker: Waker; def __lt__(self, o): ...` used inside the same module as `heapq.heappush(self.heap, TimerEntry(...))`. Generated `_executor.hpp` emits in this order: (1) class definition, (2) `heappush<TimerEntry>(...)` call inside an inline method body, (3) `template<> struct tpy::is_value_type<...::TimerEntry> : std::true_type {}` at the end of the file. C++ rejects (3) because (2) already instantiated `heappush` while seeing the default `is_value_type<T> = false_type`, deducing `param_val_or_ref_t<T> = T&` and `val_or_ref_t<T> = T&`. **Workaround:** route the value through tuples + a parallel dict (the shape `lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py:Executor.timer_heap` shipped — see comment on `timer_heap` field), or restructure so the consumer that instantiates the template lives in a downstream module whose `.hpp` includes the value-type module's `.hpp` (the specialization is visible before instantiation). **Fix:** in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py:929-932`, emit each value-type record's `is_value_type` specialization right after the record's class body, *before* any inline method bodies / templated calls that might depend on it. Same family as the `@native` value-type namespace bug below — both stem from `is_value_type` being emitted at file end rather than per-class. Surfaced while attempting to swap `Executor`'s tuple+dict timer heap to `list[TimerEntry]` during async v1.x post-Phase-4 cleanup.
- **[LOW small]** `@native(...)`-decorated value-type record emits `tpy::is_value_type<...>` specialization in the module's own C++ namespace instead of the `@native` rename target.
  Reproducer: declare `@native("tpy::ExecutorHandle") class ExecutorHandle(ValueType): ...` in a module with `# tpy: cpp_namespace("tpystd::asyncio::_executor")`. The C++ class is `tpy::ExecutorHandle` (per `@native`), but codegen emits `template<> struct tpy::is_value_type<tpystd::asyncio::_executor::ExecutorHandle> : std::true_type {};` — a specialization for a non-existent type. C++ fails with `template argument 1 is invalid`. Lives in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/generator.py:929-932` where `module_to_cpp_namespace(self.ctx.module_name)` is hardcoded as the namespace; the `@native` rename should override it for records with linkage targeting an external C++ name. **Workaround:** don't inherit `ValueType` on `@native` records — TPy then treats them as reference types, which breaks other code (locals bound by `T&` lvalue reference can't bind to rvalue returns); the better workaround is to register the type in `tpyc/type_def_registry.py` with `is_value_type=True` (the path Waker / Poll use). Hit during async v1.x Phase 3 while attempting to store an `ExecutorHandle` field on `_ExecutorScope` for thread-local save/restore — sidestepped by skipping the save in the scope guard since v1 asyncio.run doesn't nest. **Third symptom (silent overhead, no compile error):** an `@native` POD that *isn't* declared `ValueType` (the workaround above) is then treated as a *reference* type by codegen, so any coroutine-local of that type gets wrapped in `std::optional<T>` even though the type is a 16-byte POD. Verified for `ExecutorHandle` -- the coroutine state struct in `executor_bindings_smoke/expected/include/main.hpp:29` emits `std::optional<::tpy::ExecutorHandle> h`. Inflates coroutine frame size and adds a runtime check per access. Fix: register the type via `tpyc/type_def_registry.py` with `is_value_type=True` (current best practice for `@native` POD types).
- **[LOW small]** Nested-type access through a module-qualified class chain (`m.Outer.Inner`, `m.Outer.Kind.A`) is not implemented; only the bare-name shape (`from m import Outer; Outer.Inner`) works.
  Reproducer: `import m; m.Outer.Inner()` -- where `Outer` is imported from `m` and has a nested `class Inner` -- falls through the module-qualified dispatcher (which only handles class constants and staticmethods) into the bare-name analysis path and errors as "'m' is not a variable". Same for `m.Outer.Kind.A` (nested enum value through module-qualifier). The fix is symmetric to the existing module-qualified class-constant path: extend `_analyze_field_access`'s module-qualified branch to also call into the nested-type resolution helpers (`_resolve_nested_type_access`, `_resolve_nested_chain`). Workaround: `from m import Outer` then `Outer.Inner`. Surfaced as a deferred item during the module-qualified static-method-call fix.
- **[MED small]** Cross-module nested-type qualified names (`Outer.Inner`, `Outer.Kind`) rejected as type annotations: `Parse error: Unsupported qualified type: Outer.Inner`.
  Reproducer: `from m import Container` (where `Container` has nested `class Inner` and `class Kind(Enum)`); then `def f(x: Container.Inner) -> ...` errors at parse with `Unsupported qualified type: Container.Inner`. Construction (`Container.Inner()`) works, enum value access (`Container.Kind.BOX`) works, but the same dotted name in annotation position fails. Module-level alias `InnerAlias = Container.Inner` doesn't help: the alias resolves at sema but `Container.Inner` in the assignment fails the same way. CPython accepts both annotations. Affects local and cross-module nested types equally -- not strictly a cross-module bug. Lives in the parser's type-ref grammar, which doesn't accept dotted names whose head is a class binding (only dotted modules like `typing.Optional`). Fix: extend the type-ref parser to accept `<bare-name>.<bare-name>` chains where the head resolves to a record or enum at type-resolution time. Workaround: avoid annotating with the nested type (use the parent type or restructure to top-level). Surfaced by `/tpy-fuzzy-test cross-module class member access` (probes 12, 14 of `non-call-attribute-access`).
- **[LOW small]** Misleading `'X' is not a variable` error fires for several distinct unsupported patterns, blaming the class/module name rather than the actual problem.
  Same error string surfaces for: (1) `Foo.nonexistent_method(...)` -- class exists, method lookup failed (CPython: `type object 'Foo' has no attribute 'X'`); (2) `Foo.nonexistent_field` -- same; (3) `f = Foo.staticmethod` (taking method as value without calling) -- class exists, taking-method-as-value isn't supported. Not cross-module-specific; same error for local classes. A parallel misleading case: bound-instance-method as value (`m = inst.add`) errors with `Record 'Calculator' has no field 'add'` even though `add` is a (callable) method, not a missing field. Each fallthrough should emit a targeted message ("class 'X' has no static method 'Y'", "static methods can only be called, not taken as values", "bound method references are not supported"). Lives in `_analyze_name`'s fallback at `tpyc/sema/expressions.py:540` and the equivalent field-access fallthroughs. Surfaced by `/tpy-fuzzy-test cross-module class member access`.
- **[LOW small]** Importing the same module/name twice in the same file (mixing un-aliased and aliased forms) confuses the binding pipeline and drops the un-aliased symbol.
  Two variants surfaced. Variant A (codegen): `from m import X; from m import X as Y` -- subsequent use of `X` (e.g. `X.make(7)`) emits unqualified C++ `X::make(7)` but no `using ::tpyapp::m::X;` is emitted in the generated header (only `using Y = ::tpyapp::m::X;`). C++ compile error: `'X' has not been declared`. Variant B (sema): `import m; import m as alias` -- bare `m.X.method()` errors at sema as `Undefined variable: 'm'` (the alias import shadows the un-aliased binding). The same pattern affects `import pkg.sub` + `from pkg import sub`: only the last form is usable. Workaround: pick one form per file. Lives in import-binding bookkeeping (sema) and `using`-emission (codegen). Surfaced while writing a regression test for module-qualified static method calls (attempted to cover both shapes in one file).
- **[LOW medium]** `@nocopy` class cannot accept a sibling instance as a borrowed `__init__` parameter -- forces awkward factory patterns for shared-pointer-shaped types.
  Reproducer: a class like `Rc[T]` (or any `@nocopy` smart-pointer wrapper) wants a "share" constructor that takes another `Rc[T]` by borrow, bumps a shared refcount, and stores the same internal pointer. Two natural shapes both fail. `def __init__(self, x: Rc[T])` is rejected as "@nocopy type 'Rc[T]' cannot be copied into 'x: Own[Rc[T]]'" -- the param is implicitly treated as `Own[Rc[T]]`. Explicit `def __init__(self, x: Ptr[Rc[T]])` parses, but overload resolution against a `Rc(take_ptr(self))` call site picks the wrong overload (matches `Own[T]` with `T = Ptr[Rc[T]]` instead of the Ptr-taking sig). Net effect: no clean way to write a sharing constructor as a method on the class. Workaround: `tplib.Rc` uses a single underlying `__init__(cell: Ptr[_RcCell], payload: Ptr[T])` plus a `clone()` method that handles the refcount bump and constructs the new `Rc` internally -- avoids the sharing-constructor entirely. Proper fix would involve either (a) allowing borrow semantics for the receiver-typed param of a @nocopy class's `__init__` without implicit `Own[]` lifting, or (b) better overload resolution preference rules so `Ptr[Concrete]` beats `Own[Generic]`. Surfaced while implementing Rc[T].
- **[LOW medium]** Direct inheritance of a generic `@dynamic` protocol with `T` in method-parameter position is sema-rejected as an interim guard; the proper codegen fix is still pending.
  Reproducer: `@dynamic class Sink[T](Protocol): def put(self, val: T) -> None: ...`; `class IntSink(Sink[Int32]): def put(self, val: Int32) -> None: ...`. Without the guard, the base virtual emits `virtual void put(::tpy::param_val_or_ref_t<T> val) = 0` (instantiates to `void put(const int32_t&)` for `T=Int32`), while the direct-inheritor's override emits `void put(int32_t val) override` -- different signature, override does not match, derived class remains abstract. The structural-conformance (adapter) path correctly uses `param_val_or_ref_t<T>` in the override; only the direct-inheritor path skips this. Return-position `T` (e.g. `get() -> T`) is unaffected. **Current state:** `validate_record_inheritance` rejects this shape with a diagnostic pointing users at structural conformance (test: `error_protocol_dynamic_generic_inherit_param_T`). **Pending fix:** record codegen should emit the override using `param_val_or_ref_t<concrete_T>` (or equivalently `const concrete&` for value types) so both inheritance paths work; the sema guard can then be removed. Surfaced by `/tpy-fuzzy-test generic @dynamic protocols`.
- **[LOW small] A `@dynamic` protocol with exactly ONE method, whose method takes a REQUIRED (non-optional) reference-type param (`dict`/`list`/class/`readonly[ref]`), drops the param under `Box[P]` dispatch -- sema rejects the call as `'<method>' expects 0 argument(s), got 1`.** `@dynamic class P(Protocol): def take(self, h: dict[str, str]) -> None: ...`; `class C(P): def take(self, h): ...`; `b: Box[P] = Box(C()); b.take(d)` errors `'take' expects 0 argument(s), got 1`. **Avoided by** (any one): a *multi-method* protocol (2+ methods -- `reqparam.py`/`voidreq.py` work), an *optional* reference param (`h: dict[str,str] | None = None` works -- this is why SSL Inc-5's `_Connection.request` is unaffected), a *value/view* param (`str`/`Int32` -- `request(self, method: str, url: str)` works), or a *direct* call on the conformer (`C().take(d)` works -- only the `Box` forwarding path drops it). Loud (sema error), not silent. Smells like a single-method-`@dynamic`-protocol special path (functional/callable-style) in the `Box` method-forwarding / call-arg-count resolution that mishandles a required reference param. Reproducers: `/tmp/agents/ssl/inc5/bugrepro/onemethod.py` (no mutation), `onemut.py` (mutating), `check_ro3.py` (same drop via `readonly[dict]`, also single-method). Distinct from the const-mismatch fixed on `fix-dyn-override-param-const` (that was non-generic reference-param override const-ness; this is arg-count drop on the single-method box-forward path). Surfaced probing nominal `@dynamic` inheritance for SSL Increment 5. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A `@readonly` method overriding a `@dynamic` protocol's `@readonly` method with a reference-type param (`dict`/`list`/class) fails the C++ build -- the concrete override renders the param `const T&` (a plain `@readonly` method's non-self params are rendered const) while the `@dynamic` base pure-virtual renders it non-const `T&` (`protocols._dynamic_param_list`/`to_cpp_param` ignores the method's readonly-ness for params), so the override does not bind the `const`-qualified virtual slot and the class stays abstract.** `@dynamic class Sink(Protocol): @readonly def describe(self, labels: dict[str,str]) -> Int32: ...`; `class Counter(Sink): @readonly def describe(self, labels): ...` emits base `virtual int32_t describe(ordered_map&) const = 0` vs override `int32_t describe(const ordered_map&) const override` -> g++ "marked 'override', but does not override" + "invalid cast to abstract class". Same const-mismatch FAMILY as the one fixed on `fix-dyn-override-param-const`, but a distinct mechanism: that fix suppressed const-*inference* (`mutated_params=None`), which cannot help here because the override's param is genuinely const. The fix is on the BASE side + needs a design call: should a `@readonly` `@dynamic`-protocol method's reference param render const on both sides (make `_dynamic_param_list` honor `_is_readonly_method` for params, so base pure-virtual + Adapter + override all agree on `const T&` -- matches how `@readonly` concrete methods already render params) or non-const on both? NON-`@readonly` `@dynamic`-override ref params work (the `fix-dyn-override-param-const` fix); only the `@readonly` combination is broken. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage) of `fix-dyn-override-param-const` when adding `@readonly`-override coverage. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (with the design call).
- **[LOW medium]** Variable re-export from a cycle peer fails at sema with "'X' not found in module".
  `from a import LIMIT` (where `a` is a cycle peer that itself does `from c import LIMIT`, c being a non-cycle module) hits "'LIMIT' not found in module 'a'". Same root cause as the type alias entry below: pre-population covers records / enums / protocols / functions but not variables, and at the consumer's bind_imports time the cycle peer's `exports.variables` is still empty. Pre-popping variables would carry the parser's still-unresolved `TypeRefNode` into the binding's `info` slot and crash downstream sema. Fix requires deferring variable type resolution or splitting pre-pop into a "name only" pass that runs before resolution and a "type fill" pass after. Workaround: define the variable in a non-cycle module and have the consumer import directly from there.
- **[LOW medium]** Type alias re-export from a cycle peer fails at sema with "'X' not found in module".
  `from b import ShapeAlias` (where `b` is a cycle peer of the consumer and defines `type ShapeAlias = Int32`) hits "'ShapeAlias' not found in module 'b'" -- the consumer's bind_imports runs before `b`'s, so `b.exports.type_aliases` is still empty, and the cycle re-export pre-population pass doesn't cover type aliases. Records / enums / functions are pre-pop'd; type aliases are skipped because the alias body is an unresolved `TypeRefNode` at pre-pop time and downstream sema would crash on a `TypeRefNode` reaching a `TpyType` slot. Fix requires either (a) a third pass that re-pre-pops type aliases after each module's `_resolve_module_refs` runs, or (b) deferring type alias resolution further so `_register_from_attribute_binding` can lazily fetch the resolved body. Workaround: define the alias outside the cycle and have both peers import from there.
- **[LOW small]** `from . import *` (dot-only, package-level star) silently no-ops; failure surfaces at downstream call sites with no diagnostic at the import line.
  Reproducer: inside `pkg/consumer.py`, `from . import *` is accepted without any diagnostic. The module's local definitions (e.g. `def get_value() -> ...`) then fail to show up in `pkg.consumer`'s exports, and a downstream `from pkg.consumer import get_value` errors as `'get_value' not found in module 'pkg.consumer'`. CPython treats `from . import *` as star-importing from the current package's `__init__`. TPy's compile-time expansion appears to corrupt the consumer module's export pipeline when this form is used. Fix should either implement the CPython semantics (star from package `__init__`) or reject the form at parse time with a precise diagnostic. Workaround: spell out the target as `from .module import *` or use explicit named imports.
- **[LOW small]** `from pkg import *` doesn't propagate submodule bindings exposed via `pkg.__init__.__all__`.
  Reproducer: `pkg/__init__.py` does `from . import utils; __all__ = ["utils"]`; consumer does `from pkg import *`; `utils` is not in scope (`Undefined variable: 'utils'`). CPython works. The compile-time star-import expansion walks the source's `module_attributes` and `ast.imports` but treats submodule bindings (module-namespace names) differently from records/functions/enums/variables/type aliases. Workaround: `from pkg import utils` named import.
- **[MED medium]** Borrow checker doesn't flag `old = d.get(k); del d[k]; use(old)` as a dangling pointer use.
  Use-after-free not caught by borrow checker: `old = d.get(k); del d[k]; use(old)` -- `dict.get()` returns a pointer into the dict's internal storage. `del d[k]` frees that entry, making `old` a dangling pointer. The subsequent `use(old)` is undefined behavior (reads garbage). The borrow checker should warn that `old` borrows from `d` and `del d[k]` invalidates it. Workaround: reorder to use `old` before `del`.
- **[MED medium]** Readonly-deduced method returning `Own[Span[T] | list[T]]` fails because const Span doesn't convert to declared non-const Span.
  Readonly-deduced method returns `Span[const T]` but declared return type has `Span[T]`: when a method is deduced readonly, `list_slice` on the const container returns `std::span<const T>`, but the return type `Own[Span[T] | list[T]]` uses non-const `std::span<T>`. The implicit conversion fails. Fix: propagate readonly into Span members of union return types when the method is deduced const, or emit a const_cast in codegen.
- **[LOW small]** `Covariant[T]` converting constructor codegen assumes direct field-pointer covariance; would fail for any future class whose field type wraps T through a non-covariant layer (e.g. a hypothetical `class Foo[T](Covariant[T]): _cell: Ptr[GenericWrapper[T]]`).
  Latent today -- no stdlib type triggers the bug since the Rc[T] rewrite (branch `rc-dyn-protocol`) moved Rc to a non-generic `_cell: Ptr[_RcCell]` + `_payload: Ptr[T]` layout where `_payload` is directly pointer-covariant (`Child*` -> `Parent*` in C++) like Box's `_ptr: Ptr[T]`. The codegen limitation remains for any future class shape that interposes a non-covariant template wrapper between the covariant base T and the field. Original reproducer (now obsolete): the pre-rewrite `class Rc[T](Covariant[T]): _cell: Ptr[_RcCell[T]]` emitted a covariant converting ctor initializing `_cell(std::move(__other._cell))` where `__other._cell` was `_RcCell<U>*` and `_cell` expected `_RcCell<T>*` -- C++ pointer covariance doesn't propagate through `_RcCell`. Proper fix: codegen should detect when a covariant-base field requires conversion (not just move) and either emit the proper construction (delegating to a per-type conversion helper) or sema-reject the class with a clear message pointing at the wrapping field.
- **[LOW small]** Sema's user-function-call dispatch path doesn't resolve `PendingViewType` in the inferred type substitution; the unresolved type flows into `resolved_function_info.params` and is only resolved at codegen time on a per-call-site basis. Builtin functions explicitly pass `resolve_pending_view=True` to `_resolve_call_overloads`; user functions (including generic static methods like `Rc.new`) don't.
  Reproducer: any `Rc.new(s)` / `Box(s)` / generic `def f[T](x: Own[T])` call with a `PendingStrType` lvalue argument leaves `PendingStrType` baked into the resolved function's param types. Currently safe because the codegen sites that read these param types route through `self.types.type_to_cpp` (which calls `_resolve_pending_view`) -- specifically the `gen_call_arg` Own[str] copy-temp path was fixed in this branch by swapping `own.wrapped.to_cpp()` -> `self.types.type_to_cpp(own.wrapped)`. But any new codegen site that does `.to_cpp()` directly on a param/return type (there are 100+ such direct calls in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/` today) is a latent crash for the same bug class. Architectural fix: pass `resolve_pending_view=True` in `_analyze_user_function_call`'s `_resolve_call_overloads` call (calls.py around line 3767) -- mirrors the builtin path -- so resolved_function_info never carries PendingViewType. Until then, any new codegen direct-`.to_cpp()` call on a sema-derived type should be reviewed for this hazard. Surfaced during `/tpy-review` of the gen_call_arg fix.
- **[LOW small]** `gen_call_arg`'s `Own[T]` copy-temp-and-move pattern fires uniformly across types, including trivially-copyable ones where the `std::move` is a no-op and the temp is pure noise.
  Reproducer: `s = "hi"; Rc.new(s)` emits `std::string_view __tmp_1{s}; Rc<std::string_view>::new_(std::move(__tmp_1));` -- correct but verbose; `Rc<std::string_view>::new_(s)` would suffice. The temp+move is meaningful for `std::string` (transfers heap buffer) and for non-trivially-copyable T in general; for trivially-copyable resolved types (`std::string_view`, primitives, `std::span`) it adds two redundant lines per call site. Lives in the `is_any_str_type` branch of `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py:864-873` (and by extension the parallel `auto`-deduction branch). Fix: gate the temp+move on a trivially-copyable check on the resolved Own[T] inner; pass the source through directly otherwise. Cosmetic only -- no correctness or perf concern (the move-on-trivially-copyable is a no-op in optimized builds). Surfaced reading the snapshot for `tests/cases/tplib/rc_new_str_lvalue` after the recent gen_call_arg fix.
- **[LOW small]** Generator expression that yields a `.clone()` (or any `Own[T]` rvalue) of an `@nocopy` element type infers its element type as `Own[T]`, which then fails `Iterable[T]` conformance at the consumer.
  Reproducer: `def total(items: Iterable[Rc[Node]]) -> Int32: ...; total(x.clone() for x in originals)` errors with `Type <genexpr>[Own[Rc[Node]]] does not conform to protocol Iterable[Rc[Node]] in argument 'items'`. The list-comprehension counterpart `[x.clone() for x in originals]` works (since the elements flow into a `list[Rc[T]]` slot which auto-unwraps the `Own[]`); the genexpr path doesn't strip the `Own[]` from its yielded element type before checking conformance against `Iterable[T]`. Fix: the genexpr's effective element type for protocol-conformance purposes should be the unwrapped `T`, not `Own[T]` -- the consumer borrows each yielded value the same way for-each does. Lives in the genexpr type-inference path in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` (parallel to where comprehensions decide their result element type). Workaround: materialize via list comprehension instead. Surfaced while probing comprehension @nocopy fix siblings (`/tmp/agents/comp_probes/probe_genexpr_clone2.py`).
- **[LOW small]** Comprehension body's mutation of the loop var doesn't propagate back to the source container, so a const-borrowed parameter source can't be iterated for `.clone()` (or any non-readonly method) in a comprehension. The for-loop pattern propagates this correctly.
  Reproducer: `def clone_all(src: list[Rc[Node]]) -> Own[list[Rc[Node]]]: return [x.clone() for x in src]` -- C++ rejects with `passing 'const Rc<Node>' as 'this' argument discards qualifiers` because `src` stays `const std::vector<Rc<Node>>&`. The for-loop equivalent `for x in src: out.append(x.clone())` works -- sema's mutation propagation marks `src` as mutated, lifting `const` from the parameter so iteration produces non-const elements. Comprehension sema should run the same propagation: when the comp loop var ends up in `mutated_loop_vars`, attribute that mutation to the iter source and feed it through the existing param-mutation inference. Lives in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` `_enter_comp_scope` (post-body) -- mirror the for-loop's mutation-propagation hookup in `tpyc/sema/statements.py`. Workaround: assign `src` to a local first, or use a local-constructed source. Surfaced while writing `tests/cases/list/list_comp_clone_return` (the test had to be reworked to construct its source locally).
- **[LOW small]** Aliasing a const-source element/borrow into a local then using it through that local produces ill-formed C++ (C++-caught, not a silent miscompile). Two variants, both rooted in field-path-keyed borrows that don't reconnect to the owning param: (1) aliased *read* -- the subscript flavor (`e = o.items[0]; return e.v` when `o` is const) is now fixed: `_is_const_indirect`'s const-rooted-receiver arm (mirrored in THIR's `_f1_is_const`) const-binds a subscript/method-call bound off a const-rooted receiver. The named-accessor-call flavor (`x = o.b.get(); return x.v` when `o` is const) is still open: an `@auto_readonly` accessor result bound to a local keeps the receiver mutable to stay sound (a non-const local alias requires a mutable source), so the local binds a non-const reference (`Foo& x`) to a const source -- the never-mutated-local const-binding limitation (locals are non-const by default, tracked in TODO.md; pinned by the `read_named` body in `tests/cases/readonly/inferred_readonly_borrow_const`). (2) aliased *mutating-method* -- `e = o.items[0]; e.bump()` does not demote `o`, because the non-readonly-method-call mark in `sema/methods.py` uses `through_field=False` (it must -- a callee that turns out readonly should not demote), so it doesn't climb the field-path borrow root to the param; `o` stays const and the mutating `bump()` fails C++ const-checking. Direct (un-aliased) forms work; both variants resolve via Phase-2 deferral of borrow-rooted demotion once callee const-ness is known. Surfaced building `auto-readonly-usage-clone-selection`.
- **[LOW small]** The `TypeParamRef`-returning call local-decl still picks `val_or_cref_t` vs `val_or_ref_t` purely on `fi.is_readonly`, with no const-rooted-receiver check -- the same stale-twin hole that `_is_const_indirect`'s const-rooted-receiver arm now closes for subscript/method-call borrow locals, left unpatched for this sibling path. A generic non-value-`T`-returning method called via a const-rooted receiver inside an *inferred*-readonly method resolves the mutable twin (`fi.is_readonly` False), so it emits `val_or_ref_t` (non-const) while C++ overload resolution picks the const twin -- a build error, not a silent miscompile. Sites: `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:2397` (AST) and its mirror `tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py:4050` (THIR). Fix shape: gate the trait pick on receiver-constness the same way the new borrow-local arm does (ideally via the shared predicate that would fix the root cause -- see TODO.md). Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the inferred-readonly borrow-const fix.
- **[LOW small, believed-unreachable today]** The const-rooted-source chain walk (`is_const_union_source` in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py`, `_f1_const_rooted_source` in `tpyc/thir/lower/predicates.py`, both reused by the new const-rooted-receiver arm) only matches the chain's *base name* against the const-param/const-local sets; it never consults `FieldInfo.is_interior_mutable`. A subscript/method-call whose receiver chain passes through an `unsafe_interior_mutable[Ptr[T]]` field (e.g. `Mutex`/`RwLock`/`Rc`/`Arc` internals) off a const-rooted root would get force-bound `const` purely because the outer root is const, defeating that field's readonly-escape purpose -- a build error (over-const), not a silent miscompile. Not triggered by any current corpus use (all `unsafe_interior_mutable` uses today are value-typed reads or direct mutating calls, not indirection-needing subscript/method-call results). Fix shape: have the chain-walk helpers consult `FieldInfo.is_interior_mutable` at each field hop so const-rootedness tracks the same field-level exemption sema's readonly-propagation already honors. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the inferred-readonly borrow-const fix.
- **[LOW small]** `addr_taken_roots` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) is not transparent to a borrowing `@auto_readonly` accessor call, unlike `_root_name_of_expr`. So `yield o.b.get()` and `return o.b.get()` (the paths that root through `addr_taken_roots`) neither mark the receiver mutated nor register a `return_borrows_from` borrow contract for the enclosing function/generator -- a method that yields/returns a *mutable* accessor result is not inferred as mutating self, and the receiver param stays `const`. C++-caught (returning/yielding a mutable `T&` through a `const`-bound receiver fails to compile), not a silent miscompile. Fix shape: make `addr_taken_roots` climb through a borrowing accessor call the same way `_root_name_of_expr` does. Surfaced by /tpy-review of `auto-readonly-usage-clone-selection`.
- **[LOW small]** Foreach scope binding (`bind_loop_var(is_foreach=True)`) discards the loop var name from `loop_vars` on inner-scope exit unconditionally, evicting an outer for-loop's tracking when the names match.
  Reproducer: `for x in outer: y = [x.clone() for x in inner]` -- when the inner comprehension's scope exits, `loop_vars.discard("x")` removes the name even though the outer `for x` is still in flight. After the comprehension, `mark_loop_var_mutated("x")` becomes a no-op for the outer loop, so subsequent mutations on the outer `x` aren't tracked, producing wrong `const_loop_var` decisions or incorrect auto-consuming-iter activation. Pre-existing for nested for-loops with shadowed names; the recent comp-fix that switched comprehensions to `is_foreach=True` extends the affected combinations to comp/for and comp/comp pairs.
