Same pattern at the if-body extraction (4018), elif chain (4195), else-body (4050). **Repro shape**: `while isinstance(x, A): assert isinstance(x, A); x.a_method()`. Same root cause, second trigger: a USER local literally named `__x` collides with the narrowing alias for `x` at the same C++ scope -- `_fresh_alias_local` consults only `declared_persistent_aliases`, never the function's actual declared-variable set, so `auto& __x = ...` redeclares the user's local (surfaced by the U3 /tpy-review probe; reproduced identically on the AST and THIR paths). **Fix:** either pass `persistent=True` at these four sites, or have the persistent=False emission register the alias name in `declared_persistent_aliases` so the suffix bump kicks in for nested re-narrowing; for the user-local trigger, the freshness check must also consult declared variable names.
- **[LOW small]** Two modules independently declaring structurally-identical recursive union aliases collide in `compiler.union_wrapper_index` via `setdefault` (first-write-wins). The second module's `iter_imported_recursive_unions` then yields the first module's `RecursiveUnionInfo` (origin != second_module_name), so the second module's wrapper-struct definition is skipped while its uses still reference the (missing) local symbol. **Repro shape**: modules A and B both declaring `type Tree = int | list[Tree]` independently (no cross-import). Link error: undefined reference to `tpyapp::b::Tree`. **Fix:** key the index by `(members, defining_module)` or accept the duplication and emit a wrapper in every defining module's TU. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_for_each` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:4868`) snapshots `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` around the for-body but does NOT save/restore `literal_facts` or `protocol_narrowings`. Body-level isinstance/literal narrowings on free (non-loop) variables leak past the loop end. `_gen_while` at 4444-4445 correctly snapshots all four maps; `_gen_for_each` is divergent. **Repro shape**: `for x in xs: assert isinstance(item, SomeProto); ...` where `item` is a non-loop variable. After the loop, downstream code sees a stale `protocol_narrowings[item]` and routes the wrong typed path. **Fix:** mirror `_gen_while`'s save/restore of all four maps.
- **[LOW small]** `_gen_try_throw` between-block restores at `tpyc/codegen_cpp/statements.py:3354, 3368-3369, 3378-3379` reset `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` between try/handlers/else, but do NOT touch `literal_facts` or `protocol_narrowings`. A narrowing introduced inside one handler leaks into the next handler or the else body. **Repro shape**: `try: ... except A as e: assert isinstance(p, SomeProto); ... except B: ...`. The second handler's emit sees the first handler's stale `protocol_narrowings[p]` entry. **Fix:** include `literal_facts` and `protocol_narrowings` in the snapshot/restore tuple between try-body / handler / else-body.
- **[LOW small]** `tpyc/codegen_cpp/context.py:1057` -- `LocalScopeSnap` captures `narrowed_vars` and `declared_persistent_aliases` but excludes `isinstance_init_locals`. The dataclass docstring states "If a new what-locals-exist field is added to CodeGenContext, add it here too" -- this field was missed. Constexpr-elif early-return paths at statements.py:3975/4014 emit `}` + return without running the per-iteration `isinstance_init_locals.pop(...)` at 4030-4031, leaking the entry past the if-chain's `restore_local_scope` (4029). Subsequent `_emit_isinstance_extractions` for the same variable finds the leaked entry and routes reads through a dead C++ local. **Fix:** add `isinstance_init_locals: dict[str, str]` to `LocalScopeSnap` and to the snap/restore helpers in `CodeGenContext`.
- **[LOW small]** `make_union` sort order can diverge across phases because `UnionType.__str__` now consults `compiler.union_display_names` (populated mid-sema by `_register_union_wrappers` neighbors at analyzer.py:572). The sort key (`str(t)`) for an inner aliased union changes from the expanded form (e.g. `"Circle | Rect"`) to the alias name (`"Shape"`) once display_names is populated. The same logical union, constructed before vs. after that moment, produces two different canonical `members` tuples; lookups in `union_wrapper_index` / `union_display_names` / `union_alias_names` keyed by `typ.members` then split into two entries. Likely latent today (each union is constructed once per pipeline pass), but a future macro / substitute_type_params / regeneration code path could trigger it. **Fix:** stabilize the sort key independently of display-name registration -- either sort by a phase-invariant string (e.g. `expanded_str()`) or by a member identity that doesn't depend on `__str__`. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[LOW small]** `compiler.union_display_names` cross-module first-write-wins picks the dependency-topological winner. If a vendored stdlib and the user's module each declare `type X = A | B` with the same canonical members tuple, the vendored module's name leaks into the user's diagnostics. Diagnostic-quality issue (no correctness impact); fix shape: key display_names by `(members, defining_module)` and pick by the diagnostic's owning module, or prefer the entry module's name. Surfaced by /code-review.
- **[MED small]** `Task[T]` handle escaping `asyncio.run`'s scope carries a dangling `Ptr[Awaker]` in its stamped `_waker`. Calling `task.cancel()` on the escaped handle post-run dispatches `wake() -> Awaker.mark_runnable(...)` through a destroyed `Executor`, dereffing freed memory. Same v1 invariant `_ExecutorScope` (in `lib/tpy/asyncio/_executor.py`) documents for every stamped Waker, but the M10 cancel-runnable-mark hook broadened the surface from "Wakers stored in the executor's timer_heap" to "every `Task[T]` returned by `asyncio.create_task`". `drain_spawned_with_cancel` walks `self.slots` and cannot reach external handles. `Waker.wake`'s `try/except BaseException` only covers caught exceptions, not raw memory faults. This is NOT a borrow-param escape (the borrow checker sees a legal ownership move of an owned `Rc` handle; it has no visibility that the handle internally aliases a shorter-lived scoped resource) -- so escape analysis does not reach it. **Disposition (v1): accepted documented footgun** -- the `Task._waker` field comment already documents the invariant, and `asyncio.run` drains non-escaped spawned tasks at teardown, so an escaped-then-cancelled handle is the only reachable path and is unusual. The clean fix is (b) make `Awaker` an `Arc`/`Weak` handle so a post-run wake is a safe no-op -- a runtime design change touching the Awaker protocol / Waker / every wake site, folded into the same THIR-region effort that owns `create_task` ref-param escape (same lifetime model). Option (a) (executor tracks issued `Task` handles and nulls their wakers at `_ExecutorScope.__del__`) is a smaller available-now alternative if this becomes reachable in practice. Surfaced by /code-review of the task-cancel-mark-runnable branch.
- **[MED small]** Async coro **and sync generator** capturing `&list[i]` (or other container-element addresses) via the pointer-form Optional[NonValue] param shape inherits a borrow-lifetime hazard: any list mutation (push_back, pop, clear, insert) during the resumable's lifetime invalidates the captured `T*` pointer, including across suspension/yield boundaries. Existing borrow-checker covers some `T&` shapes via `return_borrows_from` but not this `T*` source: `tpyc/codegen_cpp/expressions.py::_gen_optional_ptr_arg` lifts `T&` to `&T` at the call site, the resulting pointer goes into the resumable-frame, and the caller's borrow checker doesn't see this as an escaping borrow with cross-suspension lifetime. Pre-existing class of bug, newly reachable since `_classify_params` (gen_async.py) and `gen_generator_struct` (gen_generators.py) started emitting `const T*` / `T*` for pointer-form Optional resumable params on the `async-scope-reset` branch. Fix shape: extend `analyzer.py`'s `gen_borrows` injection (currently `func.is_generator` only, line ~1088) to also cover `func.is_async`, then add mutate-during-resumable-lifetime detection at the call site. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the `async-scope-reset` branch. **Same hazard class, additional route (generator-resumable migration Phase D2):** the for-loop pointer-form loop var binds `it = &(*(*__for_it_N)++)` (begin_end) or `it = &(unwrap_ref(...))` (iter_next) -- a `T*` aliasing a container element held across a `yield`/`await` inside the loop body. A structural mutation of the iterable during iteration invalidates it. Partially mitigated by the existing mutation-during-iteration *warning* (e.g. `tests/cases/iterators/gen_borrow_mutate_complex` warns on `items.append(...)` while iterating), but the warning is non-fatal, so the dangle is reachable; same fix direction applies. **Note (declaration-driven yield ABI):** this is now the genuine *residual* of the borrow ABI -- the *consumer-side* retention of a yielded borrow past its iteration step is checked (re-yield-onward / nested-`def` capture rejected; return/stash caught by dangling/lifetime; value-stores copy), but a *caller* that mutates/frees the borrowed container while the generator is live remains only a warning, and is not closed by the consumer-escape check. **Further route (Phase F3):** tuple-unpack for-loops over `list[tuple[..., Rec]]` add a two-level alias -- `__for_tup = T*` aliases the container element, then each non-value unpack target binds `t = &std::get<i>(*__for_tup)` (a `Rec*` into the same element). Same container-mutation invalidation hazard, same partial warning mitigation, same fix direction. **Further route (statement-level borrow alias):** a single-assign (`a = items[0]`) or a tuple-unpack borrow target from a call/name source (`a, b = first_two(items)`) held across a suspension now binds a `T*`/`const T*` frame field aliasing the live source (the borrow-alias-across-suspension fix). Same container-mutation-during-suspension invalidation hazard as the for-loop and param shapes; same escape-analysis fix direction.
- **[LOW small]** `async for` sema branch (`tpyc/sema/statements.py::_analyze_async_for`) ships with two of the four safety analyses that the sync for-loop branch applies; two remain:
   1. **Readonly propagation**: `async for x in some_readonly_aiter:` does not wrap `x` in `ReadonlyType` even when the iterable is readonly-qualified. Near-zero exposure for typical user-defined async iterators (the readonly would have to be on the iterable's value type).
   2. **Dangling-temporary diagnostic**: for iterables that are calls with `return_borrows_from`, the sync branch warns when the borrowed source is a temporary; async-for skips this check. Async iterators are typically user records (not borrow-returning stdlib calls), so exposure is rare.
   Iterable borrow tracking (TpyName path) and `loop_var_iterable` map population shipped with v1.5 M6 -- see `tests/cases/async/async_for_warn_iter_mutation`. Surfaced by /tpy-review of M6.
- **[LOW small]** Missing diagnostic: passing `coro: Awaitable[T]` (non-`Own`) to a consuming sink emits invalid C++ instead of a sema error.
  Reproducer: `def consume_it[T](coro: Awaitable[T]) -> Task[T]: return _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro)`. The non-`Own` param is a borrow (emitted as `T_coro&`); the sink consumes its arg via `make_executor_owned_task<T>(CoroT&&)`. Codegen correctly doesn't move from a borrow, but sema doesn't reject either, so `CoroT` deduces to `T_coro&` and `std::optional<T_coro&>` fails the C++ build with an unhelpful template-stack error. **Workaround:** annotate the param as `Own[Awaitable[T]]`. **Fix:** sema should detect "borrow protocol passed to consuming-only sink" and emit a TPy diagnostic suggesting `Own[Awaitable[T]]`.
- **[LOW small]** Missing diagnostic for non-last-use of an `Own[T]` arg passed to a forwarding-style `@cpp_template`; user gets an unhelpful C++ template error instead of a TPy sema error.
  Reproducer: `def caller[T](coro: Own[Awaitable[T]]) -> Task[T]: task = _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro); return _make_executor_owned_task[T](coro)`. Codegen auto-moves `Own[T]` args at last use (post v1.2 step 2); the first call is not last-use, so `coro` is emitted as a bare lvalue, the C++ forwarding ref deduces `CoroT = T&`, and `std::optional<T&>` fails to compile with a deep template-stack error. **Workaround:** copy explicitly via `tpy.copy(coro)` if you need two uses (only valid for copyable T), or refactor to call once. **Fix:** sema should detect "Own[T] referenced after a non-last-use consume site" and emit a clear TPy diagnostic. (Before v1.2 step 2 this was a runtime use-after-move via a hard-coded `std::move({N})` in the template; now it's a C++ build failure.)
- **[MED small]** Generic `Own[...T...]` params are exempt from the Own-param dangling-return rejection, so a non-value instantiation can still return a dangling borrow. `def first_val[T](items: Own[list[T]]) -> T: return items[0]` is accepted at the generic level (the body is analyzed pre-instantiation, where a `T` return may be by-value), but instantiated with a reference type the returned element borrows the dying owned list. Concrete `Own[T]` roots are rejected (`_name_is_param_or_global` Own-awareness, `tests/cases/auto_move/error_own_param_borrow_return`); the exemption is `contains_type_param` in the same predicate. Closing it needs instantiation-aware return checking (or a conservative generic-level rule that exempts only value-bounded `T`, e.g. `T: ValueType`). Surfaced scoping the Own-param provenance fix.
- **[MED medium]** Simple-generator yields holding references into the iterable (`tuple[..., T]` borrow form) dangle if the iterable is structurally mutated between polls.
  Both simple-for-NativeIterable and simple-while branches in `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py` emit a `make_generator<std::tuple<..., T&>>` slot when the yield type is a non-Optional tuple of non-value elements -- the yielded tuple holds a `T&` into the iterable's backing storage. If the consumer or the lambda's post-yield code structurally mutates the iterable (`items.append(...)`, `items.clear()`, dict insert that triggers rehash), already-yielded references dangle. The borrow checker doesn't track iterator-captured containers, so the unsafe pattern compiles silently. Fix path: extend the borrow checker to track "iterable captured by reference in generator lambda", reject structural-mutation methods on it for the duration of any outstanding yielded reference. In the meantime, falling back to value-form yields (`elem_type.to_cpp()` instead of `to_cpp_return()`) trades performance for safety -- could be wired behind an opt-in if user impact warrants.
- **[MED medium]** `async def` with reference-typed parameters: a `Task<T>` constructed via `asyncio.create_task` (or `Task::from_coro`) that escapes the caller's scope can dangle on those parameters.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py:_classify_params` stores non-value-type async-def params as C++ reference fields (`T& param`) in the coroutine struct. When the struct is moved into a `Task<T>` via `create_task` and the Task outlives the parameter's source storage, the reference becomes dangling. Per `docs/ASYNC_DESIGN.md` "Parameter capture": "For escaping calls (`create_task(some_async(items))` where `items` is local to the caller), the borrow checker rejects it." That rejection is not yet implemented. Workaround: pass `Own[T]` so the coroutine struct stores by value, or pass via `Box[T]` / `Rc[T]`, or borrow only module globals (they outlive `asyncio.run`). **Fix is gated on the IR/region migration -- do not bolt this onto the current borrow checker.** A prototype escape+discharge check confirmed the accept/reject boundary works, but it had to key borrow facts on string root names and hardcode the `Task` type / `gather` names -- exactly the "string-key => not designed yet" smell `CLAUDE.md` warns against, and enough ad-hoc machinery that it fights the migration it should be built on. The right shape (Rust's model): this is a lifetime feature. `create_task` should declare a run-stable / `'static`-equivalent **bound on its coroutine argument** in its signature, enforced by the general region checker -- so a user's own `spawn` gets safety for free with no compiler edits; the borrow-a-local-but-join variant is a structured **`TaskGroup`** API whose block scope bounds the borrowed tasks (cf. `std::thread::scope`). Neither is expressible until TPy has lifetimes/regions (the THIR/MIR work); until then this stays an open, documented footgun with the workarounds above. **Extended by M7 (generic async free functions / methods, branch `feat-generic-async-free-funcs`)** to the `_CoroParamKind.TYPE_PARAM` storage form: when T is non-value at instantiation, `val_or_ref_t<T>` resolves to `T&`, so a generic async free function or method called with a borrowed reference-type arg whose source goes out of scope before the next poll has the same dangling-ref class -- not a new symptom, just a new path through which the existing hazard can manifest. Same fix applies. **Extended by H4 (protocol-param generators)** to two more vectors: (a) `_CoroParamKind.STATIC_PROTOCOL` -- a static-protocol param captured as `T_<pname>` deduces to a reference for an lvalue arg, so the same escape applies (`create_task(consume(local_iterable))`); (b) the **sync-generator** field-store form, newly reachable now that protocol-param generators compile -- a generator that borrows a reference-type iterable (`T_it = list&`) stored into a field/container/global (`self.g = echo(local_xs)`) dangles when `local_xs` goes out of scope, with only a non-fatal "mutation while borrowed" warning at the mutation site and no escape diagnostic. The borrow-checker escape analysis is the same fix; it must cover generator factories, not just `create_task`. **Extended by the `_CoroParamKind.FN` form** (Fn params in resumable generators): the callable is forwarded and stored by value in the frame (sound for the callable object itself), but an `Fn` lambda that captures a local *by reference* (`[&local]`) and is passed into an escaping generator dangles when `local`'s scope ends -- the lambda's interior reference targets are not tracked, distinct from the lvalue-arg-deduces-a-reference route but the same escape class and same IR-gated fix.
- **[MED medium]** Borrowed-mode `await` operands can dangle if the awaited reference-type object does not outlive the suspension.
  Async codegen stores reference-type awaitables (for example `Future[T]`) as a raw pointer in the coroutine frame so `await f` preserves object identity and another task can complete the same Future. This is correct for stable lvalues such as frame fields, but it is unsafe if the operand is a temporary or a shorter-lived local whose lifetime ends before the awaiter resumes. The missing borrow check is the same shape as the escaping `create_task` capture bug above: any borrowed await operand must be proven to outlive the coroutine suspension, or the compiler should reject it with a clear diagnostic. **Same gap, now reachable via more arg shapes (await of a static-protocol-param coroutine):** `await consume(rows[0])` where `consume(it: Iterable[int])` and `rows` is a param/field borrows `rows[0]` by reference into the sub-future (the forwarding-deduction field type deduces `U&` for the lvalue) and dangles if the container is structurally mutated across the suspension -- no diagnostic. Sound for a stable, unmutated source (the named-iterable case is the intended, shipped form); the fix is the same await-operand escape/outlives-suspension check, which should cover the inline static-protocol-arg path (`_extra_template_args_for_await`) too. Collection-literal / comprehension args of this shape are already rejected cleanly; the residual is the stable-but-mutable lvalue (subscript / field-chain element). **Another vector (async-with borrowed manager):** a reference-type lvalue `async with mgr:` now stores `mgr` as a `T*` borrow in the coroutine frame (`__with_ctx_<n> = &mgr`) so `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` act on the original (the fix that unified async-with with sync `with`). Sound when `mgr` outlives the suspension (the common synchronous-shaped case); but if the async-with-containing frame escapes (e.g. `create_task`) past `mgr`'s lifetime, the manager pointer dangles -- same missing escape/outlives check, IR-gated like the rest of this entry. **Another route (abandonment-cleanup destructor):** a generator/coroutine frame suspended inside `with mgr:` (borrowed lvalue manager, `with_borrowed_fields` -> `T*` frame field) that ESCAPES the manager's scope -- verified reachable: `def make() -> Own[Iterator[int]]: cm = CM(); g = gen(cm); next(g); return g` compiles with no diagnostic -- now dereferences the dead manager in the frame DESTRUCTOR's `__exit__` cleanup as well as on the resume path (the resume-path deref predates the destructor). Same escape-analysis fix; the destructor adds the abandon-without-resume trigger.
- **[MED medium]** Borrow tracker only checks param names; global mutations are silent (`ref = g[0]; g.append(x)` produces no warning).
  Globals not tracked in borrow system: `mark_param_mutated` only checks `current_param_names` (`context.py:688`), so global variable mutations are invisible to the borrow tracker. `ref = g[0]; g.append(x)` produces no warning.
- **[LOW small]** Non-self param mutation through a FIELD alias borrow is silently dropped from mutation propagation.
  `def f(p: Container) -> None: alias = p.field; alias.mutating_method()` -- the alias is a FIELD borrow of `"p.field"` in the borrow tracker. `methods.py:790` calls `mark_param_mutated("alias")`; `alias` is not in `current_param_names`, and `effective_storage_through_borrows("alias")` returns the dotted key `"p.field"`, which is also not in `current_param_names` -- so `p` is never flagged mutated. Symmetric to the self-mutation gap that was fixed by extending `_is_self_call_deferred` / the methods.py chain walk via `_local_traces_to_self`, but the non-self analog wasn't generalized. Fix shape: extend `mark_param_mutated` so a dotted key whose root is a param name marks the root, mirroring how `loop_var_iterable`'s dotted iterables already do (`context.py:1024-1028`).
- **[LOW small]** Phase-1-immediate vs Phase-2-deferred asymmetry in mutation tracking for borrow-traced receivers; can silently block readonly inference on chained-call methods.
  `tpyc/sema/methods.py:798+` chain walk marks `self_mutated` directly in Phase 1 when chain_root is "self" or borrow-traces to self -- this is conservative and doesn't consult the callee's actual mutation facts. The non-chained path (`_is_self_call_deferred` + call edge with `receiver_is_self=True`) defers to Phase 2 and propagates only if the callee actually mutates. A method that is genuinely readonly but happens to invoke a non-readonly sub-method through a chained call (e.g. `frame.get().pure_read()` where `pure_read` is auto-inferred const) hits the chain walk via the `not info.is_readonly` gate -- which fires because the inference hasn't run yet at Phase 1. Pre-existing conservatism extended in the BUGS-204 fix to cover borrow-traced chain roots. Fix shape: lower the chain walk to recording a call edge with `receiver_is_self=True` (covering each call in the chain) so Phase 2 settles it precisely, mirroring the non-chained path.
- **[MED medium]** Nested function analysis runs in isolated borrow state; nonlocal mutations inside closures don't propagate.
  Nonlocal mutations invisible to outer scope: nested function analysis runs in isolated state (`save_function_state`/`reset_function_tracking`). Mutations to nonlocal variables inside closures don't propagate back to the outer function's borrow tracker.
- **[MED medium]** Borrow check runs in Phase 1 using direct mutation facts; transitive structural mutation through helper methods is missed.
  User-type indirect structural mutation not detected: `_is_invalidating_method` uses `direct_structural_mutated_params` (Phase 1), so `add_twice()` calling `self.add()` which does `self._items.append()` is not caught. Phase 2 propagates `structural_mutated_params` transitively, but the borrow check runs during Phase 1. Fix: add deferred borrow checks after Phase 2, similar to `resolve_pending_borrow_checks`.
- **[LOW medium]** `v = s[0]` where `s: Span[str]` registers `s` as the str-borrow source, but mutations to the backing container don't invalidate `v` (alias chain).
  `Span[str]` subscript view: `SpanType.subscript_borrows()` is intentionally not overridden because `v = s[0]` registers `s` as the str-borrow source, but mutations to the backing container (`arr[0] = "x"` where `s = Span[str](arr)`) call `mark_str_borrowers_mutated("arr")` -- missing `s`. Fix requires `mark_str_borrowers_mutated` to chase the borrow tracker's alias chain so backing-container mutations also invalidate views borrowed through spans.
- **[LOW large]** Only built-in view types (Span, Ptr) are tracked as borrows; user-type view tracking needs the field-vs-class annotation design.
- **[MED small]** Codegen-side narrowing kill misses reassignments inside non-suspending leaf compounds in resumable frames. `resumable_cfg.CFGBuilder._kill_narrowings` is applied per top-level statement in `_build_block`; an inner `if` with no suspension is appended whole to the basic block, so a reassignment of a narrowed variable inside it is never seen and later resume cases re-establish the stale narrowing binding. Sema-side fact kills (loops/handlers/finally, `InitTracker.apply_fact_kills`) don't cover this -- it's the codegen CFG's own narrowing re-establishment. Fix shape: `_kill_narrowings` should walk leaf compounds recursively (or reuse `prescan.collect_fact_kills` on the leaf statement). Surfaced by the 2026-06-10 compiler audit (gap finding on resume narrowing).
- **[LOW small]** A local that is mutable at loop entry but rebound to a readonly source inside the loop body is mutated at the top of the body with no sema rejection (iteration 2 writes through the readonly binding; C++ const backstop catches some shapes). Same single-pass back-edge hole as the narrowing/value-range family fixed by `apply_fact_kills`, but the readonly-ness lives in scope variable types, not in the `FlowFacts` families, so the kill-set mechanism doesn't cover it. Fix shape: at loop entry, re-merge scope types for body-rebound names with the documented "readonly on either branch -> readonly after" policy. Surfaced by the 2026-06-10 compiler audit.
- **[LOW small]** Definite-assignment in `finally` bodies assumes the no-exception path: a variable first assigned inside `try` is treated as definitely-assigned in `finally`, but a mid-try exception reaches the finally with it unbound (C++: default-constructed hoisted slot; CPython: NameError). Deliberately left as-is when the flow-fact kill-sets landed -- the try-vars-hoisted-into-finally pattern depends on it, and the blast radius is wrong-value reads, not memory unsafety. Fix shape: track a "maybe-unassigned on exception path" set for finally bodies and warn (or runtime-check) on reads.
- **[LOW small]** Alias-group fact invalidation misses rebound pointer-locals. `NarrowingTracker._alias_group` reads prescan's `alias_sources`, which deliberately excludes reassigned locals (they become rebindable `T*` pointer-locals, so which object they alias is flow-dependent). A mutation through such an alias (`h2 = h; ...; h2.clear()` where `h2` is later rebound elsewhere in the function) therefore does not invalidate field facts rooted at `h`. Sound handling needs flow-sensitive alias tracking (MIR places/loans); until then the static never-reassigned group is the covered subset. Surfaced implementing the flow-fact kill-sets.- **[MED small]** Field-access call arguments don't invalidate the passed object's field facts. Both the live invalidation (`NarrowingTracker.invalidate_field_facts_for_call` / `_for_method_call`) and the kill-set collector (`prescan._kills_in_expr`) add only bare `TpyName` arguments to the mutated-receivers set, so `f(h.opt)` -- passing a field by mutable reference -- leaves `h.opt.*` sub-path narrowing facts and `len(h.opt)`-derived range facts alive after a callee that mutates the object. Subscript arguments (`f(xs[i])`) share the gap and need separate handling: `_expr_to_narrowing_key` has no subscript form, so the conservative kill there is the container root. Unsoundly elides checks in the same class as the fixed loop/handler holes, but requires sub-path facts on a passed field to bite. Fix shape: extend both sites to `_expr_to_narrowing_key(arg)` (dotted paths), invalidating the key's subtree across its alias group; for subscript args, fall back to `_expr_root_name(arg.obj)` as a receiver.
- **[MED small]** Closures mutating captures without `nonlocal` are invisible to fact invalidation. `closure_written_names` / `prescan._collect_nested_def_writes` collect only `TpyNonlocal`/`TpyGlobal` rebind targets, but a nested def that mutates a captured object in place (`captured.field = None`, `captured_xs.append(...)`) declares neither -- so calling it (`f()`, no name args) invalidates nothing on the live path, and the loop/handler kill-sets inherit the same blindness. Field facts and len-derived ranges on the captured name survive the call: stale check elision, same class as the fixed `nonlocal` leg. Adjacent to the existing nested-function borrow-state entry above (that one is borrow state; this is the narrowing/range-fact leg). Fix shape: collect field-write roots and mutated receivers (not just rebind targets) from nested-def bodies into `closure_written_names`-style receiver kills.
- **[LOW small]** Calls don't invalidate narrowing on NON-VALUE module globals; currently shielded by the reassignment restriction. The VALUE-type leg is FIXED (facts for value-type module globals die at every call site, `sema/narrowing.py` `invalidate_closure_written_facts`); the non-value leg stays dormant because reassigning a non-value global from a function is rejected outright ("Cannot reassign global variable of non-value type") -- the stale fact becomes unsound only if that restriction is ever lifted, so the invalidation must widen to non-value globals in the same change. Residual precision gap: the kill is not gated on callee mutation facts (Phase-1/2 could prove a callee readonly and keep the fact), and a `Final` value-Optional global's facts are killed over-conservatively (Final cannot be rebound -- rejects-valid on a rare shape). Fix shape for both residuals: gate the kill on callee mutation facts / Final-ness.
- **[LOW medium]** Hoisted `string_view` (or `const auto&`) loop variable still references container memory after the loop; mutation post-loop dangles it.
  Hoisted non-value loop variable as view: `for s in items: ... print(s)` where `s` is `std::string_view` -- the hoisted `s` is a view into the container, not a copy. If the container is mutated between loop exit and use of `s`, the view dangles. Same concern exists with `const auto&` loop vars. Consider emitting a value copy for hoisted non-value loop variables.
  Additional route (dict-accessor aliasing work): a post-loop-used BORROW-form tuple-unpack target hoists as `T* v = nullptr;` and is assigned per iteration. On a ZERO-iteration loop (empty container) a post-loop read derefs the null pointer -- silent segfault where CPython raises NameError (verified). Same zero-iteration class as the pre-existing hoisted shapes (disengaged optional / stale value), now with a deterministic crash. A test cannot pin this until the class is fixed (UB has no stable snapshot). Proper fix: a sema definite-assignment diagnostic for post-loop reads of loop-bound names not provably assigned (loop provably entered), covering all hoisted shapes at once.
- **[LOW small]** Structural mutation warnings treat `setdefault` as invalidating while element borrows are live, producing a false positive for the existing-key case: `lst = d.setdefault(k, []); ...; d.setdefault(k2, [])` warns "Mutation of 'd' while borrowed" even though `ordered_map` nodes are heap-stable and insert never invalidates element borrows. `__setitem__` carries `@native_preserves_refs` for exactly this reason; whether `setdefault` (and other node-stable inserters) should too needs its own analysis -- the flag is a safety assertion and `setdefault`'s new `V&` return interacts with later `del`/`pop` of the same key. Surfaced by /tpy-review of the dict-accessor aliasing branch.
- **[MED medium]** Async return convention is borrow-form for pointer-repr Optional, bare reference types, and generic `-> T` (the async borrow-return ABI), but NOT yet for the pointer-variant Union sibling, which still diverges from the sync convention:
  - **Pointer-variant Union async return** (`async def -> A | B: return h.pet`): `_ret_cpp` keeps `Poll<std::variant<A,B>>`. The await-**return** ABI half is straightforward and was prototyped working: `_ret_cpp` -> `Poll<variant<A*,B*>>`, the return lifts via `to_ptr_variant` (the cluster-#9 `convert()` chokepoint), the await binding assigns (not `.emplace()`) into a bare `variant<A*,B*>` frame field, and `isinstance` narrowing uses `std::get<A*>` -- all driven by classifying the union frame-local as a ptr-variant local (`context.py` frame-local loop, mirroring the pointer-repr Optional branch). The **blocker** is the consumer side: classifying union frame-locals as ptr-variant broadly regresses a *direct* storage binding (`pet = h.pet`, union field -> local) -- the async body emits a plain `pet = h.pet` assignment with NO `to_ptr_variant` lift, where the sync var-decl emits `pet = ::tpy::to_ptr_variant(h.pet)`. Unions have a value-vs-pointer-variant binding duality that Optionals lack (a pointer-repr Optional local is uniformly `T*`), so the clean Optional precedent does not transfer. A correct fix needs EITHER (a) async-body frame-local bindings to apply form-conversion lifts for storage sources -- a general async-binding gap overlapping the deferred 2c -> THIR work (migrate async-body binding sites onto `convert()`); OR (b) classify ONLY await-result union locals as ptr-variant (thread await-bind-target info into the `context.py` classification), leaving direct-bound union locals storage. Repro: `async def get(h) -> A | B: await asyncio.sleep(0); return h.pet` then `t = await get(h); if isinstance(t, A): t.x` -- g++ `holds_alternative<A>(frame_slot<variant<A,B>>)`. Attempted under `/tpy-fix-bug` and backed out (the broad classification regressed `pet = h.pet`); the await-return ABI groundwork is understood and re-applicable once the consumer-binding approach is chosen.
  - **async-generator yield slots** and **`Task[T | None]` results** -- likely the same storage-vs-borrow convention gap on the yield / Task-payload path; unverified. **Recursive-union wrapper returns** also keep storage form (no pointer spelling for the wrapper; `async_return_form` classifies them STORAGE), so a param-rooted wrapper return copies where sync aliases -- same family, folded into the Union consumer-binding work.
  Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` for the Union consumer-binding + narrowing work. The scalar Optional fix landed on `borrow-storage-convert-chokepoint` (audit finding B41).
- **[MED medium]** A borrowed pointer-repr `T | None` loses its borrow provenance when it flows through a ternary or if/else branch-join, so a later narrowed `return` move-guts the borrowed source.
  Repro: `def first[T](xs: list[T]) -> T | None: for x in xs: return x; return None` then `def via(xs, c): y = first(xs) if c else None; if y is not None: return y; return -1`. `first` returns a borrow (`T*` into `xs`); the ternary join (`make_union`) correctly keeps the pointer-repr Optional (post-B38) but drops the call-result borrow fact, so codegen treats `y` as an owning `T | None` local and emits `return std::move((*y))` -- gutting `xs[0]`. Observable for heap-backed `T` (`int`/BigInt -> 0, `str`/`list` emptied); invisible for trivially-copyable `T` (`Int32`). The plain-assignment form (`y = first(xs)`, no join) is correct -- borrow provenance survives and no move is emitted. Same defect via the `if c: z = first(xs) else: z = None` branch-merge. CPython returns the value without disturbing the source; no TPy diagnostic. This is the B30 / cluster-#5 family (auto-move on a borrowed pointer-repr source); the join is where provenance is lost. **Workaround:** bind the borrowing call to a plain local before the conditional (`y = first(xs)`), or declare the callee return `Own[T] | None` so the value is genuinely owned. **Fix shape:** propagate the call-result (and field/element) borrow fact through ternary / branch-merge joins in the BorrowTracker, so the last-use move at `return` is gated on provable absence of a borrow -- the gate `fix-auto-move-borrow-gate` applied at direct consume sites, extended to join-merged locals. Surfaced while landing the B38 `force_pointer_repr`-join fix (cluster #9), which unmasked it (the pattern did not compile before).
- **[LOW small]** Borrow/storage chokepoint (`convert()`) residuals -- latent, surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the chokepoint branch. (a) `FormValue.move` is set nowhere (defaults False) and only read on the Optional STORAGE->BORROW path; a future caller that needs a move but forgets to set it gets a silent copy-instead-of-move -- wire it at the move-aware site or drop the field. (b) Untested suspicion: a walrus from a Union value-variant field (`if (t := h.pet) is ...:`) may hit the non-value non-Optional fallback `(name = code, *name)` which dereferences a `std::variant` directly (likely ill-formed); may be sema-blocked earlier. Whole class is reabsorbed by THIR form-as-node-fact (IR_DESIGN OQ9). (The `source_form()` TpyCoerce-peeling inconsistency that was also here is fixed -- `source_form` now peels `TpyCoerce` internally so all call sites classify the underlying source uniformly.)
- **[MED small]** `return StrView(local_str)` not caught -- explicit StrView construction from a local owned string slips through the return-dangling check.
  Both `def f() -> StrView: s = some_str(); return StrView(s)` (non-generic) and `def f[T](x: T) -> StrView: s = str(x); return StrView(s)` (generic) compile silently and produce a dangling view at runtime. The output starts with garbage bytes because `s` is destroyed at function return. The check at `compatibility.py:check_dangling_reference` covers the implicit-coercion case (returning `s` directly with declared return `StrView` is rejected) and explicit `StrView(s + "!")` constructed from a temporary, but the `_view_constructor_arg` path gates on `expr.call_type is not None` -- which is not set when `StrView(s)` is analyzed as a regular function call. The fallback `is_dangling_return(TpyCall(StrView, ...))` reaches the "regular function call -- assume safe" branch and returns False. Same gap inherited by the new lambda/yield view-dangling checks for `lambda x: StrView(local_str)` patterns where `local_str` is a named local rather than constructed inline. Fix: extend `_view_constructor_arg` to recognize `StrView(...)` / `BytesView(...)` / `Span[...](...)` calls regardless of the `call_type` setting, then check whether the constructor's argument is dangling.
- **[LOW small]** `_inherits_protocol` (sema, `tpyc/sema/type_ops.py:1215-1238`) and `directly_implements_dynamic` (codegen, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/protocols.py:225-268`) disagree on non-`@dynamic`-rooted protocol chains.
  Reproducer shape (not yet exercised by a test): `class Concrete: ...` implementing a regular protocol `NonDynProto` that extends a `@dynamic DynProto`. For `inferred[T] = Concrete` with LHS hint `Container[DynProto]`, sema's Layer 1 (`_apply_lhs_hint_to_function_return`, branch `rc-dyn-protocol`) sees `_inherits_protocol(Concrete, DynProto) = True` (walks via `is_subtype` which includes protocol-extends-protocol chains) and skips the LHS-hint switch, keeping `T = Concrete`. Codegen's `directly_implements_dynamic(Concrete, DynProto)` returns `False` (only walks user-record inheritance through `@dynamic` ancestors), so it would emit an Adapter wrap -- contradicting sema's "no wrap needed" claim. End state: either a C++ compile error or silent slicing, depending on the wrapper. Pre-existing divergence; newly reachable on the function-inference path after `rc-dyn-protocol` shipped Layer 1. **Fix shape:** align the two predicates -- either codegen learns to walk through non-`@dynamic` protocol intermediates, or sema's helper restricts to the same path codegen uses. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the `rc-dyn-protocol` branch (safety-model specialist). Note (single-alloc Rc): a third "does X C++-inherit @dynamic P's base?" predicate now exists -- `ProtocolChecker.directly_implements_dynamic` (sema, `tpyc/sema/protocols.py:151`), a hand-copied mirror of the codegen helper used to decide `representational_subst_params`. It tracks codegen today, but the lockstep surface is now four-way: the isinstance-through-protocol-borrow work added a fourth caller of the sema mirror at `tpyc/sema/calls.py` (`_validate_polymorphic_subclass_dispatch` -> `self.protocols.directly_implements_dynamic`). A fix here must keep the sema mirror and codegen copy identical while reconciling `_inherits_protocol`. The clean end-state is one shared predicate both phases call (defer to THIR, cf. the default-ctor-predicate unification in TODO.md).
- **[LOW small]** `AliasRef.is_send()` / `is_sync()` return `False` unconditionally, so a recursive union alias whose members are all `Send` (or `Sync`) reports as not-Send / not-Sync when the alias placeholder is encountered.
  `tpyc/typesys.py::AliasRef` is the parser-emitted self-reference inside a recursive alias body (`type Tree = int | list[Tree]` -- the inner `Tree`). Returning `False` matches the prior bare-`NominalType` placeholder behavior so codegen branching (`UnionType.is_value_type()`, `uses_pointer_repr()`) stays unchanged. The correct value would resolve the alias and aggregate its members, but that's a fixpoint computation. Reachable when sema asks Send/Sync of a `UnionType` member that is an `AliasRef` (or container thereof, e.g. `list[Tree]`). Latent: no current test exercises a recursive union across a thread boundary where rejection would be incorrect. **Fix shape:** treat the alias body's resolved Send/Sync as a least-fixed-point over members, breaking the recursion conservatively (e.g. assume `True` during own resolution and refine if a member proves `False`); or resolve the AliasRef via the registry at the question site. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the union/variant unification.
- **[LOW small]** Async coro frame holds `Counter& __self` bound to `*__for_itr_0` where `__for_itr_0` is an `std::optional<Counter>` frame field; the reference's validity depends on the optional not being reset/reassigned between emplace calls.
  `tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_async.py` emits the for-loop iterable as a `std::optional<T>` slot (loop-source storage) and a `T&` field for the sub-coroutine's iterator binding. Current emission only `emplace()`s the optional once per loop entry, so the reference stays valid for the loop's lifetime; however, the invariant "optional not reset between emplace and final read" is not enforced anywhere. Any future change that resets `__for_itr_N` on loop restart (e.g. a `continue`-to-top rewrite, or a generator iterable that re-yields) would silently dangle every pending `T&` capture in the sub-coro frame. **Fix shape:** either store the iterable by value-with-fixed-address (e.g. `T` directly in a frame field) so no dependent reference can ever dangle, or add an invariant assertion at every reset site that no sub-coro is currently parked on this iterable. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` of the async-control-flow branch.
- **[MED medium]** Post-`is None` isinstance narrowing on a recursive-union `T | None` parameter produces wrong C++.
  Repro: `type Tree = int | list[Tree]; def f(t: Tree | None) -> int: if t is None: return -1; if isinstance(t, int): return t; return len(t)`. Sema correctly narrows `t` to `list[Tree]` for the post-`isinstance(t, int)` early-return path, but `_filter_union_codegen_facts` in `tpyc/sema/statements.py` filters the fact out because the declared type post-`_fix_recursive_optional_annotations` is `OptionalType(AliasRef('Tree'))` -- not a UnionType for the existing `isinstance(_peel(declared), (UnionType, AnyType))` check. Even when the filter is loosened (peel OptionalType(AliasRef) -> alias body), the downstream codegen at `_emit_isinstance_extractions` substitutes `narrowed_vars[t] = '__t'` while the variable was originally rendered as `Tree* t` (pointer-form Optional), so reads of `t` continue to emit the `(*X)` deref pattern -- producing `(*__t)[0]` and `__len__((*t))` where `__t` is a std::vector and `t` is a struct, both ill-formed. **Both layers** need a coordinated fix: (1) `_peel` in `_filter_union_codegen_facts` should resolve `OptionalType(AliasRef)` to its alias body so the fact survives the filter; (2) `_emit_isinstance_extractions` should detect the pointer-form-Optional input shape (`var_name` is a `Tree*` param) and bind the alias as a value reference (`auto& __t = std::get<list<Tree>>(t->value);`) rather than as a wrapper-struct alias that the caller then re-derefs. **Workaround today:** introduce a local copy of the variable after the `is None` check (`tree = t  # mypy: assert-not-none`) and isinstance-narrow that local; the local's storage form is the wrapper struct directly, not Tree*. Surfaced by the union/variant unification's attempt to add coverage for this shape.
- **[LOW small]** Inner `__finally_exc_<n>` slot can stay stale across CFG-finally re-entry when the inner finally body itself raises. The slot is set by the inner TryRegion's catch when the try body raises; it is cleared by inner `AsyncFinallyExit`'s rethrow / forwarding code. But if the inner finally body raises a NEW exception *before* reaching AsyncFinallyExit (the raise propagates directly to the outer catch via the C++ stack), inner's AsyncFinallyExit never runs and inner's slot stays populated with the original exception. Today the inner is exited and the coro continues to the outer finally, so the stale field is never re-read -- but a future re-entry to the same inner try (e.g. inside a loop) would observe stale state. Fix when triggered: clear `__finally_exc_<n>` at inner FinallyRegion entry, OR at the C++ catch that captures the new exception in an outer region. Latent today; not introduced by H3, but surfaced during the design pass.
- **[LOW small]** `@dataclass` `__post_init__` runs **twice** when a child dataclass overrides a parent dataclass's `__post_init__`. Reproducer: `Base` and `Child(Base)` both define `__post_init__`; `Child(...)` runs `Base.__post_init__` (via the `super().__init__()` chain in the synthesized init) *and* `Child.__post_init__` (appended to `Child`'s own init), whereas CPython runs only `Child.__post_init__` once. Root cause: TPy's dataclass `__init__` chains `super().__init__()` and uses static dispatch, so a parent's appended hook call always fires; CPython flattens fields into one `__init__` with no super chain. The override is already flagged by the existing "Method 'Child.__post_init__' hides 'Base.__post_init__'" warning, so it is not fully silent. Single-hook and inherited-hook (child without its own) cases are correct. **Fix shape:** suppress the parent's appended `__post_init__` call when a descendant re-declares it, or flatten dataclass field-init like CPython instead of chaining `super().__init__()`. Surfaced fixing the `__post_init__`-ignored bug.
- **[LOW small]** `@function_macro` ignores `MACRO_DEPS`: `_populate_macro_deps` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) has class-macro (`all_records()`/`pending_macros`) and builder-macro branches but no `module.functions` walk, so a function-macro module declaring `MACRO_DEPS` does not get its deps folded into `macro_ns` -- synthesized code referencing a dep symbol would fail resolution. Latent: no current function macro uses `MACRO_DEPS`. **Fix shape:** add a `module.functions` branch mirroring the class/builder paths. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `@function_macro` is not recognized through a re-export chain: there is no `SymbolKind.FUNCTION_MACRO`, and `_resolve_macro_chain` / `_MACRO_KINDS` (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`, `tpyc/symbol_binding.py`) enumerate only CLASS/CALL/BUILDER macro kinds. A `@function_macro` imported via an intermediate re-exporting module is not installed as a macro binding, and `_resolve_macro_chain`'s registry probe omits `get_function_macro`, so the lookup silently fails. Latent: the tests import the macro directly. **Fix shape:** add `FUNCTION_MACRO` to `SymbolKind`/`_MACRO_KINDS` and the registry probe. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `FunctionMacroContext.replace_expr` (`tpyc/macro_api.py`) has no guard against reusing one `new` node instance for two distinct `old` nodes; sema then annotates the shared AST object with conflicting coercion/type facts from two call sites (last write wins), a silent miscompile path. No current macro does this. **Fix shape:** deep-copy `new` per replacement, or reject a node already spliced elsewhere. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small]** `FunctionMacroContext.annotate_local` via the `TypeInfo.raw_type` escape hatch can set a local's declared type to a sema-internal `RefType`: `_analyze_var_decl` rejects `OwnType`/`ReadonlyType` on locals but has no `RefType` guard, so a macro could inject a `RefType`-annotated local that bypasses the borrow-form/storage-form distinction. Latent: no current macro extracts `raw_type`. **Fix shape:** reject `RefType` (and audit other sema-internal wrappers) in `_analyze_var_decl` alongside `OwnType`/`ReadonlyType`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the `@function_macro` spike.
- **[LOW small] A compile-time-only call (`assert_send[T]()` / `assert_sync[T]()`) used in a value position emits an undeclared C++ identifier instead of a clean sema rejection.** The codegen elision (`TpyCall.compile_time_assert`) only fires in the `TpyExprStmt` arm, and sema doesn't reject a `VOID`-typed call result in a value position, so `x = assert_send[T]()` reaches expression codegen and emits a call to a nonexistent C++ function -> g++ error. Toolchain-caught (not silent UB), and the asserts are statement-only by intent so this is misuse. Pre-existing `VOID`-in-value-position gap shared with `print()` (but `print` emits valid C++). **Fix shape:** reject a compile-time-assert call (or any `VOID` result) used as a value in sema, with a clear "statement-only" message. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the Send/Sync Phase 3 diagnostic surface.
- **[LOW small] `re.sub` / `Pattern.sub` `count` is typed `Int32`, narrower than CPython's arbitrary-precision `int`.** A `count` above `Int32.MAX` (2147483647) would overflow where CPython accepts it. Practically unreachable (nobody passes 2B+ replacements), but the signature diverges. **Fix shape:** widen `count` to `int` (BigInt) or document the cap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the re.sub count feature.
- **[LOW small] An owned-`String` (`tpy.String`, param `const std::string&`) overload param has the same string-literal mis-rank as `str`, but the str-literal pin excludes it.** The pin in `gen_call_arg` (`_wants_str_literal_pin` + the `is_str_type`/`is_str_view_type` guard) only fires for `str`/`StrView` params (C++ `std::string_view`); a `String` param renders as `const std::string&`, where a bare `"x"` literal (`const char*`) still has `const char*->bool` outranking the user-defined `->std::string`, so an overloaded callee with a `String` param and a competing `bool`/pointer overload would silently mis-bind. Excluded deliberately because `std::string_view("x")` does not implicitly convert to `const std::string&`. **Fix shape:** for a `String` (owned) param, pin to `std::string("x")` instead of the view form. Unverified whether any current code uses a `String`-typed overload param. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness) of the str-literal overload pin.
- **[LOW small, perf] A `native_global` constant reads through an extern global at each use instead of an immediate.** A `Final[T] = native_global("sym", binding="C")` lowers every use site to a load of the extern symbol (`::sym`), where a plain `Final[T] = <literal>` would be a compile-time constant the C++ compiler folds into an immediate. The socket/errno constants in `socket_impl.cpp` (`tpy_const_sol_socket = SOL_SOCKET`, etc.) thus cost a memory load per use (e.g. in `setsockopt`/`getsockopt`/errno comparisons) rather than an immediate -- a tiny but real regression vs the previous hardcoded `Final` literals. Inherent to native_global's cross-TU model: the value comes from a system macro visible only in `socket_impl.cpp` (the header is deliberately hidden from consumer TUs), so the consumer can't see it at compile time. **Fix shapes (any):** (a) LTO would const-propagate the global-initialized-from-constant across TUs; (b) mark the C++ definitions `const`/`constexpr` and teach native_global to emit a matching `extern const` decl, enabling more aggressive folding; (c) for non-cross-compilation builds, let tpyc query the host's constant values and emit literals (wrong for cross-compilation, so gated). Surfaced by the macOS socket-constant portability (`feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`). Optimization, not a defect.
- **[LOW small] The macOS self-pipe / kqueue wake-fd setup ignores `fcntl` failures (review-only `#else` branch).** In `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/signal_impl.cpp` `create_wake_fds()` (macOS branch) the `fcntl(F_SETFL, O_NONBLOCK)` and `fcntl(F_SETFD, FD_CLOEXEC)` return values on the self-pipe ends are unchecked; likewise the kqueue fd's `fcntl(F_SETFD, FD_CLOEXEC)` in `epoll_impl.cpp` `tpy_epoll_create()`. Two consequences if a (rare) `fcntl` fails: a blocking write end means `on_shutdown_signal` could `write()`-block in the signal handler (deadlock risk if the pipe ever fills); a missed CLOEXEC leaks the fd across `exec`. The Linux path is immune (`eventfd(EFD_CLOEXEC|EFD_NONBLOCK)` / `epoll_create1(EPOLL_CLOEXEC)` set both atomically). **Fix shape:** in `create_wake_fds()` check every `fcntl` and fail (close fds, return -1) on error; do the same for the kqueue CLOEXEC set. NOTE: the macOS `#else` branches do not compile on Linux CI, so this must be edited + built on a macOS box (or with libkqueue), not blind-patched here. Surfaced by /tpy-review (runtime-cpp-correctness, safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`.
- **[LOW small] `tpy_signal_consume`'s `close_wake_fds()` zeroes the wake-fd globals after `close()`, not before (review-relevant for future threading).** In `runtime/cpp/src/stdlib/signal_impl.cpp`, `close_wake_fds()` copies `g_wake_rd`/`g_wake_wr` into locals, `close()`s them, then zeroes the globals -- leaving a window where the still-pending signal handler reads a just-closed (potentially recycled) fd from `g_wake_wr`. Harmless under today's single-threaded asyncio (the handler runs synchronously on the same thread, never during teardown), but a real hazard if the reactor ever becomes thread-aware. Fix shape: zero the globals before the `close()` calls. Same review-only `#else`-adjacent file as the entry above; fold the fix in when that file is next edited on macOS. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`.
- **[LOW small] A `native_global` `Final` is not admitted into `analyzed_finals`, so it can't seed a later `Final` initializer.** In `tpyc/sema/statements.py` the `native_global` branch (~line 3451) sets `stmt.linkage`/`native_name`, clears `stmt.init`, and `return`s before the `analyzed_finals.add(stmt.name)` at ~line 3578. The name is registered in `final_globals` (so reassignment is blocked) but not in `analyzed_finals`, so a subsequent `X: Final[Int32] = SOL_SOCKET + 1` in the same module would be rejected as "requires a compile-time constant initializer" even though `SOL_SOCKET` is a `Final`. Pre-existing (`statements.py` not touched by `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`); no current code composes a native_global into another Final. Inconsistent invariant: a native_global Final isn't a compile-time constant in TPy's model, so admitting it into `analyzed_finals` would also let it leak into genuinely compile-time contexts (array sizes) where it can't be folded -- so the right fix may be to keep it out AND give a clearer diagnostic, not just add it. Fix shape: decide the intended semantics, then make `final_globals`/`analyzed_finals`/the array-size check agree. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `feat-macos-kqueue-reactor`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A module-level `Final[str]` initialized by string concatenation of compile-time constants is rejected as "requires a compile-time constant initializer."** `X: Final[str] = "lit" + str(other_final)` (e.g. building a version string from `tpy.version.version_info` components) does not fold, even though every operand is constant -- `str()` and `+` on strings aren't evaluated at Final-init time. Consequence: the value must be recomputed at each use site instead of being hoisted to a single module constant (`_prepare_headers` in `lib/tpy/tplib/requests.py` builds the default `User-Agent` per request for exactly this reason -- see the TODO there). Sibling of the `native_global`-Final const-fold gap above and the Final-initializer const-eval entries. Negligible cost today (tiny strings), but a real missing const-fold capability. Fix shape: const-fold string `+`/`str()` over constant operands in the Final-initializer evaluator. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) `io.BufferedReader`'s closed-file `ValueError` message diverges from CPython's per-operation text.** A closed `BufferedReader` read raises `"I/O operation on closed file."` (BufferedReader/BytesIO `_check_open` in `lib/tpy/io.py`), but CPython's `BufferedReader` raises `"read of closed file"` / `"readline of closed file"` (per-op). The io module's actual pattern is to mirror each type's CPython message (our `FileIO` and `StringIO` already match CPython's `"I/O operation on closed file"` exactly, no period; `BytesIO` matches CPython's period-suffixed form) -- so `BufferedReader` is the outlier, not a deliberate convention. Type is correct (`ValueError`) and the cpy phase catches by type, so this is observable only via `str(exc)`/`.args`. Fix shape: give `BufferedReader._check_open` per-operation messages (`read`/`readline`) matching CPython. Surfaced in the `/tpy-ready` retrospective for `io.BufferedReader`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) `print`/`str`/`repr` of a `bytes` value nested in a container renders it as a list of ints instead of `b'...'`.** `xs: list[bytes] = [b"p\n", b"q\n"]; print(xs)` outputs `[[112, 10], [113, 10]]`, CPython outputs `[b'p\n', b'q\n']`. A top-level `bytes` prints correctly (`print(b"p\n")` -> `b'p\n'` in both); only the container-element repr path renders bytes as its int-sequence view rather than the `b'...'` repr. Silent wrong output. Pre-existing, not specific to any one container -- the element-repr dispatch for `bytes` inside `list`/`dict`/`set`/`tuple` is missing the `b'...'` repr. Surfaced building `io.BufferedReader` (`readlines()` returns `list[bytes]`; the test prints elements individually to avoid it). Reproducer `/tmp/agents/bufrd/probe_listbytes.py`. Fix shape: route container-element rendering of `bytes` through the same `b'...'` repr used at top level. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] (rejects valid code) A record in the ENTRY module sharing an imported enum's bare name makes the enum's OWN module header reference `tpyapp::__main__`.** A module `m` declaring `class Grade(IntEnum)` plus `def rank(g: Grade = Grade.HIGH)`, imported by an entry module that happens to declare an unrelated `class Grade`, emits `tpyapp::__main__::Grade::HIGH` into `m.hpp`'s own declaration and fails the C++ build (`'tpyapp::__main__' has not been declared`). The bare-name enum slot (`TypeRegistry.enums`) is resolved against the entry module's shadow while generating a DIFFERENT module's header, and the sema module id then leaks where the structural namespace belongs -- the same `__main__`-vs-structural-name trap `codegen_cpp/functions.py::default_to_cpp`'s local-Final arm documents. Needs no materialized default: a plain declaration-side enum-member default suffices. Loud (toolchain-caught). Pre-existing -- the enum render arm is untouched by the positional-defaults fix. Repro `/tmp/agents/eshadow/`. Fix shape: resolve the enum through `find_enum_by_qname` against the module being generated rather than the bare `enums` slot, and map the module id through the structural-name mapping. Surfaced writing a classmethod regression case with a deliberate name shadow. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] (rejects valid code) A user coroutine named `run` in the ENTRY module emits `::tpyapp::__main__::run<...>`, which does not compile.** `async def run() -> None` plus `asyncio.run(run())` in a single-file program fails the C++ build with `'tpyapp::__main__' has not been declared`: the `create_task`/adapter call path qualifies the user coroutine with the SEMA module id (`__main__` for the entry point) where the C++ namespace is the structural name (`tpyapp::main`) -- the same trap `codegen_cpp/functions.py::default_to_cpp`'s local-Final arm documents and avoids. Renaming the coroutine (or moving it out of the entry module) works, so it is the entry-module id specifically, not the name `run` colliding with `asyncio.run`. Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Repro `/tmp/agents/reg/runname.py`. Fix shape: route that qualification through the same structural-name mapping the rest of codegen uses instead of the raw sema module id. Surfaced writing an async regression case for the positional-defaults fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (silent wrong value) A `**kwargs: Unpack[TypedDict]` field default that is a bare name or enum member resolves in the CALLER's scope, not the TypedDict's module.** `_build_td_call` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`, used by both the free-function and method kwargs-packing paths) pads omitted TypedDict field defaults with a raw `dc_replace(fld.default_expr, loc=...)` and never stamps the declaring module, so a cross-module field default naming a `Final` constant or enum member either fails to resolve or silently binds a same-named symbol in the caller. Same defect class as the parameter-default scoping the positional-defaults fix closed; this one path was left out because it pads TypedDict FIELDS rather than params. Sits inside an already self-disclosed divergence (the parser warns that a TypedDict field default is ignored by CPython at runtime), so reachability is low. Fix shape: route the pad through the same `default_scope_module` resolver the param paths use, with the TypedDict record's declaring module. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the positional-defaults fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) An explicit `return None` in a `-> None` function emits `return nullptr;` into a void C++ function.** `def f() -> None: return None` fails the build with "return-statement with a value, in function returning 'void'"; the bare `return` and an implicit fall-off both emit correctly, so only the explicit `None` operand breaks. The return-value emit renders the `None` literal as a pointer-form null instead of recognizing that a void return takes no operand at all. `return None` is ordinary Python (and how a `__exit__` that declines to suppress is often spelled), so this is squarely rejects-valid; toolchain-caught, not silent. Repro `/tmp/agents/mm/return_none_void.py` (both spellings side by side). Fix shape: at a return site whose function returns void, emit a bare `return;` for a `None` operand rather than routing it through the value render. Surfaced writing a context manager for the capture/default-scope probe during `/tpy-merge-master`; confirmed pre-existing on both sides of that merge. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) An `Own[Rec | None]` / `Own[A | B | None]` in a RETURN or PARAM position is null-checked as if it were a pointer, so `is None` and `isinstance` narrowing fail the C++ build.** `Own` forces the owning storage form (`std::optional<Rec>` / `std::variant<monostate, A, B>`), but the None test still emits the pointer-form comparison: `def keep(r: Own[Rec | None]) -> Own[Rec | None]` with `keep(None) is None` emits `... == nullptr` against `std::optional<Rec>` ("no match for operator=="), and the union sibling additionally reads a member straight off the variant (`u.w` on `std::variant<monostate, Other, Rec>`) because the `isinstance` narrowing never unwraps the alternative. Independent of defaults -- both reproduce with no default on the param (controls `/tmp/agents/rev4/own_ret_isnone.py`, `/tmp/agents/rev4/own_union_nodefault.py`). The bare (non-`Own`) `Rec | None` param is pointer-repr and works, which is why this stayed hidden: `Own` is the only spelling that flips the representation without flipping the null check. Same borrow-vs-storage-form family as the owned value-variant move entry below. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Fix shape: route the None test and the narrowing extraction through the slot's actual form (`has_value()` / `holds_alternative` + `std::get`) rather than assuming pointer repr whenever the declared type is an optional/union. Surfaced writing the `Own[Rec | None]` default regression test during /tpy-review of the positional-defaults fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (rejects-valid, loud) A record METHOD or ctor whose value-form optional/union default names a record declared LATER in the same module still fails the C++ build.** `class Holder: def m(self, f: Fixed | None = None)` where `Fixed` is defined after `Holder` emits `std::optional<Fixed> f = std::nullopt` at the in-class declaration, where `Fixed` is only forward-declared -- "template argument must be a complete class". The free-function form of this is fixed (a free declaration precedes every record, so its default is always suppressed and filled at the call site), and a member whose alternatives are defined EARLIER is correct and deliberately keeps its C++ default. This forward-referenced member is the residual: `default_emittable_at` (`tpyc/typesys.py`) answers per-signature and cannot see record emission order, which is decided later in codegen. Toolchain-caught, not silent. Fix shape: either make the record emit order a fact available where the default decision is made, or suppress a member default whose type names a record not yet complete at that point (needs the emit-order dependency the by-value-field ordering entries above also want). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model, architecture-fit, docs-sync all found source comments citing a BUGS.md entry for this that did not exist). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[HIGH] (silent wrong value) A materialized parameter default that names a module-level constant or enum member resolves in the CALLER's scope, not the module the default was WRITTEN in.** `def f(a, b=K, c=0)` with `K: Final` in module M, called `f(1, c=9)` from another module (so `b`'s default must be filled at the call site), errors `Undefined variable: 'K'`; where the caller happens to define its own `K`, it silently binds that instead. Applies to every callable kind that materializes a default -- free functions, methods, constructors (including an INHERITED `__init__`, whose `init_params` are copied from the ancestor with no per-item provenance), classmethods, and methods reached through a generic-class instantiation. CPython binds a default in its defining scope, confirmed by probe against a shadowing caller. A `defining_module` stamp on the filled `TpyName` was built and REVERTED: the stamp itself is straightforward, but the provenance it must read is lossy in at least three independent ways -- `substitute_method_type_params` rebuilds a monomorphized `FunctionInfo` without `originating_module`; `RecordInfo` carries no per-init-param origin for an inherited ctor; and the parser's default grammar nests a bare name or enum member inside a fixed-int constructor (`Int64(WIDTH)`), which a top-level stamp does not descend into. Each was a separate silent-wrong-value defect. Fix shape: make the provenance a first-class fact rather than something reconstructed per callable kind -- carry the owning module on the default itself (or on `ParamInfo`) at registration, where it is known once, so no fill site has to derive it. **Raised MED -> HIGH** because the positional-defaults fix made the `default before a required keyword-only` shape COMPILE where it was previously a hard C++ build failure, so a build error became a silently wrong value -- the same loud-to-silent argument that raised the tuple-global entry. **Reachability is wider than the cross-module framing above suggests: a SAME-MODULE `as`-capture shadows it too.** `K: Final = 7` + `def f(a, b=K, *, c)` called inside `with CM() as K:` returns TPy `1092` vs CPython `172` -- the fill binds the with-target, not the module constant, so no second module and no deliberate shadowing are needed, just a common local name. Repros `/tmp/agents/posdef/xmod/` (cross-module) and `/tmp/agents/mm/capture_default.py` (same-module capture; confirmed pre-existing on both sides of the master merge that introduced capture-name resolution, so it is this entry's defect and not that change's). **Same root as the entry-module enum bare-name entry below, and they should be fixed together:** codegen's `default_to_cpp` resolves an enum default through `registry.get_enum(expr.obj.name)` and sema's `check_enum_member_default` does the identical bare-name lookup, so `enum_member_of` is never stamped on a default node for codegen to read. Both entries are the same defect wearing two symptoms -- a default's name provenance is re-derived at consumption instead of recorded at declaration -- so the first-class-fact fix above closes the enum one too. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] (silent CPython divergence) Keyword arguments supplied OUT OF ORDER are evaluated right-to-left after the first, not in written order.** `f(c=side(3), a=side(1), b=side(2))` with each `side` appending to a shared list logs `3, 2, 1`; CPython logs `3, 1, 2`. CPython evaluates arguments strictly left-to-right AS WRITTEN at the call site, including keywords; TPy's `resolve_kwargs` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) rebuilds a positional list in PARAMETER order, so the emitted C++ argument list carries the kwargs permuted -- and the C++ evaluation order for the remainder then lands right-to-left on GCC/Clang. Only observable when two or more out-of-order kwargs have side effects; value-correct in every case (each kwarg still reaches its own parameter). Silent, no warning, no `no_cpython.txt`. Sibling of the two-operand binary right-to-left entry above -- same underlying cause, that TPy does not pin C++ evaluation order where CPython specifies it. Fix shape: hoist out-of-order kwarg argument expressions into call-site temporaries in WRITTEN order before the call, so the permutation into parameter order moves already-evaluated values. Reproducer `/tmp/agents/posdef/kworder.py`. Pre-existing; surfaced by the cpython-parity assessment of the positional-defaults fix, which touches the same function but does not cause this. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] (rejects valid code) A default declared on a `Protocol` method or a NESTED `def` is silently DROPPED, so a call omitting that argument fails.** `class P(Protocol): def go(self, n: Int32 = 7) -> Int32: ...` with a conforming `def go(self, n: Int32)` on the impl: a generic `def use[T: P](p: T)` calling `p.go()` emits `p.go()` against `int32_t Impl::go(int32_t n) const` and dies in the C++ build (`candidate expects 1 argument, 0 provided`). The protocol declares the default, the implementation does not repeat it, and monomorphization binds the impl's signature -- so the declared default reaches nothing. Either the protocol's default should be materialized at the monomorphized call site, or declaring one on a protocol method should be rejected outright (the surface is a signature, not a definition). Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. Pre-existing; reproduces with no relation to the parameter-default type check. Repro `/tmp/agents/defck/protodrop.py`. The nested-`def` sibling has the same symptom by a slightly different route: `def outer(): def inner(n: Int32 = None) -> Int32: ...; return inner()` errors "'inner' expects 1 argument(s), got 0" because `statements.py`'s nested-def `ParamInfo` construction omits `default_expr` entirely, where the protocol path carries it into `ParamInfo` and loses it at monomorphization. Both are also the only `ParamInfo`-building sites the param-default TYPE check is not wired into (TODO.md) -- a default that never reaches codegen cannot be type-checked meaningfully either, so the two gaps close together. Repro `/tmp/agents/defck/protodrop.py` and `/tmp/agents/defck/nested.py`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity) An integer literal bound to a `float` parameter prints as a float, where CPython prints an int.** `def f(x: float = 1) -> float: return x` with `f()` prints `1.0`; CPython prints `1`. Same for an explicit int argument (`f(2)` -> `2.0` vs `2`), so this is the general annotation-enforcement stance rather than anything about defaults: TPy treats `float` as a type and widens the int at the boundary, CPython treats the annotation as a hint and leaves an `int`. Silent output divergence, no warning and no `no_cpython.txt`. Sibling of the Any-narrowed-float entry above. Not currently noted in `docs/LANGUAGE_FEATURES.md`, whose float section documents the assignment conversion but not the parameter/print consequence. Surfaced writing the parameter-default happy case (`tests/cases/defaults/param_default_types`), whose `as_float` returns `x + 0.5` specifically so the case asserts a value rather than a representation. Fix shape: decide whether to warn at the int-literal-into-float boundary, or document it as an acknowledged divergence in LANGUAGE_FEATURES. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (rejects valid code) A `Final[T]` module constant is legal as a PARAMETER default but rejected as a FIELD default, and the field-side message also omits enum members the field path accepts.** `STEP: Final[Int32] = 7` then `class Box: n: Int32 = STEP` errors "Default field value must be a constant expression (literal, None, or fixed-int constructor like Int32(5))", while `def f(n: Int32 = STEP)` compiles -- the two default surfaces are otherwise kept deliberately consistent (they share `check_enum_member_default` and now `check_default_value_type`). The same message also fails to list enum members, which `_validate_const_field_default` does accept via `check_enum_member_default`, so a user who hits it is told less than the truth. `expr_to_cpp_default` (macro_api.py) is the field path's shape gate and has no `TpyName` arm, unlike the parser's `_validate_const_default`. Fix shape: give the field path the same Final-name arm the parameter path has (deferred binding resolution, as `_validate_named_defaults` does), and list enum members in the message. Surfaced by the /tpy-ready second opinion of the parameter-default type check. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] In some cross-module contexts, `make_adapter<P, Conformer>` for a STRUCTURALLY-conforming `@dynamic` protocol emits an incomplete `Adapter<P, Conformer>` C++ type (`invalid application of 'sizeof' to incomplete type`, via `std::make_unique`).** Hit when the `stdlib/https_client` test built a `Box[_Connection]` over a named-local `HTTPSConnection` whose `_tls: ssl.SSLSocket | None` had a live SSLSocket injected, in a TU that also did `import socket` / `import ssl` / `from ssl import SSLSocket`. NARROW and not fully isolated -- the failure does NOT reproduce in reduced forms: a bare `http.client`-only `Box[_Connection]` over HTTPSConnection compiles as both an rvalue (`Box(HTTPSConnection(...))`) AND a named local (`conn = ...; Box(conn)`); adding `import ssl` to that module still compiles; and minimal cross-module `Box[P]` over simpler conformers (Own-return method, two conformers, an `Optional[@nocopy]` field from a third module) all compile (`/tmp/agents/ssl/{boxlocal,box_withssl,boxinherit2}.py`, `xbox/`). So the trigger is some interaction in the fuller context -- prime suspects: `from ssl import SSLSocket` materializing the type, and/or a live SSLSocket value in the `_tls` Optional, perturbing cross-module Adapter emit ordering -- not simply "any cross-module Box over HTTPSConnection" (my first characterization of this entry was wrong on that point). Loud (toolchain-caught), not silent. **This is a STRUCTURAL-conformance (Adapter-path) bug only.** NOMINAL conformance (`class HTTPSConnection(_Connection)`) stores the conformer directly as the protocol base -- no `Adapter` instantiated -- so it does not hit this; the SSL/HTTPS track's Increment 5 is taking the nominal-inheritance direction (decided with the user), which sidesteps this path entirely, so this bug does NOT gate Increment 5. It remains a latent codegen defect for the general structural-`Box[@dynamic]`-cross-module case. Fix shape: cross-module `make_adapter<Base, Conformer>` must force the full conformer definition (+ transitive field types) complete in the using TU, or emit the `Adapter` specialization in the conformer's defining module -- needs an Explore pass over the @dynamic Adapter emit/ordering, and a minimized repro first. Surfaced by /tpy-review of HTTPSConnection (Increment 4); re-pinned during the post-merge conformance discussion. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` + Explore survey.
- **[MED small] (parity) A mutated `Span[T]` `@export` param triggers the copy-boundary-mutation warning only for a DIRECT mutation in the function's own body -- mutation via a helper call is silently missed.** `def helper(ys: Span[Int32]): ys[0] = 9` then `@export def f(xs: Span[Int32]): helper(xs)` gets NO warning, even though `helper`'s mutation genuinely aliases through the call (verified: a plain TPy program shows `f`'s buffer actually changes) and the boundary still copies in, so the divergence (mutation invisible to the Python caller) is real and unwarned. Root cause: `tpyc/compiler.py`'s `_warn_export_copy_boundary_mutation` reads `FunctionInfo.mutated_params`, which for `Span[T]` is populated only from direct-mutation facts -- `mutation_propagation.py`'s call-edge propagation (Phase 2) is gated by `typesys.param_has_mutable_borrow_surface`, which returns `False` for `Span[T]` because it is a value type (`is_value_type() == True`), so a callee's mutation of its own `Span[T]` param never propagates into the caller's `mutated_params` the way it does for `list`/`dict`/`set` (excluded from that same gate because they are NOT value types). The direct-mutation case (element assignment/`.sort()` inside the `@export` function's own body) warns correctly; only the transitive (through-a-helper-call) case is missed. Silent divergence, no diagnostic. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the Span[T] buffer-protocol marshalling rung (`interop-span-buffer`). Fix shape: touches shared Phase-2 propagation infrastructure (`param_has_mutable_borrow_surface`) used beyond just this warning, so changing it needs real impact analysis, not a symptom patch -- needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
