- **[LOW small] (rejects valid code) Definite-return analysis does not treat an or-pattern with a wildcard alternative as always-matching.** A match whose final arm is `case X(...) | _:` (every arm returns) still errors "can reach the end of the function without returning a value", forcing a dead trailing `return` -- the program is valid Python (and valid by TPy's own semantics: the wildcard alternative makes the arm irrefutable, so the match cannot fall through). Loud (compile error), not silent. Root cause: the match termination/exhaustiveness fold treats only a bare wildcard/capture arm as always-matching; an or-pattern containing a wildcard alternative is not folded. Cross-ref THIR: the routed record tier (`_match_record_arm_always`) DOES fold this shape, so THIR-routed hosts merely carry the dead trailing return the sema check demands -- fixing this changes no emitted code for them, only removes the spurious requirement. Fix shape: an or-pattern is irrefutable iff any alternative is; fold that into the match termination analysis. Surfaced writing the record or-wildcard-alternative unit during /tpy-review of the thir-compr-rock branch.
- **[LOW small] (rejects valid code / diagnostic inconsistency) sema's unreachable-arm check does not unwrap an `as`-wrapped literal field sub-pattern.** `case Point(x=(0 as v)):` -- `_is_constraining_sub_pattern` (`tpyc/sema/match.py`) sees the `as` wrapper, not the literal inside, classifies the arm always-matching, and any later arm errors "unreachable case after wildcard pattern" -- but codegen DOES emit the `x == 0` field condition, so the rejected program would behave correctly. Loud (spurious sema error). Fix shape: unwrap as-patterns before the literal/constraining classification (and audit the three `_is_constraining_sub_pattern` call sites for the same blindness). Surfaced by the THIR record-pattern cell (thir-match-record); the as-wrapped field sub-pattern row is wholly deferred on the THIR side, so the gate never admits the shape.

- **[MED, pre-existing, latent] A non-generic self-referential record whose Send/Sync answer depends on its own answer is mis-derived as NOT Send/Sync -- rejects valid code with a circular diagnostic.** `class Node: kids: list[Node]` (or the flagship `class Node: next: Arc[Node] | None`) makes `assert_send[Node]()` / `assert_sync[Node]()` fail with "Node is not Send ... because Node lacks Send". Root cause: registration (`tpyc/sema/registration.py`, `is_send = all(f.type.is_send() ...)`) computes the record's cached `is_send`/`is_sync` by walking fields **before** the result is assigned, so a field whose Send-ness recurses back into the same record reads the not-yet-set `RecordInfo.is_send`/`is_sync` dataclass default (`False`). The greatest-fixed-point re-entrancy guard (`_evaluating_send`/`_evaluating_sync` in `NominalType.is_send`/`is_sync`) covers only the *generic*-record per-instantiation re-walk branch, not this non-generic cached-bool path. Pre-existing (reproduces at the merge-base via `list[Node]`, no Arc/conditional-override code involved); `Arc[T]`'s conditional override just makes it reachable through the common `Arc<Node>` graph/tree pattern (`Rc[Node]` never hit it -- Rc's own raw-pointer fields make it unconditionally non-Send, so its `is_send` short-circuits without recursing into the payload). Fails safe (rejects valid code; no miscompile/UB). Fix must span {generic, non-generic} x {is_send, is_sync} -- extend the fixpoint guard (or defer the cached-bool computation) so a self-reference resolves to the greatest fixed point -- and land a regression test covering BOTH the `list[Node]` and `Arc[Node]` shapes; a narrow patch at the conditional-override site alone would leave `list[Node]` broken. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the Threading V2 Arc[T] branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.

- **[LOW small] Sema-emitted warnings are dropped from the diagnostic output when a later validation error aborts the compile.** Analyzer warnings are collected per module during body analysis, but a `CompileError` raised by a post-sema validation pass (e.g. `the ext-module validator (tpyc/interop/module_validators.py)`) aborts before those warnings are surfaced, so the output holds only the error -- a warning that fired on an earlier line silently vanishes. Pipeline-wide and pre-existing (another entry in this file already notes an error "masking any earlier warnings" in passing). Practical bite: a test case cannot pin a `# tpyc: warning(...)` alongside a later error (hit writing `error_export_class_property_class_setter` -- the getter borrow-return warning vanished once the setter error fired). Fix shape: flush already-collected analyzer warnings into the diagnostic output before (or alongside) a post-sema CompileError. Surfaced by /tpy-review (convention-compliance, docs-sync) of the exposed-class @property rung.


- **[MED small] (silent CPython divergence) `**kwargs: Unpack[TypedDict]` call sites evaluate keyword-argument EXPRESSIONS in TypedDict field-declaration order, not call-site order.** Sema's kwargs-pack rewrite (`_pack_kwargs_into_typed_dict` / `_build_td_call`, `tpyc/sema/calls.py:659-753`) reorders the kwargs into field order BEFORE codegen, so `connect(port=make_port(), host=make_host())` evaluates `make_host()` first (host is declared first in the TypedDict) where CPython evaluates `make_port()` first (textual order). Probe-confirmed under both `tpy` and CPython. Silent: no warning, no `no_cpython.txt`, and every `tests/cases/kwargs/kwargs_unpack_*` case writes kwargs already in field order, so the cpy phase can never see it -- a test-adequacy gap on top of the bug. Both codegen paths inherit the divergence identically (the 2026-07-22 THIR TypedDict-ctor arm mirrors the already-reordered call byte-for-byte, so this is NOT a THIR issue). Fix shape: evaluate out-of-order kwarg expressions into temps in call-site order, then construct the pack from the temps (or document + warn). Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity) of the capture/kwargs THIR wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (silent wrong behavior + double diagnostics, latent) A `@function_macro` on an `@auto_readonly` (or implicit-auto-readonly `__deref__`/`__getitem__`/`__span__`) method runs twice and misroutes its deferred callback.** Method cloning (`expand_methods_for_record`, `tpyc/sema/registration.py:1159`; `_clone_auto_readonly`/`_clone_auto_own`/`_apply_implicit_auto_readonly`, `tpyc/sema/method_expansion.py`) runs BEFORE pass 5.5 (`_expand_function_macros`, `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`) and builds the mutable+const twin via `dataclasses.replace` WITHOUT overriding the `pending_macros`/`pending_deferred_sema_macros` list fields (`default_factory=list` on `TpyFunction`), so both twins share the same list references. Pass 5.5 then walks both twins in `record.methods` and `run_function_macros` (`tpyc/sema/function_macros.py`) does not clear `pending_macros` after firing, so the macro runs ONCE PER TWIN: (a) any `ctx.warning`/`ctx.error` the macro emits fires twice; (b) worse, a `defer_until_sema_complete` callback is appended twice to the shared `pending_deferred_sema_macros`, but the drain empties it on the MUTABLE twin's context and no-ops on the const twin -- so a deferred callback meant to see the const-projected body NEVER runs against it. Body rewriting itself is mostly benign (mutable shares the original body ref, const has its own deepcopy, so identity-based `replace_expr` lands once per twin). Latent: no in-tree macro applies `@function_macro` to a cloned method yet, and untested (the `function_macro_on_method` happy case uses a plain instance method, a staticmethod -- exempt from cloning -- and a free function). Newly reachable by the method-macro feature (`feat-method-function-macros`). Fix shape is a DESIGN decision, not a patch: either deepcopy/clear/dedup `pending_macros`+`pending_deferred_sema_macros` per twin so a shared list can't double-fire, or reject the `@function_macro` + cloning-decorator combination until the twin interaction is designed. Add a regression case (`@auto_readonly` + `@function_macro` on one method) alongside. Surfaced by /tpy-ready retrospective second opinion of `feat-method-function-macros`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (or `/tpy-add-feature` for the reject-vs-dedup design).
- **[LOW small] (rejects valid code) A RENAMED cross-module plain-union alias (`from mod import Shape as MyShape`) fails to resolve at a local-var annotation -- "Unknown type: Shape".** `MyShape` maps back to the defining module's `Shape`, but the local-decl annotation resolver then looks up `Shape` (which is not bound in the importing module -- only `MyShape` is) and errors `Unknown type: Shape`. The un-renamed form (`from mod import Shape`) compiles; a renamed *generic* recursive alias (`Tree as T2`) also resolves. Pre-existing, sema-level, independent of THIR. Reproduces at `error_union_type_alias_cross_module_renamed` (`c: MyShape = Circle(...)`) and even in param position (a member arg to a renamed-union param is rejected "expected Shape, got Circle"). **Latent codegen divergence behind it:** were the alias to resolve, a pointer-variant `UNION_RVALUE` storage slot (`MyShape __slot_N = ...`) would expose a display-vs-alias name mismatch -- the AST path spells it via `union_alias_names` (the importing module's "MyShape"), THIR via `union_display_names` (the defining module's "Shape", kept by `setdefault`) -- so `_union_storage_val_cpp` (`tpyc/thir/lower/predicates.py`) would emit "Shape" where the AST emits "MyShape". Currently unreachable (the sema rejection blocks any such slot), so THIR stays byte-identical; when the rejection is lifted, `_union_storage_val_cpp` must read `union_alias_names` (or fall back to AST) and both must be re-verified together. Fix shape: bind the renamed local name to the resolved alias so a local-decl annotation resolves it the way the param-signature path already does. Surfaced by /tpy-review (wave5+6) building the display-vs-alias guard case. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] (missing diagnostic, loud) A COMPREHENSION or an owning builtin-container-method result returned directly from a borrow-returning function (`-> list[T]`) passes sema and dies as a raw g++ error instead of the located "Cannot return local or temporary as reference".** `def f(xs: list[float]) -> list[float]: return [x * 2.0 for x in xs]` (and `return xs.copy()`) reach the C++ compiler as `cannot bind non-const lvalue reference of type 'std::vector<double>&' to an rvalue`, with no source location and no mention of `Own`. The check is present and fires correctly on every neighbouring shape: `return []`, `return list(xs)`, `return g()` (a user fn returning `Own[list[float]]`), `y = [x * 2.0 for x in xs]; return y` (bound to a local first) and `return R(1)` for a record all get the clean located error, and the `Own[list[float]]`-declared inverse (`return xs` where the return is `Own`) gets its own clean "Cannot return borrowed value ... without explicit copy()". Two independent holes in `is_dangling_return` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py:2656`), which enumerates AST node kinds: (1) it has arms for `TpyArrayLiteral`/`TpyDictLiteral`/`TpySetLiteral` but NONE for `TpyListComprehension`/`TpyDictComprehension`/`TpySetComprehension`, so a comprehension falls through to the `Default: assume safe` tail -- the fail-closed net just above it only catches expressions whose type `is_borrowing_view_type`, and `list[float]` is not one, so the guard does not cover a comprehension of a reference type; (2) the `TpyMethodCall` arm's builtin-container exemption (`~2846`, "Builtin container methods (list.pop, ...) also return Own[T] but are exempt") was written to keep the Optional pointer-repr `&(obj.method())` path from tripping, but it sits in the shared predicate, so it also suppresses the plain reference-return check for `xs.copy()`. Loud (toolchain-caught), never silent -- no wrong runtime behavior, only a confusing error with no location. Workaround: declare `-> Own[list[T]]`. Fix shape: add the comprehension arms, and move the builtin-`Own` method exemption to the pointer-repr call site rather than the shared predicate; a node-kind enumeration that decides "safe by default" wants a reflection guard (assert every rvalue-producing expression node has an arm) or the next new node kind reopens the same hole silently. Surfaced probing the CPython-interop boundary (`@export def normalize(xs: list[float]) -> list[float]`); reproduces identically in a plain non-ext module, so it is unrelated to interop. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.

## Safety / borrow checker
- **[MED small] A MIXED owned+borrow tuple local in a RESUMABLE frame parks a raw pointer into caller-owned storage across every suspension, unchecked.** `p = make_mixed(b)` inside a generator / `async def`, for `make_mixed(b: Box) -> tuple[Own[Box], Box]`, renders `::tpy::frame_slot<std::tuple<Box, Box*>>` -- element 1 points at the CALLER's object, and the frame outlives the call that built it, so a parked generator whose caller-side `Box` dies reads freed memory on resume. Nothing ties the pointee's lifetime to the frame handle's. The MECHANISM is not new: `FrameLocalKind.PTR_ALIAS` has stored the same raw pointer for a plain non-value local since long before this shape existed, and this is the same family as the `__iter__`-captures-`self` / str-view / varargs frame-capture entries above -- what is new is REACHABILITY, since a mixed tuple local previously failed the C++ build in a resumable body and so could not be written at all. The shipped case (`tuple/mixed_own_tuple_frame_local`) does not open the dangling window either: `main()` keeps the caller's objects alive for the whole synchronous drive, so nothing in the corpus proves this safe. Fix belongs with the family, not with the tuple: a frame that captures a borrow needs its handle's lifetime tied to the pointee (or an escape check at the frame-construction site). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the mixed-tuple decl-path fix.
- **[LOW small] THIR: `copy()` of a NARROWED pointer-repr `Optional[record]` name at the return sink drops the deref -- emits `A(a)` where `a` is `const A*` (uncompilable under `--thir-codegen`).** `def f(a: A | None) -> A: if a is not None: return copy(a)` -- the return sink classifies its copy source against `scope.admission_pointers()`, which deliberately strips pointer-repr Optional-record names, so `copy_plain_record_source` accepts the pointer as a plain-record source; the `BORROW_BIND` read then renders the name bare (by design there -- record receivers spell `->`), giving `A(a)` instead of `A(*a)`. The other three copy sinks classify against the full `lc.pointers` and exclude the shape. DORMANT: no corpus case exercises it, so the byte-diff and ratchet stay green; it becomes live wherever `--thir-codegen` meets this pattern, and blocks the eventual THIR cutover. Verified pre-existing -- reproduces byte-for-byte on master 31c0cd431 in a clean worktree, so the copy-row helper fold (which preserved the asymmetry exactly) neither introduced nor widened it. Surfaced by the /tpy-review safety pass on the fold. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An await-borrow through a BOUND handle registers no transitive alias: `c = s.get(); r = await c` leaves `r` untracked as a borrower of `s`.** The direct form (`r = await s.get()`) registers via `_register_call_result_borrow`'s TpyAwait arm (recursing into the awaited call), but a TpyName operand has no traceable roots at the await site -- the handle binding registered `c -> s`, and once `c` is consumed by the await only `r` remains, so a later move of `s` is no longer demoted. Narrow window (requires the two-step bind + a post-await consume of the source); the return-position sibling is fail-closed (`return await c` on a bound borrow handle is conservatively rejected -- a rejects-valid wart of the same shape). Fix shape: at handle-consume time, transfer (or copy) the handle's registered borrows to the await-result binder. Surfaced implementing the /tpy-review await-provenance fixes on the async borrow-return ABI branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] A whole-container `Span` binding registers NO borrow, so mutating the container while the span lives warns nothing -- `sp = Span[int](xs); xs += [3]` (or `.append`) reallocates under a live span (silent UAF).** `Span[T](container)` construction is invisible to the borrow tracker: `_borrow_storage_root`/`addr_taken_roots` (`tpyc/sema/context.py`) do not handle the `TpyCall` view-constructor shape, so no loan exists for the mutate-while-borrowed machinery to check (element/slice borrows DO register). Pre-existing and reachable via `.append()` on master; the pending-list `+=` fix widens the reachable surface (unannotated literals now resolve to reallocating vectors where the Array form refused to compile). Adjacent Span/view-lifetime entries below track element/iterator shapes but not this whole-container binding. Fix shape: register a view borrow at the `Span(container)`/view-constructor call, rooting through the argument like the slice arm does. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the pending-list aug-assign fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] An ESCAPING closure capturing `self` holds a raw `this` that can outlive the receiver.** Sync nested defs / lambdas in methods now capture `self` as `this` (pointer, alias semantics); when the closure escapes the method (stored into a `Callable` field/container or returned), nothing ties the closure's lifetime to the receiver object -- if the receiver dies first the stored `this` dangles, where CPython's closure keeps the object alive. Record RELOCATION invalidates it too, and is far more common than death-before-call: a move of the receiver (container growth, `Own[T]` transfer) destroys-and-reconstructs the object, leaving the stored `this` pointing at the old storage. Sibling of the general escaping-ref-capture soundness class (local captures have escape checks; `self` has none). Fix shape: extend the nested-def/lambda escape analysis to treat a captured `self` like a captured reference param. Surfaced implementing the nested-def self-access fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW, latent, believed-unreachable] A generator/coro frame escaping the C++ block holding its hoisted rvalue-arg temp would dangle -- nothing ties the temp's scope to the frame's.** The call-site rvalue hoist (a reference-type rvalue arg to a resumable factory materializes `T __tmp_N = ...;` in the enclosing block, the frame borrows it) assumes the frame never outlives that block. A generator var assigned inside a loop/if body but hoisted to function scope (used after the block) would leave the frame's ref field pointing at the dead block-scoped temp. NOT reachable today: every route probed is independently blocked (cross-branch hoisted `Iterator[T]` storage codegen bug; the coroutine must-use rule; @dynamic protocols not supporting generator methods). Filed so the gap is not silently reintroduced-as-live when one of those blockers is fixed. Fix shape: emit the arg temp at the same scope the generator handle is hoisted to (or reject the escaping shape) -- a lifetime tie the borrow checker cannot express pre-region-model. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the readonly-resumable-param branch.
- **[LOW small] Borrow-returning a module global is inconsistently rejected: an UNANNOTATED top-level binding errors ("Cannot return local or temporary as reference" -- misleading, a global is durable), an ANNOTATED one is accepted, and a global literally named `self` is accepted even in a free function.** `g = Box(1); def get() -> Box: return g` is rejected while `g: Box = Box(1)` compiles and runs -- the inferred binding apparently isn't in `global_scope.bindings` when `_name_is_param_or_global` (`tpyc/sema/compatibility.py`) checks it. Separately, that helper's `name == "self"` fast-path answers True with no receiver context, so a free function returning a module global named `self` passes the durability check by name alone (harmless C++ -- globals ARE durable -- but the acceptance is name-based, not storage-based; `tests/cases/interop/warn_export_class_return_self`'s `get_global` leans on it). Loud reject-valid on the first face, inconsistency on the second; no miscompile. Fix shape: register inferred top-level bindings before body analysis (or teach the helper to see them), and gate the `self` fast-path on actually being inside a method. Surfaced by /tpy-review (test-coverage W3) of the identity-preserving-returns branch. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A `@readonly` method returning bare `self` (`-> "Cls"`) const-poisons the caller-side local only at the C++ level -- sema accepts a mutation TPy should reject.** `@readonly def me(self) -> "Box": return self; y = b.me(); y.v = 99` compiles at the TPy level and dies in g++ ("assignment of member ... in read-only object"): the C++ method is `const` and returns `const Box&`, but sema doesn't track the returned borrow as readonly (annotating the return `-> readonly[Box]` DOES reject correctly at sema). Toolchain-caught (loud), rejects-valid-diagnostic gap, not silent. Since the operator-dunder borrow-return fix, the OPERATOR face shares the gap: `c = a + b` with a plain `-> "Acc"` borrow-returning `__add__` binds `const Acc&`, and `c.n = 5` dies the same way in g++ instead of a located sema error (the `readonly[...]` spelling rejects cleanly at sema; `Own[...]` is the mutable-copy escape hatch). Fix shape: a readonly (incl. implicitly-readonly dunder) method's borrow return should be readonly-typed (or at least the caller-side binding const-tracked) -- same "readonly is sema-layer-only" family as the adjacent entries; one fix covers method and operator spellings. Reproducer /tmp/agents/glob/ro2.py. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model, verified by probe) of the identity-preserving-returns branch; operator face by the operator-dunder borrow-return fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Binding the borrow-returning result of a method call or operator over TEMPORARY receiver/operands only WARNS; any later read of the binding is use-after-free.** `c = Acc(3).peek()` and `t = Acc(4) + Acc(2)` (borrow-returning `peek`/`__add__`) warn "Result borrows from temporary receiver/argument ... destroyed at end-of-statement" but still compile under default flags and read freed storage if used (the test toolchain's `-Werror=dangling-reference` catches most shapes; user builds don't). The binding itself is never valid to read, so the warning tier is arguably wrong -- consider promoting to a sema error (the async sibling `error_bind_receiver_temp` already errors for its shape). The operator face is pinned warn-level by `tpyc/test_operator_borrow_binding.py` (a snapshot case can't be committed -- the emitted C++ genuinely dangles and fails the suite's warn flags). Surfaced by the operator-dunder borrow-return fix (cpython-parity agent flagged the method face as pre-existing). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (tier decision).
- **[LOW, latent, believed-unreachable] THIR's `foreach.consuming_iter` trusts sema's `stmt.consuming_iter_fi` unconditionally, where the AST for-loop re-derives movability and self-corrects.** THIR's `_for_consuming_route` (`tpyc/thir/lower/statements.py`) emits the consuming/moving form (`own_iter(std::move(items))`) whenever sema set `consuming_iter_fi`; the AST path (`codegen_cpp/statements.py` `_gen_consuming_iter`) additionally re-runs `_is_last_use_movable` and silently falls back to the non-consuming aliasing iterator if that recheck disagrees. If sema's `is_auto_move_use` and codegen's `_is_last_use_movable`/`movable_locals` could ever diverge for a for-loop iterable, THIR would MOVE the container where AST ALIASES it -- a genuine behavior split (not just a byte-diff mismatch). Believed UNREACHABLE: two /tpy-review specialists (cpython-parity + codegen-correctness) independently traced it to a single forward pass with no retroactive demotion of the same node id, so `consuming_iter_fi` and `all_last_uses` cannot diverge between sema-decision-time and codegen-read-time; no corpus case demonstrates a split. Filed so it is not re-litigated each wave. Fix shape (if ever reached): have THIR mirror the AST's `_is_last_use_movable` recheck, or make sema's decision authoritative for both paths. Surfaced by /tpy-review (cpython-parity + codegen-correctness) of the wave-12 auto_move consuming-iter arm.
- **[LOW, latent] A `Mutex`/`RwLock` guard outliving a dropped bare (non-`Arc`) `Mutex`/`RwLock` dangles (residual guard-escape hole after the runtime-lock backstop landed).** The guard holds a raw `Ptr` into the lock's heap cell with no ownership tie, so if a bare `Mutex`/`RwLock` is dropped while a guard is still live, the guard's cell pointer dangles. The two *access-without-lock* holes are now closed at runtime: guards carry a `_locked` flag (`lib/tpy/tpy/sync.py`, set in `__enter__`, cleared in `__exit__`), and `get`/`set`/`__deref__` raise `RuntimeError` if it isn't held -- so a guard bound without `with` (never acquired) or used after its block (`with ... as g:` as-var outlives the block) aborts loudly instead of racing the payload (`tests/cases/threading/guard_misuse_rejected`; the CPython stub mirrors the check). This remaining hole is the *lifetime* half -- the guard outliving the lock object -- which the runtime flag can't catch (the flag lives on the guard; the cell is already freed), so it stays region/lifetime-model-gated. Same family as the `@property`/generator borrow-escape entries below. Separately (unchanged): re-locking the same `Mutex` on one thread (`std::mutex` is non-recursive) deadlocks with no diagnostic. Not expressible as a passing test (silent UAF). Fix shape: tie the guard's borrow lifetime to the lock object (needs the region/lifetime model). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of Threading V3; access-holes hardened via /tpy-fix-bug. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` once the region model lands.
- **[MED, latent, deferred: MIR] A payload borrow extracted from a live `Mutex`/`RwLock` guard and used past the `with` block races/dangles even when the lock object stays alive (e.g. `Arc[RwLock[T]]`).** Distinct from the bare-lock guard-lifetime hole above: there the *lock object* is dropped; here the lock stays alive (the `Arc` keeps the cell), but a reference pulled *out* of the guard -- `with r.read() as g: leaked = g` (or `leaked = g.get()`, or a borrow of a reference-type field/element reached through the guard) -- outlives the guard's `with` scope and is then accessed with no lock held. The runtime `_locked` backstop only gates access *through the guard object* (`get`/`set`/`__deref__`); a raw borrow bound to an outer name bypasses it. Another thread holding a write guard concurrently then races it: for `Arc[RwLock[list]]` a writer's `append` can reallocate the buffer the escaped borrow points into -> concurrent-access data race + use-after-free, no diagnostic. Hits the canonical shared-mutable pattern; needs only a borrow bound past the block (a lock-discipline violation the type system doesn't catch). Same region/lifetime-model family as the bare-lock entry above and the `@property`/generator borrow-escape entries below -- the payload borrow's lifetime must be tied to the guard's `with` extent, which whole-function borrow facts can't express. Not expressible as a passing test (silent UAF/race). Fix shape: register the guard's `__deref__`/`get` result as a loan scoped to the guard's `with` extent and reject its escape (the MIR `Place`/`LoanInfo` model with extent-aware loans). The same escape extends to the LOCK HANDLE itself: `MutexGuard.raw_mutex()` (added for `Condvar.wait`) returns a `Ptr[_RawMutex]` that, bound past the `with`, lets a later `cv.wait()` unlock/relock a mutex outside any critical section -- corrupting lock discipline, not just racing stale data. (Access through the guard is gated by `_require_locked`; a raw `Ptr` bound to an outer name is not -- same whole-function-facts gap.) Surfaced by design review of the RwLock Sync bound (the safe `Arc[RwLock[list]]` case) + /tpy-review of the Condvar primitive. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` once the region model lands.
- **[LOW, pre-existing, latent] Returning a bytes LITERAL into an `Optional[BytesView]` (`-> BytesView | None`) return slot builds an owned `std::vector<uint8_t>` temporary that the returned `std::optional<std::span<const uint8_t>>` aliases -- a dangling span, no diagnostic.** A `-> BytesView | None` function `return b"..."` emits `return ::tpy::bytes_literal_owned(...)`, whose owned-vector rvalue is implicitly converted into the `optional<span>` return; the span points into the temporary, which is destroyed at the end of the return statement, so the caller reads freed storage (silent UAF/garbage). Confirmed pre-existing on the AST path (`tpyc --cxx`, no `--thir-codegen`) and mirrored byte-identically by THIR, so the value-repr-Optional[bytes] wave neither introduces nor worsens it. The str side is safe by construction (`TpyStrLiteral` never materializes an owned temporary at a `StrView | None` return -- only the bytes-literal owned/view split has this footgun). Sibling of the `bytes|None` truthiness and `Optional[str]` view-source-return entries in `## Compiler bugs`. Fix shape: reject (or diagnose) a materializing owned bytes source at a `BytesView`-inner optional return, or force the literal to its static-storage span form there. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the value-repr Optional[bytes] THIR wave. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED, latent] A borrow taken from a nullable owning field survives that field being nulled/reassigned, then reads freed storage (silent UAF).** `raw = r.raw; r.close(); raw.read(4)` -- `Response.raw` (`@property` returning `self._raw`, an `HTTPResponse | None`) hands out a borrow of the held object; `close()` (and `with r:` `__exit__`) does `self._raw = None`, dropping it in place; the previously-taken `raw` handle (or a partially-consumed `iter_content`/`iter_lines` generator sourced from `_raw`) then reads freed/reused storage. ASan-confirmed garbage, no compiler diagnostic and no runtime check. Reachable via ordinary intended use: early-abort a stream then touch a saved `.raw`, or let an `iter_content` handle outlive a `with r:` block. Same family as the `@property` view-of-field-dangles-on-reassignment (606) and generator-captures-self (607) entries, but for a nullable OWNING field explicitly reassigned by a public `close()` -- this diff (`Response._raw`/`close`/`raw`/`iter_content`/`iter_lines`, all new) is what makes it concretely reachable. Not expressible as a passing test (silent UAF), so tracked here only. Fix shape: a borrow of a field must be invalidated when the field is reassigned/nulled (needs the region/lifetime model), or `close()`-style reassignment of a borrowed-from field must be rejected while a live borrow exists. Reproducers `/tmp/agents/streamreview/probe3.py`, `probe4.py`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of `requests` streaming. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` once the region model lands.
- **[LOW small] Calling a (correctly-const) method on a container-subscript element blocks readonly/const inference for the enclosing container, so a read-only method/param is emitted mutable.** `for name in other._store: self._store[name] = other._store[name].copy()` and `... self._store[name].matches(...)` -- the enclosing `CookieJar.update(other)` and `header_for(self)` bodies only READ `other`/`self`, but sema emits `void update(CookieJar& other)` / non-const `header_for` (vs the sibling `CaseInsensitiveDict::update(const CaseInsensitiveDict&)` which reads only tuple fields off the subscript and stays const). It cascades: `_request_on`'s `send_cookies: CookieJar` param is non-const purely because it calls the non-const `header_for`. NOT unsound (mutable-when-const-possible is safe) and no current call site is blocked, but it weakens the API (a caller holding only `readonly[CookieJar]` couldn't pass it as `other` or call `header_for`). Same const-receiver-drops-the-right-lowering family as the const-dict `in` entry this feature filed (its const container-read reference-local sibling has since been fixed); here it's a Phase-2 mutation-propagation gap -- a container-subscript method-call receiver isn't re-verified once the callee's readonly status resolves. Workaround: none needed (safe). Fix shape: after Phase-2 resolves a called method's readonly verdict, propagate it back to the subscript-receiver's container so the enclosing method/param can still infer const. Reproducer: `lib/tpy/tplib/requests.py` `CookieJar.update`/`header_for`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the requests cookie feature. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] Passing a self/param-field alias as a positional arg to a mutating FREE FUNCTION records no mutation-call edge, so the enclosing method can be wrongly inferred readonly (const receiver) -- ill-formed C++.** `v = self.inner; bump_val(v)` where `bump_val(x: Inner)` mutates `x`: `_record_mutation_call_edges` (`tpyc/sema/calls.py`) resolves the arg only against the caller's OWN params (`name_to_idx`/`resolved`), never `"self"` or a `"self.field"` storage key, so the structural mutation of `self.inner` through the free-function call isn't propagated back to the receiver; the method infers `const` and the `const Inner& v = this->inner;` bind (or the `bump_val(v)` call passing `const Inner&` into an `Inner&` param) fails the C++ build. The method-call face IS handled (Phase-2 `_is_self_call_deferred` re-verifies a `r = self.inner; r.bump()` receiver), so the gap is specific to free-function positional args off a self/param-rooted alias. Toolchain-caught, not silent; pre-existing (reproduces at merge-base, independent of the borrow-local const-drop fix -- before the fix it hard-fails at the wrongly-const alias bind, after at the call site, same program). Workaround: mutate through a method call, or pass the field directly. Fix shape: `_record_mutation_call_edges` must resolve a free-function arg that is an alias of a self/param field back to that storage root and record the mutation edge, mirroring the method-call path. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the borrow-local const-drop fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] A `@property` (or method) returning a StrView/BytesView borrow of a stable field dangles with no diagnostic when the source field is then reassigned/mutated.** `n = obj.name` where `@property def name(self) -> StrView: return self._label` binds `n` as a `string_view` into `obj._label`; a later `obj._label = "..."` (or an obj move/drop) leaves `n` pointing at freed/reallocated storage. Both single-level (`obj.name`) and nested-chain (`c.box.name`) forms. Root cause: `_view_source_is_temporary` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) treats any `TpyFieldAccess` -- including a property getter call -- as non-temporary, so the pending-view source-mutation tracking never engages; and `_borrow_storage_root`/`_storage_key` key on the property's own name (`"obj.name"`), not the field it actually borrows from (`"obj._label"`), so a mutation of the real source isn't seen as invalidating. Property-blind, pre-existing (reproduces at merge-base, independent of the owned-str-return fix that added the promotion path). No diagnostic; reachable only by separating the view's lifetime from the source mutation (uncommon). Fix shape: make `_view_source_is_temporary` and the storage-root derivation property-aware (route through `property_getter_call` + `return_borrows_from` to the real borrowed field), or region/lifetime work -- IR-gated. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the @property owned-str-return fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW, latent] A generator `__iter__` (or any generator method) captures `self` by raw pointer; the frame dangles if the object is moved/destroyed while the generator is live.** `for k in headers` over a `CaseInsensitiveDict.__iter__` (`def __iter__(self): for lk in self._store: yield self._store[lk][0]`) holds a raw `this` in the generator frame; normal `for k in obj:` keeps `obj` alive for the loop, but binding the iterator and then moving/dropping `obj` (or iterating a temporary that is freed) reads freed memory. Pre-existing generator-captures-self pattern, not specific to CID -- but `__iter__` is the first method shape that exposes it to ordinary user iteration. No diagnostic. Reachable only by separating the iterator's lifetime from the object's (uncommon). Fix shape: a generator frame borrowing `self` must keep the receiver alive (or the borrow checker must reject an iterator that outlives its receiver) -- region/lifetime work, IR-gated. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the CaseInsensitiveDict `__iter__`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` once the region model lands.
- **[LOW small] The single-`__init__` `raise X(args)` arg loop skips `check_own_param`, so ownership diagnostics are silently missed on raise-site ctor args.** `tpyc/sema/statements.py` (~1855, the non-overloaded `record.init_params` branch) analyzes and coerces each arg but never calls `check_own_param`, unlike the overloaded-raise sibling loop just above it and every normal ctor path -- an `Own[T]` param on an exception's `__init__` gets no `@nocopy` rejection and no copies-into-owned-storage warning when raised (plain construction of the same class warns/errors correctly). Diagnostic gap only (codegen still copies/moves as usual); no repro committed -- needs an exception class with an `Own[T]` ctor param raised with a non-last-use local. Fix shape: add the `check_own_param` call to that loop, mirroring the overloaded branch. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the pending-arg-marks fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small, latent] An `@overload` generator implementation's body-derived facts are stamped onto the LAST STUB's FunctionInfo, using the impl's param indices.** The impl is not registered as callable, so `_analyze_function` resolves `func_overloads[-1]` (the last stub's FI) and writes the impl's body facts there (`tpyc/sema/analyzer.py:~1530-1571` borrow/mutation facts incl. `generator_borrow_param_indices`; `~1284-1300` `frame_locals`/`frame_subframes` Send/Sync materials). Two consequences: (1) if a later stub declares fewer params than the impl, an impl-derived borrow index can exceed the stub's arity and corrupt `return_borrows_from`; (2) frame materials land on a stub FI whose `is_generator` is False, so any consumer gating on `fi.is_generator` before reading them misreads. Not triggered today -- `itertools.repeat` and the `overload_generator` test both have a last stub of equal arity to the impl, and no current consumer hits case (2). Became reachable when the @overload-ed generator crash was fixed (the body path was previously unreachable). Fix shape: stamp impl body facts onto an FI that represents the impl (or onto every stub by the stub's own param mapping), not blindly onto `func_overloads[-1]`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the overloaded-generator fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] A value->`Ptr` address-of coercion (`UPCAST_TO_PTR` / `record_to_ptr`) applied in a CONSTRUCTOR-ARG (or general call-arg) position does not register the borrow with the borrow-tracker, only in a local-var-decl init position.** `compatibility.py`'s `_check_compat` marks the source param mutated/addr-taken for these coercions, but the borrow-tracker's `add_borrow` registration is wired only for the var-decl-init site, so `T(&x)`-style argument coercions don't create a tracked loan. Latent: a program that takes `&x` as a call/ctor arg and then mutates `x` while the resulting pointer is live would not get the expected borrow-conflict diagnostic. Pre-existing, but first reached by routing struct-like `@builtin_type` ctors through the normal record-ctor path (`_make_waker` passes `&handle` into `Waker(...)`); dormant there because `handle` isn't mutated after. Fix shape: register the loan at the call/ctor-arg coercion site too, mirroring the var-decl-init path. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the @builtin_type-ctor normal-path routing. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (parity, not unsound) Binding a borrow-yield's `next()` result to a local silently COPIES where CPython aliases.** `g = gen(); a = next(g); b = next(g)` over a generator that yields a borrow (`Iterator[list[int]]` yielding a frame-local / param / self-field) binds `a`/`b` as independent owned copies snapshotted at each `next()`, so a later mutation through `a` is not seen via `b` (or the source), nor vice versa -- CPython aliases all of them. No warning fires (unlike the `copies ... into owned storage` warning that DOES fire for a container-insert / field-store of the same value). Memory-safe (the copy owns its storage, so it survives frame destruction); purely an observable copy-vs-alias divergence. Pre-existing and independent of the frame-local borrow-yield feature -- it manifests identically for the param/self-field borrow yields that already worked; the consumer-side binding is what copies, regardless of the producer root. The for-loop consumer is unaffected (the loop var is a live borrow -- mutation IS visible on resume, matching CPython). **Fix shape:** either bind `next()` of a borrow-yield as a borrow (alias) under the same ephemeral-borrow rule the loop var uses, or at minimum emit the existing copy-into-owned-storage warning so the divergence is acknowledged + `copy()`-silenceable. Surfaced probing the yield-frame-local-borrow feature.
- **[MED small, deferred: MIR] Reassigning a narrowed value-Optional iterable to `None` inside a resumable-frame for-loop dangles the frame's iterator (use-after-free).** In a generator/async, `for x in b: b = None; yield x` over a narrowed `b: bytes | None` / `str | None` sets the frame's owned `std::optional<V>` field to `nullopt`, destroying the buffer the frame's `__for_it` / `__for_end` iterator fields borrow into; the next resume reads dangling iterators (UB). Sema's reassign-of-borrowed check skips value-Optionals (`OptionalType(bytes).is_value_type()` is True), so no diagnostic fires. This is another instance of the general resumable-iterable reassign-escape gap (a borrow into the iterable invalidated by reassigning/mutating it mid-loop) -- NOT a fresh root cause; the value-Optional shape became reachable when narrowed-value-Optional for-loop iteration was fixed (it was a C++ build error before). The non-resumable (sync) case is safe -- `string_view`/`span` iterators point into external data, not the optional object. Region-checker territory (cf. the async borrow-escape / `TaskGroup` work, IR-gated); do NOT re-attempt as a standalone value-Optional patch. Requires a deliberate reassign-while-iterating pattern. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the narrowed-Optional for-loop fix.
- **[LOW small] Field-assign through a raw `Ptr` to a readonly-payload `Deref` (`Ptr[Rc[readonly[T]]]`) is ungated -- it can reach the readonly payload silently.** The deref-target readonly check in `_enforce_readonly_assignment_target` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) excludes `PtrType` receivers (a `Ptr[readonly[T]]` keeps its own "assign through read-only pointer" diagnostic via `is_readonly_ptr`). But a `Ptr` whose pointee is itself a readonly-payload `Deref` handle -- `p: Ptr[Rc[readonly[Counter]]]`, then `p.n = 5` (auto-deref through the Ptr, then through the Rc, onto the readonly payload) -- is gated by neither: `is_readonly_ptr(Ptr[Rc[...]])` is False (the Ptr pointee isn't `readonly`), and the new deref-target check skips all `PtrType` receivers. Pre-existing relative to the deref-readonly fix (neither closed nor worsened by it), and obscure (requires a raw `Ptr` to an `Rc[readonly[T]]`), but the readonly payload is reachable for mutation with no diagnostic. Fix shape: when the receiver is a `Ptr` whose pointee is a `Deref` type, still peel the inner deref chain for a readonly target (don't blanket-skip `PtrType`). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the deref-readonly-assignment fix.
- **[LOW small] An `unsafe_interior_mutable` field's readonly-boundary exemption only fires for a direct `self.<field>.method()` call, not a chained receiver.** `tpyc/sema/methods.py` -- the `interior_receiver` guard that suppresses self-mutation demotion checks `isinstance(expr.obj, TpyFieldAccess) and expr.obj.accessed_field_is_interior`. A chained call whose receiver is not directly the interior field access -- e.g. `self._cell.get_inner().mutate()` (where `get_inner()` returns something rooted in the cell) -- would NOT be recognized as interior, so the mutation roots back to `self` and demotes the enclosing method. Not exploitable by current `Rc`/`Weak` code (every `_cell` call is single-level: `self._cell.incr_strong()` etc.), but fragile given `unsafe_interior_mutable`'s semantics are stated generally ("mutations reached through the field"). Fix shape: climb a method-call chain to its field-access root when deciding `interior_receiver`, mirroring how `_root_name_of_expr` is transparent to accessor calls. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the interior-mutable feature.
- **[LOW small] `Rc[readonly[T]]`'s no-laundering guarantee has no C++-type backstop -- a `@native` function can mutate the payload.** `readonly[Rc[T]].clone()` yields `Own[Rc[readonly[T]]]` at the TPy level, but `ReadonlyType.to_cpp()` strips the modifier, so the C++ local is a plain `Rc<Counter>` whose mutable `get()` overload is callable. TPy sema enforces the readonly-payload contract (a TPy caller can't reach the mutable `get()`), but a `@native` function handed the C++ value, or any C++-level code, can. Pre-existing for `readonly[T]`-of-reference generally (readonly is a sema-layer/`const&`-param construct, not a deep C++ type), but materially worsened by the interior feature because `Rc`'s no-laundering soundness story rests on this invariant. No fix without a distinct C++ type for `Rc<const T>` (a real `Rc` instantiation over a const payload, vs the current modifier-strip); revisit if `@native` interop with `Rc[readonly[T]]` becomes load-bearing. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the interior-mutable feature.
- **[LOW small] `_view_source_is_temporary`'s method-call arm assumes a view-returning method borrows its RECEIVER, ignoring `return_borrows_from` arg indices.** `tpyc/sema/statements.py` -- for a `TpyMethodCall` whose return `is_borrowing_view_type`, the predicate recurses into `expr.obj` (receiver) only, unlike `is_dangling_return`'s method branch which checks each `return_borrows_from` index. A hypothetical view-returning method that borrows an ARG (not the receiver) with a stable receiver but a temporary arg would be classified non-temporary -> an inferred view local would NOT promote and an explicit `StrView` bind would not reject, binding a dangling view with no diagnostic. Latent: no method in scope today returns a view borrowing an arg rather than the receiver (str/bytes/Span views all borrow the receiver). Fix shape: mirror `is_dangling_return`'s per-index `return_borrows_from` traversal in `_view_source_is_temporary` (and ideally consolidate the two -- see the TODO.md duplication note). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the view-lifetime fix.
- **[MED] A str/bytes SELF-view (`a[3:9]`, `s.strip()`) silently dangles when its source local is rebound, because str/bytes self-views follow a warn-not-demote model and the warning misses plain rebinds.** `a = make(); v = a[3:9]; a = make()` (slice) and `s = make(); x = s.strip(); s = make()` (method) both leave `v`/`x` a `std::string_view` into the freed first buffer -- no diagnostic, no demotion -- diverging from CPython (where the local holds an independent value). Verified matrix: a **container-element** view (`x = c[0]`, list/dict/record) DEMOTES to owned on any source mutation OR rebind (sound, no warning); a **str/bytes self-view** (slice or view-returning method on a str/bytes local) instead only WARNS "while borrowed" on `+=` / method-mutation and is SILENT on a plain rebind -- so rebind falls through both guards. Two facts behind it: (1) the borrow-conflict warning treats augmented-assign / method-mutation as a conflict but not a plain rebind; (2) the view-vs-owned source-root tracking in `_infer_new_local_view_type` (`tpyc/sema/statements.py`) registers only `TpySubscript`/`TpyFieldAccess` *element* sources in `view_source_borrows_map`, never a str/bytes self-source (slice of a str local, or a view-returning call rooted at its receiver via `return_borrows_from`), so those views have no demotion path at all. Distinct from (and not a hole in) the compound-view-roots fix, which demotes correctly on rebind for the element sources it tracks. **Chosen fix (direction A -- the sound, model-unifying one):** register str/bytes self-source views in `view_source_borrows_map` rooted at the borrowed source (the sliced str/bytes local; a view-returning call's `return_borrows_from` receiver/args) so they DEMOTE to owned on mutation/rebind exactly like container-element views -- one model for all view sources. (The weaker alternative -- merely extending the borrow-conflict warning to rebinds -- was rejected: it labels the UB instead of removing it and keeps two divergent safety models.) This flips `tests/cases/view_lifetime/warn_view_source_mutation`'s str/slice cases from warn-and-view to demote-to-owned (the warnings become unnecessary, consistent with that test's own note that they are a stopgap pending last-use precision) -- an existing-snapshot change to confirm before regenerating. Surfaced by /tpy-fix-bug of the compound-view-source root tracking fix; own branch.
- **[MED small] A local bound from a native `-> V` reference-returning method aliases its source with no escape/dangling check.** Now that native `-> V` methods alias (the native-record exclusion was removed), `p = obj.accessor()` binds a `T*`/`T&` into `obj`'s storage -- but native stubs carry `return_borrows_from=None`, so `_register_call_result_borrow` registers no loan and `is_dangling_return(TpyMethodCall)` returns False. A local aliasing storage that then dies (local receiver) or is structurally mutated, or the alias returned/stored-in-field/inserted-into-container, escapes with no diagnostic. Broadened (not introduced) by the native-aliasing change -- before it the call copied, so there was no alias to track; same family as the dict-view field-escape entry below and the StrView/Span field gap. There is also no static check that a bare `-> V` native binding actually returns `V&` in C++ (a by-value binding mistyped `-> V` dangles silently -- caught here only because the full-suite audit happened to exercise it; see the `-> V`/`Own[V]` contract note in `docs/NATIVE_INTEROP.md`). Fix shape: give native `-> V` reference-returning methods a declared `return_borrows_from` (receiver borrow) so the escape/dangling checks have a root. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the native-return-convention fix.
- **[MED small] Storing a dict view in a FIELD escapes the view-lifetime checks: `self.view = d.items()` of a local dict dangles silently.** Returning a locally-backed view is caught (`is_borrowing_view_type` feeds provenance tracking and `is_dangling_return`), but field assignments bypass `check_dangling_reference`, and `check_escape` skips dict views because their TypeDef says `is_value_type=True`. Same pre-existing class as the StrView/Span field-storage gap, newly reachable for `dict_keys`/`dict_values`/`dict_items` since the views became first-class borrowing wrappers (dict-accessor aliasing work). Fix shape: route field stores of `is_borrowing_view_type` values through the same provenance check returns use (or reject storing views in fields outright until region tracking lands). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model).
- **[LOW small] An `@readonly` method that mutates `self` THROUGH a callable arg gets no sema diagnostic.** `def poke(self): self.cb(self.data)` where `cb: Callable[[list[Int32]], None]` and the stored callback mutates its arg: the callable-value call correctly marks `self.data` mutated (so const inference demotes `poke` when undecorated), but an explicit `@readonly` decorator on `poke` is not rejected at sema -- the only backstop is the C++ const-method check at build time. Same class as mutation through external/builtin callee facts (a readonly method calling an unknown-mutation callee), and a sibling of the readonly-vs-callable-field entry in the Compiler-bugs section (`Wrap[T]` / `cmp_to_key`). **Fix shape:** when the eager opaque-callee mutation mark sets `current_self_mutated` inside a method whose `@readonly` is explicit (not deduced), emit the same "readonly method mutates self" diagnostic the direct-mutation path raises. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the callable-values fix.
- **[MED small] `is_rvalue_source` classifies a subscript on an RVALUE container as a borrow -- binding it takes a pointer/reference into a dying temporary.** `tpyc/value_category.py` (~line 100) returns False for any plain `TpySubscript` without recursing into the container's own rvalue-ness (unlike the `TpyFieldAccess` arm), so `v = f()[0]` / `(v := f()[0])` with `f() -> Own[list[T]]` emits `&(f()[0])` (or a `T&` bind) into the expired temporary. Pre-existing for statement bindings; the walrus borrow arm widens reach to expression positions (conditions, argument lists). Fix (recurse + materialize, NOT copy): detect the rvalue container by recursing into `expr.obj` (mirror the `TpyFieldAccess` arm), then MATERIALIZE the rvalue container/receiver into an owning function-scope local and alias the element/borrow into it -- the exact idiom `match` already uses for a non-lvalue subject (`auto __match_subject = <temporary>`, `tpyc/codegen_cpp/match.py`, gated by `_match_subject_is_lvalue`, which is the same recursive lvalue-of-`expr.obj` test) and `_borrow_tuple_rhs`'s owning-call storage slot. This is NOT the "copy the element" path: a reference-type element is never duplicated -- we extend the CONTAINER's lifetime (own it in a slot) and the borrow aliases into it, matching CPython. Sound iff the borrow does not outlive the slot's scope: `return <borrow>` is already caught ("Cannot return local or temporary as reference"); field/container-store and yield-across-suspend of the borrow still ride the general escape guards (the liveness work). The expression-position case (`print(f()[0].x)`) needs the container hoisted at expression scope, not just at a var-decl -- more than `match` handles (it owns its whole statement). The narrow rvalue-receiver record-`__getitem__` REJECT landed on `fix-accessor-generic-returns` (`_tag_record_getitem`, `tpyc/sema/expressions.py`; test `tests/cases/generics/error_getitem_temp_receiver`) is the sound stopgap this general fix subsumes -- replace that reject with materialization here. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the branch-decl borrow-fact materialization; the receiver-materialization direction confirmed against `match`/tuple-RHS precedent in a follow-up /tpy-review discussion. **THIR note (wave 4):** the new pointer-repr `Optional[record]` `'subscript'` call-arg face (`_optional_ptr_arg_face`, `tpyc/thir/lower/predicates.py`) could have routed this dangling shape into a call-arg AND a resumable-frame-capture position (`takes_optional(make_list()[i])` -- worse there: the dangle persists across suspend/resume). That face now GATES against it -- it rejects unless the subscript container resolves through `_subscript_container_recv_type` (an lvalue name / admitted field), mirroring `_borrow_elem_subscript_shape`; an unproven-Optional container is also rejected (the AST `deref_check` would be dropped by `subscript_prechecked`). The AST path still has this root-cause gap (unfixed); THIR merely declines to route the dangerous shape.
- **[MED medium] An `@overload`-grouped borrowing callee escapes the auto-move borrow gate in BOTH directions.** Calls into an overload group resolve to the MATCHED stub FunctionInfo, but body-analysis facts land only on the group's last entry (`get_function(name)[-1]`, see the finalize sites in `tpyc/sema/analyzer.py`): non-last stubs keep `return_borrows_from=None` forever. Backward-defined overloaded callees therefore never register the call-result borrow (the gate misses a real borrow -- silent use-after-move family), and the forward-reference pending set (`_seed_pending_borrow_fact_fis`) seeds only `[-1]`, so the conservative `OPAQUE` path also never fires for a stub-resolved call. **Fix shape:** propagate finalize facts to every group member -- but the param indices of `return_borrows_from` are per-signature, so the propagation needs an index mapping from the implementation's params to each stub's (or per-stub body analysis for bodied stubs); naive copying is wrong for reordered/defaulted overloads. Until then, seeding all group FIs into the pending set alone would over-demote backward-defined overloads (permanent OPAQUE) and must not ship by itself. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the forward-ref gate fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A generator METHOD's receiver borrow is not represented, so consuming the receiver while its generator lives escapes the auto-move gate.** The generator frame stores `__self` by reference, but `return_borrows_from` cannot carry `-1` for generator methods: `-1` blocks readonly inference (a self-borrowing return pins non-const), and stamping it flips every generator method non-readonly -- observed breaking const callers of stdlib `re.Pattern.finditer`. So `g = obj.walk(...); take(obj); next(g)` silently iterates a moved-from receiver (same family as the iterable-param case, which IS gated via the param indices). **Fix shape:** either represent the receiver borrow in a channel that readonly inference ignores (a separate `frame_borrows_self` fact consumed by `_register_call_result_borrow`), or make the readonly-inference check distinguish "returns self" from "frame references self with the method's own constness". Surfaced implementing the generator-method borrow facts (forward-ref gate review round). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW medium] A borrowing callee in a cross-module import CYCLE escapes the auto-move borrow gate.** The same-module forward-reference hole is closed (the analyzer seeds `pending_borrow_fact_fis` so unanalyzed same-module callees register conservative `OPAQUE` borrows), but a cycle peer's function whose body is analyzed after the current module's leaves `return_borrows_from=None` at the bind with the FI outside the pending set -- no borrow is registered and a later consume of the argument still auto-moves while the bound result borrows it (silent use-after-move). Requires an import cycle plus a borrowing callee in the not-yet-analyzed peer; same staleness family as the cross-cycle readonly-method deferral. **Fix shape:** extend the pending-set seeding to cycle-peer modules (the compiler knows the SCC), or defer those binds to the workspace-wide finalize pass like cross-module borrow checks. Residual of the forward-ref gate fix (`fix-forward-ref-borrow-gate`). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A borrow hidden inside a NESTED call argument escapes the auto-move borrow gate (asyncio.create_task shape).** `task = asyncio.create_task(total(xs))` -- `total`'s coroutine temporary stores `xs` by reference, `create_task` retains the coroutine inside the returned Task, but `_register_call_result_borrow` only relates the bound name to the OUTER call's `return_borrows_from`, so no `task -> xs` borrow is registered and a later consume of `xs` moves it while the pending task still iterates it: TPy prints `1 0` where CPython prints `1 6` (bind create_task, consume `xs`, `await task`), no diagnostics. Direct coroutine binds (`c = total(xs)`) are unaffected -- the coroutine must-use rule rejects them. The general form is any `outer(inner(xs))` where `inner` borrows and `outer` retains its argument. Note: async defs also do not set the generator-style `return_borrows_from` for their reference-stored params (`analyzer.py` gates on `is_generator` only); that fact alone is dead code today because of the must-use rule, so set it together with the transitive registration, not separately. **Fix shape:** when an argument expression is itself a call whose `return_borrows_from` roots at a name, register the outer call's bound result as borrowing that root (conservative: the callee may retain the temporary). Residual of the audit root-cause #5 fix. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED medium] A loop-var alias outliving its loop escapes the auto-move borrow gate: consuming the iterable post-loop dangles the alias.** `for x in xs: last = x` then `drop(xs)` then `last.read()` -- `last` is a reference into `xs`'s element storage, but the only BorrowTracker edge on `xs` is the `"__for_iter"` sentinel, which the gate excludes (it never expires and would block consuming-iteration activation), and the prescan alias map relates `last` to `x`, not to `xs` (the loop-var -> iterable relation lives only in `loop_var_iterable`). The post-loop consume therefore still auto-moves `xs` while `last` dangles -- same silent use-after-move family as the forward-referenced-callee entry above. **Fix shape:** at for-loop analysis, register the loop var (and transitively its aliases) as a NAMED element-borrower of the iterable -- not the shared sentinel -- so the gate sees it; or fold `loop_var_iterable` into the gate's known-borrow sources. Real fix is loop-scoped borrow expiry in the MIR place/loan model. Residual of the audit root-cause #5 fix, surfaced by the /tpy-ready retrospective. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED] Non-scalar match arm bindings are `auto&` borrows into the subject's storage; mutating the subject root in the arm body (or guard) dangles them.** The visible and method-call mutation shapes are now WARNED; only mutation through an ALIAS of the subject and via an opaque (unknown-readonly) method remain silent. FREE-COPY-SCALAR bindings (fixed int, bool, `Char`, float, enum) are COPIED at the arm, so they survive subject mutation and match CPython at zero cost (a register move) -- this closed the originally-verified [HIGH] shapes, whose bound fields were scalar (`Int32`). The binding form is a single sema fact (`bind_by_value` on each capture node, set from `is_free_copy_scalar` via the shared `iter_capture_bindings` traversal); codegen's `auto`/`auto&` choice AND the dangle warning both read it, so they cannot drift -- no capture path can silently alias a scalar (a missed path defaults to a warned `auto&`, and a codegen emit site without the per-arm map asserts). The copy is deliberately restricted to scalars: copying a heap-backed value type (`str`, `BigInt`), a view, or a reference type would pessimize the common path (performance is design goal #1) or diverge from CPython aliasing, so those keep the zero-cost `auto&` borrow and dangle if the subject root is mutated while the binding lives. The stopgap (`_warn_arm_subject_mutation`, gated on a non-scalar binding being present) now warns on: assignment to the subject path/owner prefix, an invalidating container method on the subject base (both synchronous), AND a non-readonly method call whose receiver prefix-aliases the subject (deferred to `finalize_borrow_checks` once readonly is settled; `resolve_pending_match_subject_checks`). Pointer-variant LOCAL subjects are safe (slot model). **Remaining SILENT residual:** mutation through an ALIAS of the subject (`p = h; p.swap()` / `p.pet = ...` -- needs alias tracking) and via an opaque method whose readonly verdict is unknown (`is_readonly is None`, e.g. some `@native`). **Sound fix:** register the surviving non-scalar arm bindings (and `__match_subject` for field/element sources) as loans of the subject root in the BorrowTracker for the arm's extent, and reject root mutation while they live -- a compile-time check, never a runtime copy (a copy would violate the performance goal for `str`/`BigInt`). Needs the MIR `Place`/`LoanInfo` model with arm-EXTENT-aware loans, which the current whole-function mutation facts cannot express (the deferred-check resolver is extent-blind) -- an IR-migration deliverable (see `docs/IR_DESIGN.md`). Dropped from [HIGH] to [MED] now that the verified silent shapes (scalar copy; non-scalar method-call) are closed; the remaining alias/opaque residual is rarer. Audit finding G2.
- **[LOW small, deferred: MIR] readonly is not projected onto a NESTED borrow-form tuple element on subscript.** `t[i]` where `t: readonly[tuple[..., tuple[Int32, Box], ...]]` returns the inner `tuple[Int32, Box]` without `readonly`, so a write through `t[i][1].field` is not rejected at sema. Root cause: the four subscript readonly-projection sites in `tpyc/sema/expressions.py` (tuple, container, protocol, record) all gate on `not elem.is_value_type()`, and `TupleType.is_value_type()` is unconditionally True -- so a tuple element (even one with borrow-form sub-elements) is treated as a copied value and skipped. The non-nested cases work (`readonly[tuple[Box, ...]]` projects `readonly[Box]`). Same family as the `readonly[list[tuple[...]]]` element-write entry above. **Fix shape:** unify the four gates to project readonly when the element is a borrow-form tuple (`TupleType.has_ref_elements()`), not only when `not is_value_type()`. Surfaced by /tpy-review (architecture-fit) of the readonly-tuple-projection fix.
- **[MED, deferred: MIR] General destructive move-out of aggregate members is unimplemented beyond the whole-rvalue-tuple-unpack slice.** Moving ownership *out* of an aggregate member is supported only for whole-tuple unpack of a fresh rvalue (`a, b = make_pair()` distributes each `Own[T]` element to a movable owned local -- shipped). Named-source whole-tuple unpack (`t = make_pair(); a, b = t`) is now supported (the source is moved into the temp at its last use). Still unsupported: partial element move-out (`take(pair[0])` leaving `pair[1]` live), record-field move-out (`take(rec.field)` for a `@nocopy` field), constant/dynamic-index move-out. These need per-place partial-move state tracking: representing `pair[0]` / `rec.field` as individually movable places with their own consumed flags, propagating "partially moved" up to the aggregate (so the whole value can't be used/moved once a part is), flow-sensitive merge of partial-move state across branches, use-after-partial-move diagnostics, and rejection of un-trackable dynamic-index moves -- the `Place(Local/Field/Index/...)` move-state model the borrow-tracker/MIR notes describe as future work. Reading members in place (borrow) is unaffected. Surfaced implementing the narrow whole-tuple-unpack slice (Send/Sync Phase 4 channel). **Fix shape:** add per-place move-state tracking to the borrow/move analyzer; until then multi-owner handoff routes through whole-tuple unpack or `Rc.clone()`. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug` (large; architectural).
- **[LOW small]** A generator that captures a `str` parameter as a `std::string_view` and is iterated later than its source's lifetime dangles, with no diagnostic. `re.Pattern.finditer` (now a true generator) takes `subject: str` and holds the view across suspensions; `for m in p.finditer(make_temp_string()): ...` (or storing the generator and iterating after the source string is gone) reads freed storage. Instance of the unenforced view-lifetime model (the type system doesn't tie a captured view's lifetime to the generator object). Pre-existing in kind, but the generator form widens the window vs the old eager-`list` `finditer`, which consumed the view immediately. Reachable for any generator (free function or method) that captures a `str`/view param and outlives the argument. Fix shape belongs with the broader view-lifetime work (tie captured-view provenance to the returned generator's lifetime). Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the finditer-as-generator change.
- **[LOW small]** A generator that captures a `*args` varargs pack by value and then escapes its defining frame dangles, with no diagnostic. `_build_capture_list` (`tpyc/codegen_cpp/gen_generators.py`) captures a `tpy::varargs<...>` pack by value into the generator lambda; the pack's `indirect_` member points into the caller's `__tmp_N` stack array (`std::array<T*, N>` -- the primary `varargs<T>` template's indirect form, NOT the direct-only value specialization). `__tmp_N` lives at the caller's frame scope, so iterating the generator within that frame is sound (the common `for x in merge(...)` / `list(merge(...))`), but a generator that ESCAPES the frame -- `g = merge(a, b); return g`, or storing it in a longer-lived container -- leaves the captured pack pointing at freed stack storage. No TPy diagnostic. Same unenforced view-lifetime family as the captured-`str`-view generator entry above; the type system doesn't tie the captured pack's lifetime to the generator object. Reachable for any generator with a varargs param that outlives its call frame. Fix shape belongs with the broader view-lifetime / region work (tie captured-pack provenance to the returned generator's lifetime) -- IR-gated, same region/lifetime family as the deferred create_task escape. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of heapq.merge's varargs generator.
- **[LOW small] (precision, not unsound)** A `@native` method emitted through the frontend-IR plugin path (`tpyc/frontend_ir/lower.py` `_route_decorators` -> `BUILTIN_LOWERING(tpy.native)`) cannot express `native_preserves_refs`. That property is set by a *separate* decorator (`@native_preserves_refs`, qname `tpy.extern.native_preserves_refs`) which is not in `CORE_DECORATORS`, so a plugin can't emit it -- `_route_decorators` rejects it as an unknown decorator. `_is_invalidating_method` (`tpyc/sema/methods.py`) reads `native_preserves_refs` only for *builtin* records; plugin-emitted natives are user-defined and take the `direct_self_mutated` fallback, so the borrow checker conservatively treats every plugin-lowered native method as invalidating iterators/borrowed refs, potentially yielding spurious invalidation diagnostics. Latent: no in-tree plugin emits native methods yet. Fix shape: add a `native_preserves_refs` entry to `CORE_DECORATORS` (or a `_NativeSpec` kwarg) and thread it onto `TpyFunction.native_preserves_refs` in lowering. Surfaced by `/tpy-review` (safety-model).
- **[LOW small] Temporary-rvalue-into-view-storage is rejected for `list`/`set` `append`/`add`/`insert` only; the sibling sinks are uncovered.** The view-lifetime fix (audit #10 item 5) rejects `list[StrView].append(make())` / `set.add` / `list.insert` of a temporary, but the same dangling-view store is still silently accepted for: `dict[StrView, ...]` / `dict[..., StrView]` literal keys/values and `d[k] = make()`, `list.extend([make()])`, list/set/dict LITERAL elements (`[make()]` typed `list[StrView]`), and `tuple`/comprehension element stores. (StrView FIELD stores are the separate Future field-lifetime feature, below.) Param/local-derived views stored into an ESCAPING container are also still accepted -- that needs container-lifetime (region) analysis, not just rvalue rejection. **Fix shape:** route every element-into-view-storage coercion through one chokepoint that rejects a temporary source (extend the `_view_source_is_temporary` check at the literal-element and dict/extend coercion sites); overlaps audit #11 (literal elements copy). Surfaced implementing audit #10 item 5. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An explicit `s: str = <view>` annotation is silently downgraded to view storage, binding owned-copy temporary into a `std::string_view` -- dangling.** `def use(sv: StrView): s: str = sv` emits `std::string_view s = std::string(sv);` -- the `str` (owned) annotation is overridden to view storage by the pending-view machinery (`is_view_compatible_source` returns view-safe for a view-typed source, so `initialized_from_owned=False`), yet the source coercion still materializes an owned `std::string(sv)` temporary; the result is a view into a destroyed temporary. Same for `s: str = make().strip()`. Root cause: the pending-view storage decision ignores an explicit OWNED annotation (it should force owned storage when the user wrote `str`/`bytes`), and the storage-vs-coercion choice is uncoordinated. Pre-existing; surfaced probing the view-lifetime cluster (audit #10). **Fix shape:** when the local has an explicit owned (`str`/`bytes`) annotation, resolve to owned storage unconditionally (no view downgrade); keep the owned-copy coercion. Distinct from the inferred-local promotion (#10 item 3), which routes `initialized_from_owned=True` to owned storage and is unaffected. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[LOW small] (precision, not unsound)** `await x` reads the awaitable's `__poll__` `is_readonly` at Phase-1 analysis time (`_analyze_await` -> `_extract_awaitable_inner`, `tpyc/sema/expressions.py`); auto-readonly is a Phase-2 fixpoint fact. A *user-defined* awaitable whose `__poll__` is only inferred `@readonly` in Phase 2 (no `@readonly` annotation, no direct mutation) reads `is_readonly=False` here, so `_mark_await_operand_mutated` over-marks the awaited operand's root -- blocking the enclosing async method's auto-readonly inference even though awaiting that awaitable doesn't actually mutate it. Over-approximation: conservative and never unsound (a method wrongly kept non-readonly, never wrongly readonly), and stdlib awaitables (`Task`/`Future`/`Event`/`SleepFuture`) all mutate in `__poll__` so they're unaffected. Fix shape: defer the await-mutation mark to Phase 2 via a call edge (mirroring `_is_self_call_deferred` for `self.method()`), or consult the Phase-2-finalized `__poll__` readonly verdict. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the await-self-mutation fix.
- **[LOW small] (by design, not a regression) Post-sema `replace_expr` injects AST that bypasses sema re-analysis.** `PostSemaFunctionMacroContext.replace_expr` (inherited from `FunctionMacroContext`, `tpyc/macro_api.py`) splices nodes after pass 7, so the injected nodes get no mutation-fact collection (no `call_edges` for new calls -> Phase-2 propagation can miss a mutating call -- `PostSemaFunctionMacroContext.note_param_mutated` is a manual escape hatch for the param-rooted mutating-call sub-case, but it only seeds `direct_mutated_params`, not `direct_structural_mutated_params`, so an emitted *structurally*-mutating call (append/insert/clear on a param) is still unrecorded), no borrow-check registration, no `deref_check` insertion, and no narrowing/readonly enforcement. The contract is documented (the caller must `set_expr_type` every emitted node and is responsible for safety), and this is inherent to the whole macro system (pre-sema `replace_expr`, builder-trace `emit_function`, and class macros already inject un-rechecked AST) -- a known limitation, not a defect introduced here. A macro that injects only value-type expressions (the shipped test) is safe. **Fix shape (future):** restrict the post-sema surface to value-type replacements (enforce via `type_of`/`set_expr_type`), or re-run the relevant sema checks over injected subtrees once the IR makes that cheap. Surfaced by /tpy-review (safety-model) of the post-sema function-macro phase.
- **[MED, latent] A view param (`Span[T]`) stored into a field passes sema with no diagnostic -- the field dangles when the caller's buffer dies (silent UAF).** `def keep(self, v: Span[Int32]) -> None: self._s = v` compiles clean; the stored span outlives whatever backing the caller passed. At the `@export` CPython boundary the glue's copy-in vector dies when the wrapper returns, making the store an unconditional UAF on the next payload read for any exposed METHOD taking a `Span` param (property `Span` setters are rejected outright for exactly this reason -- a setter's purpose is storing -- so only the method surface remains exposed). The existing view-escape entries in this section cover the RETURN direction; this is the param-stored-into-field direction. Not expressible as a passing test (silent UAF). Fix shape: reject (or warn on) a view-typed param assigned into field storage, pending the region/lifetime model's loan-extent form. Probe: a `Span` setter body `self._s = v` compiled with zero diagnostics. Surfaced by /tpy-review (codegen-correctness, safety-model) of the exposed-class @property rung. Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
- **[MED small] An `Own[readonly[@dynamic P]]` param emits uncompilable C++ (abstract-base rvalue-ref signature), no sema diagnostic.** `def park(coro: Own[readonly[Cancellable[Int32]]]) -> None` renders the param as `::tpystd::coro::Cancellable<::tpy::BigInt>&& coro` -- the abstract base by rvalue reference, not the erased `unique_ptr<P>` -- because the ReadonlyType wrapper defeats `is_dyn_protocol` in `_gen_dynamic_protocol_arg`'s slot key, so the Own-dyn erasure arm never fires anywhere downstream: the call site passes the concrete coro frame (or the handle's optional slot) bare, which cannot bind the abstract-base ref (the concrete frame does not inherit it). Almost certainly a g++ error on any use; no TPy diagnostic. Related family to the readonly-protocol entries above but a distinct shape (the Own+readonly composition). THIR mirrors the AST's slot key (RAW wrapped, no readonly unwrap) so both paths stay byte-identical and the fix lands once, at the AST arm. Fix shape: either reject `Own[readonly[@dynamic P]]` params in sema (readonly-ness of an owned erased handle has no meaningful surface) or peel the readonly in the slot key and the signature render together. Surfaced by the THIR Own-arg cell's adversarial probe (2026-07-17). Needs `/tpy-fix-bug`.
